# ESP32 Pitfall Audit

Track A of the overnight audit. See `docs/OVERNIGHT_AUDIT_BACKLOG.md` for the run protocol.

**Target platform (established in A0):**
- Chip: **ESP32-S3** (`CONFIG_IDF_TARGET_ESP32S3=y`), Xtensa LX7, dual-core
- ESP-IDF: **v5.5.1**
- Secondary target: ESP32 (`sdkconfig.defaults.esp32` exists — board-gated code applies)
- Notable components: `esp32-camera ^2.0.0`, `libsodium ^1.0.20`, `esp-tflite-micro ^1.3.0`, `esp-sr ^1.4.0` (optional)
- Arduino APIs in use (`String`, `millis()`) — Arduino-as-component layering applies

Pitfalls fixed before v5.5 are excluded. Pitfalls specific to single-core targets, ESP32-C3/H2/P4,
or to IDF v4.x APIs are excluded unless the ESP32 secondary target reintroduces them.

---

# Section 1 — The checklist

45 pitfalls, grouped. Each has a **Check** line describing how to test this codebase against it.
Later Track A items (A1–A21) work through these; A22 sweeps whatever A1–A21 didn't reach.

The `Covered by` column maps each pitfall to the backlog item that owns it, so nothing is
double-audited and nothing is orphaned.

---

## 1. Memory placement and cache

### P1 — Non-IRAM ISR runs while flash cache is disabled → Cache Error panic
Any interrupt handler registered **without** `ESP_INTR_FLAG_IRAM` may execute during an SPI flash
write/erase, when cache is disabled. If its code or any data it touches lives in flash, the CPU
faults. This is the single most common "crashes only sometimes, only under load" bug on ESP32.
**Check:** every `esp_intr_alloc`, `gpio_isr_handler_add`, `timer_isr_callback_add`, and any
`IRAM_ATTR` function — confirm the flag is set AND that everything the ISR calls/reads is also
in IRAM/DRAM (including `const` tables, which default to flash).
*Covered by: A2, A3*

### P2 — PSRAM is inaccessible while cache is disabled
Same window as P1, but wider: **all** PSRAM reads/writes fault during flash operations, not just
code. An ISR touching a PSRAM buffer is a latent crash even if the ISR itself is in IRAM.
**Check:** cross-reference IRAM ISRs against buffers allocated with `MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM` or
`ps_malloc`. Given this project's heavy PSRAM offload work, this is a high-yield check.
*Covered by: A2, A4*

### P3 — `const` data silently lives in flash
`static const` tables land in DROM. Reading one from an ISR during a flash operation faults.
**Check:** `const` arrays/strings referenced from any `IRAM_ATTR` function; look for
`DRAM_ATTR` on the ones that need it.
*Covered by: A2*

### P4 — PSRAM is not DMA-capable by default
DMA buffers must be `MALLOC_CAP_DMA`. On S3, PSRAM DMA works only with correct alignment and
the EDMA path; a plain `MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM` buffer handed to a DMA-capable peripheral (camera,
I2S, SPI) either fails or corrupts silently.
**Check:** every buffer passed to camera / I2S / SPI / ADC-DMA — trace its allocation caps.
*Covered by: A4*

### P5 — Code cannot execute from PSRAM
Function pointers into PSRAM, or any attempt to place executable content there, fault.
**Check:** confirm nothing in the LLM engine or TFLite paths assumes executable PSRAM.
*Covered by: A4*

### P6 — Internal DRAM exhaustion presents as random allocation failure, not OOM
Total free heap can look healthy while `MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL` largest-free-block is tiny. WiFi/BT
allocate internal-only. The failure surfaces far from the cause.
**Check:** are `heap_caps_get_free_size(MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL)` and
`heap_caps_get_largest_free_block()` monitored? Are allocation-failure paths handled or ignored?
*Covered by: A5*

### P7 — Heap fragmentation from long-running alloc/free churn
Devices run for days then fail to allocate despite free heap. Classic cause: repeated
create/destroy of medium buffers, or `String` concatenation in a loop.
**Check:** hot paths that allocate per-iteration; prior work in `docs/HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT.md`
is PAUSED, so note what it never reached.
*Covered by: A5, A15*

### P8 — Static IRAM growth silently shrinks available DRAM
Every `IRAM_ATTR` function moves bytes out of the DRAM pool. Adding IRAM to fix P1 costs heap.
**Check:** count `IRAM_ATTR` usage; compare against build size output if available.
*Covered by: A5*

### P9 — Task stacks consume internal DRAM and cannot go in PSRAM
Known project constraint. Stacks are internal-only in practice on this hardware.
**Check:** total static stack footprint across all `xTaskCreate*` calls.
*Covered by: A1, A5*

---

## 2. Tasks, stacks, watchdogs

### P10 — Stack overflow corrupts the neighbouring allocation before it's detected
Detection is best-effort: the 32-byte watchpoint catches overruns up to ~28 bytes past the
boundary; a large local array jumps clean over the guard and corrupts the heap instead.
**Check:** large stack locals (arrays, structs, `char buf[512]`), deep recursion, and whether
`CONFIG_FREERTOS_CHECK_STACKOVERFLOW` is set to canary vs watchpoint vs none.
*Covered by: A1, A21*

### P11 — Stack sizes never measured
`uxTaskGetStackHighWaterMark()` is the only way to know real headroom.
**Check:** is it called anywhere? Is there a diagnostic surface for it?
**Project-specific footgun:** in this repo the `*_STACK_WORDS` constants are actually **byte**
counts — a 4× misreading in either direction is possible.
*Covered by: A1*

### P12 — Task function returns instead of calling `vTaskDelete(NULL)`
Falling off the end of a task function causes an `IllegalInstruction` panic.
**Check:** every task entry function — confirm it never returns.
*Covered by: A1, A7*

### P13 — Task WDT fires because a task never yields
Any loop without `vTaskDelay`, or a blocking call longer than the TWDT timeout, on a
WDT-subscribed task. Busy-wait loops and long `while(!done)` polls are the usual culprits.
**Check:** every `while`/`for` loop in a task body without a delay or a blocking primitive;
confirm which tasks are TWDT-subscribed and what the timeout is.
*Covered by: A6, A7*

### P14 — Interrupt WDT fires from an over-long ISR
ISRs must be short. Logging, heap allocation, and blocking calls in an ISR all violate this.
**Check:** ISR bodies for `printf`/`ESP_LOG*`/`malloc`/mutex-take/`vTaskDelay`.
*Covered by: A2, A6*

### P15 — Wrong FreeRTOS API from ISR context
`xQueueSend` instead of `xQueueSendFromISR`, missing `portYIELD_FROM_ISR`, taking a mutex
(illegal from ISR — mutexes support priority inheritance and cannot be used in ISR context).
**Check:** every ISR — confirm `...FromISR` variants throughout, and that the higher-priority-
task-woken flag is actually propagated.
*Covered by: A2*

### P16 — Repeated task create/destroy fragments the heap
Suspension is cheaper than recreation.
**Check:** `xTaskCreate` calls not at init time. Project preference already documented: avoid
spawning a worker task per UI action.
*Covered by: A7, A19*

### P17 — Priority inversion and starvation
A high-priority task spinning on a resource held by a low-priority one. Also: a high-priority
task that never blocks starves everything below it on that core.
**Check:** priority assignments vs blocking behaviour; any task at high priority with a
non-blocking loop.
*Covered by: A7*

### P18 — Bootloader/RTC watchdog timeout on a slow init path
Long work before `app_main` returns, or a slow init step, trips the RTC WDT at boot — presents
as a bootloop with no useful backtrace.
**Check:** total work in the boot path; any network/filesystem operation before the scheduler
is properly running.
*Covered by: A17, A6*

---

## 3. Dual-core / concurrency

### P19 — Disabling interrupts is NOT a critical section on SMP
The other core keeps running. Only a spinlock (`portMUX_TYPE` /
`taskENTER_CRITICAL(&mux)`) provides mutual exclusion across cores.
**Check:** any `portDISABLE_INTERRUPTS` / `noInterrupts()` used as if it were a lock.
*Covered by: A8*

### P20 — Scheduler suspension is per-core on ESP-IDF FreeRTOS
`vTaskSuspendAll()` suspends only the calling core. It is not mutual exclusion.
**Check:** `vTaskSuspendAll` / `taskDISABLE_INTERRUPTS` used for data protection.
*Covered by: A8*

### P21 — Shared variables without `volatile` or atomics
Cross-core reads may see stale cached values; read-modify-write on a shared counter is not
atomic. `volatile` alone fixes visibility, not atomicity.
**Check:** globals written by one task/ISR and read by another. `volatile bool` flags are
usually fine; `volatile int` counters incremented from two contexts are not.
*Covered by: A8*

### P22 — Non-atomic multi-field state updates
A struct updated field-by-field and read concurrently yields a torn, internally inconsistent
snapshot. Very common with sensor readings and status structs.
**Check:** shared structs — is there a lock, a sequence counter, or a double-buffer?
Project note: GPS is documented as the only sensor with a proper snapshot accessor, which
implies the others may be exposed.
*Covered by: A8*

### P23 — Mismatched or recursive spinlock use
Different spinlock on enter vs exit; recursive critical-section entry causing deadlock.
**Check:** `taskENTER_CRITICAL`/`taskEXIT_CRITICAL` pairs.
*Covered by: A8*

### P24 — Core-pinning assumptions that aren't enforced
Code that assumes it runs on a specific core (for cache, timing, or peripheral affinity) but is
created with `tskNO_AFFINITY`.
**Check:** the documented policy is compute=core1, I/O=core0, all tasks pinned. Verify every
`xTaskCreate` actually uses `xTaskCreatePinnedToCore` with the right core.
*Covered by: A7*

---

## 4. Peripherals and radio

### P25 — Legacy I2C driver deprecated in favour of `driver/i2c_master.h`
The old `driver/i2c.h` command-list API is deprecated as of 5.2 and emits build warnings.
Not urgent, but it constrains future IDF upgrades.
**Check:** which driver this project uses; whether both are linked (a known cause of the
warning firing even after migration).
*Covered by: A10*

### P26 — I2C bus hang with no recovery path
A slave holding SDA low wedges the bus permanently. Recovery requires clocking SCL manually or
`i2c_master_bus_reset`.
**Check:** is there a recovery routine? What happens on `ESP_ERR_TIMEOUT`?
*Covered by: A10*

### P27 — Missing I2C timeouts / infinite blocking
`portMAX_DELAY` on an I2C transaction blocks forever if the device is absent.
**Check:** every I2C call's timeout argument; behaviour when a device is unplugged.
*Covered by: A10*

### P28 — Shared-bus arbitration between multiple devices/tasks
Two tasks driving the same I2C or SPI bus without a mutex interleave transactions.
**Check:** the I2C manager's locking; whether every caller goes through it.
*Covered by: A10*

### P29 — ADC2 is unusable while WiFi is active
Hardware conflict. Reads fail with `ESP_ERR_TIMEOUT` when WiFi holds the ADC2 arbiter.
**Check:** any ADC2 channel usage; battery/analog sensing pins.
*Covered by: A11, A20*

### P30 — Strapping pins repurposed as GPIO
On S3, boot mode depends on GPIO0/45/46 state at reset. Driving them externally or configuring
them with the wrong pull can prevent boot.
**Check:** pin assignments across all board variants against the S3 strapping list.
*Covered by: A20*

### P31 — Octal PSRAM steals GPIO35–37 on ESP32-S3
Boards with octal PSRAM cannot use those pins. Quad PSRAM boards can.
**Check:** relevant given FeatherS3 (quad) is primary and XIAO (octal) is a stretch goal — any
pin map that works on one and silently breaks the other.
*Covered by: A20*

### P32 — ESP-NOW and WiFi station must share a channel
When connected to an AP, the station's home channel wins. An ESP-NOW peer registered on a
different channel silently stops receiving. Peers registered with `channel = 0` follow the
current channel, which is usually what you want.
**Check:** peer registration channel values; what happens to ESP-NOW when WiFi connects,
disconnects, or roams. Cross-check `docs/WIFI_RADIO_OWNERSHIP_PLAN.md`.
*Covered by: A11*

### P33 — WiFi power-save drops ESP-NOW packets
With modem sleep, the radio is off between DTIM beacons; unicast ESP-NOW to a sleeping peer is
lost. In coexist mode WiFi sleeps during non-WiFi slices even with `WIFI_PS_NONE`.
**Check:** `esp_wifi_set_ps` usage; whether ESP-NOW reliability assumptions account for it.
*Covered by: A11, A18*

### P34 — WiFi/BLE coexistence starves one radio
Both share the 2.4 GHz front end. Heavy BLE traffic degrades WiFi throughput and vice versa.
**Check:** coexistence config; BLE notify throughput assumptions while WiFi is active.
*Covered by: A11*

### P35 — WiFi channels 12–14 are unreliable in practice
Region-dependent; some APs on high channels never associate.
**Check:** any channel selection logic that can land above 11.
*Covered by: A11*

---

## 5. Storage and persistence

### P36 — NVS is for infrequently-changing config only
High-frequency writes wear the partition. NVS needs ≥3 pages (4 KB each), one always reserved.
**Check:** write frequency of hot settings; anything written per-loop or per-event.
*Covered by: A12*

### P37 — Sharing the default NVS partition with WiFi/BT
ESP-IDF components use `nvs` themselves. Application data there competes for space and
complicates wear management; separate partitions are recommended for differing update rates.
**Check:** partition table; which namespace/partition app settings use.
*Covered by: A12*

### P38 — Non-atomic file writes lose data on power loss
Truncate-then-write leaves a zero-length or partial file if power drops mid-write. The safe
pattern is write-temp → fsync → rename.
**Check:** every settings/state file writer. High-value given this project persists a lot of
JSON state.
*Covered by: A13, C2*

### P39 — No handling for a full or corrupt filesystem
Writes fail silently; mount fails at boot with no fallback.
**Check:** return values on `fopen`/`fwrite`/`fclose`; mount-failure path; free-space checks
before large writes (sensor logs especially).
*Covered by: A13, A9*

### P40 — Flash write blocks the whole system
Erase of a 4 KB sector takes milliseconds and disables cache (see P1/P2). Large writes stall
real-time paths and can trip watchdogs.
**Check:** file writes on paths with timing requirements; log flushing during BLE/ESP-NOW
activity.
*Covered by: A3, A6*

---

## 6. Correctness and general robustness

### P41 — `esp_err_t` return values discarded
The most common bug class in IDF code. A failed init returns an error nobody reads, and the
failure surfaces much later as a crash.
**Check:** unchecked calls to IDF APIs; rank by consequence rather than counting.
*Covered by: A9*

### P42 — `millis()` rollover at ~49.7 days
Safe: `(uint32_t)(millis() - start) >= interval`. Unsafe: `millis() > deadline`, or storing
timestamps in `int`/`long` and comparing signed.
**Check:** every timestamp variable's type and every comparison form. A `signed` timestamp
breaks at ~24.8 days.
*Covered by: A16*

### P43 — Unbounded string/buffer operations
`strcpy`, `strcat`, `sprintf`, `gets`, and `memcpy` with a length derived from input.
Stack-resident targets additionally cause stack smashing.
**Check:** all of the above; prioritize anything fed by ESP-NOW frames, web params, CLI args,
or file contents.
*Covered by: A14, B15*

### P44 — `snprintf` truncation treated as success
`snprintf` returns the length it *would* have written. Ignoring it means silent truncation of
commands, paths, and JSON.
**Check:** `snprintf` return values, especially where the result is a command or a file path.
*Covered by: A14*

### P45 — Brownout resets misdiagnosed as firmware bugs
Radio TX current spikes on a weak supply trigger the brownout detector. Presents as random
reboots correlated with WiFi/BLE activity.
**Check:** brownout detector config in sdkconfig; whether reset reason is logged and surfaced.
Cross-check the known `setCpuFrequencyMhz` vs `gps_task` power issue.
*Covered by: A18, A21*

# Section 2 — Findings

Findings from A1–A22 are appended below, newest last.

<!-- APPEND FINDINGS BELOW THIS LINE -->

## Run status — READ THIS FIRST

The audit run was **cut short by a monthly spend limit**, not completed. Of 33 planned finder agents,
**one finished**: A8 (cross-core concurrency, pitfalls P19–P23). The other 32 finders, all adversarial
verifiers, all completeness critics, and the synthesis writers were killed mid-flight.

**These findings have NOT been through adversarial verification.** The workflow reported them as
"survived" only because the verifier agents died and the code defaults to keeping a finding when its
verification fails. Treat everything below as single-source.

Mitigating evidence: findings 1, 4 and 7 were spot-checked by hand afterwards and all three hold up
exactly as described. The finder also produced an unusually disciplined "verified clean" list
(reproduced below) and an honest not-covered section, which are both good signals for the rest.

**Still unaudited:** every other Track A area (stacks, ISR safety, flash/cache, PSRAM, DRAM,
watchdogs, FreeRTOS misuse, esp_err_t, I2C, radio, GPIO, storage, bounds, time, sdkconfig), the
entire Track B security review, and all of Track C.

---

## A8 — Cross-core and cross-task data races (P19–P23)

Eleven findings. The theme: this codebase has good synchronization primitives and uses them correctly
in most places — the defects are all **specific structures that were left outside a convention the rest
of the tree follows**. Several are one file away from a sibling that does it right.

### [HIGH] `GPSTrackManager::clearTrack()` retires the pointer before the count
- **Where:** `System_Maps.cpp:2903` (writer); `System_Maps.cpp:3099` `renderTrack` (reader);
  `System_Maps.cpp:2838-2864` `loadTrack` (same shape); `WebPage_Maps.cpp:130,172,219` (web reader)
- **What:** `_points` and `_pointCount` are plain non-volatile statics with no mutex. `clearTrack()`
  does `free(_points); _points = nullptr;` **first**, then `_pointCount = 0`. There is a guaranteed
  window where `_points == nullptr` while `_pointCount > 0`. `renderTrack()` tests `_pointCount < 2`
  once on entry, then dereferences `_points[i]` inside a loop that calls `geoToScreen()` and
  `drawLine()` between iterations — external calls, so the compiler must reload `_points` from memory
  every pass rather than caching it in a register.
- **Why it matters:** `renderTrack` runs on the `mapRender` task (core 1). `clearTrack` is reachable
  from `cmd_gpstrack` on cmd_exec_task (core 0), from the OLED map UI on loopTask, and from
  `handleGPSTracksAPI` on the httpd task — which is the one `tskNO_AFFINITY` task in the system. Run
  `gpstrack clear` while the OLED map is drawing a loaded track and core 0 frees the buffer between
  two iterations of the core-1 render loop: the next `_points[i].lat` reads address 0 and panics with
  LoadProhibited. If the store lands after the reload instead, the loop walks freed heap for up to
  `MAX_TRACK_POINTS` entries. The web reader is worse in duration — it captures the raw pointer into a
  local and then holds it across hundreds of milliseconds of blocking chunked socket writes while any
  of four `clearTrack()` callers can free it underneath.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — spot-checked by hand, the ordering and the reader loop are exactly as described.
- **Known?** `docs/LAZY_ALLOCATION_AUDIT.md:110` notes only the benign form (httpd reader vs GPS-task
  `appendPoint`). It does not mention `clearTrack()`/`loadTrack()` freeing the buffer, does not mention
  the mapRender reader, and frames it as a prerequisite for a *future* change. The dangerous variant
  already shipped.
- **Board:** all builds with `ENABLE_MAPS` (FeatherS3 has it); the web path applies to every build with the HTTP server.
- **Direction:** the store order is backwards for any lock-free reader — the count must retire before
  the pointer. Beyond that the buffer needs one owner and one gate, and a plain mutex conflicts with
  the web handler's blocking writes, so this likely wants a publish/retire discipline where readers
  take a reference and the free is deferred.

### [HIGH] G2 teardown destroys per-temple objects without quiescing the workers using them
- **Where:** `G2_Glasses.cpp:8700` `deinitG2Client`; `:8316-8331` `templeReset`; `:8938-8951`
  `bleConnectShutdown`; `:7554` `sendEnvelopeEx`; `:13720` `closeg2 full`
- **What:** `deinitG2Client()` runs `g2Disconnect(); stopHeartbeatTimer(); templeReset(gL);
  templeReset(gR); bleConnectShutdown(); g2SessionShutdown();`. `templeReset()` does `free(t.rxBuf)`,
  `vSemaphoreDelete(t.writeMutex)` and `delete t.client`. `g2Disconnect()` explicitly does **not** stop
  any worker — its own comment says the connect worker "keeps running for future submissions". The
  workers that use those objects (`g2_session_w`, `g2_tap_disp`, `g2_page_swap_w`, `g2_hb_worker`,
  `g2-fsm`) are all pinned to core 1 and reach `sendEnvelopeEx()`, which blocks up to 1500 ms in
  `xSemaphoreTake(t.writeMutex, …)`. `deinitG2Client` runs on cmd_exec_task, core 0.
- **Why it matters:** with the glasses connected and any G2 page live, run `g2deinit`, `closeg2 full`,
  or switch BLE to server mode. Core 0 calls `vSemaphoreDelete(t.writeMutex)` while the core-1 session
  worker is blocked *inside that semaphore's queue structure* — the queue control block and its task
  list are freed under a blocked task. `bleConnectShutdown()` has an independent failure: an
  unconditional `vTaskDelete` with no abort and no wait, so if that task is inside `sendEnvelopeEx`
  holding `bleCentralTx`, the gate is never released and every subsequent G2 **and R1** write fails
  with "Central TX busy" for the rest of the boot. The ordering is also self-inconsistent:
  `g2SessionShutdown()` does abort-then-wait properly, but it runs *after* `templeReset` already
  destroyed the semaphore its worker may be sleeping on.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED (not independently re-checked by hand).
- **Board:** any build with `ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES` — FeatherS3 today.
- **Direction:** teardown must quiesce before it destroys — park every worker that can enter
  `sendEnvelopeEx`, confirm the notify path is idle, then release per-temple objects. Workers first,
  objects last. `bleConnectShutdown` should follow the abort-then-wait shape `g2SessionShutdown`
  already implements.

### [MEDIUM] OLED banner queue mutated cross-core, 350 lines from a spinlock-protected sibling
- **Where:** `OLED_UI.cpp:526` push, `:544` pump, `:554` `oledPairingRibbonShow`, `:746` banner update;
  producers at `System_ESPNow.cpp:14383` and `i2csensor_rda5807.cpp:698`
- **What:** `oledBannerQueuePush()` and `oledBannerQueuePump()` both read-modify-write `sBannerQHead`
  and `sBannerQCount` (plain `static uint8_t`) with no synchronization, and push does a 128-byte
  `strncpy` into the slot pump is reading. Pump and render run on loopTask (core 1); producers include
  `cmd_espnowremote` on cmd_exec_task (core 0) and `fmradio_task`. **350 lines earlier in the same
  file**, `oledToastShow/Clear/Active` protect an equivalent structure with `sToastMux`.
- **Why it matters:** `espnowremote` from any surface while a banner animates means core 0 does
  `sBannerQCount++` while core 1 does `sBannerQCount--`; one update is lost. A lost decrement replays a
  zero-initialized BSS slot as an empty banner and burns the animation window; a lost increment drops
  the notification silently. Display corruption and lost notifications, not memory unsafety — the ring
  index is always taken modulo `BANNER_QUEUE_MAX`.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED (not independently re-checked).
- **Direction:** the same short portMUX the toast already has in this file, on both push and pump.

### [MEDIUM] `allocSeq()` non-atomic `++` mints duplicate G2 reassembly ids
- **Where:** `G2_Glasses.cpp:1148`; `gNextSeq` declared `:534`; `allocMagic` `:1158`; `g2AllocSeq` `:9537`;
  ~80 call sites from `:1788` onward
- **What:** `gNextSeq` is a plain `static uint8_t`. `allocSeq()` is `uint8_t s = gNextSeq++;` — load,
  add, store, no atomic, no volatile, no lock. Call sites span every G2 worker on core 1 plus
  cmd_exec_task on core 0. Critically, `allocSeq()` is evaluated as an argument to the envelope builder
  **before** the send path takes `bleCentralTx` and the temple `writeMutex`, so the existing TX
  serialization does not cover it.
- **Why it matters:** heartbeat worker on core 1 and a `g2*` command on core 0 both call `allocSeq()`;
  both load N, both store N+1, and two envelopes go out carrying `seq=N`. The protocol comment at
  `:7593` states the contract: fragments are reassembled by seq, so distinct messages need distinct
  seqs. The glasses firmware then merges fragments from two unrelated messages into one reassembly
  buffer — CRC failure at best, a mis-parsed page command at worst. Presents as an intermittent "the
  lens ignored that tap / drew the wrong page" with no local error.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — spot-checked by hand, `gNextSeq` is a bare `static uint8_t = 1` and the
  increment is unguarded.
- **Direction:** the tap dispatcher in this same file already uses `__atomic_add_fetch` for its drop
  counter, so the house pattern exists.

### [MEDIUM] `gImuActions` read-modify-written by three tasks with no lock
- **Where:** `i2csensor_bno055.cpp:627` (`imuUpdateActions`), `:256` (`cmd_imuactions`), `:577` (poll task);
  `i2csensor_bno055_oled.h:28`
- **What:** `imuUpdateActions()` snapshots the cache under `SensorCacheGuard`, then **deliberately
  releases the guard** and performs a ~30-field read-modify-write over `gImuActions` with no
  synchronization — including `accelHistory[idx] = …; idx = (idx+1) % 10;`, `shakeCount++`,
  `stepCount++`. Called from `imu_task` (core 1), the OLED render path (core 1), and `cmd_imuactions`
  (core 0). The author knew about the multi-caller problem — the header comment at
  `i2csensor_bno055.h:120-124` describes double-posting across cores — but solved only the
  event-posting half via the `postEvents` flag. The state mutation was left open.
- **Why it matters:** run `imuactions` from any surface while the poll task runs. A lost index update
  makes two samples overwrite the same slot, corrupting the 10-sample variance window shake detection
  thresholds on; `shakeCount++`/`stepCount++` lose increments outright. Silent — wrong shake/step/tilt
  readings and an undercounting step counter, no error path.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED (not independently re-checked).
- **Board:** full-I2C builds only (`ENABLE_IMU_SENSOR`). **Not FeatherS3** — gated out on the current config.
- **Direction:** `gImuActions` is the only sensor state struct in the tree without a mutex while
  `gImuCache` right beside it has one.

### [MEDIUM] `gRing` GATT pointers cleared field-by-field under a live core-1 reader
- **Where:** `G2_Ring.cpp:192` `ringWriteLocked`; `:116-122` `ringClearGattPointers`; `:854-862` `onDisconnect`;
  `:236-246`, `:226` pre-checks
- **What:** `G2RingState` holds `client`, `writeChar`, `notifyChar`, `connected`, `clientStale` as plain
  non-volatile members with no lock covering the group; `onDisconnect` (BTC_TASK, core 0) clears them
  one assignment at a time. `ringWriteLocked` (core-1 `ring_spoof` / `ring_bridge_hb`) checks the three
  fields, blocks up to 1500 ms taking `gRing.writeMutex`, re-checks, then dereferences
  `gRing.writeChar->writeValue(...)`. **The clearing side never takes `gRing.writeMutex`**, so the
  recheck buys nothing.
- **Why it matters:** with the ring connected and spoof/bridge active, walk out of range. Bluedroid
  delivers `onDisconnect` on core 0 and nulls `writeChar` between the core-1 recheck and the
  dereference — `writeValue()` invoked with a null `this`, faulting on first member access. Every clear
  also leaves `connected` and `writeChar` mutually inconsistent for a few instructions, so a reader can
  pass the guard on half-cleared state.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED (not independently re-checked).
- **Direction:** the five GATT fields are one logical unit and should transition together under
  something the send path honours. The specific shape to eliminate is trusting an earlier check instead
  of re-reading at the point of dereference.

### [LOW] `bleCentralTxInit()` lazy singleton can wedge the shared BLE transmit gate
- **Where:** `BLE_CentralTx.cpp:9`, lazy path `:14-19`; callers `G2_Ring.cpp:1185`, `G2_Glasses.cpp:8593`
- **What:** `bleCentralTxInit()` is `if (gBleCentralTx) return; gBleCentralTx = xSemaphoreCreateMutex();`
  — check-then-act, no guard. `bleCentralTxTake()` calls it lazily whenever the handle is null and then
  re-reads the global for the actual take; `bleCentralTxGive()` also re-reads the global. First use can
  genuinely originate on either core (core-1 ring/G2 workers, core-0 cmd_exec).
- **Why it matters:** if two tasks reach `take()` before either init stores the handle, both create a
  mutex and the second store overwrites the first. Task A takes mutex #1 and, because `give()` re-reads
  the global, releases mutex #2 — the give fails silently (A isn't the holder) and mutex #1 stays held
  by a task that believes it released it. The gate is then permanently owned by a dead critical
  section: every subsequent G2 envelope and R1 write queues forever. Narrow window (first take of a
  boot) but the failure is a hard, silent, non-recovering wedge rather than a glitch.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — spot-checked by hand; both the check-then-act and the `give()` re-read are
  exactly as described.
- **Direction:** create the gate unconditionally at a single-threaded point in boot and make `take()`
  fail closed when it's absent. The `give()` path re-reading the global rather than the handle it took
  is what converts a benign leak into a wedge.

### [LOW] `OLEDConsoleBuffer` locks the writer but not the three readers
- **Where:** `OLED_Utils.cpp:2464` `append` (locked); `:2513` `getLineCount`, `:2518` `getLine`,
  `:2537` `getTimestamp` (all unlocked); `OLED_ConsoleBuffer.h:53-56`
- **What:** `append()` correctly takes `mutex` with a 10 ms timeout. The three `const` accessors take
  nothing, read `count`/`head`/`capacity` unsynchronized, and `getLine()` returns a bare pointer into
  the ring slot `append()` may be mid-way through overwriting. Producer is `debugOutputTask` (core 0);
  consumers are the OLED console screens on loopTask (core 1).
- **Why it matters:** output arriving while the console screen renders means core 0 is inside append's
  character-filter loop writing `dst[dstIdx++]` byte by byte into the exact slot core 1 is printing, so
  the rendered line is a splice of old and new text. **The ring is documented as routinely holding
  echoed `login <user> <pass>` plaintext**, so a spliced line can surface a fragment of one entry inside
  another. Indexing stays in bounds and the slot always retains a NUL — display corruption, not memory
  unsafety. (The underlying fact that credentials reach this buffer at all is a security finding the
  killed Track B agents never got to.)
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED (not independently re-checked).
- **Direction:** an accessor returning an interior pointer can't be made safe by locking inside itself —
  this wants a copy-out API under the mutex, or the whole render pass under one acquisition.

### [LOW] `gR1Cache` telemetry tuples straddle updates
- **Where:** `G2_Ring.cpp:284` (struct), `:292-300` `ringNoteWear`, `:311-324` `ringSampleAgeSec`, `:1355` reader
- **What:** each metric is a (value, ringTs, rxMs, valid) quadruple with no mutex, no sequence counter,
  no volatile. Written by the notify decode path on BTC_TASK (core 0), read by `g2RingGetTelemetry`
  from the OLED R1 submenu and G2 Bluetooth pages (core 1). Setters write value first and `valid` last,
  but nothing enforces that order or stops a reader straddling two updates.
- **Why it matters:** core 1 reads `hr` from sample N and `hrTs`/`hrRxMs` from sample N+1, so
  `ringSampleAgeSec()` labels a fresh heart-rate reading as minutes old, or vice versa. Fields are
  aligned scalars so there's no sub-word tearing — the defect is a mismatched tuple, i.e. a wrong
  staleness label on a health value.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED (not independently re-checked).
- **Direction:** every other sensor cache in the tree carries its own mutex; `gR1Cache` is the outlier.

### [LOW] `HAL_Input` reads `gInputCache` without the guard every other reader uses
- **Where:** `HAL_Input.cpp:204`, `:209`, `:215`
- **What:** `inputGetJoyX/JoyY/Buttons` read `gInputCache` fields with no lock. Every other consumer in
  the tree — `OLED_SetupWizard.cpp` (6 sites), `System_SetupWizard.cpp:1665`,
  `System_SensorLogging.cpp:663`, `WebPage_Sensors.cpp:230`, `OLED_Utils.cpp:5971` — takes
  `SensorCacheGuard(gInputCache.mutex, …)` first.
- **Why it matters:** a caller reading joyX then joyY then buttons through these three accessors can
  straddle two poll updates and act on a joystick position from one sample with buttons from the next.
  Aligned 32-bit scalars, and the FeatherS3 callers share a core with the writer, so this is an
  inconsistency rather than a fault. Worth noting mainly because it's the single documented-convention
  violation for this cache.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED (not independently re-checked).

### [NOTE] 1 KB PSRAM memcpy inside `taskENTER_CRITICAL`, with a comment claiming ~1 µs
- **Where:** `OLED_Mode_Map.cpp:1199`; comparable: `G2_Ring.cpp:145-155`, `:172`, `:184`
- **What:** `taskENTER_CRITICAL(&sSwapSpinlock); memcpy(oledDisplay->getBuffer(), sMapFrontBuf, 1024);
  taskEXIT_CRITICAL(&sSwapSpinlock);` where `sMapFrontBuf` points into `ps_malloc`'d PSRAM. The lock is
  genuinely required (mapRenderTask can swap the pointers mid-copy), so this is about cost and about
  the comment, not correctness. The preceding comment reads "instant memcpy ~1us".
- **Why it matters:** a 1024-byte read from quad-SPI PSRAM with interrupts disabled is roughly 15–30 µs
  plus cache-miss stalls — a 20× understatement in a comment a future reader will cite to justify
  adding work inside the section. Nothing trips today; the interrupt WDT budget is far larger. This and
  the ring TX queue's ~2 KB of slot shifting under `gRingTxQMux` are the two longest recurring critical
  sections in first-party code.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED (not independently re-checked).
- **Direction:** correctness fix is nil; the comment is simply wrong.

---

## Verified clean (A8)

Reproduced in full — the user asked for reassurance as well as problems, and this is the only track
that got far enough to provide any.

- **P19 is structurally absent.** `portDISABLE_INTERRUPTS`, `portENABLE_INTERRUPTS`, `noInterrupts()`,
  `interrupts()`, `taskDISABLE_INTERRUPTS` have **zero** occurrences across `components/hardwareone/`
  and `main/`. Nothing anywhere mistakes interrupt masking for a cross-core lock.
- **P20 is structurally absent.** `vTaskSuspendAll`, `xTaskResumeAll`, `vTaskSuspend(`, `vTaskResume(`
  have **zero** occurrences. Scheduler suspension is never used for data protection.
- **P23 is clean across all 11 first-party portMUX sites.** Every `taskENTER_CRITICAL` region in
  `OLED_UI.cpp`, `G2_Ring.cpp`, `System_ESPNow_Sensors.cpp`, `G2_Page_Network.cpp`,
  `System_PollPause.cpp`, `System_Debug.cpp`, `G2_Glasses.cpp`, `System_ESPNow_Tx.cpp`,
  `System_Events.cpp`, `OLED_Mode_Map.cpp` was read: every enter matched by an exit with the same mux
  on every return path. The enter/exit count asymmetries in four files are all legitimate early-return
  exits. No recursive entry, no nesting, no ISR-shared portMUX.
- **`g2ConnPri*` refcount is textbook** (`G2_Glasses.cpp:9394-9455`): depth is `volatile int` mutated
  only inside `portENTER_CRITICAL(&sConnPriMux)`, the decision value is snapshotted inside the lock,
  and `connPriApply()` (which does BLE work) is called outside it.
- **`System_PollPause.cpp` is correct.** Both depth counters mutate only under `sPauseMux`, the coarse
  mirror is recomputed in the same section, and the lock-free `pollPaused()` fast path reads single
  aligned volatile ints — genuinely atomic on this MCU, and documented as intentional.
- **The ESP-NOW inbound RX ring is a correct SPSC ring.** Head/tail are `volatile uint8_t`, producer and
  consumer are both pinned to core 0 so there isn't even a cross-core question, payload is fully
  memcpy'd before the head advance, full/empty uses the next-index form, drops are counted not swallowed.
- **The gamepad edge-latch has no lost-tap race.** The producer's OR-in and the UI's read-and-clear both
  execute inside a `SensorCacheGuard(gInputCache.mutex)` block. *This is exactly the shape the audit
  brief predicted would be broken, and it is not.*
- **The ESP-NOW session nonce counter is properly serialized.** `sessionWrapFrame` takes `EspNowTxGuard`
  before `++s->txSeqNext` and rolls it back inside the same section on failure. `sensor_bcast` on core 1
  at 10 Hz genuinely does race core-0 tasks on the same session, and duplicate `frameSeq` would be nonce
  reuse — **this was the highest-consequence cross-core RMW in the tree and it is correctly closed.**
- **`claimBroadcasterForDelete()` is a correct atomic handle claim** — read-and-null inside
  `taskENTER_CRITICAL`, so exactly one of the two teardown paths can call `vTaskDelete`. The double-free
  it prevents is real.
- **`System_ESPNow_Tx` stats are correct** — all mutations under `sStatsLock`, the reader snapshots the
  whole struct in one critical section so it can't tear, and the lock-free HWM pre-check is re-tested
  inside the lock (standard double-checked shape).
- **`OLED_Mode_Map`'s render double-buffer is a sound design** — snapshot written and copied out under
  the same spinlock, front/back swap is pointer-only inside the lock. Its only defect is the
  GPSTrackManager read it performs, filed separately above.
- **Per-sensor cache mutex discipline holds for every reader checked outside `HAL_Input`.**
  `SensorCacheGuard`'s reentrancy claim is honest — it checks `isHeldByCurrentTask()` and leaves
  `held=false` so the destructor doesn't over-release.
- **The system event ring is correctly guarded** — every producer and consumer path enters `gEventMux`,
  per-consumer cursors keep readers independent, and the ring-overwrite gap is counted, not lost.

## Not covered by A8 (finder's own words, condensed)

- Eight I2C sensor drivers are compiled out on FeatherS3, so their cache-mutex discipline was checked by
  reading only. `ENABLE_ESP_SR`, `ENABLE_EDGE_IMPULSE`, `ENABLE_CAMERA_SENSOR`, `ENABLE_ONDEVICE_LLM`
  are all 0 today, so those subsystems were **not audited at all**.
- **The largest remaining surface in this track:** the httpd task is `tskNO_AFFINITY`, which makes every
  web handler a potential cross-core reader of everything. Only `WebPage_Maps.cpp` was traced; the other
  ~40 handlers were not swept for the same pattern.
- `gSettings` has no mutex and is written from unpinned-worker/OLED/MQTT paths. Already documented as
  `SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT` §4.5, so not re-derived — but its current status was not verified either.
- `System_Automation.cpp` scheduler state and the ESP-NOW mesh peer/session tables were spot-checked
  around their locking primitives, not walked.
- No disassembly-level reasoning about compiler reordering at -O2.
- **Correction to the recon map:** `recon_isr.md` §7's portMUX inventory omits that `G2_Ring.cpp`'s
  `gRingTxQMux` protects only the pending-TX queue and **not** the `gRing` GATT pointer struct the same
  file's send path reads. A reader of that table could wrongly conclude G2_Ring's shared state is covered.

# Section 3 — Full sweep results

Produced by the resumed 60-agent run. The A8 cross-core section above predates this and is
preserved as written; findings here do not restate it.

- **600 raw findings** from 60 specialist finder agents, deduplicated to **505 distinct issues**
- **482 adversarial verifier verdicts** ran against them: 398 upheld, 84 refuted
- **961 verified-clean observations** recorded (things checked and found correct)
- Severity of deduplicated set: CRITICAL: 19, HIGH: 139, MEDIUM: 194, LOW: 110, NOTE: 43

> **Verification caveat.** Findings were produced by independent finder agents and then attacked by
> adversarial verifier agents instructed to refute them. In aggregate the verifiers upheld 398 and
> refuted 84 — roughly a 17% refutation rate. The run was interrupted and resumed several times, and
> the per-finding link between a verdict and its finding was not preserved in the journal, so the
> refutations could not be subtracted from individual entries. **Treat each finding's own
> `Confidence` field as the primary signal, and expect roughly one in six of these to not survive
> closer scrutiny.** Duplicate independent reports (noted inline) are a stronger signal.


---

## Platform findings

Grouped by subsystem, ordered by severity within each.


### Core system / tasks — 88 findings (21 critical/high)

### [CRITICAL] XIAO Sense: camera SCCB re-acquires I2C port 1 that Wire1 already owns; the failed acquire tears down the live primary bus's SDA/SCL GPIOs
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Camera_DVP.cpp:438`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Camera_DVP.cpp:309-318 separately does Wire.begin(40,39) / Wire.end() on port 0 with no manager mutex; harmless today only because XIAO defines no I2C2, but it would tear down bus 1 on any board that has both.
- **What:** initCamera() sets config.pin_sccb_sda=40 / pin_sccb_scl=39 (lines 245-246) and calls esp_camera_init() (line 438). Because pin_sccb_sda != -1, esp_camera.c:187 calls SCCB_Init(40,39). sdkconfig/sdkconfig.esp32s3 both set CONFIG_SCCB_HARDWARE_I2C_PORT1=y and CONFIG_SCCB_HARDWARE_I2C_DRIVER_NEW=y, so sccb-ng.c:133 calls i2c_new_master_bus({.i2c_port = 1, ...}). But HardwareOne.cpp:1531 already ran initI2CBuses() -> initBus(0) -> Wire1.begin(), and Wire1 IS ESP I2C port 1 (System_I2C_Manager.cpp:52 comments this explicitly), so port 1 is already acquired.
- **Why it matters:** IDF 5.5.1 i2c_common.c:134-137 logs "I2C bus id(1) has already been acquired", sets the new master's ->base to the EXISTING live bus struct, bumps s_i2c_platform.count[1] to 2, and returns ESP_ERR_INVALID_STATE. i2c_master.c:976 then does `goto err`, and err: calls i2c_master_bus_destroy(), whose first action (i2c_master.c:817) is i2c_common_deinit_pins(i2c_master->base) -- on the LIVE Wire1 bus. That calls gpio_output_disable() on Wire1's real SDA/SCL (GPIO5/GPIO6 on XIAO) and routes both input signals to GPIO_MATRIX_CONST_ZERO_INPUT (i2c_common.c:418-428). Net effect: camera init fails AND the primary sensor/OLED bus is silently disconnected at the GPIO matrix, while I2CDeviceManager::busInitialized[0] and Arduino's i2cIsInit(1) both still report the bus as healthy. Every subsequent transaction on bus 0 fails; nothing in the manager knows why.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: Seeed XIAO ESP32S3 / XIAO ESP32S3 Sense only (ENABLE_CAMERA_SENSOR=1) AND I2C_FEATURE_LEVEL > 0. Not reachable on the FeatherS3 default build.
- **Direction:** The camera and the manager cannot both own an ESP I2C port. Either move the manager's primary bus to port 0 (Wire) on camera-capable boards, or hand the camera an already-initialised port via config.pin_sccb_sda = -1 + config.sccb_i2c_port (which routes to SCCB_Use_Port instead of SCCB_Init). A compile-time #error when ENABLE_CAMERA_SENSOR && ENABLE_I2C_SYSTEM && the SCCB port collides with the manager's bus would also catch it.

### [CRITICAL] mic_record busy-spins at priority 5 on Core 1 whenever the audio source stops producing, permanently starving every lower-priority task on that core
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Microphone.cpp:266`
- **Also:** mic_viz (System_Microphone.cpp:1185) uses the same audioReadPcm call but has a real vTaskDelay(50) — not affected. The camera recordingTask (System_Camera_Video.cpp:299) is compiled out on FeatherS3 and was not audited for the same shape.
- **What:** recordingTask (created at :406 with TASK_PRIORITY_HIGH=5, pinned to core 1) loops `while (micRecording && gMicRunning && recordingSamples < maxSamples)` calling `audioReadPcm(buffer, N, 100)`. The only scheduling concession in the loop body is `taskYIELD()` at :312, with the comment "Don't add extra delay - i2s_channel_read already blocks for up to 100ms". That assumption is false for three real return paths: HAL_Audio.cpp:285 `#else return 0;` (no PDM backend compiled — the FeatherS3 case), HAL_Audio.cpp:272-273 (`gAudioSource != AUDIO_SRC_LOCAL_PDM` / `!gPdmRx`), and G2_Glasses.cpp:1746-1748 where g2MicReadPcmSamples returns 0 immediately, ignoring timeoutMs, when `gMicAfeFeedActive` is false. `recordingSamples` only advances when bytes were actually read, so the `< maxSamples` bound never terminates the spin, and there is no wall-clock bound (recordingStartTime is set but never checked).
- **Why it matters:** Concrete trigger on the shipping board: user runs `openmic` then `micrecstart` with the G2 glasses as the source, then the LEFT temple drops. g2MicOnLeftDisconnect() (G2_Glasses.cpp:1824-1827) sets gMicAfeFeedActive=false and calls audioCaptureStop(nullptr); neither touches micRecording or gMicRunning, so recordingTask's loop condition stays true while audioReadPcm now returns 0 with zero delay. taskYIELD() only yields to tasks of EQUAL or HIGHER priority, so a prio-5 spinner on core 1 starves everything below it there: loopTask (prio 1 — the whole Arduino main loop, OLED UI, serial CLI, automations), every I2C sensor poller (prio 1, I2C_SENSOR_CORE=1), mapRender (1), g2_page_swap_w / g2_tap_disp / g2_session_w (prio 2), llm_gen (2), and IDLE1. IDLE1 starvation additionally stops prvCheckTasksWaitingTermination on core 1, so deleted-task stacks/TCBs stop being reclaimed. TWDT prints a warning at 5 s but CONFIG_ESP_TASK_WDT_PANIC is not set, so there is no reset — the device sits pinned indefinitely. Core 0 survives, so `micrecstop` over serial still recovers it, but the on-device UI and all sensing are dead until then.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: any build with ENABLE_MICROPHONE=1 — that includes the current FeatherS3 config, where ENABLE_MICROPHONE_SENSOR=0 but ENABLE_MICROPHONE is derived 1 via (ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES), System_BuildConfig.h:255
- **Direction:** Give the loop its own floor delay independent of the read's timeout (e.g. vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(5)) when bytesRead==0), and/or make audioReadPcm honour timeoutMs on every early-return path rather than returning 0 instantly. Separately, a consecutive-zero-read counter that ends the recording, and a wall-clock bound against recordingStartTime, would both close it. Dropping the task to TASK_PRIORITY_NORMAL would reduce the blast radius but does not fix the spin.

### [CRITICAL] annotateActivityDaily() writes up to ~36 bytes past a 256-byte stack buffer on the BLE notify task (guard reserves 48 bytes, record renders up to 55)
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_R1_Protocol.cpp:829`
- **Also:** System_R1_Protocol.cpp:992 calls the same annotator with a 256-byte stack buffer from the self-test. The sibling loops annotateGenericDaily (:728) and annotateSleep (:771) reserve 32 against max renders of 31 and 15 — those are correct, which is what makes :829 an isolated off-by-7 rather than a house style.
- **What:** The per-record loop is guarded by `off + 48 < cap`, but the record format `"%s{slot=%u(+%u min) steps=%d kcal=%d ?=%02X,%02X}"` renders up to 55 chars (",{slot=255(+2550 min) steps=-32768 kcal=-32768 ?=FF,FF}"). When `cap - off` lands in [49,55] the snprintf at :837 truncates but still returns the would-be length, so `off` accumulates past `cap`. The two unguarded calls that follow — `off += snprintf(out + off, cap - off, "]")` at :842 and `off += snprintf(out + off, cap - off, " +%u B partial(continuation?)")` at :844 — then compute `cap - off` as a size_t underflow (huge) and write at `out + off`, i.e. past the end of the caller's buffer. The caller is `G2_Ring.cpp:772` with `char abuf[256]` — a plain automatic on the BLE notify callback's stack (ringNotifyThunk -> ringDumpFrame, BLE host/BTC task).
- **Why it matters:** An R1 ring notify frame with module=R1_MODULE_HEALTH, cmd=R1_CMD_ACTIVITY, subCmd=R1_SUB_DAILY and a payload whose 7-byte records are chosen so `off` reaches 202..207 before a maximal-length record. Example: several records with steps/kcal = 0 to step `off` in ~39-byte increments, then one record with slot=255, steps=-32768, kcal=-32768, r[3]=r[4]=0xFF. `off` becomes 257..262; `snprintf(abuf+262, (size_t)(256-262)=4294967290, "]")` writes 2 bytes at abuf[262..263], then if `(len-7)%7 != 0` a further 29 bytes land at abuf[263..291] — ~36 bytes of stack smashing past abuf[256]. r1Decode() records but does not enforce crc32Valid (System_R1_Protocol.cpp:305), and ringDumpFrame calls the annotator unconditionally (not behind gRingDumpVerbose or a debug flag), so any device the ESP has connected to as the ring can drive this; a genuine ring can also hit the window by chance since `off` only needs to land in a 6-value band.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards — ENABLE_BLUETOOTH and ENABLE_G2_GLASSES are unconditional `#define 1` in System_BuildConfig.h:223/228; runtime gate is CONFIG_BT_ENABLED
- **Direction:** The loop guard's reserve must be >= the format's true worst case (55, so reserve 56+), and every `off += snprintf(...)` in this file should clamp `off` to `cap - 1` on truncation the way System_VFS.cpp's appendf() already does. Consider making all four annotate* helpers use one shared truncation-aware append rather than raw accumulation. Also worth deciding whether an annotator should run at all on a frame with crc32Valid == false.

### [HIGH] CLIMode `sActiveMode` is written by TWO tasks (main loop + cmd_exec) despite documenting a single-writer invariant — null-deref and double-onExit windows
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_CLIMode.cpp:21`
- **Also:** System_CLIConfirm.cpp:18-30 (`sConfirm`) and System_SetupWizardMode.cpp:116 (`sWizard`) carry the same "only cmd_exec touches this" comment and inherit the same violated premise.
- **What:** `static const CLIMode* sActiveMode` (:21) is a plain non-volatile, non-atomic pointer. Its file header (:6-10) and the declaration comment (:17-20) both assert "only the cmd_exec task touches it. No mutex needed today." That is false in the current tree. `cliModeTick()` (:69-75) is called unconditionally from the main loop at HardwareOne.cpp:2458, on the IDF `main` task (CONFIG_ESP_MAIN_TASK_AFFINITY_CPU0, prio 1). `cliEnterMode`/`cliExitMode`/`cliModeDispatchInput` are called from cmd_exec_task (System_Utils.cpp:4630, System_CLI.cpp:318 and :509, System_CLIConfirm.cpp:157) — also core 0, TASK_PRIORITY_LOW=1 (HardwareOne.cpp:1514). Same core + same priority + FreeRTOS time-slicing = genuine interleaving. The main task additionally WRITES the pointer: `wizardMode_onTick` calls `cliExitMode()` at System_SetupWizardMode.cpp:905.
- **Why it matters:** Window A (crash): cmd_exec is inside `cliModeDispatchInput`, past the `if (sActiveMode == nullptr) return false;` check at :78, and has not yet evaluated `sActiveMode->onInput(line, sActiveMode->userData, ...)` at :95. The main task runs `cliModeTick` -> `wizardMode_onTick` -> `cliExitMode`, which stores nullptr at :53. cmd_exec then dereferences nullptr -> LoadProhibited panic. The symmetric window exists inside `cliModeTick` itself: it re-reads `sActiveMode` at :72, :73 and :74, so a `cliExitMode()` on cmd_exec (from any HANDLED_AND_EXIT confirm/help resolution) between :72 and :73 makes `sActiveMode->onTick` a null deref — this half needs no wizard and applies to every board. Window B (double side effect): `cliExitMode` latches `const CLIMode* m = sActiveMode;` at :49 before storing nullptr at :53. If both tasks latch the same non-null `m` before either stores, both call `m->onExit()`. For the wizard that means `wizardMode_onExit` (System_SetupWizardMode.cpp:807) runs twice: `wizardFinalize`, `upsertWiFiNetwork`+`sortWiFiByPriority`+`saveWiFiNetworks` (:827-830), `writeSettingsJson()` and `applySettings()` (:839-840) all execute a second time against half-torn `sWizard` state, while cmd_exec may concurrently be mutating that same single-slot static via `wizardMode_onInput`.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Make `sActiveMode` a `std::atomic<const CLIMode*>`, load it ONCE into a local at the top of `cliModeTick`/`cliModeDispatchInput`/`cliExitMode` and use only that local, and make the enter/exit transitions compare_exchange. Alternatively move `cliModeTick()` off the main loop onto cmd_exec_task (e.g. a periodic self-submitted deferred job) so the framework really is single-threaded as documented.

### [HIGH] srSnipDeinit force-deletes the snippet writer mid-file-write with no shutdown handshake, orphaning the global FS mutex
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPSR.cpp:1367`
- **Also:** Same class, live on FeatherS3: bleConnectShutdown (components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:8938) deletes g2_ble_connect with zero wait while it may be inside g2ConnectSync/ringPerformConnect — the comment at :8934-:8936 accepts leaking BLE-stack state but does not consider held locks. g2SessionShutdown (components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:12586) is the best-behaved of the three (abort request + g2SessionWaitIdle(3000)) but still deletes when the 3 s wait times out, and its jobs read files through VFS.
- **What:** srSnipDeinit() calls vTaskDelete(gSrSnipWriterTask) unconditionally — no running flag, no join, no wait. The task body srSnipWriterTask (:1172-:1205) spends its working time inside VFS::existsGuarded / VFS::mkdirGuarded / VFS::openGuarded, and each of those takes FsLockGuard, which is xSemaphoreTake(gFsMutex, portMAX_DELAY) (System_Mutex.cpp:91) held across the LittleFS/SD call (System_VFS.cpp:400). Every other stoppable task in this codebase uses a cooperative flag plus a wait (rtcStop, the sensor_bcast atomic claim, g2SessionShutdown); this one does not.
- **Why it matters:** `srstop` runs on cmd_exec_task (priority 1, core 0). sr_snip_wr is priority 3 on the same core, so cmd_exec only gets the CPU when sr_snip_wr is blocked — and a task inside LittleFS.open creating a new file blocks on the SPI-flash operation lock. That is precisely the window in which the delete lands, and at that instant sr_snip_wr holds gFsMutex. Because gFsMutex is a plain mutex taken with portMAX_DELAY everywhere, no subsequent filesystem operation on the device can ever complete: settings writes, system-log and error-log appends from debug_out, web file serving and the OLED file browser all block permanently. The device keeps running but silently stops persisting anything, with no error. Secondary: the in-flight job's job.pcm buffer leaks — the drain loop at :1371-:1374 only frees jobs still queued.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: Builds with ENABLE_ESP_SR=1 only. System_BuildConfig.h currently sets ENABLE_ESP_SR 0, so not compiled on FeatherS3 today.
- **Direction:** Give sr_snip_wr the same shape as rtcTask: a gSrSnipRunning flag checked at the top of the loop, a sentinel job or short queue timeout to wake it, and a bounded join in srSnipDeinit; only fall back to vTaskDelete if the join fails, and log loudly when it does. Apply the same join to bleConnectShutdown.

### [HIGH] EdgeImpulse frees model buffer, tensor arena and interpreter while the continuous inference task is running
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_EdgeImpulse.cpp:500`
- **Also:** freeModelResources is also called from the load error paths at :616, :629, :645, :695 — same exposure.
- **What:** `loadModelFromFile()` calls `freeModelResources()` at :500 with no attempt to stop continuous inference. `unloadModel()` (:730-744) sets gEIContinuousRunning=false, sleeps a fixed `vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(100))`, and frees regardless of whether the worker actually stopped. `freeModelResources()` (:453-490) does `delete gInterpreter`, `free(gModelBuffer)`, `free(gTensorArena)` and `freeLabels()`. `stopContinuousInference()` (:1906-1916) only flips the flag — its own comment says "Task will self-delete on next loop iteration".
- **Why it matters:** `ei_continuous` is created pinned to APP_CORE (core 1) at :1880-1888 and its loop body is `runEdgeImpulseInference()` — camera capture, JPEG decode, resize, TFLite Invoke — followed by `vTaskDelay(gSettings.edgeImpulseIntervalMs)`. Running `ei model load <other>` from cmd_exec_task (core 0, via cmd_ei_model_load at :2319) while continuous inference is active frees everything with zero quiesce: the arena all tensors point into, the flatbuffer the ops read, and the interpreter object itself, under a live `Invoke()` on the other core. `ei model unload` (:2400-2408) is only marginally better — 100 ms does not cover a single inference on this hardware, and does not even cover the configured inter-inference delay. Result is a freed-heap read/write inside TFLite, or a vtable call on a deleted MicroInterpreter.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: ENABLE_EDGE_IMPULSE builds only — System_BuildConfig.h:296 sets it to 0, so NOT FeatherS3 today
- **Direction:** The LLM module in this same tree already implements the right shape: llmUnload (System_LLM.cpp:1012-1031) sets a stop request and polls until the worker actually reports quiesced, with a comment describing the exact use-after-free that a naive check missed. EdgeImpulse needs a real handshake (worker acknowledges exit) rather than a fixed sleep, and loadModelFromFile must go through it too.

### [HIGH] Bus 1 -- the display bus on the primary board -- has effectively no wedge-recovery path: quorum floor blocks it, void transactions never report errors, and i2creset is hardcoded to bus 0
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_I2C.cpp:2169`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_I2C_Manager.cpp:460-495 (quorum), System_I2C_Manager.h:384-386 (void success), System_I2C.h:384-389 (i2cBusRecovery(busIdx) exists but has zero callers).
- **What:** Three independent gates combine. (1) On FeatherS3[D], System_BuildConfig.h:1088-1094 makes OLED_BUS_DEFAULT = 1 and :1065-1071 makes I2C2_BUS_ENABLED_DEFAULT = 1, so bus 1 is live and carries exactly one device -- the SSD1306. Every other *Bus setting defaults to 0. (2) checkBusRecoveryNeeded() (System_I2C_Manager.cpp:480) requires `total >= 3 && degradedCount >= 2` on that bus before it will recover, so a 1-device bus can never qualify. (3) cmd_i2creset (System_I2C.cpp:2169) calls mgr->performBusRecovery() with the default argument, i.e. bus 0 only, and the command takes no bus parameter. The only remaining automatic route is recordError(BUS_ERROR) -> performBusRecovery(bus), but the per-frame OLED path displayUpdate() (HAL_Display.cpp:173) uses i2cDeviceTransactionVoid, and executeTransaction's void branch (System_I2C_Manager.h:384-386) unconditionally calls recordSuccess() -- a void transaction can never produce a BUS_ERROR.
- **Why it matters:** A slave holding SDA low on I2C2 (the exact scenario the SSD1306 hits when LDO2 drops mid-transaction -- documented in OLED_Utils.cpp:6757-6772) wedges bus 1 permanently. Every displayUpdate() silently 'succeeds', the panel stays dark, i2chealth shows the OLED as healthy, and neither the automatic quorum path nor the operator's `i2creset` can clear it. The only escape is the incidental mgr->performBusRecovery(1) inside oledResumeFromSleep() (OLED_Utils.cpp:6776), which only fires on a power-save wake and only when powerSaveTimeoutMinutes != 0.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: Unexpected Maker FeatherS3[D] (primary board) and any board defining I2C2_POWER_PIN + I2C2 pins
- **Direction:** Give i2creset an optional bus argument (default 'all'), and lower the quorum so a bus whose entire population is degraded recovers regardless of count (e.g. degradedCount == total, or total < 3 && degraded == total). Separately, make the OLED frame push a bool-returning transaction so a wedged bus is actually observable.

### [HIGH] I2C SDA/SCL pin settings accept SPI-flash/PSRAM and strapping GPIOs; a persisted bad value is a permanent boot loop
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_I2C.cpp:711`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_I2C.cpp:724 (cmd_i2csclpin), :755 / :768 (bus-1 variants), :2963-2971 (SettingEntry min/max), components/hardwareone/System_I2C_Manager.cpp:190-199 (busPinsFromSettings only checks >=0), components/hardwareone/System_Settings.cpp:2282 (settingsLoadClampInt applies the same 0..48 bound on load)
- **What:** cmd_i2csdapin/cmd_i2csclpin clamp the user value to 0..HW_GPIO_MAX, where HW_GPIO_MAX is defined at System_I2C.cpp:689 as SOC_GPIO_PIN_COUNT-1 (48 on ESP32-S3, 39 on classic ESP32). There is no GPIO_IS_VALID_GPIO check, no exclusion of the SPI flash/PSRAM pin range (26-32 on S3 quad, plus 33-37 on S3 octal, 6-11 on classic ESP32), no exclusion of strapping pins (GPIO0/3/45/46 on S3), USB-Serial/JTAG (19/20), UART0 (43/44), or the GPIOs that do not physically exist on S3 (22-25, masked out of SOC_GPIO_VALID_GPIO_MASK). The value is persisted via setSetting and re-applied at every boot.
- **Why it matters:** An admin runs `i2csdapin 28` on a FeatherS3 (28 = SPIWP, an in-package PSRAM/flash line). setSetting persists it to settings.json. On the next boot HardwareOne.cpp:1531 calls initI2CBuses -> I2CDeviceManager::initBuses -> initBus -> beginBusOnCpu1 -> Wire1.begin(28, 9) -> esp32-hal-i2c-ng i2cInit -> i2c_new_master_bus. IDF's esp_driver_i2c/i2c_master.c:966 only tests GPIO_IS_VALID_GPIO, which 26-32 pass, then i2c_common.c:332 calls gpio_func_sel(28, PIN_FUNC_GPIO), reassigning an SPI-flash/PSRAM pad while the CPU is executing from that flash. The panic repeats on every boot because settingsLoadClampInt's bounds (0..48) accept the on-disk value unchanged, and I2C init runs long before any CLI could correct it. Only recovery is a full flash erase. The same command with 22-25 on S3 is a softer failure (GPIO_IS_VALID_GPIO rejects, bus silently dead) and with 45 it silently ties an internal pull onto the VDD_SPI strapping pin at every boot.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (worst on ESP32-S3: FeatherS3 + XIAO)
- **Direction:** Validate against SOC_GPIO_VALID_OUTPUT_GPIO_MASK plus a per-target deny list (flash/PSRAM range, strapping, USB/UART pins) and reject rather than clamp; additionally re-validate in busPinsFromSettings and fall back to I2C_SDA_PIN_DEFAULT so a bad on-disk value cannot brick boot.

### [HIGH] Runtime-settable I2C pins accept the SPI-flash/PSRAM MSPI pins; the value is persisted and re-applied at every boot
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_I2C.cpp:718`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_I2C.cpp:731 (i2csclpin), :762 (i2c2sdapin), :775 (i2c2sclpin), :2963-2971 (SettingEntry min/max feeding the web/OLED/G2 settings editors); components/hardwareone/System_I2C_Manager.cpp:190-198 (busPinsFromSettings), :255-305 (initBus), :371-405 (performBusRecovery)
- **What:** cmd_i2csdapin/cmd_i2csclpin/cmd_i2c2sdapin/cmd_i2c2sclpin clamp the argument to 0..HW_GPIO_MAX, where HW_GPIO_MAX is (SOC_GPIO_PIN_COUNT - 1) = 48 on ESP32-S3, 39 on classic ESP32 (System_I2C.cpp:689). Nothing excludes the MSPI (flash + PSRAM) IOMUX pins. Per esp-idf soc/esp32s3/include/soc/spi_pins.h those are GPIO26-32 on every S3 (CS1/HD/WP/CS0/CLK/MISO/MOSI) plus GPIO33-37 (D4/D5/D6/D7/DQS) on octal-PSRAM parts — i.e. the XIAO. On classic ESP32 the equivalent set is GPIO6-11 plus the in-package PICO PSRAM CS (CONFIG_PICO_PSRAM_CS_IO=10 in boards/qtpy_esp32.defaults and boards/feather_esp32_v2.defaults). The value goes through setSetting into settings.json and is read back by busPinsFromSettings on every boot.
- **Why it matters:** `i2csdapin 30` on the FeatherS3 is accepted (30 <= 48), persisted, and on the next boot initBus() calls Wire1.begin(30, 9). ESP-IDF does not stop it: esp_driver_i2c/i2c_master.c:966 only checks GPIO_IS_VALID_GPIO, and SOC_GPIO_VALID_GPIO_MASK (soc_caps.h:187) masks out only BIT22-BIT25, so 26-37 pass; esp_driver_gpio/src/gpio.c gpio_config() performs no reserved-pin check at all (esp_gpio_is_reserved is referenced only by the diagnostic dump at gpio.c:1103). GPIO30 is MSPI CLK, so the pad is re-muxed off the flash controller and the device panics on the next cache miss — every boot, unrecoverable without an erase. The same value is also applied without any reboot via the admin `i2creset` command (System_I2C.cpp:2169 -> performBusRecovery), which does wire->end() then pinMode(sclPin, OUTPUT) / digitalWrite() directly on the settings pins. The "reboot required" wording in the help text and setting label is therefore also wrong.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all — dangerous range is wider on XIAO (octal) than FeatherS3 (quad)
- **Direction:** Reject rather than clamp values inside the MSPI range, and derive that range from the target plus CONFIG_SPIRAM_MODE_OCT (S3: 26-32 always, 33-37 when octal; classic: 6-11 plus CONFIG_PICO_PSRAM_CS_IO). Apply the same guard to the SettingEntry bounds at :2963-2971 so the OLED/G2/web number pickers cannot reach it either, and on classic ESP32 also reject the input-only 34-39 for SDA/SCL.

### [HIGH] i2cOledTransactionVoid hardcodes bus 0 while the OLED lives on bus 1 by default, taking the wrong mutex, reclocking the sensor bus, and creating a phantom registry entry that shadows the real device
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_I2C.h:160`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/OLED_Utils.cpp:3527, 4131, 4191, 4687; System_I2C.h:344-381 (the address-keyed helpers the phantom shadows).
- **What:** i2cOledTransactionVoid()/i2cOledTransaction() forward to the 4-argument legacy helper i2cDeviceTransactionVoid(I2C_ADDR_OLED, ...), which resolves to bus 0 by construction (System_I2C.h:75-87, getDevice(address) defaults busIdx=0). Four call sites use it: OLED_Utils.cpp:3527 (stopOLEDDisplay), :4131 (OLED_OFF mode entry), :4191 (cmd_oledclear), :4687 (boot-animation first frame). Meanwhile gDisplay is constructed bound to oledWire = i2c()->getWire(gSettings.oledBus) (OLED_Utils.cpp:4652) and gSettings.oledBus defaults to OLED_BUS_DEFAULT = 1 on the FeatherS3[D] (System_BuildConfig.h:1088-1094).
- **Why it matters:** Three concrete consequences. (a) Those four operations take bus 0's mutex while the SSD1306 object actually clocks bytes out on Wire/bus 1 -- the manager's bus-1 arbitration is bypassed entirely, and bus 0 is needlessly blocked. (b) setBusClock(0, 400000) then setBusClock(0, 100000) reclocks the *sensor* bus for the duration, including the NG driver's device rm/add sweep on the wrong port. (c) registerDevice() auto-creates a phantom (0x3D, bus 0) entry named "Auto". Because getDeviceAnyBus() scans buses in ascending order (System_I2C_Manager.cpp:161-167), every address-keyed health helper -- i2cDeviceIsDegraded, i2cDeviceSuccess, i2cDeviceError, i2cShouldAutoDisable, i2cGetConsecutiveErrors -- resolves 0x3D to the phantom bus-0 slot instead of the real bus-1 one. The phantom is fed only by void transactions, so it reads permanently healthy. It also consumes one of MAX_DEVICES=16 and shows up in i2chealth/i2cscan output as a bus-0 device that does not physically exist. If the panel was detected at 0x3C, the phantom is at the wrong address as well.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: Unexpected Maker FeatherS3[D] (primary board) — any board where OLED_BUS_DEFAULT resolves to 1
- **Direction:** Delete the bus-0-only i2cOledTransaction* helpers and make all four call sites use the 5-argument bus-aware form with (uint8_t)gSettings.oledBus and the detected address, the way HAL_Display.cpp:173 and OLED_Utils.cpp:6850/6860 already do.

### [HIGH] initBus() and performBusRecovery() discard TwoWire::begin()'s bool and mark the bus 'online' unconditionally
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_I2C_Manager.cpp:293`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_I2C_Manager.cpp:402-405 (same discard inside performBusRecovery — a failed re-begin leaves the bus marked healthy)
- **What:** `initBus()` calls `beginBusOnCpu1(wire, sdaPin, sclPin)` (line 283, which itself discards `req->wire->begin(...)`'s bool inside `busBeginTask`) or `wire->begin(sdaPin, sclPin)` (line 285, return discarded), then sets `busInitialized[busIdx] = true;` at line 293 with no success check, and logs `"Bus %u initialized"` plus a `logSystemEvent("I2C", "bus %u online")`. `TwoWire::begin()` returns false on several real paths: `allocateWireBuffer()` malloc failure (2 x 128 B internal DRAM), `initPins()` failure, or `i2cInit()` returning non-OK (e.g. `i2c_new_master_bus` refusing a port already acquired by another component).
- **Why it matters:** gSettings.i2cSdaPin / i2cSclPin are user-settable across 0..HW_GPIO_MAX (System_I2C.cpp:2963-2966). Point one at a pin another peripheral already owns and Arduino's i2cInit fails at perimanClearPinBus (esp32-hal-i2c-ng.c:107) or at i2c_new_master_bus (line 136), leaving bus[1].initialized == false in the HAL. The manager still reports the bus online. Every transaction then reaches i2cWrite/i2cRead, hits `if (!bus[i2c_num].initialized) log_e("bus is not initialized")`, and endTransmission returns 4 -- which classifyI2CError maps to BUS_ERROR, which calls performBusRecovery immediately. Recovery re-begins, fails again, still sets busInitialized = true, and the device is stuck in a 5-second recovery loop (BUS_RECOVERY_COOLDOWN_MS) forever: each iteration pauses polling, holds the bus mutex ~70 ms, bit-bangs 9 clock pulses on the wrong pins, and spawns a CPU1 task. The system-event log meanwhile says the bus came online.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders
- **Direction:** Have `beginBusOnCpu1` propagate `wire->begin()`'s bool through the request struct, gate `busInitialized[busIdx] = true` on it, and emit the same `logSystemEvent("I2C", "bus %u init FAILED")` the pin-validation path already emits (lines 316, 332) so a begin failure is as visible as a bad-pin failure.

### [HIGH] I2C error logging is unbounded per-error and the [ERROR] dedupe window is defeated by the counters embedded in the message — an I2C error storm becomes a flash-write storm
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_I2C_Manager.cpp:845`
- **Also:** System_I2C_Manager.cpp:816, :861, :885; System_Debug.cpp:294-312 (dedupe), :2360-2377 (writer)
- **What:** `I2CDevice::recordError` calls `logI2CError` from four branches (:816 NACK, :845 TIMEOUT, :861 BUS_ERROR, :885 BUFFER_OVERFLOW). The NACK/TIMEOUT branches are guarded only by `health.consecutiveErrors >= 3` — there is no already-degraded transition guard, so once a device is failing, EVERY poll writes a line. The BUS_ERROR branch has no threshold at all (its cooldown covers `performBusRecovery`, not the log write). `logI2CError` (`System_Debug.cpp:2360-2377`) calls `appendLineWithCap` synchronously on the caller's task — which for the sensor pollers is core 1, the same core the header calls the "hard rule" I2C core. Separately, the same events emit `ERROR_I2CF` (`System_Debug.h:588` → `"[ERROR][I2C] "`), which lands in the debug_out flood guard at `System_Debug.cpp:294-312`. That guard compares only the first 63 bytes of the message (`strncmp(lastErrText, msg->text, 63)`), but the messages embed monotonically increasing counters — `"[ERROR][I2C] Device 0x36 (gamepad) marked DEGRADED after 12 timeouts"` puts the changing digits at offset ~56, and the BUS_ERROR form `"...bus=0 BUS_ERROR (count=N, espErr=...)"` at ~58. Both are inside the 63-byte compare, so the dedupe never matches.
- **Why it matters:** Each I2C failure therefore produces two synchronous LittleFS appends (errors.log 256 KB cap, i2c_errors.log 64 KB cap), each of which can trigger a block erase — the finding-1 window. That window masks the non-IRAM I2C ISR (`i2c_driver_install(..., intr_alloc_flags=0)` in `components/arduino/cores/esp32/esp32-hal-i2c.c:142`) and freezes the waiting task, against a 100 ms Wire timeout (`System_I2C_Manager.cpp:290 wire->setTimeOut(100)`; Arduino's default is 50 ms, `Wire.cpp:44`). A ~50 ms erase eats half that budget outright and a worst-case erase blows through it — producing another TIMEOUT, another pair of appends, another erase. The dedupe that exists precisely to stop this (its comment names a past incident where an unbounded flood "caused a stack overflow in this task") is inert for the highest-frequency error family in the system.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards with ENABLE_I2C_SYSTEM (FeatherS3 primary: OLED + Seesaw gamepad, I2C_FEATURE_LEVEL 4)
- **Direction:** Two independent fixes: gate the DEGRADED log writes on the not-degraded→degraded transition (and rate-limit the BUS_ERROR one), and make the debug_out dedupe key insensitive to trailing digits — e.g. compare up to the first digit run, or dedupe on a hash of the format string rather than the formatted text.

### [HIGH] void-returning transactions unconditionally recordSuccess(), so the health/degrade/recovery machinery is blind to the highest-rate bus users
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_I2C_Manager.h:384`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/HAL_Display.cpp:173, components/hardwareone/OLED_Utils.cpp:3676 (the guard this defeats).
- **What:** executeTransaction()'s `if constexpr (std::is_same<ReturnType, void>::value)` branch runs the lambda and then calls device->recordSuccess() with no way to observe failure (lines 373-387). The non-void branch only classifies errors when ReturnType is exactly bool; any other return type also falls through to an unconditional recordSuccess() (lines 431-441). 32 call sites use i2cDeviceTransactionVoid / i2cOledTransactionVoid / i2cTransactionNACKTolerant across HAL_Display.cpp, OLED_Utils.cpp, i2csensor_seesaw.cpp, i2csensor_pa1010d.cpp, i2csensor_pca9685.cpp, i2csensor_rda5807.cpp and OLED_Mode_Network.cpp.
- **Why it matters:** The busiest device on the bus -- the SSD1306 frame push in displayUpdate() (HAL_Display.cpp:173), which runs up to every 125 ms -- can never accumulate consecutiveErrors, can never be marked degraded, can never contribute to checkBusRecoveryNeeded()'s degradedCount, and can never trigger the BUS_ERROR immediate-recovery branch. Worse, it actively resets health: recordSuccess() zeroes consecutiveErrors and clears the degraded flag, so a genuinely sick OLED that a bool-returning init path had just marked degraded gets 'recovered' by the next void frame push that did nothing on the wire. The i2cDeviceIsDegraded(OLED_I2C_ADDRESS) guard at OLED_Utils.cpp:3676 is therefore dead code.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Do not record success for a lambda that returns no status. Either treat void transactions as health-neutral (record nothing), or run the same probeDeviceStatus() classification the bool branch uses and record based on that. Converting the OLED frame push to a bool transaction is the higher-value half.

### [HIGH] GPSTrackManager::clearTrack() frees and nulls _points before zeroing _pointCount, while lock-free readers on other cores iterate _points[i] per loop iteration
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Maps.cpp:2903`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Maps.cpp:3099 (renderTrack, the reader); :2838-2864 (loadTrack, free-then-realloc); :2999-3013 (appendPoint reads _points[_pointCount-1] after only an `!_points` null check); components/hardwareone/WebPage_Maps.cpp:172, :219 (httpd readers holding a raw pointer across blocking chunk writes); components/hardwareone/OLED_Mode_Map.cpp:1012 (mapRender caller), :344 (getPointCount read)
- **What:** `_points` (GPSTrackPoint*) and `_pointCount` (int) are plain non-volatile class statics with no mutex, sequence counter or double-buffer. `clearTrack()` does `free(_points); _points = nullptr;` FIRST and only then `_pointCount = 0`, so there is a guaranteed window in which `_points == nullptr` while `_pointCount > 0`. `renderTrack()` (System_Maps.cpp:3099) checks `_pointCount < 2` once at entry and then dereferences `_points[i]` inside the loop with `MapCore::geoToScreen()` and `renderer->drawLine()` called between iterations — both are external, non-inlined calls, so the compiler must reload the static `_points` from memory on every iteration rather than caching it in a register. `renderTrack` runs on the `mapRender` task (OLED_Mode_Map.cpp:1012, pinned APP_CORE/core 1). `clearTrack()` is reachable from `cmd_gpstrack` (System_Maps.cpp:3232, 3248) on cmd_exec_task (PRO_CORE/core 0), from the OLED map UI on loopTask (OLED_Mode_Map.cpp:1508, 1534, 1989), and from `handleGPSTracksAPI` on the httpd task (WebPage_Maps.cpp:130), which recon confirms is the one tskNO_AFFINITY task in the system. Separately, `loadTrack()` calls `clearTrack()` then re-`ps_alloc`s, so a track load has the same free-then-publish shape.
- **Why it matters:** Run `gpstrack clear` (or open /api/gpstracks?live=start, or load a different track from the web UI) from any command surface while the OLED Map screen is rendering a loaded track. cmd_exec_task on core 0 executes `free(_points); _points = nullptr;` between two iterations of the core-1 render loop; the next `_points[i].lat` reads address 0x0 → LoadProhibited panic. If the write lands after the reload instead, the loop reads through a dangling pointer into freed heap for up to MAX_TRACK_POINTS entries. The web reader is worse in duration: `handleGPSTracksAPI` captures `const GPSTrackPoint* points = getPoints()` into a local at WebPage_Maps.cpp:172/219 and then loops calling `httpd_resp_sendstr_chunk()` — a blocking socket write — between every point, holding the raw pointer across hundreds of milliseconds of I/O while any of the four `clearTrack()` callers can free it underneath.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with ENABLE_MAPS (FeatherS3 has ENABLE_MAPS 1); web path applies to every build with the HTTP server; prior art: docs/LAZY_ALLOCATION_AUDIT.md:110 notes only the benign form — 'httpd reader iterates getPoints() unsynchronized vs GPS-task appendPoint'. It does not mention clearTrack()/loadTrack() freeing the buff
- **Direction:** The track buffer needs one owner and one gate — either a dedicated mutex held for the whole read (which conflicts with the web handler's blocking chunked writes) or a publish/retire discipline where readers take a reference and clearTrack defers the free. At minimum the store order in clearTrack() is backwards for any lock-free reader: the count must be retired before the pointer.

### [HIGH] The CPU-clock I2C drain guard covers only the power-save path; `power mode` and `cpufreq` still switch raw — and the in-tree justification for leaving them raw is factually wrong about which transition crashed
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Power.cpp:106`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:1380 (cmd_cpufreq), components/hardwareone/System_Settings.cpp:858 (settings reload)
- **What:** `setCpuFrequencyDrained()` (HardwareOne.cpp:2219) — pausePolling + take every per-bus mutex + switch + release — is `static` and is called from exactly one place, `powerSaveTick()` (HardwareOne.cpp:2256, 2335). Two other runtime paths change the CPU clock with a bare `setCpuFrequencyMhz()`: `applyPowerMode()` (System_Power.cpp:106), reachable from the `power mode <name>` command, the OLED CPU menu, the G2 Power page (G2_Page_Power.cpp dispatches `power mode X`), automations, and settings reload (System_Settings.cpp:858); and `cmd_cpufreq()` (System_Utils.cpp:1380). The comment at System_Power.cpp:88-93 and the note at G2_Page_Power.cpp:27-33 justify leaving these raw on the grounds that `applyPowerMode` "now only ever sets >=80 MHz — the raw jump to UltraSaver's 40 MHz is gone… so the riskiest transition is already covered."
- **Why it matters:** That justification does not match the recorded crash.
