The 2026-07-17 panic(4) loop was the idle-entry transition 240 MHz → 80 MHz with `gps_task` mid-I2C — an 80 MHz target, exactly the class `applyPowerMode` still performs raw. `power mode PowerSaver` from a non-super-admin surface (or an automation) while any sensor task is inside `executeTransaction` reproduces the same 240→80 switch with zero quiescing: `applyPowerMode` neither pauses polling nor takes any bus mutex. The 40 MHz argument is a red herring; the mitigation reasoning that keeps these two sites unguarded is unsound.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: KNOWN as a gap — flagged in the G2_Page_Power.cpp:27-33 code comment and in the prior power-save-crash session notes. Reported here because the STATUS/reasoning in that prior note is wrong: it claims 
- **Direction:** Promote `setCpuFrequencyDrained` out of HardwareOne.cpp (it has no OLED dependency other than sitting inside `#if ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY`) into System_Power.cpp and route `applyPowerMode` and `cmd_cpufreq` through it. Correct the two stale comments while you are there — the crashing transition was 240→80, not 240→40. Secondary, already noted in the prior session: `setCpuFrequencyDrained` still switches when a bus-mutex take times out (HardwareOne.cpp:2226 leaves `held[b]` null and falls through), which is a deliberate choice but worth an explicit WARN so a wedged-bus switch is visible in the log.

### [HIGH] All nine sensor create helpers call eTaskGetState() on a dangling TaskHandle_t whose TCB the idle task has already free()d
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_TaskUtils.cpp:180`
- **Also:** System_TaskUtils.cpp:226, :243, :261, :279, :298, :316, :334, :352 (the other eight helpers). Read-only pointer *comparisons* against the same stale handles at System_Debug.cpp:783-786 and :937-939 are not a UAF but can false-match a new task that reuses the freed TCB address and silently suppress its debug output.
- **What:** Every createXTask() helper opens with `if (gXTaskHandle != nullptr) { eTaskState state = eTaskGetState(gXTaskHandle); if (state == eDeleted || state == eInvalid) gXTaskHandle = nullptr; }`. The sensor tasks self-delete through SENSOR_TASK_EXIT (= vTaskDelete(nullptr)) and, by documented design (System_TaskUtils.h:215-216), never clear the handle. All of them are created dynamically via xTaskCreateLogged -> xTaskCreatePinnedToCore, so ESP-IDF's idle task reaps them: prvCheckTasksWaitingTermination (esp-idf/components/freertos/FreeRTOS-Kernel/tasks.c:4566) -> prvDeleteTCB (:4895) -> vPortFreeStack(pxTCB->pxStack) + vPortFree(pxTCB). The stored handle is therefore a pointer into freed internal-DRAM heap, and eTaskGetState (tasks.c:1624) dereferences it — `listLIST_ITEM_CONTAINER(&(pxTCB->xStateListItem))` at :1643 — inside taskENTER_CRITICAL(&xKernelLock).
- **Why it matters:** It works today only by accident: uxListRemove() sets pvContainer = NULL just before the free, and eTaskGetState maps a NULL container to eDeleted (tasks.c:1696-1700). CONFIG_HEAP_POISONING_DISABLED=y (sdkconfig:2055) means the block is not scrubbed, so an un-reused freed TCB still reads NULL. The moment that ~200-byte internal-DRAM block is handed to another allocation — routine on this device, which churns internal DRAM constantly — the bytes at the xStateListItem.pvContainer offset are arbitrary. A non-NULL value that matches none of the kernel lists falls through to `eReturn = eReady` (tasks.c:1706-1710), so the helper leaves the stale handle in place, `if (gXTaskHandle == nullptr)` is false, and the function returns true having created nothing: the sensor reports as started and never polls. A value that happens to equal &xSuspendedTaskList sends the kernel on to read pxTCB->ucNotifyState[] from the same freed block. Note the codebase already knows these handles go stale — System_MemoryMonitor.cpp:289 and System_TaskUtils.cpp:557 both carry the comment "if false, handle may be stale (task self-deleted)" and gate on the running flag before touching the handle. This is the one place that dereferences it unguarded.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all — on the current FeatherS3 config only createInputTask() is compiled in (CUSTOM_ENABLE_GAMEPAD 1); on full-I2C builds all nine
- **Direction:** Clear the handle at the point of truth instead of probing a freed pointer: have SENSOR_TASK_EXIT take the handle global and NULL it immediately before vTaskDelete(nullptr) (the task is the last thing that can legitimately touch it), and drop the eTaskGetState block entirely. If the eTaskGetState probe must be kept for another reason, it needs a liveness cross-check against a uxTaskGetSystemState snapshot the way reportAllTaskStacks already does at System_TaskUtils.cpp:549-555.

### [HIGH] The Arduino loop is NOT on APP_CORE — setup(), loop() and all OLED I2C run on Core 0, violating the header's own "hard rule"
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_TaskUtils.h:134`
- **Also:** HardwareOne.cpp:1531/1536/1683/1776/1873 (boot I2C on main task), HardwareOne.cpp:2448 powerSaveTick->setCpuFrequencyDrained, HardwareOne.cpp:1603 oledEarlyInit, HardwareOne.cpp:1655/1669 oledUpdate during setup()
- **What:** `System_TaskUtils.h:134` states "APP_CORE (1) — Arduino loop, compute/render, and all I2C work run here", and :152-156 makes "ANY task that touches the shared I2C/Wire bus" a Core-1 rule with "no exceptions". That premise is false in this build. `sdkconfig:603` has `# CONFIG_AUTOSTART_ARDUINO is not set`, and `components/arduino/cores/esp32/main.cpp:25` guards both `loopTask` and Arduino's own `app_main` on that symbol — so `loopTask` (which would have been pinned to `CONFIG_ARDUINO_RUNNING_CORE=1`) is never created. `main/hardwareone-idf.cpp:26-53` defines its own `app_main` that calls `setup()` then `loop()` inline, so `hardwareone_setup()` and `hardwareone_loop()` execute on the IDF `main` task: 8192 B stack, priority 1, pinned to Core 0 by `CONFIG_ESP_MAIN_TASK_AFFINITY_CPU0=y` (sdkconfig:1773). That task does shared-bus I2C every lap: `HardwareOne.cpp:2441` oledUpdate() -> updateOLEDDisplay() -> `HAL_Display.cpp:173` `i2cDeviceTransactionVoid(gSettings.oledBus, OLED_I2C_ADDRESS, 400000, 15, ...)`, a ~20 ms SSD1306 framebuffer push on the same managed bus as the sensors.
- **Why it matters:** Core 0 also carries the Wi-Fi task (prio 23), the BT controller (23), BTC_TASK, and espnow_task (prio 5, wakes every 10 ms). The main task sits at priority 1, one step above IDLE0, so it is the single most-preemptible I2C actor in the system and it is on the busiest core. The header's own rationale says exactly this situation — "preempted mid-transaction storms the bus -> panic(4). This is what crash-looped the FeatherS3 out-and-about" — and `HardwareOne.cpp:1608-1610` repeats it as the reason `sensor_queue_task` is pinned to Core 1 "because it runs the I2C device-init transactions". Yet the boot path runs that same class of work directly on Core 0: `initI2CBuses()` (:1531), `rtcEarlyBootSync()` (:1536, :1683), `discoverI2CDevices()` (:1776), `processAutoStartSensors()` (:1873). `powerSaveTick()` -> `setCpuFrequencyDrained()` (:2170) grabs every bus mutex and switches the CPU clock from Core 0 too. The misplacement is invisible in every inventory because `CONFIG_ARDUINO_RUNNING_CORE=1` is still present in sdkconfig (dead, since AUTOSTART is off) and reads as if the loop were on Core 1.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (identical in sdkconfig.esp32:392/1381 for the ESP32 secondary target)
- **Direction:** Decide deliberately whether the display/loop I2C belongs on Core 0 or should move to a Core-1 render task, then correct the two false claims in System_TaskUtils.h:134 and :152 so the policy text matches the build. Whichever way it goes, the `main` task needs to appear in the task inventory as an I2C actor with its real core.

### [HIGH] hardwareone_loop() runs on core 0, contradicting the documented "Arduino loop and all I2C work run on APP_CORE(1)" policy — and it does I2C
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_TaskUtils.h:134`
- **Also:** main/hardwareone-idf.cpp:26-53 (app_main runs loop() inline); sdkconfig:603,609,614,1773
- **What:** System_TaskUtils.h:133-146 states the placement policy: "PRO_CORE (0) — Wi-Fi / BLE / ESP-NOW radio + I/O paths live here. APP_CORE (1) — Arduino loop, compute/render, and all I2C work run here" and `constexpr BaseType_t I2C_SENSOR_CORE = APP_CORE;` with "Core that every I2C-touching task pins to (APP core). Core 0 is saturated by...". In reality there is no Arduino loopTask: `CONFIG_AUTOSTART_ARDUINO is not set` (sdkconfig:603), so components/arduino/cores/esp32/main.cpp's `loopTask` (which would honour CONFIG_ARDUINO_RUNNING_CORE=1, sdkconfig:609) is never created. main/hardwareone-idf.cpp's `app_main` calls `setup()` then loops on `loop()` -> `hardwareone_loop()` directly on the ESP-IDF main task, and `CONFIG_ESP_MAIN_TASK_AFFINITY_CPU0=y` (sdkconfig:1773) pins that task to core 0. Neither CONFIG_ESP_MAIN_TASK_AFFINITY_* nor CONFIG_AUTOSTART_ARDUINO nor CONFIG_ARDUINO_RUNNING_CORE is pinned in sdkconfig.defaults / .esp32s3 / .esp32 / boards/, so this is an inherited default, not a decision.
- **Why it matters:** hardwareone_loop() performs I2C from core 0 every iteration/interval: `oledUpdate()` (HardwareOne.cpp:2441 -> Adafruit_SSD1306 over Wire) and `updateBattery()` every 10 s (HardwareOne.cpp:2344 -> MAX17048 fuel gauge over I2C, System_Battery.cpp:177-210). That is precisely the contention two separate workarounds already exist for: CONFIG_ESP_INT_WDT_TIMEOUT_MS was raised 300->1500 ms because "i2c_hw_fsm_reset enters nested spinlocks (I2C + periph clock), which can stall under heavy WiFi/TLS peripheral contention" (sdkconfig.defaults:172-175), and System_WiFi.cpp:1090-1094 disables WiFi power save because "if I2C error recovery simultaneously holds I2C_ENTER_CRITICAL on Core 0 waiting for periph_spinlock, Core 0 spins with interrupts disabled and trips the interrupt WDT". Anyone reading System_TaskUtils.h will conclude that path was moved off core 0 and reason about core budgets (and `perftop`'s IDLE0/IDLE1 headroom) from a false premise.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (both S3 boards and both ESP32-classic boards use the same app_main)
- **Direction:** Either set CONFIG_ESP_MAIN_TASK_AFFINITY_CPU1=y (pin it in sdkconfig.defaults so it survives regeneration) so reality matches the written policy, or correct System_TaskUtils.h:133-146 to say the main loop is on PRO_CORE and that its I2C work is the known exception. Also note CONFIG_ARDUINO_EVENT_RUNNING_CORE=1 (sdkconfig:614) puts the `arduino_events` I/O task on the compute core, the mirror image of the same policy.

### [HIGH] No write-failure detection exists in any atomic-write helper; on a full LittleFS writeTextAtomic renames an empty tmp over users.json and the device boots into the blocking setup wizard with every account gone
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:812`
- **Also:** Same helper backs automations.json, pending_users.json, boot_anchors.json, notifications.json, pet.json. Same undetectable-ENOSPC class in System_Settings.cpp:1077 / :1169 / :3126 and WebServer_Server.cpp:963, which check only bytesWritten == 0.
- **What:** writeText() (:812-825) opens with "w", calls f.print(in) and discards the return value, then returns true unconditionally after a successful open. writeTextAtomic() (:827-848) builds on it, so the tmp is judged good purely by the open succeeding. Underneath, Arduino's File::write is fwrite into a 4096-byte fully-buffered stdio stream (components/arduino/libraries/FS/src/vfs_api.cpp:335-341, setvbuf at :250/:281), File::flush() is void and swallows fflush/fsync errors (:352-358), and File::close() is void and swallows fclose's error (:294-306). For any payload <= 4 KB an ENOSPC is therefore completely invisible; above 4 KB it only shows up as a short count that nobody compares against measureJson/length. serializeJson cannot help either: ArduinoJson 7.4.2's CountingDecorator (Serialization/CountingDecorator.hpp) accumulates whatever Print::write returns and never aborts.
- **Why it matters:** LittleFS reaches ~0 free (see the free-space finding). A login fires updateUserLastSeen -> writeTextAtomic(USERS_JSON_FILE) (System_User.cpp:1098-1136). The tmp create succeeds (metadata commit fits), the data write fails, print/flush/close report nothing, writeText returns true, and VFS::rename replaces the good users.json with a 0-byte file. Next boot: initFilesystem's first-character check skips it (empty), loadAndIncrementBootSeq (System_User.cpp:3457-3477) fails deserializeJson, renames it to users.json.bad — overwriting any previous .bad, and there is no other backup — and detectFirstTimeSetupState (HardwareOne.cpp:1319) sets SETUP_REQUIRED. firstTimeSetupIfNeeded (HardwareOne.cpp:1668) then blocks on waitForSerialInputBlocking/OLED input *before* WiFi starts at :1696, so an unattended headless unit never comes back on the network and every account is unrecoverable.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: Partly SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT.md §4.7 (weak success check). The buffered-stdio reason the check cannot work, and the users.json consequence chain, are new.
- **Direction:** Make writeText return false when print()'s count != in.length(), and add an explicit int rc = fsync/fclose check (or a size_t verify by re-stat'ing the tmp before rename) so the rename is gated on the tmp actually being complete. Keeping one users.json.prev generation would also turn this from unrecoverable into a one-line boot recovery.

### [HIGH] HTTPS silently downgrades to plain HTTP when the cert/key pair is unreadable or mismatched, and that pair is written by two independent truncating writes with no return values checked
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:1535`
- **Also:** WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp:895-896 restores the same pair; WebPage_Settings.h:2348-2352 uploads them one at a time through the generic upload handler, with the same non-atomic pairing.
- **What:** certgen writes /system/certs/https_server.crt (:1535-1541) and then /system/certs/https_server.key (:1543-1549) as two separate "w" opens. Neither cf.print() nor kf.print() has its return value checked, there is no staging file, no flush, no rollback, and no ordering guarantee between the two commits. This is the only two-file cryptographic invariant in the tree written non-atomically. On the read side, startHttpServer (WebServer_Server.cpp:5176-5282) loads both files; if either is missing/empty, or if httpd_ssl_start fails because the cert does not match the key, it logs one line and falls through to the plain-HTTP branch on port 80 while gSettings.httpsEnabled stays true.
- **Why it matters:** Reset or brownout between :1541 and :1549 leaves a new certificate paired with the previous key (LittleFS copy-on-write keeps the old key intact precisely because its "w" open never synced). At next boot certsOk is true — both files are non-empty — httpd_ssl_start fails on the mismatch, and the code takes the else branch at :5272-5276: 'falling back to plain HTTP'. The web UI, including credential login and every /api/* call, is then served unencrypted on port 80 with no persistent indicator beyond one boot-time log line. Same downgrade if either file is truncated by an ENOSPC write. This is the concrete answer to 'does the device fail open on persistence corruption': for TLS, yes.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Write both PEMs to .tmp files, verify both lengths, then rename both — and make the second rename the commit point. On the read side, when httpsEnabled is true but the certs fail to load or ssl_start fails, refuse to start plain HTTP (or start it bound to a loopback/recovery-only handler set) rather than silently serving the full UI in cleartext.

### [HIGH] cmd_certgen discards both PEM write returns and declares success, so a failed write silently downgrades the device to plain HTTP at the next boot
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:1540`
- **Also:** WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp:912-926 has the same HTTPS→HTTP fallback for the restore-only server
- **What:** After every mbedtls step is correctly error-checked, the persistence step is not: `cf.print((const char*)certPem); cf.close();` and `kf.print((const char*)keyPem); kf.close();` (System_WiFi.cpp:1540 and :1548) discard both return values. The function then unconditionally fills gDebugBuffer with "Self-signed %s certificate generated and saved" and posts SYSEVT_CERT_GENERATED.
- **Why it matters:** An RSA-2048 key PEM is ~1.7 KB and the cert ~1.2 KB — both under the 4 KB stdio buffer, so even a return check would not see ENOSPC; nothing verifies the files afterwards either. On the next boot startHttpServer() (WebServer_Server.cpp:5183-5196) accepts the certs on the sole test `sHttpsCertData.length() > 0 && sHttpsKeyData.length() > 0`, httpd_ssl_start() then fails to parse the truncated key, and control falls into the plain-HTTP branch at :5279-5310 binding port 80. gSettings.httpsEnabled stays 1, so every status surface still says HTTPS is on while the login form, session cookies and the /api/cli command bridge are served in cleartext. The downgrade emits a broadcast warning at boot only — a headless device with no serial attached shows nothing.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_HTTPS 1 in System_BuildConfig.h:100, active whenever the HTTP server is compiled in)
- **Direction:** Check both print() returns, then reopen and confirm the on-disk size equals the PEM length before reporting success; refuse to post SYSEVT_CERT_GENERATED otherwise. Separately, have startHttpServer parse the cert with mbedtls_x509_crt_parse before accepting it rather than testing length > 0.

### [MEDIUM] BATTERY_ADC_PIN is never read — the ADC channel is hardcoded ADC1_CHANNEL_7, and the in-tree instructions for the original FeatherS3 would point it at GPIO8 (I2C1 SDA)
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Battery.h:12`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Battery.cpp:104 (adc1_config_channel_atten), :127 (adc1_get_raw), :117 (logs BATTERY_PIN, not BATTERY_ADC_PIN); components/hardwareone/System_BuildConfig.h:914-916 (the wrong instruction), :784/:804/:832/:872/:917/:972/:989 (every board defines BATTERY_ADC_PIN); docs/BOARD_SWITCHING.md:246
- **What:** `#define BATTERY_ADC_CHANNEL ADC1_CHANNEL_7` is a fixed value in the header, while BATTERY_ADC_PIN is defined per board in System_BuildConfig.h. adc1_config_channel_atten and adc1_get_raw both use the fixed channel; the pin macro is only ever printed in a log line. Today the only board with BATTERY_BACKEND_ADC=1 is the Adafruit Feather ESP32 V2 with BATTERY_ADC_PIN 35, and on the original ESP32 GPIO35 is ADC1_CH7, so the two agree by coincidence.
- **Why it matters:** System_BuildConfig.h:914-916 tells the user that for an original (non-[D]) FeatherS3 they should set `BATTERY_ADC_PIN=2, BATTERY_BACKEND_ADC=1, BATTERY_BACKEND_FUEL_GAUGE=0`. Doing exactly that compiles cleanly and then calls adc1_config_channel_atten(ADC1_CHANNEL_7) = GPIO8, which is I2C_SDA_PIN_DEFAULT on that same board (System_BuildConfig.h:897). The legacy ADC driver re-muxes the pad to analog, so the primary STEMMA QT bus — fuel gauge, RTC, gamepad, every sensor — goes dark, and the battery voltage reads whatever the I2C line happens to sit at. docs/BOARD_SWITCHING.md:246 repeats the same broken promise for XIAO Plus ("ADC on GPIO10, BATTERY_ADC_PIN=10").
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: trap applies to any ESP32-S3 board switched to the ADC backend; correct today only on Adafruit Feather ESP32 V2
- **Direction:** Derive the channel from BATTERY_ADC_PIN (a small compile-time pin→ADC1 channel map per target, with a #error on a pin that has no ADC1 channel) instead of hardcoding ADC1_CHANNEL_7, and fix the FeatherS3 comment and BOARD_SWITCHING.md so they stop prescribing a pin the code cannot honour.

### [MEDIUM] BATTERY_ADC_PIN is decorative — the ADC channel is hardcoded ADC1_CHANNEL_7, so the documented FeatherS3 ADC recipe would read GPIO8 (the live I2C1 SDA pin)
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Battery.h:12`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Battery.cpp:104 (adc1_config_channel_atten), :117 (logs BATTERY_PIN, not BATTERY_ADC_PIN), :127 (adc1_get_raw), components/hardwareone/System_BuildConfig.h:804 / :832 / :872 / :917 (per-board BATTERY_ADC_PIN), :914-916 (the wrong instructions)
- **What:** System_Battery.h hardcodes `#define BATTERY_PIN 35` and `#define BATTERY_ADC_CHANNEL ADC1_CHANNEL_7`. adcBackendInit() and adcBackendSample() use BATTERY_ADC_CHANNEL exclusively and the boot log prints BATTERY_PIN. Nothing anywhere reads the per-board BATTERY_ADC_PIN macro — I grepped the whole tree; its only occurrences are the board-block definitions themselves plus two comments.
- **Why it matters:** Today it is accidentally right: the only board with BATTERY_BACKEND_ADC=1 is the Feather ESP32 V2 on classic ESP32, where ADC1_CH7 == GPIO35 == BATTERY_ADC_PIN. But System_BuildConfig.h:914-916 explicitly instructs a maintainer: 'For the original FeatherS3 with VBAT_SENSE on GPIO2, set BATTERY_ADC_PIN=2, BATTERY_BACKEND_ADC=1, BATTERY_BACKEND_FUEL_GAUGE=0'. Following that on ESP32-S3 leaves the channel at ADC1_CHANNEL_7, which on S3 maps to GPIO8 — the FeatherS3's I2C1 SDA (the same bus the MAX17048 fuel gauge and every STEMMA QT sensor sit on). The build compiles, reports a plausible-looking voltage derived from I2C bus traffic on GPIO8, and adc1_config_channel_atten() reconfigures a pad the I2C driver owns.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: Feather ESP32 V2 today (accidentally correct); breaks on any S3 board that switches to the ADC backend
- **Direction:** Derive the channel from BATTERY_ADC_PIN (adc_oneshot_io_to_channel, or a small per-target table) instead of hardcoding it, and add a static_assert that the resolved pin lands on ADC1 — never ADC2, which is unusable while WiFi is up.

### [MEDIUM] nvs_flash_erase() on NO_FREE_PAGES silently wipes BLE bonds and the WiFi driver's stored config; the comment asserts the partition holds only recreatable calibration
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_BootState.cpp:16`
- **Also:** sdkconfig:771,806,1852; System_BootState.cpp:44-55 (nvs_commit RV discarded)
- **What:** On ESP_ERR_NVS_NO_FREE_PAGES or ESP_ERR_NVS_NEW_VERSION_FOUND the code calls `nvs_flash_erase()` then re-inits, justified by the comment "Its only contents are WiFi calibration and this boot state — both recreatable". That is false for this build: sdkconfig has CONFIG_BT_ENABLED=y, CONFIG_BT_BLUEDROID_ENABLED=y and CONFIG_BT_BLE_SMP_ENABLE=y (:771-806), so Bluedroid's bonded-device store lives in the same default `nvs` partition; CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_NVS_ENABLED=y (:1852) with zero calls to `esp_wifi_set_storage(WIFI_STORAGE_RAM)` anywhere in the tree, so the driver's last-used SSID/PSK live there too. The partition is 0x6000 = 24 KB = 6 pages, one of which NVS reserves, shared by PHY calibration + WiFi + BLE bonds + this key. `nvs_flash_erase()`'s return value is also discarded, as is `nvs_commit()`'s in writeCount (:53).
- **Why it matters:** Once 24 KB of shared NVS fills — plausible after enough BLE bondings and WiFi config writes, since 6 pages is barely above the 3-page minimum — the very first NVS user at boot (bootStateInit, reached from initFilesystem:194 well before WiFi or BT init) erases the whole partition to recover a boot counter. Every paired BLE device (R1 ring, G2 glasses, phone) loses its bond and must be re-paired, with a single ERROR line as the only trace. The recovery is silently disproportionate to the thing it is protecting.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; prior art: SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT.md §Environmental notes cleartext WiFi creds in NVS. The erase-wipes-BLE-bonds consequence and the incorrect comment are new.
- **Direction:** Either give bootstate its own small nvs partition so the erase is scoped to recreatable data, or drop the erase entirely and let sNvsReady stay false (the accessors already degrade to 0). At minimum, fix the comment and emit a logSystemEvent so a mass un-pairing is attributable.

### [MEDIUM] Camera frame DMA targets PSRAM; a concurrent flash erase blocks GDMA→PSRAM and produces the FB-OVF flood the debug dedupe was written for
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Camera_DVP.cpp:345`
- **Also:** System_Camera_DVP.cpp:406-410 (the non-PSRAM-DMA fallback already routes DMA to a DRAM line buffer — that variant is not exposed)
- **What:** `config.fb_location = CAMERA_FB_IN_PSRAM` (:345, :406) with `fb_count` 1-2 (:347, :386, :408). In `psram_mode` the esp32-camera S3 backend points its GDMA descriptors straight at the PSRAM frame buffer (`managed_components/espressif__esp32-camera/target/esp32s3/ll_cam.c:513-514, 535-538`), and its ISRs are NOT cache-safe (`# CONFIG_LCD_CAM_ISR_IRAM_SAFE is not set`, sdkconfig:2804; `# CONFIG_GDMA_ISR_IRAM_SAFE is not set`, sdkconfig:1553). On ESP32-S3 peripheral access to external RAM goes through the same cache/MMU the flash driver disables, so for the duration of finding 1's window neither the ISR nor the DMA can make progress.
- **Why it matters:** A ~50 ms (worst case several hundred ms) stall against a 33-66 ms frame period means the camera FIFO/descriptor chain overruns, producing `cam_hal` EV-EOF-OVF / FB-OVF errors. Each of those is an `ESP_LOGE` that reaches the debug_out error path and — see finding 4 — appends to `errors.log`, which is another flash write, which is another erase. This is not speculative history: the dedupe comment at `System_Debug.cpp:285-291` names "cam_hal FB-OVF bursts" as the flood that "once caused a stack overflow in this task". I am marking this PLAUSIBLE rather than CONFIRMED because the GDMA-blocked-by-cache-off behaviour is Espressif platform behaviour I could not prove from source in this tree, and because the board is not the one currently built.
- **Confidence:** PLAUSIBLE — board: XIAO ESP32S3 Sense only (ENABLE_CAMERA_SENSOR gated at System_BuildConfig.h:183-189; 0 on the primary FeatherS3)
- **Direction:** When this board is next built, check whether the PSRAM_DMA-disabled configuration at :406-410 (DMA→internal line buffer, FB still in PSRAM) is the safer default while any filesystem logging is active; and confirm the FB-OVF error text cannot re-enter the errors.log write path.

### [MEDIUM] The camera's SCCB driver claims I2C port 1 — the same hardware port as Wire1/bus 0 — with nothing preventing the two from being built together
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Camera_DVP.cpp:245`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Camera_DVP.cpp:309-318 (raw Wire begin/probe/end with no bus-1 mutex)
- **What:** `sdkconfig` sets `CONFIG_SCCB_HARDWARE_I2C_PORT1=y` (line 2791) and `CONFIG_SCCB_HARDWARE_I2C_DRIVER_NEW=y`, so esp32-camera compiles `sccb-ng.c`, whose `SCCB_I2C_PORT_DEFAULT` is 1. `initCamera` sets `config.pin_sccb_sda = 40` (>= 0), which makes `esp_camera_init` take the `SCCB_Init(sda, scl)` branch (esp_camera.c:187) rather than `SCCB_Use_Port`. `SCCB_Init` calls `i2c_new_master_bus` with `.i2c_port = 1` (sccb-ng.c:114-141). The I2C manager maps `wires[0] = &Wire1` (System_I2C_Manager.cpp:52), and `Wire1` is `TwoWire(1)` → also ESP I2C port 1. Additionally, `initCamera` does a raw pre-probe on `Wire` (= port 0, the manager's bus 1): `Wire.begin(40, 39)` at line 309, four `beginTransmission`/`endTransmission` pairs, then an unconditional `Wire.end()` at line 318 — all without touching the bus-1 mutex, and `Wire.begin()` is a no-op if the port is already up (Wire.cpp:297-300), so on a bus-1-enabled board it would probe the wrong pins and then deinit an in-use bus.
- **Why it matters:** On a XIAO ESP32S3 Sense built with `I2C_FEATURE_LEVEL != 0`, whichever subsystem initialises second loses port 1: if `initI2CBuses()` runs first (it does — HardwareOne.cpp:1571, before camera start), `SCCB_Init` gets `ESP_ERR_INVALID_STATE` and the camera fails with "sccb init err" permanently; if the camera ever comes up first, `Wire1.begin()` fails and — per the separate `initBus` finding — the manager still reports bus 0 online and every sensor enters a recovery loop. The only thing preventing this today is a prose instruction in System_BuildConfig.h ("Set to DISABLED for the XIAO ESP32S3 Sense build"), with no `#error` enforcing it. The `Wire.end()` bypass is inert on XIAO (bus 1 is unavailable there) but is a live foot-gun if a camera board ever gains a second I2C port.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: XIAO ESP32S3 Sense (ARDUINO_XIAO_ESP32S3_DEV + XIAO_ESP32S3_SENSE_ENABLED) only — ENABLE_CAMERA_SENSOR is 0 on every other board
- **Direction:** Add a compile-time guard in System_BuildConfig.h: `#if ENABLE_CAMERA_SENSOR && ENABLE_I2C_SYSTEM` → `#error` naming the port-1 collision, or flip the camera to `CONFIG_SCCB_HARDWARE_I2C_PORT0` and reserve port 0 for it. The raw `Wire` probe block (System_Camera_DVP.cpp:304-322) is debug scaffolding that duplicates what `esp_camera_init` already reports — deleting it removes an unsynchronised bus-1 begin/end pair for free.

### [MEDIUM] System_Debug's enqueueChunk advertises ISR safety it does not have: PSRAM message pool, flash-resident code, flash-resident literal, and unpropagated FromISR wake
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Debug.cpp:729`
- **Also:** the same ISR/task fork exists at :415-419 (growth refusal) and :738-746 (uxQueueMessagesWaitingFromISR); those two are correct as written
- **What:** enqueueChunk() (:735-772) branches on xPortInIsrContext() and is documented at :729-734 as "ISR-safe (uses the *FromISR APIs in interrupt context)"; growDebugPoolIfNeeded() (:415-419) reinforces this by explicitly refusing heap growth in ISR context and deferring it to the consumer. But the DebugMessage pool it writes into is PSRAM: the primary pool is ps_alloc(..., AllocPref::PreferPSRAM, "debug.pool") at :622-625, and the growth block is heap_caps_malloc(..., MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM | MALLOC_CAP_8BIT) at :438-439. The ISR branch does msg->timestamp = millis() (:757), memcpy(msg->text, text, copy) (:760), and markTrunc() (:762), which memcpy's the string literal "[CUT]" (:724). None of this is IRAM/DRAM resident: the linker map places enqueueChunk and debugQueuePrintf at 0x4207xxxx and millis at 0x4200eccc, all in flash-mapped IROM, and "[CUT]" is ordinary .rodata in DROM. Separately, all three FromISR calls (:752, :764, :767) pass NULL for pxHigherPriorityTaskWoken and there is no portYIELD_FROM_ISR() anywhere in components/hardwareone/.
- **Why it matters:** If this branch ever executes it violates P1 (handler code in flash), P2 (message pool in PSRAM), P3 ("[CUT]" literal in DROM) and P15 (dropped higher-priority-task-woken flag) simultaneously — during any SPI-flash write or erase, all four are inaccessible and the ISR panics rather than logging. Today the branch is unreachable: I verified independently that first-party code registers zero interrupts (zero esp_intr_alloc / gpio_isr_handler_add / attachInterrupt / timer_isr_callback_add hits, zero IRAM_ATTR/DRAM_ATTR, and zero bytes from libhardwareone.a / libhardwareone_libs.a / main.a in the 0x4037xxxx-0x4038xxxx IRAM range of build/hardwareone-idf.map), and the one path an IDF/vendor ISR could take into this module — idfLogVprintf at :1075 — correctly bails to the previous vprintf handler before touching the queue. So the concrete failure is not a live crash but a false safety guarantee: the next author who adds a genuine ISR and calls debugQueuePrintf/broadcastOutput on the strength of the :729 comment gets a hard, load-dependent panic instead of a log line. Note the codebase already reasons correctly about this elsewhere — System_Automation.cpp:3681-3689 refuses PSRAM for the event drain buffer for exactly this class of reason.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (PSRAM builds; on a no-PSRAM board the pool falls back to internal via AllocPref::PreferInternal at :622, which removes the P2 half only)
- **Direction:** Decide which way this goes rather than leaving it half-built. Either delete the ISR branch and state that the debug system is task-context-only (cheapest, and matches reality — nothing installs an ISR), or make the claim true by keeping a small internal-DRAM slice of the pool reserved for ISR producers and marking the enqueue path plus its helpers IRAM_ATTR with the literal in DRAM_ATTR. If the branch is kept, the FromISR calls should take a real BaseType_t and the caller should yield.

### [MEDIUM] Debug message pool is PSRAM while enqueueChunk advertises and implements an ISR path that memcpys into it
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Debug.cpp:624`
- **Also:** Same pattern reasoning is applied correctly elsewhere: OLED_Utils.cpp:2421-2425 and System_Automation.cpp:3711-3715 both spell out MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL rather than inheriting a tunable.
- **What:** gDebugPoolPrimary is ps_alloc'd with PreferPSRAM whenever hasPsram (:623-625), and the growth half is heap_caps_malloc(..., MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM | MALLOC_CAP_8BIT) at :438-439. enqueueChunk (:735) is documented at :729 as "ISR-safe (uses the *FromISR APIs in interrupt context)", branches on xPortInIsrContext() at :738, and in that branch pulls a pool slot with xQueueReceiveFromISR (:752) and then does msg->timestamp = ..., memcpy(msg->text, text, copy) (:757-762) — writes straight into a PSRAM buffer from interrupt context.
- **Why it matters:** This is pitfall P2 in its exact form: an interrupt that is allowed to run during a flash operation (i.e. one allocated with ESP_INTR_FLAG_IRAM) touching PSRAM faults with "Cache disabled but cached memory region accessed". Today the branch is unreachable — first-party code installs zero ISRs (0 hits for esp_intr_alloc/gpio_isr_handler_add/attachInterrupt/IRAM_ATTR in components/hardwareone), and the one vendor route in, idfLogVprintf, bails on xPortInIsrContext() at :1075 before reaching enqueueChunk. The defect is the contract: the file tells a future author that DEBUGF from an ISR is supported, and the first IRAM-flagged ISR that takes that invitation crashes intermittently and only under flash write load. The pool-growth path at :415-420 already reasons correctly about ISR context (it refuses to allocate) — so the author considered ISR context and still left the PSRAM write in.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all boards with PSRAM
- **Direction:** Either state the coupling in the enqueueChunk header ('ISR-safe with respect to FreeRTOS APIs only - the message pool is PSRAM, so this branch must never actually run from an IRAM ISR'), or delete the ISR branch outright since it is unreachable, which also removes the P15 nit that its three *FromISR calls pass NULL for pxHigherPriorityTaskWoken with no portYIELD_FROM_ISR.

### [MEDIUM] Live system-log writer discards `printf()` and all four `appendLineWithCap()` returns, so errors.log / system-events.log / events.log stop recording with no indication
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Debug.cpp:267`
- **Also:** System_Utils.cpp:939 (command-audit.log) discards the same bool
- **What:** In the `debug_out` task: `gSystemLogFile.printf(...)` at :267 and :269 with returns discarded, `gSystemLogFile.flush()` at :275 (void), and `appendLineWithCap(...)` at :306 (errors.log), :320 (system-events.log) and :330 (events.log) with the bool discarded at every site. The handle is held open across writes and flushed only every 20 messages or 5 s (:271-278).
- **Why it matters:** These four files are the device's always-on record: `[ERROR]`, `[EVENT]`, `[EVLOG]` and the `log start` capture. When the filesystem fills, `printf` returns short and `appendLineWithCap` returns false, all six returns are thrown away, `gSystemLogRunning` stays true, and `log status` reports the capture is running. The operator's entire diagnostic surface goes quiet at exactly the moment something is wrong, and the thing that would have told them — an `[ERROR]` line about the failed write — is itself routed through the same dead path. Compounding it, the open handle means a commanded reboot loses up to 20 lines / 5 s anyway (`System_Utils.cpp:2105` restarts after a bare `delay`), so the last writes before a fault are the least likely to survive.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: docs/LOGGING_TAG_AUDIT.md covers the parse/tagging side of these same files; the write-failure side is not in it.
- **Direction:** Latch a `gLogWriteFailed` flag when `appendLineWithCap` returns false or `printf` short-writes, surface it in `log status` / `logstatus` / the web logging pane, and post it once as a SYSEVT through the non-file sinks (serial + web mirror still work). A single one-shot signal is enough; the goal is that "the logs went quiet" is distinguishable from "nothing happened".

### [MEDIUM] Every ERROR_*F / DEBUG_*F / broadcastOutput() call before initDebugSystem() is silently discarded, including the settings-load failure reason
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Debug.cpp:737`
- **Also:** System_Debug.cpp:775 (debugQueuePrintf), System_Debug.cpp:901 (broadcastOutputCore), System_Debug.h:440-445 (DEBUGF_QUEUE)
- **What:** `enqueueChunk()` opens with `if (!gDebugOutputQueue || !gDebugFreeQueue) return false;` (System_Debug.cpp:735-737) and `debugQueuePrintf()` early-returns on the same condition (:775). There is no direct-Serial fallback on either path, and `broadcastOutputCore()` (:901) funnels into enqueueChunk with no fallback of its own. `initDebugSystem()` — which creates those queues — runs at HardwareOne.cpp:1476, but `initFilesystem()` (:1310), `detectFirstTimeSetupState()` (:1319), `readSettingsJson()` (:1345), `writeSettingsJson()` (:1355), `readDebugJson()` (:1363), `initializeCommandSystem()` (:1372), `initMutexes()` (:1469) and `initSensorQueue()` (:1472) have all already run.
- **Why it matters:** `readSettingsJson()` reports its failures exclusively through `ERROR_STORAGEF` — "Failed to open settings file" and "JSON parse error: %s" (System_Settings.cpp, ~+23 and +36 from the function head). `ERROR_STORAGEF` expands via `DEBUGF_QUEUE` (System_Debug.h:597, :440-445) straight into `debugQueuePrintf`, which returns without printing because the queue does not exist yet. So on a device whose settings.json is truncated or corrupt, the operator sees only the generic `logSystemEvent("SETTINGS", "settings.json load FAILED — running on defaults")` at HardwareOne.cpp:1349 and never learns *why*. The same blackout covers `broadcastOutput("[build] Firmware: ...")` at :1459 and any diagnostics inside command registration. The codebase already has the correct pattern and uses it in exactly one place: `logSystemEvent()` (System_Debug.cpp:828-844) detects the null queue, prints straight to Serial, and stashes a copy in a pre-init ring that `flushPreInitEvents()` (:690, :859) replays into the durable log. broadcastOutput and the whole ERROR_*F family never got that fallback.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Give enqueueChunk the same null-queue fallback logSystemEvent already implements — direct Serial plus the pre-init ring — so the earliest and most failure-prone stretch of boot is not the one stretch with no diagnostics.

### [MEDIUM] `enqueueChunk()` advertises itself as ISR-safe but fills a PSRAM-resident message pool -- a false contract that would fault during any flash write
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Debug.cpp:735`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Debug.cpp:415-420 (`growDebugPoolIfNeeded` correctly refuses to allocate in ISR context -- that half of the ISR story is right, which is what makes the PSRAM half look intentional)
- **What:** `enqueueChunk()` is documented at :729 as "ISR-safe (uses the *FromISR APIs in interrupt context)" and branches on `xPortInIsrContext()` at :738, taking `uxQueueMessagesWaitingFromISR` (:744), `xQueueReceiveFromISR` (:752), `xQueueSendFromISR` (:764,:767). The `DebugMessage` slots it hands out come from `ps_alloc(..., AllocPref::PreferPSRAM, "debug.pool")` at :624 and, on growth, `heap_caps_malloc(..., MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM | MALLOC_CAP_8BIT)` at :438-439 -- strictly PSRAM. Lines :757-762 then write `msg->timestamp`, `msg->category`, `msg->routing` and `memcpy(msg->text, text, copy)` -- roughly 260+ bytes into PSRAM -- inside the ISR branch. `DEBUG_MSG_SIZE` is 256 (System_Debug.h:212).
- **Why it matters:** PSRAM is unmapped whenever the flash cache is disabled, and this build has `# CONFIG_SPI_FLASH_AUTO_SUSPEND is not set` (sdkconfig:2560), so every LittleFS/NVS write and erase opens such a window. If anyone wires a real interrupt handler allocated with `ESP_INTR_FLAG_IRAM` to any DEBUG_*/broadcastOutput path -- which the :729 comment explicitly invites -- a log line emitted during a settings save becomes a Cache-disabled load/store panic, not a dropped message. Even outside a flash window a PSRAM write from an ISR costs roughly an order of magnitude more than a DRAM write, so the "safe" path is also the slow path. Today nothing reaches it: first-party code installs zero ISRs, and `idfLogVprintf` explicitly bails before `enqueueChunk` in ISR context (:1075). So this is a live trap in a published contract rather than an active crash.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards with PSRAM (FeatherS3 quad PSRAM = the primary board); degrades to correct-but-still-mislabelled on a PSRAM-less build
- **Direction:** Either delete the ISR branch and the ISR-safety claim outright (nothing calls it, and `idfLogVprintf` already has the correct fallback), or seed a handful of the free-queue slots from `MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL` and hand only those out when `xPortInIsrContext()`. Leaving a false ISR-safe contract on a PSRAM buffer is the worst of the three options.

### [MEDIUM] cmd_i2cscan drives 252 raw I2C probes from cmd_exec_task, which is pinned to core 0 — the "no exceptions" I2C core rule is violated
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_I2C.cpp:829`
- **Also:** Same exposure for any other command handler that transacts inline rather than routing through the sensor queue. Sensor start/stop is clean — those go through enqueueDeviceStart (System_I2C.cpp:345) into sensor_queue_task on I2C_SENSOR_CORE.
- **What:** System_TaskUtils.h:154-158 states: "ANY task that touches the shared I2C/Wire bus (use I2C_SENSOR_CORE). This is the hard rule — no exceptions. An unpinned I2C task that floats onto the saturated Core 0 and is preempted mid-transaction storms the bus -> panic(4)." cmd_i2cscan (registered at System_I2C.cpp:2295) loops addr 1..126 on each initialised bus calling i2cPingAddress + i2cConfirmPresent (System_I2C.h:259, :236, :298), each of which takes the bus mutex and issues a real wire->beginTransmission/endTransmission. Every CLI surface funnels to cmd_exec_task, which HardwareOne.cpp:1514 pins to core 0 with an explicit `0`. The same policy block at System_TaskUtils.h:161-163 separately assigns cmd_exec to core 0, so the policy contradicts itself and the weaker bullet wins in code.
- **Why it matters:** Running `i2cscan` from serial, web /api/cli, MQTT, BLE, the OLED or the glasses executes up to 252 address probes plus read-backs on the saturated radio core, each holding the bus mutex across a live transaction. This is the exact preempted-mid-transaction condition the header blames for the FeatherS3 out-and-about crash loop (panic(4)/INT-WDT, docs/NewCapture 2026-07-22) — only now it is deliberate rather than accidental, and it is the single densest burst of bus traffic the firmware ever produces.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: All boards with ENABLE_I2C_SYSTEM
- **Direction:** Resolve the policy contradiction explicitly. Either route bus-scanning commands through the existing sensor-queue processor (already on I2C_SENSOR_CORE) the way sensor starts are routed, or state in System_TaskUtils.h that the I2C rule binds background pollers only and that operator-initiated scans accept the risk — but do not leave the header asserting "no exceptions" while the densest I2C burst in the firmware runs on core 0.

### [MEDIUM] imuBus / thermalBus / tofBus / apdsBus / servoBus are accepted, validated and persisted but never read; the drivers hardcode &Wire1
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_I2C.cpp:2981`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/i2csensor_bno055.cpp:390,1179; i2csensor_mlx90640.cpp:541; i2csensor_vl53l4cx.cpp:342; System_I2C.cpp:818-821.
- **What:** The settings registry exposes imuBus, thermalBus, tofBus, apdsBus and servoBus as 0..1 dropdowns labelled '0|I2C1,1|I2C2' with CLI commands imubus/thermalbus/tofbus/apdsbus (System_I2C.cpp:818-821, 2981-2985). Grepping gSettings.<name> across components/hardwareone returns zero consumers for all five -- the only references are the setter command and the registry row. The corresponding drivers bind bus 0 unconditionally: i2csensor_bno055.cpp:390 `new Adafruit_BNO055(55, addr, &Wire1)` and :1179 `Wire1.beginTransmission(I2C_ADDR_IMU)` inside the 4-arg (bus-0) i2cTaskWithTimeout; i2csensor_mlx90640.cpp:541 `gMLX90640->begin(..., &Wire1)`; i2csensor_vl53l4cx.cpp:342 `setI2cDevice(&Wire1)`. Contrast inputBus (17 refs), fmRadioBus (16), oledBus (16), rtcBus (6), fuelGaugeBus (6), gpsBus (5), presenceBus (3), which are all genuinely honoured.
- **Why it matters:** A user who physically moves the thermal camera to the vertical STEMMA QT and runs `thermalbus 1` gets 'reboot required' and a persisted setting, but after the reboot the driver still hammers bus 0. The device NACKs, hits three consecutive errors, and is marked degraded -- and if two or more such mis-routed sensors are on a bus 0 populated with three or more devices, checkBusRecoveryNeeded() crosses the >66% threshold and tears down the healthy primary bus on a 5-second cadence. The setting is worse than absent because it advertises a capability that silently produces bus faults.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (drivers are compiled out on the default FeatherS3 build; live on any build enabling IMU/thermal/ToF/APDS/servo); prior art: Related to the 'stored-but-never-read settings' cluster in docs/SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT.md, but these five bus settings are not among the ~13 listed there.
- **Direction:** Either wire these five settings through to their drivers the way rtcResolveBus/presenceResolveBus/fuelGaugeResolveBus already do (resolve bus -> getWire(bus) -> 5-arg transaction helper), or remove the settings and the CLI commands so the UI stops offering a move that cannot happen.

### [MEDIUM] `i2creset` can only recover bus 0 — there is no operator path to recover bus 1, which carries the OLED on the primary board
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_I2C.cpp:2169`
- **What:** `cmd_i2creset` calls `mgr->performBusRecovery()` with the default argument, i.e. `busIdx = 0` (System_I2C_Manager.h:251). The command ignores `argsInput` entirely (only `RETURN_VALID_IF_VALIDATE_CSTR()` touches it) and its registry entry (line 2288) advertises no argument. `mgr->pausePolling()` at line 2165 is the blanket form, so it pauses BOTH buses while recovering only one. The bus-aware wrapper `i2cBusRecovery(uint8_t busIdx = 0)` exists in System_I2C.h:384 but nothing in the CLI ever passes 1; the only bus-1 recovery in the tree is the hardcoded `performBusRecovery(1)` inside `oledResumeFromSleep` (OLED_Utils.cpp:6776), which only runs on a power-save wake.
- **Why it matters:** On the FeatherS3[D], `oledBus` defaults to 1 and `i2c2Enabled` defaults to 1, so the display — the device most likely to wedge the bus, since the code at OLED_Utils.cpp:6756-6773 documents that the SSD1306 leaves SDA stuck LOW when it loses Vcc mid-transaction — is on bus 1. If bus 1 wedges outside a sleep/wake cycle, `i2creset` reports "[I2C] Bus reset complete" while having done nothing to the wedged bus, and the only remaining recovery is a reboot.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: FeatherS3[D] (the only board with bus 1 wired and enabled by default)
- **Direction:** Accept an optional bus argument (`i2creset [0|1|all]`, default all) and loop `performBusRecovery(b)` over initialised buses; pair the blanket `pausePolling()` with per-bus pauses or keep the blanket pause but recover every bus under it. The output should name the bus(es) actually recovered.

### [MEDIUM] checkTaskStackSafety's overflow bailout is itself ~1.2 KB deep — it runs snprintf and log_e at the moment it declares the stack nearly exhausted
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_I2C.h:213`
- **Also:** callers: i2csensor_seesaw.cpp:472, i2csensor_ano_encoder.cpp, i2csensor_mlx90640.cpp, i2csensor_bno055.cpp, i2csensor_vl53l4cx.cpp, i2csensor_apds9960.cpp, i2csensor_sths34pf80.cpp, i2csensor_pa1010d.cpp, i2csensor_ds3231.cpp, i2csensor_rda5807.cpp
- **What:** When uxTaskGetStackHighWaterMark drops below totalStackWords/20 (5%), the function allocates `char reason[64]`, calls snprintf (192 B frame -> _vsnprintf_r 144 -> _svfprintf_r 800), then sensorStatusBumpWith, then log_e (Arduino -> esp_log -> vprintf). That is roughly 1.2 KB of additional depth. For the 3072-byte sensor tasks the trip threshold is 153 bytes of lifetime-minimum free stack.
- **Why it matters:** Marked PLAUSIBLE rather than CONFIRMED because uxTaskGetStackHighWaterMark returns the lifetime minimum, not the current depth — at the moment of the check the task is normally shallow, so the bailout usually fits. But the design is inverted: the routine whose job is to prevent an overflow is one of the deepest things the sensor tasks run, and it only runs in the state where headroom has already been proven scarce. If the historical minimum was set by a path that is also live now (e.g. the ESP-NOW broadcast chain during a bus fault, see the captureEspNowFrame finding), the bailout is what tips it over.
- **Confidence:** PLAUSIBLE — board: all boards with I2C sensor tasks
- **Direction:** Make the bailout allocation-free: drop the snprintf (pass sensorName straight through to a fixed cause string), and use the queued debug path rather than log_e's vprintf chain. Setting the enable flag false and returning true is the only load-bearing part.

### [MEDIUM] Per-transaction clock switching does a heap rm/add of every device handle under the new i2c_master driver, on the OLED frame path
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_I2C_Manager.cpp:533`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_I2C.h:250-253 and :283-289 (probe/confirm helpers, two setClock calls each); components/arduino/cores/esp32/esp32-hal-i2c-ng.c:389-440.
- **What:** setBusClock() calls wire->setClock(hz) whenever the requested rate differs from currentClockHz[bus], and executeTransaction() calls it twice per transaction (device clock in, default clock out). Under IDF >= 5.4 the active Arduino HAL is esp32-hal-i2c-ng.c, whose i2cSetClock (lines 389-440) loops over all 128 dev_handles and, for each non-NULL one, calls i2c_master_bus_rm_device() then i2cAddDeviceIfNeeded(). i2c_master_bus_rm_device (IDF i2c_master.c:1131-1153) does two free()s; i2c_master_bus_add_device does two calloc()s. The device DB mixes rates: 400 kHz for VL53L4CX, seesaw input, SSD1306 and MAX17048; 100 kHz for BNO055, MLX90640, PA1010D, RDA5807, DS3231, STHS34PF80, PCA9685 (System_I2C.cpp:221-249).
- **Why it matters:** displayUpdate() pushes the SSD1306 framebuffer at 400 kHz up to every 125 ms (OLED_Utils.cpp:3662, gSettings.oledUpdateInterval default 125; animated modes run at animationFPS, default 10). Each frame therefore triggers two full rm/add sweeps over every address the HAL has ever transacted with on that port -- on a populated bus that is roughly 8 devices, i.e. ~32 free()s and ~32 calloc()s of small internal-DRAM blocks per frame, plus 2 x delayMicroseconds(50) of busy-wait. The 400 kHz seesaw/ToF polls add more. Under the legacy driver this same call was a pure timing-register reprogram (esp32-hal-i2c.c:317-341) with no allocation, so the NG migration silently converted a register write into sustained small-block heap churn -- directly relevant given the paused HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT work. i2cscan is worse: i2cProbeAddress (System_I2C.h:250-253) does two setClock calls per address across 126 addresses.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Stop round-tripping the clock. Either keep the bus permanently at the highest rate every device on it tolerates, or cache the last-set rate per bus and only change it when the incoming device genuinely needs a different one (drop the restore-to-default on transaction exit). If per-device rates must be preserved, talk to i2c_master directly with per-device handles carrying their own scl_speed_hz instead of going through TwoWire::setClock.

### [MEDIUM] The I2C ISR core-pin workaround is hardcoded to bus 0, but the input device's bus is a user-settable setting (`inputbus 1` silently reverts the Int-WDT mitigation)
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_I2C_Manager.cpp:280`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_I2C_Manager.cpp:401 (same hardcoded `busIdx == 0` in performBusRecovery)
- **What:** `beginBusOnCpu1()` (:239-253) spawns a short-lived CPU1-pinned task so that `TwoWire::begin()` -> Arduino `i2cInit` -> `i2c_driver_install(port, mode, 0,0,0)` (components/arduino/cores/esp32/esp32-hal-i2c.c:141) calls `esp_intr_alloc` on CPU1, pinning the legacy I2C driver's interrupt off the Bluedroid controller's core. I verified the premise still holds on IDF 5.5.1: intr_alloc_flags is literally `0`, and `esp_intr_alloc` pins to the calling core. But `initBus()` applies it only under a hardcoded `if (busIdx == 0)` (:280), and `performBusRecovery()` repeats the same hardcoded test (:401). The gamepad/ANO encoder's actual bus is `gSettings.inputBus` (System_Settings.h:817, default 0 at :247), which is exposed as the runtime command `inputbus <0|1>` (System_I2C.cpp:813, registered :2274) and as an editable settings field (System_I2C.cpp:2976, range 0..1, "reboot required"). `wires[0] = &Wire1` and `wires[1] = &Wire` (System_I2C_Manager.cpp:52-53).
- **Why it matters:** Set `inputbus 1` and reboot. The input device now lives on bus 1, so `initBus(1, ...)` takes the `else` branch at :284-286 and calls `wire->begin()` on the *calling* core -- the boot/main task on CPU0, the same core as `btdm_controller_task`. The exact condition the mitigation was written for is restored: the comment at :201-212 documents that a glitchy transaction on the gamepad bus makes the legacy driver re-arm its command from inside the ISR, and that interrupt storm monopolizes CPU0 and trips the Interrupt WDT (CONFIG_ESP_INT_WDT_TIMEOUT_MS=1500) -> device reset. Nothing warns the user; `inputbus` reports success and the only symptom is a reboot loop under BLE + gamepad load. `performBusRecovery` has the same hole, so even a bus-0 device that recovers onto a different index is unprotected.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards with I2C_FEATURE_LEVEL>0 AND ENABLE_BLUETOOTH=1 (FeatherS3 primary: I2C_FEATURE_LEVEL=4, ENABLE_BLUETOOTH=1)
- **Direction:** Gate on the configured bus rather than the literal 0 -- e.g. `if (busIdx == (uint8_t)gSettings.inputBus)` at both sites. `initBus` already reads `gSettings` via `busPinsFromSettings` (:190-199) and runs at CRASH_PHASE_HARDWARE, well after settings load, so the value is available. Alternatively pin both buses to CPU1, since neither ISR benefits from living on the BLE core.

### [MEDIUM] `[MEMSAMPLE]` DRAM total underflows to ~4.19 million KB — the primary internal-DRAM surface prints garbage for total and 0% used
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_MemoryMonitor.cpp:180`
- **Also:** The codebase already gets this right elsewhere and treats ESP.getHeapSize() as the DRAM total directly: System_Utils.cpp:3498 (printMemoryReport) and System_Utils.cpp:4051 (cmd_memreport json). Related stale comments asserting the opposite: G2_Glasses.cpp:24 ("ESP.getFreeHeap() includes PSRAM") and G2_Glasses.cpp:8133 ("ESP.getFreeHeap() is misleading here") — both false on arduino-esp32 3.x, though the code at those sites uses the precise heap_caps call anyway.
- **What:** `size_t dramTotal = totalHeap - totalPsram;` where `totalHeap = ESP.getHeapSize()` and `totalPsram = ESP.getPsramSize()`. This repo vendors arduino-esp32 3.x, whose EspClass::getHeapSize() is `heap_caps_get_total_size(MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL)` (components/arduino/cores/esp32/Esp.cpp:159-161) — already internal-only. Subtracting the ~7.8 MB SPIRAM total from a ~300 KB internal total underflows size_t to ≈4.29e9. dramUsedPercent at :183 is then computed from that value (and overflows again in the *100 term).
- **Why it matters:** With PSRAM present, `totalHeap` (~0.3-0.4 MB internal) minus `totalPsram` (multiple MB) wraps `size_t`. `dramTotal` becomes ~4,286,900,000; `dramUsedPercent = ((dramTotal - dramFree) * 100) / dramTotal` (:183) then overflows the 32-bit multiply and lands on 0. The two `[MEMSAMPLE]` DRAM lines print e.g. `DRAM: 60/4186492 KB (0% used)` on every sample. Both target boards set `CONFIG_SPIRAM=y` (`sdkconfig`, `sdkconfig.esp32`), so this fires on every build. `dramFree`/`dramMinFree`/`dramLargest` and the pressure + fragmentation logic are unaffected — only the total and the percent are wrong, which is why it has survived: the numbers that matter still move. The sibling `cmd_memreport --json` at `System_Utils.cpp:3984` and `printMemoryReport()` at `:3431` both use `ESP.getHeapSize()` directly as the DRAM total and are correct.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all (any build with CONFIG_SPIRAM=y — both feathers3 and esp32 defaults)
- **Direction:** `ESP.getHeapSize()` is already the internal-DRAM total — drop the subtraction. The stale comment about `heap_caps_get_total_size()` "not available in ESP-IDF v5.3.1" should go too; the project is on v5.5.1 and `System_Utils.cpp:3431` already does it the simple way.

### [MEDIUM] applyPowerMode() and cmd_cpufreq change the CPU clock without the I2C drain guard that was added for the gps_task panic
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Power.cpp:106`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:1379 (cmd_cpufreq); components/hardwareone/HardwareOne.cpp:2170-2187 (the guard itself, static).
- **What:** setCpuFrequencyDrained() (HardwareOne.cpp:2170-2187) is `static` in HardwareOne.cpp and is reachable only from powerSaveTick()'s idle-enter (line 2286) and wake (line 2207). It pauses polling and takes every per-bus mutex before switching. Three other clock-change sites bypass it entirely: applyPowerMode() calls setCpuFrequencyMhz(targetFreq) raw at System_Power.cpp:106; cmd_cpufreq calls it raw at System_Utils.cpp:1379; and applyPowerMode is itself the dispatch target for `power mode X` from CLI, the OLED Power menu and the G2 Power page.
- **Why it matters:** This is the exact race that caused the 2026-07-17 reset=panic(4) crash loop (documented in HardwareOne.cpp:2281-2285): switching the CPU clock while gps_task was mid-I2C transaction. Any admin can reproduce the unguarded switch with `power mode performance` or `cpufreq 160` while the GPS/thermal/OLED tasks are polling. cmd_cpufreq is not covered by the in-code note at G2_Page_Power.cpp:27-33, which mentions only the `power mode` path; and that note's mitigation argument ('applyPowerMode now only ever sets >=80 MHz') does not apply to cmd_cpufreq's 80/160/240 transitions either, which still go through rtc_clk_cpu_freq_set_config_fast and the _xt_tick_divisor update.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: Partially KNOWN — in-code note at components/hardwareone/G2_Page_Power.cpp:27-33 flags the `power mode` path. cmd_cpufreq is not mentioned there and no docs/*.md covers it.
- **Direction:** Move setCpuFrequencyDrained() into System_Power.cpp (or a shared header), make it the single chokepoint, and route applyPowerMode and cmd_cpufreq through it. Leaving three raw call sites means the guard protects one of four paths.

### [MEDIUM] applyPowerMode() overwrites and persists oledBrightness on every boot, so a user-configured brightness can never survive a reboot
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Power.cpp:117`
- **What:** applySettings() ends with applyPowerMode(gSettings.powerMode) (System_Settings.cpp:857-858), which runs at boot (HardwareOne.cpp:1516) AFTER readSettingsJson() has loaded the user's values. applyPowerMode computes targetBrightness = (gPowerModes[mode].displayBrightnessPercent * 255) / 100 and, if it differs from the loaded value, calls setSetting(gSettings.oledBrightness, targetBrightness) (:117-120) - which writes settings.json.
- **Why it matters:** oledBrightness is a first-class user setting (OLED_Settings.cpp:22, range 0-255, cmdKey "oledbrightness", editable from CLI, web and the OLED settings editor). Set `oledbrightness 100` on the default power mode (mode 0 Performance, displayBrightnessPercent 100 -> target 255). It persists correctly. Reboot: readSettingsJson loads 100, applySettings -> applyPowerMode(0) sees 100 != 255, calls setSetting -> 255 is written to flash. The user's value is gone and the file was rewritten during boot. The same happens on Balanced (204) and PowerSaver (128): the setting is only stable at the exact value its power mode dictates, which makes it effectively unusable. It also means a full settings.json rewrite on every boot for any user who has ever touched brightness.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all with ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY; prior art: KNOWN - docs/SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT.md §4.10 lists it as LOW "runtime-only values leak to disk". Status unchanged, but the framing understates it: this is not a leak of a runtime value, it is a pers
- **Direction:** Treat the power mode's brightness as a runtime display value (apply it to the panel without touching gSettings), or apply it only on an explicit mode CHANGE rather than on every applySettings() including the boot one.

### [MEDIUM] The three stack-watermark warning surfaces cover only 10-13 hardcoded tasks, and the warnings section prints a false all-clear when DEBUG_PERFORMANCE is off
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_TaskUtils.cpp:730`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_MemoryMonitor.cpp:274-287 (12-task list, no debug_out, no espnow_tx, no G2 workers); components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:3624-3634 (10-task list); components/hardwareone/System_TaskUtils.cpp:533-547
- **What:** uxTaskGetStackHighWaterMark IS called in ~25 places, but every surface that *warns* is keyed to a hardcoded task list. reportAllTaskStacks' knownTasks[] (:533) holds 13 entries; System_MemoryMonitor.cpp's tasks[] (:274) holds 12; System_Utils.cpp's appTasks[] (:3624) holds 10. None of the three includes any of the 13 G2/R1 workers (g2-fsm 2560 B, g2_hb_worker 2048-3072 B, g2_page_swap_w 3584 B, g2_tap_disp 6400 B, g2_ble_connect 5120 B, g2_session_w, ring_bridge_hb 3072 B, ...), mapRender, sensor_bcast, llm_gen, i2c0_begin, or the media tasks. debug_out appears only in System_Utils.cpp's list, which emits no warning at all — and System_TaskUtils.h:66 documents debug_out as HW-observed at 81% used (~764 B free), i.e. the single tightest task in the system has no warning coverage anywhere. Separately, the WARNINGS loop at :730 does `if (!isDebugFlagSet(DEBUG_PERFORMANCE)) continue;` for every task, so with that flag off no task is examined, hasWarning stays false, and the block prints "[OK] No critical warnings - all tasks healthy".
- **Why it matters:** The tasks with the smallest stacks and the deepest call chains are exactly the ones with no monitoring: the G2 workers hold the four smallest stacks in the firmware and run BLE, page-render and FS code. A near-overflow on any of them is invisible until it crashes. And the false all-clear is worse than no output — an operator running the diagnostic with DEBUG_MEMORY on but DEBUG_PERFORMANCE off gets an explicit statement that all tasks are healthy from a loop that inspected none of them. The generic 'system task' fallback at :638 does print a real HWM for unlisted tasks, but it fabricates allocBytes = free + 4096 and prints everything in integer KB, so a task with 764 B free renders as "0" and triggers nothing.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: Adjacent to recon_prior.md's note that HWM instrumentation coverage was never audited (P9/P11 row); the coverage gap and the false all-clear are new.
- **Direction:** Drive the warning pass off uxTaskGetSystemState's own usStackHighWaterMark for every task in the snapshot rather than a hardcoded name/handle list, so a new task is covered the day it is created. Separately, the '[OK] all tasks healthy' line should only print when the check actually ran — otherwise say the check was skipped.

### [MEDIUM] `reportAllTaskStacks()` prints the heap-fragmentation percentage inverted — 100% means "perfectly contiguous", 5% means "almost unusable"
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_TaskUtils.cpp:716`
- **Also:** Correct implementations to copy: System_MemoryMonitor.cpp:215, System_Utils.cpp:3510. Secondary mismatch at the same site: `heapFree` comes from `ESP.getFreeHeap()` = `heap_caps_get_free_size(MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL)` (System_TaskUtils.cpp:451) while `largestFreeBlock` uses `MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL|MALLOC_CAP_8BIT` (:452) — different pools in one ratio.
- **What:** `fragPercent = (largestFreeBlock * 100) / heapFree` is the *contiguity ratio*, but line 719 labels it `"  Heap Fragmentation:   %2u%% (largest block vs free)"`. Two sibling surfaces in the same tree compute the same quantity correctly as `100 - (largest*100)/free`: `System_MemoryMonitor.cpp:215` and `System_Utils.cpp:3510`. So the one report that runs unattended on a 60 s timer is the one that lies, and it lies in the reassuring direction.
- **Why it matters:** `hardwareone_loop()` calls `reportAllTaskStacks()` every 60 s whenever `DEBUG_MEMORY` is set (`HardwareOne.cpp:2319-2326`) — i.e. exactly when someone is chasing a memory problem. A healthy unfragmented heap (largest == free) prints "Heap Fragmentation: 100%", and a heap so shredded that the largest block is 5% of free prints "Heap Fragmentation: 5%". An operator watching the 60 s report over a multi-day soak reads the number falling toward 0 as the heap *defragmenting* while it is in fact collapsing. The `[!] FRAGMENTATION` warning 36 lines later (`:751`) uses a correct `largest < free/2` test, so the same report contradicts itself.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Make the printed quantity match its label (subtract from 100), or relabel it "Largest/Free". Whichever way it goes, it should match the two sibling computations so the three surfaces can be read against each other.

### [MEDIUM] Stack high-water marks are collected but only 14 of 38 firmware tasks are ever sized in a report — the tightest tasks are the unreported ones, and perftop prints a fabricated allocation for them
- **Status: RESOLVED 2026-08-23 for `memreport` and `reportAllTaskStacks()`.** Both now take every task's stack and TCB size from one shared allocator walk (`taskHeapMeasureSnapshot()`, `System_TaskUtils.h`) — every task in the `uxTaskGetSystemState` snapshot gets a measured Stack/Used/Used%, kernel tasks included; `kAssumedUsedBytes`, `TCB_SIZE = 104` and `memreport`'s `appTasks[]` are gone, and an unmeasurable stack prints `?` and is excluded from the totals rather than invented. Still open: `[MEMSAMPLE]`'s 12-row `tasks[]` in `System_MemoryMonitor.cpp` is the remaining hand-maintained table, and `reportAllTaskStacks()` still picks its *warning* set from `knownTasks[]`.
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_TaskUtils.cpp:533`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_MemoryMonitor.cpp:274 (TaskEntry tasks[], 12 entries), components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:3690 (appTasks[], 10 entries), System_TaskUtils.cpp:646 (kAssumedUsedBytes), System_TaskUtils.cpp:673 (TOTALS row)
- **What:** Three reporting surfaces each enumerate a hard-coded task list: perftop's knownTasks[] (13), [MEMSAMPLE]'s tasks[] (12), memreport's appTasks[] (10). Their union is 14 of the 38 firmware tasks. Unlisted: g2-fsm (2560 B), g2_hb_worker (2048-3072 B), g2_page_swap_w (3584 B), g2_tap_disp (6400 B), g2_session_w, g2_ble_connect, g2_net_scan, g2_wifi_pending, g2_ring_pending, g2_ai_test, g2_img_probe, ring_spoof, ring_bridge_hb, sensor_bcast, mapRender, i2c0_begin, mic_record, mic_viz, plus the media/inference set. For unlisted tasks System_TaskUtils.cpp:646-650 computes allocBytes = HWM_free + 4096 and usedBytes = 4096.
- **Why it matters:** g2-fsm has a 2560-byte stack. If it reports 1200 B free, perftop prints 'Stack ~5 KB, Used ~4 KB, 78%' — a total larger than the task's real allocation and a 'used' figure that is a constant, not a measurement. Those invented numbers are then summed into the TOTALS row at :673, so the whole stack accounting is wrong. Meanwhile the three tasks with the least absolute headroom in the system have no percentage anywhere, so the operator has no signal before the panic.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: recon_prior.md:302 says the byte budget is known but 'HWM instrumentation coverage is not' — this closes that gap.
- **Direction:** Drive all three reporters from one registry keyed by TaskHandle_t that xTaskCreateLogged populates at create time with the size it just passed, instead of three hand-maintained tables. For genuinely unknown (IDF/Bluedroid) tasks, print only the measured free bytes and leave the alloc/used/percent columns blank rather than assuming 4 KB.

### [MEDIUM] Time-anchor registryPersist() renames an unverified temp over the live registry, so a failed write replaces good anchors with an empty file
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_TimeAnchors.cpp:110`
- **Also:** System_TimeAnchors.cpp:142 (append path, unchecked f.print)
- **What:** registryPersist() (System_TimeAnchors.cpp:99-113) opens .anchors.tmp, writes each anchor with `f.print(line)` (:110) discarding the return, closes, and then calls VFS::renameGuarded(tmpPath, kAnchorsPath, ...) at :113 — also discarding its bool, and with no check that the temp contains anything. writeAnchorLine() at :142 has the same unchecked f.print on the live append.
- **Why it matters:** The comment above the function says the temp+rename exists 'so a power cut mid-write can't truncate the live registry' — but the write-failure case walks straight through it. On a full filesystem the tmp ends up empty or short, the rename succeeds (metadata-only), and .anchors.csv is replaced by a truncated registry. The anchors map boot number → wall-clock epoch, which is what promotes boot-relative millis timestamps in the per-day sensor CSVs to real times; losing them makes every already-recorded row from those boots permanently unresolvable. registryPersist is called from registryPrune, i.e. on the ordinary housekeeping path, not only on rare events.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Sum the f.print returns, compare against the expected total, and skip the rename (deleting the temp) if they disagree; check renameGuarded's bool as well.

### [MEDIUM] `memreport`'s "TOTAL ACCOUNTED" adds up bytes ever allocated, not bytes currently held — the allocation tracker has no free-side hook
- **Status: RESOLVED 2026-08-23 (report side).** `TOTAL ACCOUNTED`, `total_known`, the `if (dram_used > total_known)` vanishing guard and `Static Over-Estimate` no longer exist. The tracker section is labelled cumulative and is never summed; the TOTALS block sums only measured task stacks + TCBs + allocator metadata and prints a signed `UNATTRIBUTED` residual that cannot disappear (`docs/MEMORY_REPORT_FIX_PLAN.md`). The tracker itself still has no free-side hook — that half is unchanged.
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:3590`
- **Also:** System_MemoryMonitor.cpp:332-344 consumes the same counters. Fragile-but-currently-latent adjacent detail: the second accumulation loop at System_Utils.cpp:3576 starts at index `displayed`, treating a display *count* as a *sorted-array position* — it double-counts if any tracker entry is ever `isActive == false` while `gAllocTrackerCount` covers it, which today only happens during `sampleMemoryState`'s temporary unmark window (:367-370).
- **What:** `total_known += tracked_dram;` where `tracked_dram` sums `gAllocTracker[].dramBytes` (:3552, :3580). Those counters are only ever incremented — `memAllocDebug()` (`HardwareOne.cpp:407-440`) does `gAllocTracker[idx].totalBytes += size; count++` on each allocation, and there is no counterpart on the free path: `ps_delete()` calls bare `free()` (`System_MemUtil.h:250`), there is no `ps_free`, and every G2/web caller frees with plain `free()`. `memAllocDebug` also returns early on `if (!ptr)` (:411), so *failed* allocations are never recorded either — and there is no `heap_caps_register_failed_alloc_callback` anywhere in `components/hardwareone/` or `main/`.
- **Why it matters:** Tags are reused per event, not per lifetime: `g2.pb.rebuild-list`, `g2.pb.create-text`, `g2.showTextPage.copy`, `g2.pageSwap.itemDup`, `task.pressure`, `memreport.tasks` are each allocated and freed on every page render / report. `totalBytes` for those tags therefore grows without bound while actual usage stays flat. `printMemoryReport()` prints that sum as `TOTAL ACCOUNTED` (:3945) against `ACTUAL DRAM USED`, and the diagnostic block that explains the gap is guarded by `if (dram_used > total_known)` (:3950) — so once cumulative allocations exceed live usage (quickly, with G2 in use) the "Unaccounted DRAM" / "Static Over-Estimate" lines vanish entirely and the report shows an inflated accounted figure with no caveat. `[MEMSAMPLE]`'s `AllocTracker: N entries, X KB total` line (`System_MemoryMonitor.cpp:342`) reads the same counters and has the same meaning drift. Tracking is opt-in (`memsample track on`), so this only bites the person actively hunting a leak.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Either give the tracker a free-side hook so the counters mean "currently held" (a `ps_free(ptr, tag)` used by the ps_alloc call sites), or relabel every consumer to say "cumulative allocated" and stop rolling it into `total_known`. Recording failed allocations (dropping the `!ptr` early-out, or registering `heap_caps_register_failed_alloc_callback`) would also close the fact that nothing in the firmware counts allocation failures at all.

### [MEDIUM] `factoryreset` discards `esp_timer_create()`'s return after `users.json` is already deleted — a failure leaves the device with no auth database, no reboot, and a "complete" message
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:2202`
- **What:** `factoryreset_confirmed` (System_Utils.cpp:2166-2220) deletes `users.json` via `VFS::removeGuarded` (checked, :2180) — an irreversible step — and then schedules the reboot with `esp_timer_create(&timerArgs, &timer);` at :2202 and `esp_timer_start_once(timer, 1000000);` at :2203, both `esp_err_t`, both discarded. `timer` is initialized to `nullptr` at :2194.
- **Why it matters:** If `esp_timer_create` fails (it heap-allocates the timer struct from internal DRAM; the device runs at 29–39 KB free internal with the LLM loaded), `timer` stays null. `esp_timer_start_once` NULL-checks and returns ESP_ERR_INVALID_ARG rather than crashing — so there is no panic, no log, and no reboot. Execution continues to post `SYSEVT_FACTORY_RESET`, write the "factory reset — users.json deleted; rebooting to setup wizard" event, and return the success string. The device is now in a state nothing else in the codebase anticipates: `users.json` is gone from disk but the in-RAM user table and the caller's authenticated session are both intact, and first-time setup never arms because the reboot that would trigger it never happens. Any subsequent path that persists the user table (a `lastSeen` update on the next login, any `userCRUD` write) re-creates `users.json` from the stale RAM copy, silently undoing the reset the operator was told had completed.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Check `esp_timer_create`; on failure fall back to the inline `ESP.restart()` path — after a successful `users.json` delete, rebooting immediately is strictly better than not rebooting at all, and the audit line has already been written by that point.

### [MEDIUM] command-audit.log rotation has no boot recovery, so a reset inside its remove-then-rename window destroys the always-on audit trail and strands a permanent orphan
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:939`
- **Also:** System_Filesystem.cpp:1900-1908 (remove-before-rename); the remove is also unnecessary on LittleFS — lfs_rename replaces an existing destination atomically, only FAT requires it
- **What:** `logCommandExecution` appends every non-quiet command to a hard-coded literal path `/system/sys_logs/command-audit.log` with a 500 KB cap via `appendLineWithCap`. That function's rotation does `VFS::remove(dest)` then `VFS::rename(tmp, dest)` (System_Filesystem.cpp:1900-1908) — a genuine window where the log does not exist. `cleanupLogOrphan` is called at boot for exactly six paths (System_Filesystem.cpp:94-99: LOG_OK/LOG_FAIL/LOG_I2C/LOG_ERROR/LOG_EVENTS/LOG_EVENT_STREAM); command-audit.log is not among them, and `/system/sys_logs` is not in the boot `.tmp` sweep directory list (:139-144, which covers only "/", "/system", "/system/users", "/system/users/user_settings", "/maps" and optionally "/system/llm").
- **Why it matters:** A power cut or watchdog reset between :1899 and :1905 leaves command-audit.log absent and command-audit.log.tmp holding the surviving 85% tail. No boot path promotes it (case (c) in cleanupLogOrphan is never run for this file), so the audit log restarts empty; the next rotation's VFS::remove(tmpPath) at :1872 then deletes the orphan outright, making the loss permanent. The remove-before-rename is also unnecessary here: the comment at :1896 claims LittleFS requires the destination to be absent, but lfs_rename replaces an existing destination atomically — only FAT does.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders
- **Direction:** Give command-audit.log a named constant next to the other LOG_*_FILE definitions, register it with cleanupLogOrphan at boot, and gate appendLineWithCap's `VFS::remove(dest)` on `getStorageType() == SDCARD` so the window does not exist on flash at all.

### [MEDIUM] `gBootEpochUsOffset` is latched once from an unsynced clock at boot and is never refreshed by `timeset` or an RTC sync, stranding admin-log timestamps on the `[ms=N]` fallback
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:749`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:756-764 (lazy-once refresh), :1969 (cmd_timeset), HardwareOne.cpp:1448 (the premature latch), i2csensor_ds3231.cpp:210/245 (settimeofday with no re-anchor).
- **What:** `timeSyncUpdateBootEpoch()` sets `gBootEpochUsOffset = (int64_t)now*1000000 - esp_timer_get_time()` gated on `now > 0`. `getTimestampPrefixMsCached()` (:756) calls it lazily only `if (gBootEpochUsOffset == 0)`. HardwareOne.cpp:1448 calls `logBootAnchorToLogs()` — which goes through `buildTimestampPrefix()` -> `getTimestampPrefixMsCached()` — at ~2 s of uptime, which is BEFORE `rtcEarlyBootSync()` at :1536 and long before NTP. At that moment `time()` returns uptime seconds, so the offset latches to a small non-zero value (the sub-second remainder, roughly -0..-1e6 us) instead of staying 0. Because it is non-zero, the lazy refresh never runs again. The only other refresh is inside `logTimeSyncedMarkerIfReady()` (System_Debug.cpp:2319), which is reached from exactly one call site (System_Utils.cpp:2402) behind a successful `getLocalTime()` — i.e. the NTP path only.
