- **What:** `out = f.readString();` is unchanged. `fs::File` does not override `readString`, so this resolves to `Stream::readString()` (`components/arduino/cores/esp32/Stream.cpp:229-237`), which is `String ret; int c = timedRead(); while (c >= 0) { ret += (char)c; c = timedRead(); }` — one virtual single-byte `read()` through the VFS/LittleFS layer per byte, and one `String::operator+=` per byte. `String::changeBuffer` (`WString.cpp:206`) grows by `newSize = (maxStrLen + 16) & ~0xf` via `realloc` — exact-fit, 16-byte granular, no doubling. With `CONFIG_SPIRAM_MALLOC_ALWAYSINTERNAL=16384` every such String under 16 KB is internal DRAM.
- **Why it matters:** This is the engine behind the fragmentation the whole prior audit was chasing: a 40 KB `automations.json` or `users.json` read costs ~40,000 single-byte VFS reads and ~2,500 reallocs that each grow the block by 16 bytes. `readText` is on the path of `isAdminUser` (per command, per guarded FS op, per directory entry), the automation scheduler tick, and settings load. I am reporting it only as a status correction, not as a new finding: it is HOT_PATH short-list #1 and its `Fix:` line is one of the eleven the paused FixVerify pass never returned on. Verifying it matters because the doc's own prescription is known-wrong — `docs/HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT.md` records that `out.reserve(f.size()+1)` was hand-proven *strictly worse* (it forces `String::move()` onto memmove instead of a buffer steal).
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: KNOWN — docs/HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT.md §"The short list" item 1 and §S1. Status confirmed UNCHANGED as of the current working tree; the doc's own `reserve()` prescription is recorded there as refuted.
- **Direction:** Direction only, since the obvious fix is the refuted one: the lever is bypassing `Stream::readString` entirely — size the file, allocate once, and use a bulk `readBytes` into it. Anything that keeps the per-byte `+=` loop, `reserve()` included, does not address it.


### BLE / G2 / R1 — 28 findings (9 critical/high)

### [CRITICAL] g2_img_probe fallback puts a FreeRTOS task stack in PSRAM, then the task does LittleFS reads — guaranteed Cache-disabled panic
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp:1109`
- **Also:** Contradicts the project rule as stated in this same tree at System_LLM.cpp:173-174 ("They must be INTERNAL: the scheduler and ISR path touch a task's stack and TCB, and task stacks in PSRAM have never been safe on this HW"), which is why System_LLM.cpp:266 correctly uses MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL. These are the only two xTaskCreateStatic sites in the tree.
- **What:** spawnImgProbeWorker() first tries xTaskCreatePinnedToCore(imgProbeWorkerInternalStack, "g2_img_probe", 8192, ...). If that fails (no contiguous 8 KB internal block), it allocates the stack with heap_caps_malloc(kStackBytes, MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM | MALLOC_CAP_8BIT) at :1108-1109 and starts the task with xTaskCreateStaticPinnedToCore(...) at :1114. The TCB is correctly internal; only the stack is external. This is the sole MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM stack allocation in the tree (grep for MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM near stack/tcb returns exactly this one site), and it directly contradicts System_LLM.cpp:173-177, which allocates its worker stack MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL with the note "the scheduler and ISR path touch a task's stack and TCB, and task stacks in PSRAM have never been safe on this HW."
- **Why it matters:** Every SPI-flash operation on this build disables the cache: SPI_FLASH_CACHE_NO_DISABLE = (CONFIG_SPI_FLASH_AUTO_SUSPEND || (SPIRAM_FETCH_INSTRUCTIONS && SPIRAM_RODATA) || APP_BUILD_TYPE_RAM) and all three are off (sdkconfig:2560, 1670, 1671, 402), so spi1_start() takes the cache_disable(NULL) branch (esp-idf/components/spi_flash/spi_flash_os_func_app.c:105-124) for READS as well as writes. That path calls spi_flash_disable_interrupts_caches_and_other_cpu(), whose first statement is assert(esp_task_stack_is_sane_cache_disabled()) (cache_utils.c:127); that helper returns esp_ptr_in_dram(sp) ONLY — it has no CONFIG_FREERTOS_TASK_CREATE_ALLOW_EXT_MEM branch (cache_utils.c:56-65). Assertions are compiled in (CONFIG_COMPILER_OPTIMIZATION_ASSERTIONS_ENABLE=y, ASSERTION_LEVEL=2, sdkconfig:722/727), so the result is abort() -> panic -> reboot. Concrete path: imgProbeWorkerImpl() -> fn() (its own comment at :981-983 says the probes make VFS::*Guarded() calls, e.g. Q25's frame_XX.bmp checks) or the post-probe rebuild buildImageAnimatedChooseRows() -> VFS::existsGuarded -> canRead -> checkPerm -> resolveRole (System_Filesystem.cpp:1549-1567) -> isSuperAdminUser/isAdminUser (System_User.cpp:278-303), which reads /system/users/users.json off LittleFS on every call. The identity captured is the paired user via currentAuthContext(), not "system", so the SOURCE_INTERNAL short-circuit at System_Filesystem.cpp:1550 does not apply. Even with assertions removed the CPU would fault on the next stack access with the cache off. Net: the fallback that exists to keep the feature working under memory pressure converts a soft failure into a hard reboot.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 3 finders; board: any board with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES (file guard at G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp:88) — includes the primary FeatherS3 build
- **Direction:** Treat "no internal stack available" as a spawn failure rather than a reason to relocate the stack — the function already has a clean all-paths-failed return at :1131-1135 and the caller can surface a lens message. If a fallback is wanted, retry with a smaller internal stack rather than an external one, and consider whether CONFIG_FREERTOS_TASK_CREATE_ALLOW_EXT_MEM should be off so a future regression fails at xTaskCreateStatic instead of at runtime.

### [CRITICAL] imgProbeWorker frees its own task stack, TCB, and context block while still executing on them, then calls vTaskDelete(NULL) through the freed TCB
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp:1052`
- **Also:** System_LLM.cpp:250-288 shows the correct handling of the same problem — its comment at :168-171 explicitly names the hazard ("a task cannot observe its own deletion, the IDLE task unlinks its TCB some unbounded time after it stops running") and the LLM worker therefore never frees its own stack/TCB.
- **What:** imgProbeWorker() is the entry function for a task created with xTaskCreateStaticPinnedToCore whose stack is ctx->stack (heap_caps_malloc MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM, :1108) and whose TCB is ctx->tcb (:1110). At :1052 it calls heap_caps_free(ctx->stack) — the very stack the function is running on — then at :1053-:1054 continues to execute two more heap_caps_free calls whose `ctx` operand lives in that freed frame, then calls vTaskDelete(nullptr) at :1055, which writes to the already-freed TCB and hands it to the idle task's termination list for a later read.
- **Why it matters:** Concretely: heap_caps_free(ctx->stack) returns the 8 KB block to TLSF, which writes free-list pointers into the block payload and may coalesce it with neighbours. Interrupts are enabled and the other core is running, so any concurrent allocation of <=8 KB can be handed this exact block while g2_img_probe is still pushing frames onto it -> silent stack/heap cross-corruption. heap_caps_free(ctx->tcb) then clobbers the first words of the StaticTask_t: TLSF stores next_free/prev_free at payload offsets 0 and 4, which overlays pxTopOfStack and the head of xStateListItem. vTaskDelete(nullptr) immediately afterwards does vListInsertEnd(&xTasksWaitingTermination, &pxTCB->xStateListItem) into that freed block, and the following context switch stores SP into pxCurrentTCB->pxTopOfStack, also in the freed block. Later the IDLE task runs prvCheckTasksWaitingTermination -> prvDeleteTCB, which reads pxTCB->ucStaticallyAllocated out of the freed (possibly reused) memory; if that byte no longer reads tskSTATICALLY_ALLOCATED_STACK_AND_TCB the code takes the vPortFree(pxTCB->pxStack) branch and double-frees the already-freed PSRAM stack. Trigger: run any image probe from the G2 Tests lens page at a moment when the internal 8 KB xTaskCreate at :1094 fails (the documented dual-G2 + WiFi heap-pressure condition), then let the probe finish.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 7 finders; board: all (ENABLE_BLUETOOTH=1 and ENABLE_G2_GLASSES=1 are unconditional #defines in System_BuildConfig.h:223/228, so this compiles on FeatherS3/esp32s3 and on the QT Py/Feather-V2 esp32 boards alike)
- **Direction:** A task can never release its own stack or TCB. Hand ownership of the ImgProbeTaskCtx to a reaper that runs after the task is gone - e.g. keep the ctx pointer in a file-static that the next spawnImgProbeWorker() frees before allocating a new one, or enable CONFIG_ENABLE_STATIC_TASK_CLEAN_UP_HOOK (currently off, sdkconfig:3228) and free from vApplicationStaticTaskCleanupHook. Simplest fix given finding #2 is to delete the static-task fallback entirely and let spawnImgProbeWorker return false when the internal xTaskCreate fails.

### [HIGH] deinitG2Client()/`closeg2 full` delete per-temple writeMutex and rxBuf, and vTaskDelete the BLE connect worker, without quiescing the core-1 G2 workers or the BTC notify callback that use them
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:8700`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:13720-13721 (`closeg2 full` → templeReset from cmd_exec); :8938-8951 (bleConnectShutdown unconditional vTaskDelete); :7554-7575 (sendEnvelopeEx TOCTOU on t.writeMutex); :2083 (handleNotify writing t.rxBuf from BTC_TASK); Bluetooth.cpp:895, :2029 (other entry points)
- **What:** `deinitG2Client()` runs `g2Disconnect(); stopHeartbeatTimer(); templeReset(gL); templeReset(gR); bleConnectShutdown(); g2SessionShutdown();` in that order. `templeReset()` (:8316-8331) does `free(t.rxBuf)`, `vSemaphoreDelete(t.writeMutex)` and `delete t.client`. `g2Disconnect()` (:9060) explicitly does NOT stop any worker — its own comment says the shared connect worker 'keeps running for future submissions'. The workers that use those objects are all pinned to APP_CORE (core 1): `g2_session_w` (:12563), `g2_tap_disp` (:11432), `g2_page_swap_w` (:11233), `g2_hb_worker` (:8154), `g2-fsm`. They reach `sendEnvelopeEx()` (:7554), which checks `if (!t.writeMutex) return Fail;` and then blocks in `xSemaphoreTake(t.writeMutex, pdMS_TO_TICKS(G2_SEND_ENVELOPE_MUTEX_MS))` — 1500 ms (:154). `deinitG2Client` runs on cmd_exec_task (PRO_CORE/core 0) via `g2deinit` (:15046), `blemode server` (Bluetooth.cpp:2029) and `initBluetooth` (Bluetooth.cpp:895). The ordering is also self-inconsistent: `g2SessionShutdown()` (:12579) does abort + `g2SessionWaitIdle(3000)` before deleting its task, but it is called AFTER templeReset has already destroyed the semaphore that worker may be sleeping on. `bleConnectShutdown()` (:8938) is worse — an unconditional `vTaskDelete(gBleConnectTaskH)` with no abort, no wait and no check of what that task is holding. `templeReset` also frees `t.rxBuf` in two statements (`free()` then `= nullptr`), and `handleNotify()` (:2083-2145) memcpys into `t.rxBuf` on BTC_TASK after only an `if (!t.rxBuf)` guard.
- **Why it matters:** With the glasses connected and a G2 lens page live (any Apps/viewer/Health screen keeps `g2_session_w` busy), run `g2deinit`, `closeg2 full`, or switch BLE to server mode. cmd_exec on core 0 calls `vSemaphoreDelete(t.writeMutex)` while the core-1 session worker is blocked inside that semaphore's queue structure for up to 1500 ms → the FreeRTOS queue control block and its task list are freed under the blocked task → heap corruption or an assert in the queue internals when the worker times out. The `bleConnectShutdown()` vTaskDelete has an independent failure: if `g2_ble_connect` is inside `sendEnvelopeEx` holding `bleCentralTx` or a temple writeMutex when it is deleted, that mutex is never given back and the shared BLE transmit gate is wedged for the rest of the boot — every subsequent G2 and R1 write silently fails with 'Central TX busy'. The `free(t.rxBuf)` / `t.rxBuf = nullptr` pair is preemptible by BTC_TASK (higher priority than cmd_exec's TASK_PRIORITY_LOW, same core 0), so an in-flight notify delivered in that window memcpys up to rxCap bytes into freed heap.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: any build with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES — FeatherS3 today
- **Direction:** Teardown needs to quiesce before it destroys: stop/park every worker that can enter sendEnvelopeEx and confirm the notify path is idle, and only then release the per-temple objects. `bleConnectShutdown`'s bare vTaskDelete should follow the abort-then-wait shape `g2SessionShutdown` already implements, and the whole sequence should run workers-first, objects-last.

### [HIGH] LC3 audio decode runs inline on the Bluedroid BLE notify task and consumes ~6 KB of BTC_TASK's 8192 B stack in one call chain
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:1880`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:1849 (micWavWritePacketLocked, second 1600 B pcm[]), :1931 (WAV writer call), :1948 (AFE-feed pcm[]), :1998-2005 (audioNotifyThunkL/R), :1908-1918 (inline gMicRecFile->write on the same stack)
- **What:** handleAudioNotify() is the registered BLE notify callback for the G2 audio characteristic (registered at :2021 via audioNotifyThunkL/R at :1998/:2002), so it executes on the Bluedroid BTC_TASK stack. It declares int16_t pcm[800] twice — once in the inlined micWavWritePacketLocked (:1849) and once in the AFE-feed block (:1948) — and calls lc3_decode() from both. Measured directly from the shipped ELF (build/hardwareone-idf.elf, HEAD c76f528): handleAudioNotify's prologue is `entry a1, 0x6c0` = 1728 B; lc3_decode = 288 B; lc3_decode call8's lc3_mdct_inverse whose prologue is `entry a1, 0xf40` = 3904 B. Adding the Arduino-BLE dispatch above it (BLEDevice::gattClientEventHandler 80 + BLEClient::gattClientEventHandler 176 + BLERemoteCharacteristic::gattClientEventHandler 80 + std::function invoke 32 = 368 B) and Bluedroid's btc_thread_handler 32 + btc_gattc_cb_handler 80, the chain reaches roughly 6.4 KB of the 8192 B CONFIG_BT_BTC_TASK_STACK_SIZE. The same callback also does a blocking LittleFS write (gMicWavFile->write, :1863) on that stack. grep of the disassembly confirms handleAudioNotify is the ONLY function in the image that reaches lc3_decode — nothing defers it.
- **Why it matters:** With the G2 mic streaming (micrecord / WAV capture / ESP-SR AFE feed), every 205 B BLE audio packet arrives at ~50 Hz and drives this chain. lc3_mdct_inverse alone is a single 3904 B frame — 48% of the whole BTC_TASK stack. Because sdkconfig sets CONFIG_FREERTOS_CHECK_STACKOVERFLOW_CANARY (16-byte canary, checked only at context switch) and leaves CONFIG_FREERTOS_WATCHPOINT_END_OF_STACK unset, a frame that large jumps clean over the canary window and corrupts whatever heap block sits below BTC_TASK's stack, with no panic and no diagnostic. The margin is ~1.7 KB and shrinks further with any extra Bluedroid depth (encrypted link, second GATT client, congestion callbacks). This also directly contradicts the project's own BTC deferral discipline: three separate comments in this file (:2718-2722, :2994-2999, :11486-11500) state BTC_TASK is "~3-4 KB", and route far lighter UI work through tapDispatcherEnqueue* for exactly this reason. The audio path was added later and never got the same treatment.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: any build with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES — i.e. FeatherS3 today (ENABLE_MICROPHONE is derived on via G2 BLE audio)
- **Direction:** Apply the same deferral the tap/page paths already use: have handleAudioNotify copy the 205 B packet into a queue and let a dedicated worker task (sized for lc3_mdct_inverse's 3904 B frame plus the FS write) do the decode and the file append. If the decode must stay inline, at minimum verify the real BTC_TASK high-water mark while streaming — nothing in the firmware currently measures it.

### [HIGH] Half-connected G2 recovery runs the full BLE connect + GATT-discovery chain on the 2048–3072 B heartbeat worker, while the project's own connect worker is 5120 B and marked "don't go lower"
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:8063`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:8137-8154 (runtime stack sizing), :7921 (attemptMissingArmRecovery), :8344 (connectTemple)
- **What:** heartbeatWorkerTask (:8073) calls recoveryHeartbeatTick() every 5 s, which at :8063 calls attemptMissingArmRecovery() (:7921). That function runs a 3-second BLEScan, then calls connectTemple() (:8344) — BLEDevice::createClient, BLEClient::connect, setMTU, getService, getCharacteristic x2, registerForNotify, plus getServices()/getCharacteristics() map walks on the failure branches, two BROADCAST_PRINTF calls (256 B stack buffer each) and a dozen DEBUG_G2F calls (debugQueuePrintf = 352 B frame -> Print::vprintf 128 B). Measured frames from the ELF: attemptMissingArmRecovery 400 B, connectTemple 256 B, BLEScan::handleGAPEvent 560 B, BLEAdvertisedDevice::parseAdvertisement 400 B, BLERemoteService::retrieveCharacteristics 224 B, BLERemoteCharacteristic::retrieveDescriptors 192 B, BLEUUID::toString 192 B. The heartbeat worker's stack is chosen at runtime at :8137-8154: 3072 B normally, 2560 B when internal DRAM < 9000, and 2048 B when internal DRAM < 7000.
- **Why it matters:** The identical connectTemple path is given a dedicated 5120 B worker (bleConnectInit, :8912-8921) whose own comment says "Don't go lower — connect-time service discovery has genuinely unbounded depth on certain failure paths." The heartbeat worker runs it on 40–60% of that. Worse, the 2048 B variant is selected precisely when internal DRAM is exhausted by dual BLE clients — which is also when a temple is most likely to have dropped and recovery is most likely to fire. Summing the measured frames on the recovery chain lands at roughly 2.2–2.4 KB before any Bluedroid semaphore-wait depth or an interrupt frame, so the 2048 B configuration cannot complete it and the 3072 B one has a few hundred bytes of margin. Failure mode is a stack overflow inside the BLE stack — with canary-only detection it may corrupt the neighbouring heap block rather than panic cleanly. Note the manual path is safe: cmd_g2recover (:15228) calls the same function from cmd_exec_task's 8192 B stack, so this only bites the automatic retry.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: any build with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES — FeatherS3 today
- **Direction:** Route the recovery attempt through the existing 5120 B g2_ble_connect worker (g2SubmitBleConnect / BleConnectJob) instead of running it on the heartbeat stack — the queue and worker already exist and are the documented home for this call chain. The heartbeat tick should only decide *whether* to recover and submit the job.

### [HIGH] G2 mic capture writes the filesystem synchronously on the Bluedroid BLE-notify task, and the SD-availability gate does not constrain the destination
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:1909`
- **Also:** G2_Glasses.cpp:1861 (WAV write), :14570-14577 and :14692-14706 (path resolution in both commands)
- **What:** `handleAudioNotify` (:1880) is the LC3 audio notify callback, reached via `audioNotifyThunkL/R` (:1998-2005) registered with `registerForNotify`. The file itself repeatedly identifies this context as "the BLE notify task" / BTC_TASK (:1905, :2073-2079 latches `gBleNotifyTaskHandle = xTaskGetCurrentTaskHandle()`, :9668-9688 builds a deadlock guard around it). Inside that callback: `gMicRecFile->write(data, len)` (:1911) and `micWavWritePacketLocked` → `gMicWavFile->write(pcm, 1600)` (:1861). Neither takes `gFsMutex`, but both go through the VFS to `esp_littlefs`, whose per-mount recursive mutex is taken with `portMAX_DELAY` (`managed_components/joltwallet__littlefs/src/esp_littlefs.c:1194`). `cmd_g2micrec`/`cmd_g2micwav` gate on `VFS::isSDAvailable()` (:14557, :14657) and then only prefix `/sd/` when the argument does NOT start with `/` (:14574-14576, :14702-14704) — an absolute path such as `g2micwav start "/logging_captures/a.wav"` passes the SD gate and routes to LittleFS via `fsForPath`.
- **Why it matters:** Frames arrive at ~50 packets/s (the code's own figure). On the WAV path that is 1600 B every ~20 ms = 32 KB/s, i.e. a LittleFS block erase roughly every 125 ms. Each erase is the finding-1 window: BTC_TASK — the single task servicing both G2 temples AND the R1 ring notify callbacks — is frozen for ~50 ms while GATT events queue behind it. The raw path (205 B/pkt) is 10 KB/s, an erase every ~0.4 s. Additionally, because the write goes through the shared littlefs mutex, a concurrent log rotation (finding 3) blocks BTC for the rotation's full multi-second duration. `kMicWavMaxBytes` is 16 MB (:1545) against a 10604 KB LittleFS partition, so a LittleFS-destined WAV also fills the partition before its own cap fires, and littlefs GC cost climbs as it does. The in-file comment at :14673-14675 states the assumption that was violated: "this path additionally writes each packet to SD".
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: any board with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES — includes the primary FeatherS3 (System_BuildConfig.h:223,228)
- **Direction:** Reject any destination that does not resolve to the SD tier in both `g2micrec start` and `g2micwav start` — the SD gate already exists, it just is not applied to the resolved path. Longer term the per-packet write belongs on a worker fed by a ring, not on the notify callback.

### [HIGH] CONFIG_SPIRAM_ALLOW_STACK_EXTERNAL_MEMORY is pinned in shared defaults and a live code path puts an 8 KB task stack in PSRAM, then does LittleFS I/O on it
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp:1108`
- **Also:** sdkconfig.defaults:231 (pinned); sdkconfig:2028 / :3229 (live value + deprecated alias); components/hardwareone/System_LLM.cpp:266-277 (correctly uses MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL for its static stack — the right pattern)
- **What:** sdkconfig.defaults:231 pins `CONFIG_SPIRAM_ALLOW_STACK_EXTERNAL_MEMORY=y` (IDF 5.5 renames it to `CONFIG_FREERTOS_TASK_CREATE_ALLOW_EXT_MEM`, live at sdkconfig:2028). `spawnImgProbeWorker()` uses it: when the internal-heap `xTaskCreatePinnedToCore` fails, it allocates the 8 KB stack with `MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM | MALLOC_CAP_8BIT` (:1108-1109) and hands it to `xTaskCreateStaticPinnedToCore` (:1114). The worker then runs `imgProbeWorkerImpl` -> the probe fn, which touches the filesystem: `VFS::existsGuarded(...)` and `g2ReadBmp4bppFromVfs(...)` in g2ProbeImageQ25SdFrameAnimation (G2_Glasses.cpp:21798+), plus DEBUG_G2F/logging paths.
- **Why it matters:** IDF's own Kconfig help for this option (components/freertos/Kconfig:587-598) says it "should only be used for tasks where the stack is never accessed while the cache is disabled." A LittleFS access goes esp_partition_read/write/erase (managed_components/joltwallet__littlefs/src/littlefs_esp_part.c:43,60,76) -> esp_flash_* -> spi_flash_disable_interrupts_caches_and_other_cpu on the calling core. With the calling task's own stack in PSRAM, the flash routine's own locals become unreachable the moment the cache is turned off. This also contradicts the standing project rule that task stacks never go in PSRAM on this hardware. Second failure mode: the Kconfig default is `n` for IDF_TARGET_ESP32, so on the ESP32-classic boards, if the pinned line is ever dropped or a fullclean regenerates without it, `xTaskCreateStaticPinnedToCore` hits `configASSERT(portVALID_STACK_MEM(puxStackBuffer))` (esp-idf/components/freertos/esp_additions/freertos_tasks_c_additions.h:299) and aborts — assertions are on (CONFIG_COMPILER_OPTIMIZATION_ASSERTIONS_ENABLE=y, sdkconfig:722) — rather than returning the nullptr the code (and the comment at System_LLM.cpp:274, "xTaskCreateStatic* only returns null on invalid params") expects.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards where G2 is compiled (feathers3, xiao_s3, qtpy_esp32 has BT off so the page is excluded); the configASSERT-abort variant is ESP32-classic only
- **Direction:** Drop the PSRAM-stack fallback and fail the probe cleanly when internal heap is short, or keep the stack internal and shrink the frame. If the fallback is kept, it must not call any code that touches flash/NVS/LittleFS. Independently, the `xTaskCreateStatic* only returns null on invalid params` comment should be corrected — an out-of-range stack pointer aborts.

### [HIGH] g2_img_probe's PSRAM-stack fallback calls heap_caps_free on the stack it is currently executing on, then keeps running on it
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp:1049`
- **Also:** spawn site G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp:1114 (xTaskCreateStaticPinnedToCore); enabled by sdkconfig:3229 CONFIG_SPIRAM_ALLOW_STACK_EXTERNAL_MEMORY=y and sdkconfig:2028 CONFIG_FREERTOS_TASK_CREATE_ALLOW_EXT_MEM=y
- **What:** imgProbeWorker() does `imgProbeWorkerImpl(ctx->fn); heap_caps_free(ctx->stack); heap_caps_free(ctx->tcb); heap_caps_free(ctx); vTaskDelete(nullptr);`. ctx->stack is the 8 KB SPIRAM buffer the task is running on and ctx->tcb is its live StaticTask_t. The first free returns through memory that has already gone back to the allocator; the next two frees, the call into vTaskDelete, and the scheduler's own post-delete handling all touch freed blocks.
- **Why it matters:** This path fires whenever the internal 8 KB xTaskCreate at :1094 fails — the documented dual-BLE-plus-WiFi DRAM-pressure state. Any concurrent allocation on another core can hand out that PSRAM block between the free and the vTaskDelete, at which point the worker's remaining frames and the scheduler's TCB access corrupt live data. Freeing the TCB before vTaskDelete also means the kernel dereferences released memory to unlink the task.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: any build with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES — FeatherS3 today; prior art: docs/STACK_TO_PSRAM_CANDIDATES.md:208-210 notes this path places a task stack in PSRAM and calls it 'not a precedent to copy'; it does not mention the use-after-free.
- **Direction:** A task cannot release its own stack or TCB. Hand the ctx pointer to a deferred reaper (an idle hook, a one-shot esp_timer, or the existing page-swap worker) that frees stack/TCB after the task is gone, and have imgProbeWorker only call vTaskDelete(nullptr).

### [HIGH] 8 KB task stack allocated in PSRAM and handed to xTaskCreateStaticPinnedToCore, for a worker that performs LittleFS reads and Bluedroid BLE writes
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp:1109`
- **Also:** None - this is the only PSRAM task stack in the tree.
- **What:** ctx->stack = heap_caps_malloc(8192, MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM | MALLOC_CAP_8BIT) is passed as the stack buffer to xTaskCreateStaticPinnedToCore at :1114. CONFIG_FREERTOS_TASK_CREATE_ALLOW_EXT_MEM=y (sdkconfig:2028, reached via the deprecated alias CONFIG_SPIRAM_ALLOW_STACK_EXTERNAL_MEMORY=y in sdkconfig.defaults:231 and the rename map at esp-idf/components/freertos/sdkconfig.rename:8), so xPortcheckValidStackMem() accepts the external pointer and the task really runs. The task body then does exactly the two things IDF forbids for an external-memory stack.
- **Why it matters:** CONFIG_SPI_FLASH_AUTO_SUSPEND is not set (sdkconfig:2560), so every LittleFS read runs spi_flash_disable_interrupts_caches_and_other_cpu - the cache goes down while esp_flash_read keeps executing on the calling task's own stack. g2ProbeImageQ25SdFrameAnimation (G2_Glasses.cpp:21818-21822) calls VFS::existsGuarded up to 64 times and then g2ReadBmp4bppFromVfs (G2_Glasses.cpp:17271-17297) which does existsGuarded + openGuarded + read; on a LittleFS path each of those is a flash op. The moment the cache drops, the task's stack is unreadable -> Cache disabled but cached memory region accessed panic. esp_flash_write reinforces this: its bounce buffer is uint32_t temp_buf[8] on the caller's stack (esp_flash_api.c:1087-1094), used as write_buf while the cache is off. Independently, imgProbeWorkerImpl (:979-1007) takes a G2FastLinkGuard and drives BLE TX through Bluedroid for the whole probe, and IDF's Kconfig help for this option says the stack may only be used by a task that 'does not call on ROM code in any way (no direct calls, but also no Bluetooth/WiFi)'. Trigger: any image probe run while the internal 8 KB xTaskCreate at :1094 fails, which the code's own comment says happens under dual G2 + WiFi heap pressure and which System_LLM.cpp:255-258 independently corroborates ('the largest free internal block is only ~9 KB').
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (unconditional G2/BLE build). Worse on the ESP32-classic boards: sdkconfig.defaults.esp32:47 sets CONFIG_SPIRAM_CACHE_WORKAROUND=n and :38 sets CONFIG_FREERTOS_PLACE_FUNCTIONS_INTO_FLASH=y, and IDF's own Kconfig help singles ESP32 out as the target where PSRAM stacks cannot be made safe.; prior art: Partially - docs/STACK_TO_PSRAM_CANDIDATES.md:200-212 names this exact site but only to say it is 'not a precedent to copy... a degradation path, not a chosen design'. The prior doc never connects it 
- **Direction:** Delete the static/PSRAM fallback and let spawnImgProbeWorker return false when the internal xTaskCreate fails (the callers at :1582, :2232, :2409, :2437, :2466, :2503, :2536, :2569, :2594, :2622 already all handle a false return). If a fallback is wanted, retry with a smaller internal stack rather than an external one. Removing this branch also removes finding #1.

### [MEDIUM] allocSeq() does a non-atomic ++ on a shared uint8_t called from five tasks across both cores; a lost increment mints duplicate G2 envelope reassembly ids
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:1148`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:1158 (allocMagic, identical pattern), :9537 (g2AllocSeq public wrapper); ~80 allocSeq call sites spanning :1788 through :22720
- **What:** `gNextSeq` is a plain `static uint8_t` (:534). `allocSeq()` (:1148) is `uint8_t s = gNextSeq++;` — a load/add/store with no atomic, no volatile, no lock. There are ~80 call sites and they are spread across every G2 task: `g2_hb_worker` (:14250 DevCfgHeartbeat), `g2_session_w`/`g2_page_swap_w`/`g2_tap_disp` (the CreateList/CreateText/UpdateText/Shutdown builders at :9842, :10109, :12033, :16300, :17798, :19280 and many more), `g2_net_scan`, and cmd_exec_task via the `g2*` CLI commands. All the G2 workers are APP_CORE; cmd_exec_task is PRO_CORE. Critically, `allocSeq()` is evaluated as an argument to the envelope builder BEFORE the send path takes `bleCentralTx` and the temple `writeMutex`, so the existing TX serialization does not cover it. `g2AllocSeq()` (:9537) re-exports it to other translation units.
- **Why it matters:** With the heartbeat worker running at its normal cadence on core 1 and a user typing a `g2*` command on core 0, both call `allocSeq()`. Core 0 loads gNextSeq=N, core 1 loads N, both store N+1 — two different envelopes go out carrying seq=N. The protocol comment at :7593 states the contract explicitly: 'All fragments share the same seq byte — firmware reassembles by seq, not arrival order. Distinct envelopes for distinct messages must use distinct seqs.' The glasses firmware therefore merges fragments belonging to two unrelated messages into one reassembly buffer, producing a CRC failure at best and a mis-parsed page command at worst — which presents as an intermittent 'the lens ignored that tap / drew the wrong page' with no local error. The same pattern applies to `gNextMagic`/`allocMagic` (:537, :1158).
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: any build with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES — FeatherS3 today
- **Direction:** A counter shared by five tasks on two cores needs to be atomic — the tap dispatcher in this same file already uses `__atomic_add_fetch` for its drop counter, so the house pattern exists. Alternatively hoist the allocation inside the existing bleCentralTx/writeMutex window, but that changes the builder call shape at ~80 sites.

### [MEDIUM] g2micwav/g2micrec write to the filesystem inline on the Bluedroid host task at audio rate, and an absolute non-/sd path silently targets internal flash
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:1925`
- **Also:** Raw .lc3 recorder has the same shape: G2_Glasses.cpp:1907-1919 (write) and :14570-14577 (path handling). Routing decision: System_VFS.cpp:378-380 fsForPath / getStorageType — anything not under /sd is LittleFS.
- **What:** handleAudioNotify() runs on BTC_TASK (the Bluedroid callback task, core 0) and, per notify packet, calls micWavWritePacketLocked() which does gMicWavFile->write(pcm, 1600) inline (:1862). Packet cadence is 5 LC3 frames x 10 ms = one packet per 50 ms, i.e. 20/s and 32 KB/s of file writes on the BLE host task. cmd_g2micwav gates on VFS::isSDAvailable() at :14657 and defaults to /sd/g2_mic-<ts>.wav, but a user-supplied path that already starts with '/' is used verbatim (:14697-14705) — only a bare relative name gets the /sd/ prefix. So `g2micwav start "/rec.wav"` passes the SD-availability gate and then writes to LittleFS.
- **Why it matters:** On the LittleFS destination, 32 KB/s means roughly eight fresh 4 KB blocks per second, so ~8 sector erases plus ~64 page-program bursts per second — each one a full dual-core cache-off stop initiated from inside the BLE host callback. At ~25-50 ms per sector erase that is a 20-40% duty cycle of both cores parked, sustained for the length of the recording. The link itself survives (the BT controller ISR is vendor IRAM code and keeps answering connection events), but Bluedroid's own BTC/BTU queues, the R1 ring notify path, the tap dispatcher and the ESP-NOW/WiFi tasks all share core 0 and are unschedulable through every window; the mic path's own non-blocking xSemaphoreTake(…, 0) guards mean dropped audio packets rather than blocking, so the recording degrades itself. Even on the intended /sd destination this is still a synchronous SPI SD write per notify packet on the BLE host task, but that one does not disable the flash cache. The file header comment says "dump raw mic packets to SD" and the code checks SD availability, so a flash destination is clearly unintended rather than a deliberate option.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards with ENABLE_G2_GLASSES
- **Direction:** Force the destination onto /sd (reject or rewrite any non-/sd path, matching the SD-availability gate that already exists), and consider handing the write to a worker via the existing tapDispatcher/deferred-job pattern so no filesystem call happens on BTC_TASK.

### [MEDIUM] Five gSettings fields are mutated through setSetting() but have no SettingEntry, so each change rewrites the whole settings.json and persists nothing — the values silently revert to constructor defaults every boot
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:15112`
- **Also:** G2_Page_CameraSettings.cpp:712-713 assigns the fields directly and then submits `g2streamres WxH` specifically to get the (non-existent) persistence.
- **What:** cmd_g2streamres calls setSetting(gSettings.g2StreamWidth, wEff) and setSetting(gSettings.g2StreamHeight, h) (:15112-15113). cmd_sr_tuning_gain/agc/vad call setSetting on gSettings.srAfeGain/srAgcMode/srVadMode (System_ESPSR.cpp:3647, :3669, :3692). I scanned every `&gSettings.<field>` reference in the tree (284 explicit SettingEntry rows plus the 118 generated debug-flag rows and 41 sub rows) against the 454 fields of struct Settings: these five fields appear in no registry row and in no bespoke writeJson/readJson (unlike meshes[]/bondsByMesh/BLE peers, which have their own serialisers). writeRegisteredSettings therefore never emits them and readRegisteredSettings never reads them.
- **Why it matters:** setSetting() only checks `field != value`, assigns, and calls writeSettingsJson(). The write succeeds and returns true, the command replies 'G2 stream resolution set to 160x120' / 'AFE gain set to 3.0. Restart SR to apply.', and a full ~5 KB settings.json read-merge-serialize-rename cycle runs — but the value is not in the document. After a reboot g2StreamWidth/Height are back to the ctor values 96/96 (System_Settings.h:339-340) and srAfeGain/srAgcMode/srVadMode to 1.0/2/3 (:383-385). The code asserts the opposite in three places: G2_Glasses.cpp:15082 'Stream dims live in gSettings.g2StreamWidth/Height (persisted across reboots)', :19128 'Source of truth is gSettings.g2StreamWidth/Height (persisted...)', and G2_Page_CameraSettings.cpp:711 'cmd_g2streamres persists asynchronously via setSetting'.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: g2StreamWidth/Height: all builds (ENABLE_BLUETOOTH=1 and ENABLE_G2_GLASSES=1 unconditionally, System_BuildConfig.h:223/228). srAfeGain/srAgcMode/srVadMode: ESP-SR builds only (ENABLE_ESP_SR is 0 by default, System_BuildConfig.h:293).
- **Direction:** Either add SettingEntry rows for the five fields to the appropriate modules (g2 → bluetooth/g2 section, sr → espsr section) so the registry round-trips them, or drop the setSetting() calls to plain assignments and correct the three comments. Adding a debug-only assertion in setSetting that the field resolves to a registered entry would catch the next instance.

### [MEDIUM] g2_hb_worker shrinks its stack to 2048 B exactly when DRAM is scarce, and at that size the heartbeat's own debug printf reaches ~89% of it
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:8138`
- **Also:** beatOne() DEBUG_G2F sites in G2_Glasses.cpp (three within the first 40 lines of beatOne); the task is absent from all three HWM report tables (System_TaskUtils.cpp:533, System_MemoryMonitor.cpp:274, System_Utils.cpp:3690)
- **What:** startHeartbeatTimer picks 3072 / 2560 / 2048 bytes from heap_caps_get_free_size(MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL|MALLOC_CAP_8BIT) — the only runtime-chosen stack size in the tree. Frames from the ELF: heartbeatWorkerTask 32, beatOne 432, debugQueuePrintf 352, vsnprintf 64, _vsnprintf_r 144, _svfprintf_r 800. With the G2 debug flag on, one heartbeat costs 1824 B — 89% of the 2048-byte floor — before any interrupt frame. sendPbFragmented (384 B) plus the BLE writeValue chain runs on the same stack from beatOne.
- **Why it matters:** The two preconditions compound rather than cancel: the 2048 B tier is selected only when internal DRAM is under 7000 B, which is the same dual-BLE-plus-WiFi state in which the operator is most likely to turn on `debug g2` to investigate. DEBUGF_QUEUE gates on the flag before entering debugQueuePrintf, so with the flag off the deep frames are not taken — flipping the flag is what converts a working heartbeat into an overflow. Nothing reports this task's HWM, so there is no warning first.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: any build with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES — FeatherS3 today
- **Direction:** Either hold the heartbeat worker at a single fixed size and let the create fail loudly when DRAM is short (it already has a refuse-to-start path at :8147), or keep the tiers but suppress the variadic debug path on the small tiers. Adding g2_hb_worker to the handle-keyed HWM registry would at least make the margin visible.

### [MEDIUM] Lazy singleton worker init (page-swap, hijack FSM, BLE connect, session) is unsynchronised and reachable from four different task contexts on both cores
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:11220`
- **Also:** Same shape at G2_HijackFsm.cpp:315 (hijackFsmInit, called from hijackFsmDispatch :346/:356 which is reached from the page-swap worker, BTC notify and cmd_exec), G2_Glasses.cpp:8905 (bleConnectInit via g2SubmitBleConnect, called from both CLI/cmd_exec and G2_Ring.cpp:1283/:1303/:1332), and G2_Glasses.cpp:12556 (g2SessionEnsureWorker). tapDispatcherInit (G2_Glasses.cpp:11416) is NOT exposed — its only callers are on the single Bluedroid notify task.
- **What:** pageSwapInit() is `if (gPageSwapQueue) return; gPageSwapQueue = xQueueCreate(...); xTaskCreatePinnedToCore(pageSwapWorkerLoop, "g2_page_swap_w", ...)` with no mutex, no atomic and no critical section between the test and the assignment. It is called from pageSwapEnqueue (:11265) and g2EnqueueLensJob (:11299). I traced g2EnqueueLensJob's callers to four distinct task contexts: espnow_task (core 0, prio 5) via g2ESPNowAppOnRxText <- processMeshHeartbeats (System_ESPNow.cpp:9374, G2_Page_ESPNow.cpp:1299); the esp_timer task (core 0, prio 22) via notifyClearTimerCb (G2_Glasses.cpp:13463-13489, dispatch_method = ESP_TIMER_TASK); cmd_exec_task (core 0, prio 1) via the hijack cmd-completion callbacks (G2_Page_Network.cpp:928, G2_Page_LED.cpp:62, G2_Page_Automations.cpp:88, G2_Glasses.cpp:5856); and g2_tap_disp (core 1, prio 2) via pageSwapEnqueue.
- **Why it matters:** Two ways in. Same-core preemption is the likely one: cmd_exec_task (prio 1) enters pageSwapInit, passes the null test, and is preempted inside xQueueCreate's allocation by the esp_timer task (prio 22) firing notifyClearTimerCb — which sees gPageSwapQueue still null and builds a second queue plus a second g2_page_swap_w task (3584 B each). True SMP overlap between the core-0 callers and g2_tap_disp on core 1 is the other. Either way one QueueHandle_t is overwritten and leaked along with anything already enqueued on it, and two worker tasks now exist. Both worker loops re-read the *global* gPageSwapQueue on every iteration (:11118), so both end up draining the surviving queue — which destroys the serialization guarantee the whole applier exists to provide: two concurrent page swaps issuing interleaved BLE frames to the temples. The window is once-per-boot and narrow, which is consistent with nobody having seen it.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: any ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES build (FeatherS3 today)
- **Direction:** Wrap each init body in a portMUX critical section (or a one-shot flag CAS) around the test-and-assign, or create all four workers eagerly at G2 client init since they are persistent-until-reboot anyway. Having the worker loops capture the queue handle in a local at task entry instead of re-reading the global each iteration would also stop the two-workers-one-queue outcome.

### [MEDIUM] Forced vTaskDelete of workers that hold portMAX_DELAY mutexes can strand gFsMutex and hang every filesystem operation on the device
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:12588`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow.cpp:9418 stopEspNowTask() force-deletes espnow_task with no quiesce at all — that task runs processMeshHeartbeats, which takes EspNowTxGuard and MeshRetryGuard. System_ESPSR.cpp:1367 force-deletes the SR snippet writer, which does inline LittleFS writes under gFsMutex. G2_Glasses.cpp:8940 force-deletes the BLE connect worker (this one is at least acknowledged in the comment at :8935). By contrast System_ESPNow_Sensors.cpp:815 does this correctly, via the claimBroadcasterForDelete() atomic-claim protocol.
- **What:** g2SessionShutdown() calls g2SessionRequestAbort() then g2SessionWaitIdle(3000), which *breaks out of its wait on timeout* (:12527-12532, logging "wait-idle timed out") and falls through to `vTaskDelete(gSessionTaskH)` unconditionally at :12588. So a session job that has not cooperated within 3 s is killed at whatever instruction it happens to be executing. FreeRTOS releases nothing on vTaskDelete: any mutex the victim holds stays held by a TCB that no longer exists.
- **Why it matters:** Session jobs do filesystem work (image/BMP loads, live-text paging), i.e. they run under FsLockGuard. FsLockGuard/fsLock take gFsMutex with portMAX_DELAY and no timeout (System_Mutex.cpp:91, :106) — as do MapCacheGuard (:129), JsonBufferGuard (:225), MeshRetryGuard (:246), I2sMicLockGuard (:166) and EspNowTxGuard (:313). Killing the holder means every later FsLockGuard on any task blocks forever, which takes down settings saves, logging, the web file endpoints, sensor logging and the OLED file browser with no error and no watchdog reset (CONFIG_ESP_TASK_WDT_PANIC is not set, and no firmware task is TWDT-subscribed). The 3 s timeout is generous for the common case, which is why this has not been seen — an image probe or a live-text page against a slow flash read is what would exceed it.
- **Confidence:** PLAUSIBLE — board: any ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES build for the session worker; ENABLE_ESPNOW for stopEspNowTask; ENABLE_ESP_SR (0 on FeatherS3) for the SR snippet writer
- **Direction:** Prefer a cooperative stop-flag + join for all four (the sensor broadcaster's claim protocol at System_ESPNow_Sensors.cpp:72-80 is the in-tree template). Where a forced delete must remain as a last resort, at minimum re-check the idle flag after the wait and skip the delete if the worker is still busy, rather than killing it — leaking a parked task is far cheaper than a stranded global FS mutex.

### [MEDIUM] G2_Health's eight history rings hold 9,376 B of internal DRAM statics — 21% of all application `.dram0` in the linked image, and no prior audit has seen them
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Health.cpp:51`
- **Also:** `gAnchors` (512 B, System_TimeAnchors.cpp, v0.99.4) is unaudited for the same reason. `gRingTxQ` (2,224 B, G2_Ring.cpp) is partially covered — LAZY §27 names only "G2_Ring cmd static bufs, 600 B", not this symbol.
- **What:** `static HealthSeries sHr, sHrv, sSpo2, sTemp, sBat;` plus `static HealthSeries sTrendHr, sTrendHrv, sTrendSpo2;` — eight instances of a struct containing `HealthSample buf[HEALTH_HIST_CAP]` with `HEALTH_HIST_CAP = 96` (`G2_Health.h:28`). Verified against the checked-in link map (`build/hardwareone-idf.map`, 2026-07-27): eight `.bss` symbols at 1,172 B each = 9,376 B in `.dram0.bss`. Total application `.dram0` (data+bss from `libhardwareone.a`) in that image is 45,472 B across 1,394 symbols, so this one file is 20.6% of it — second only to `gEventRing` (7,872 B), and larger than any single symbol. The same file already puts its 41 KB pixel grid in PSRAM: `EXT_RAM_BSS_ATTR static uint8_t sGrid[HEALTH_TILE_H][HEALTH_TILE_W];` (:79). The rings are written only from task context (`syncFromTelemetry` at :259, `g2HealthNoteSample` from the BLE notify path) — never from an ISR or a critical section — so nothing pins them internal.
- **Why it matters:** P6 is exactly this: internal DRAM disappears into statics that nobody attributes, and the failure shows up somewhere else. `System_MemoryMonitor.cpp:159` sets `HEAP_WARNING_THRESHOLD = 40960`; 9,376 B is ~23% of that entire budget, held from boot whether or not an R1 ring is ever paired. `docs/LAZY_ALLOCATION_AUDIT.md` claims its coverage accounting "proves zero unaudited application DRAM symbols ≥512 B" — that claim is now stale: `G2_Health.cpp` was added by commit f5aea65 (v0.99.3, 2026-07-26), after the audit's 2026-07-16 pass and its 2026-07-25 extension, and `System_TimeAnchors.cpp`'s `gAnchors` (512 B) arrived with c76f528 (v0.99.4, 2026-07-27). Both are unaudited.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: any build with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES (System_BuildConfig.h:707-710) — i.e. FeatherS3, the current primary build. Compiled out on boards without BT or G2.
- **Direction:** These are POD ring buffers touched only from tasks — the same `EXT_RAM_BSS_ATTR` the file already applies to `sGrid` fits them. Alternatively gate them behind first-ring-pair like the LAZY audit's other conversions. Whichever way, `docs/LAZY_ALLOCATION_AUDIT.md`'s "zero unaudited symbols ≥512 B" line needs a date-scoped caveat, because it is now wrong.

### [MEDIUM] Five plain `JsonDocument` declarations bypass the project's PSRAM allocator and build their node pool in internal DRAM — one of them on the G2 12 KB file-viewer path
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Page_Files.cpp:635`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Identity.cpp:95, :155, :387, :425 — same bypass, but small fixed-shape documents (5-7 scalar fields) on infrequent identity read/write paths, so byte impact is minor; they are convention violations rather than pressure sources.
- **What:** `JsonDocument doc; DeserializationError err = deserializeJson(doc, raw);` inside showTextFileViaWidget's pretty-JSON branch. A default-constructed ArduinoJson v7 JsonDocument uses DefaultAllocator (plain malloc/realloc); the project's own idiom is PSRAM_JSON_DOC(name), which installs psramJsonAllocator() (System_MemUtil.h:287-327). The repo has 204 PSRAM_JSON_DOC uses against 5 plain declarations — these 5 are the outliers, not the convention.
- **Why it matters:** `raw` here is the output of readTextLimited(path, raw, 12*1024), so the parsed input is up to 12 KB. ArduinoJson's pool grows in blocks that are individually well under CONFIG_SPIRAM_MALLOC_ALWAYSINTERNAL=16384, so every one of them is served from internal DRAM, and the pool for a 12 KB document is typically a multiple of the input. That runs on the g2_tap_disp worker, concurrently with the 12,304 B internal reserve from the same function and with dual BLE GATT clients up — the worst internal-DRAM moment the firmware has. serializeJsonPretty(doc, display) then grows a second internal-DRAM String on top. Because a JsonDocument allocation failure is not surfaced (deserializeJson returns NoMemory but the branch only prints the parse error), the visible symptom is "pretty view failed" with no memory signal.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all with ENABLE_G2_GLASSES=1 (current FeatherS3 config)
- **Direction:** Change all five to PSRAM_JSON_DOC(doc). For the G2 site specifically, also consider that the raw String and the pretty String are alive simultaneously — serializing straight into the existing static gFilesTextBody would remove the second one.

### [MEDIUM] P16 per-UI-action task spawning was NOT eliminated — six live create/destroy sites remain on the glasses tap path
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Page_Network.cpp:818`
- **Also:** mic_record (System_Microphone.cpp:406), mic_viz (:1213), cam_record (System_Camera_Video.cpp:421), sr_task (System_ESPSR.cpp:2632), ei_continuous (System_EdgeImpulse.cpp:1880), ring_spoof / ring_bridge_hb (G2_Ring.cpp:1576, :2063) are also non-init creates but are per-session (CLI start/stop), not per-tap, so their churn rate is far lower.
- **What:** Verified per-action creates that survive on the current build: g2_net_scan 4096 B per WiFi-scan tap (G2_Page_Network.cpp:818); g2_wifi_pending 4096 B per WiFi toggle and per connect-saved tap (:1134, :1360); g2_ring_pending 4096 B per R1 connect tap (:1665); g2_ai_test 4096 B per AI-card test tap (G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp:522); g2_img_probe 8192 B per image probe (:1094/:1114); i2c0_begin 4096 B per bus init and per bus recovery (System_I2C_Manager.cpp:244, reached from initBus :283 and performBusRecovery :400); mic_record 4096 B per recording start (System_Microphone.cpp:406); mic_viz 4096 B per visualiser start (:1213). The large consolidation work is real and visible (G2_Glasses.cpp:10945, :11231, :13452, G2_Glasses.h:144 all record retired spawn-per-action paths), but the pattern is not gone.
- **Why it matters:** docs/HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT_MECHANICS.md identifies exactly these — "per-UI-action G2 task stacks, each needing one *unbroken* internal block" — as the real big-contiguous internal-DRAM consumer, on a device whose normal free internal heap already sits below System_MemoryMonitor's own 40,960 B warning threshold. Each of these is a create/destroy cycle, so the cost is fragmentation, not steady-state bytes. Failure is soft and already visible in-tree: G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp:1091 documents the image probe falling back to a PSRAM stack "when dual G2 + WiFi leaves too little contiguous internal heap", and G2_Glasses.cpp:19069 notes "QVGA+ hits xTaskCreate failure on the post-viewer page-swap". All eight sites do check the create result and degrade rather than crash.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: FeatherS3 (ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES); i2c0_begin applies to all I2C boards; prior art: KNOWN — memory feedback_avoid_per_action_tasks; docs/HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT_MECHANICS.md §2(b); docs/LAZY_ALLOCATION_AUDIT.md §6.3. Status unchanged: still present, not eliminated. Reporting because the 
- **Direction:** The three G2 watchdog/scan workers (g2_net_scan, g2_wifi_pending, g2_ring_pending) are all short polling loops that could ride the existing persistent g2_session_w or g2_page_swap_w queues, or an esp_timer, the way the notification auto-clear already does (G2_Glasses.cpp:13452). i2c0_begin is the odd one out: it exists solely to run wire->begin() on CPU1 and is inherently one-shot — but see the note that beginBusOnCpu1 blocks on xSemaphoreTake(done, portMAX_DELAY) at System_I2C_Manager.cpp:251 while holding busMutexes[0] (taken at :362), so a hung i2c_driver_install there wedges bus 0 permanently.

### [MEDIUM] g2_net_scan create-failure fallback runs the full scan flow inline on g2_tap_disp's 6400-byte stack, and its comment names the wrong task
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Page_Network.cpp:818`
- **What:** spawnNetworkScanWorker() falls back to `runNetworkScanFlow(); netScanMarkFinished();` inline at :823-:824 when the 4096 B xTaskCreatePinnedToCore fails. The comment at :820-:821 says "running scan inline (BLE will stall briefly)", implying the Bluedroid notify task. The actual call chain is handleDevEvent (BTC_TASK) -> tapDispatcherEnqueue (G2_Glasses.cpp:11478) -> g2_tap_disp worker -> handleHijackMenuTap (G2_Glasses.cpp:6508) -> invokePageFromMain -> g2NetworkHandleTap (:1979) -> handleWiFiTap case 5 (:1174) -> spawnNetworkScanWorker. g2_tap_disp is created with a 6400-byte stack (G2_Glasses.cpp:11432), not BTC_TASK's 8192.
- **Why it matters:** The fallback fires only when internal DRAM cannot supply 4 KB — the same pressure that makes stack headroom scarce. It then runs work the author budgeted 4096 B for (WiFi.scanNetworks ~1 KB per the comment at :814, plus the EvenAI card builders, plus showScanResults building the list page and BLE frames) on top of whatever handleHijackMenuTap has already pushed onto a 6400-byte stack. With the canary check enabled the result is a detected abort of g2_tap_disp rather than silent corruption, but that takes the whole glasses UI down until reboot. The wrong-context comment also means anyone reasoning about this path budgets against the wrong stack.
- **Confidence:** PLAUSIBLE — board: FeatherS3 (ENABLE_WIFI && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES)
- **Direction:** Correct the comment to name g2_tap_disp, and make the fallback a user-visible failure (a toast such as "scan unavailable, low memory") rather than an inline run — the tap can simply be retried.

### [MEDIUM] G2 settings page reports a successful save as a failure whenever the bound command returns a "SUCCESS"-prefixed string — divergent success criteria between the G2 and OLED settings editors
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Page_Settings.cpp:543`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/OLED_SettingsEditor.cpp:186-190 (the divergent copy); components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4437 (the SUCCESS exemption); components/hardwareone/System_SensorLogging.cpp:1909,1916,1922,1970 (the live returns)
- **What:** The two surfaces that share System_SettingsEditorCore judge a commit differently. G2 `onSettingsCommitDone` (G2_Page_Settings.cpp:530-554) discards the `ok` flag and uses `const bool success = (result && strncmp(result, "OK", 2) == 0);`. The OLED twin `setSettingValueStr` (OLED_SettingsEditor.cpp:186-190) uses `if (!ok || strncmp(out, "Error", 5) == 0)`. The G2 criterion depends on `stampOkStatus` (System_Utils.cpp:4417) having prefixed "OK: ", but that function deliberately skips the stamp for results starting with `{`, `[`-as-JSON, and — the live case — `SUCCESS` (:4437, `if (strncmp(out, "SUCCESS", 7) == 0) return;`).
- **Why it matters:** Concrete path: the SettingEntry `healthTrackingEnabled` (System_SensorLogging.cpp:2115) is SETTING_BOOL, editable, not secret, and binds `cmdKey = "healthtrack"`. G2's bool handler at G2_Page_Settings.cpp:642-645 submits `healthtrack 0`. `cmd_healthtrack` (System_SensorLogging.cpp:1927) routes "0" to `healthTrackSet(false)`, which returns "SUCCESS: Health Track OFF" / "SUCCESS: Health Track OFF — sensor logging stopped" / "SUCCESS: Health Track OFF — R1 removed; other sensors still logging" (System_SensorLogging.cpp:1909/1916/1922). stampOkStatus leaves those untouched, so G2's check fails, `settingsShowBannerThenEntries(msg)` flashes the string as an error for 1.3 s, and the entries list is NOT re-rendered — the lens keeps displaying the stale pre-toggle value while the setting actually changed and was persisted. The same toggle on the OLED reports success correctly. Any future setting whose cmdKey command returns a JSON payload hits the identical failure.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: any build with ENABLE_G2_GLASSES (FeatherS3 and XIAO); the OLED half is unaffected
- **Direction:** Put one success predicate next to `stampOkStatus` — it owns the OK:/SUCCESS/JSON exemption table — and have both editors call it, rather than each re-deriving "did this work?" from the first two bytes of the result.

### [MEDIUM] gRing GATT pointer set is cleared field-by-field from the BLE disconnect callback on core 0 while core-1 tasks re-read gRing.writeChar between their liveness check and the dereference
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Ring.cpp:192`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/G2_Ring.cpp:116-122 (ringClearGattPointers), :854-862 (onDisconnect clear), :236-246 (ringWrite pre-check), :226 (drain pre-check), :1041-1047 (stale-client replacement, also unlocked)
- **What:** `G2RingState` (:80-91) holds `client`, `writeChar`, `notifyChar`, `connected`, `clientStale` as plain non-volatile members with no lock covering the group. `RingClientCallbacks::onDisconnect` (:854) and `ringClearGattPointers()` (:116) clear them one assignment at a time. onDisconnect runs on BTC_TASK (core 0); `ringClearGattPointers` is also called from `g2RingInvalidateLink`/`g2RingDisconnect` on cmd_exec (core 0). The readers are on core 1: `ringSpoofTaskBody` (`ring_spoof`, APP_CORE, :1529) and `ringBridgeHeartbeatBody` (`ring_bridge_hb`, APP_CORE, :2016) call `ringWrite()` → `ringWriteLocked()`. `ringWriteLocked` (:193) checks `gRing.connected && gRing.writeChar && !gRing.clientStale`, then blocks up to RING_WRITE_MUTEX_MS = 1500 ms in `xSemaphoreTake(gRing.writeMutex, …)`, re-checks the same three fields at :201, and dereferences `gRing.writeChar->writeValue(...)` at :206. `gRing.writeMutex` is never taken by the clearing side, so the recheck buys nothing against a concurrent clear.
- **Why it matters:** With the R1 ring connected and `ringspoof`/bridge heartbeat active, pull the ring out of range. Bluedroid delivers onDisconnect on core 0 and sets `gRing.writeChar = nullptr` between the core-1 recheck at :201 and the dereference at :206 — `writeValue()` is then invoked with a null `this`, faulting on the first member access. The recheck sequence also leaves `connected` and `writeChar` mutually inconsistent for a few instructions in every clear, so a reader can pass the guard on a half-cleared state. The file's own comment at :237-239 confirms the pointer becomes genuinely dangling ('after BLEDevice::deinit the pointer is dangling. Callers must g2RingInvalidateLink() before tearing down the stack'), which is the same hazard with a wider window.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: any build with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES — FeatherS3 today
- **Direction:** The five GATT fields are one logical unit and should transition together under something the send path also honours — the existing gRing.writeMutex is the natural candidate, or a short portMUX around the clear plus a single snapshot read on the send side. Re-reading gRing.writeChar at the point of dereference, rather than trusting the earlier check, is the specific shape to eliminate.

### [LOW] bleCentralTxInit() is a non-atomic lazy singleton reachable from two cores; a double-create can permanently wedge the shared BLE transmit gate
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/BLE_CentralTx.cpp:9`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/BLE_CentralTx.cpp:14-19 (lazy init inside take); components/hardwareone/G2_Ring.cpp:1185; components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:8593
- **What:** `gBleCentralTx` is a plain non-volatile static. `bleCentralTxInit()` is `if (gBleCentralTx) return; gBleCentralTx = xSemaphoreCreateMutex();` — check-then-act with no guard. `bleCentralTxTake()` (:14) calls it lazily on every take when the handle is null, then re-reads the global for the actual `xSemaphoreTake`. `bleCentralTxGive()` (:21) also re-reads the global. The two init call sites are `G2_Ring.cpp:1185` (ring init, reachable from `ringconnect` on cmd_exec, core 0) and `G2_Glasses.cpp:8593` (`initG2Client`, also cmd_exec) — but `bleCentralTxTake` itself is called from `ring_spoof`/`ring_bridge_hb` on core 1 and from the G2 workers on core 1, so first use can genuinely originate on either core.
- **Why it matters:** If two tasks reach `bleCentralTxTake()` before either init has stored the handle, both see null, both create a mutex, and the second store overwrites the first. Task A can then take mutex #1 and, because give() re-reads the global, release mutex #2 — `xSemaphoreGive` fails (A is not the holder) and mutex #1 stays held by a task that believes it released it. The gate is then permanently owned by a dead critical section: every subsequent G2 envelope and R1 write returns Busy/queued forever, with the ring TX queue silently dropping oldest frames. One mutex is also leaked. The window is the very first take of a boot, so this is narrow, but the failure mode is a hard, silent, non-recovering wedge rather than a glitch.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: any build with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH — FeatherS3 today
- **Direction:** Either create the gate unconditionally at a single-threaded point in boot and make take() fail closed when it is absent, or make the lazy path a compare-and-swap. The give() path re-reading the global rather than the handle it took is the part that converts a benign leak into a wedge.

### [LOW] Bluetooth.cpp labels BTC_TASK callback code "ISR-safe" and logs "ISR connect"/"ISR disconnect" at six sites
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/Bluetooth.cpp:373`
- **Also:** the correct statement is at Bluetooth.cpp:739; the correct terminology is also used at G2_Glasses.cpp:9172 ("runs on the Bluedroid callback task")
- **What:** Six comment/log sites in the Bluedroid callback layer describe task-context code as interrupt context: :373 "Use deferred flag pattern for logging (ISR-safe)", :390 "minimal state updates - ISR-safe", :401 "uses static lookup - ISR-safe", :432 log string "ISR connect conn=%u ...", :442 "(ISR-safe)", :474 log string "ISR disconnect conn=%u ...", :490 "Callback runs on BTC_TASK - defer logging to task context (ISR-safe pattern)". BTC_TASK is a regular FreeRTOS task pinned to core 0 with CONFIG_BT_BTC_TASK_STACK_SIZE=8192 (sdkconfig:781). The same file states this correctly at :739: "BTC_TASK is a regular FreeRTOS task (not an ISR), so PSRAM-backed allocations are safe."
- **Why it matters:** Two concrete consequences. First, the "ISR connect"/"ISR disconnect" strings are user-visible in the log stream and in docs/LOGGING_TAG_AUDIT.md's taxonomy work, so an operator reading a log believes the firmware is doing BLE bookkeeping inside an interrupt. Second, and more costly, the label sets the wrong budget for future work: an author who believes BTC is an ISR will avoid legitimate operations (heap allocation, mutex take, PSRAM access) that are perfectly safe there — while the actual constraint on that path is stack depth, not interrupt context. The file already demonstrates both beliefs simultaneously, which is how the wrong one survives.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Normalize on "BTC task" / "Bluedroid callback task" throughout, including the two log format strings, and keep the deferral pattern itself — it is right for stack-depth reasons that should be stated explicitly.

### [LOW] `Bluetooth.cpp` calls BTC_TASK code "ISR"/"ISR-safe" in seven places, and the false premise silently suppresses the BLE slot-exhaustion diagnostic
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/Bluetooth.cpp:385`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:6098 -- the doxygen block on `onEspNowDataSent` says `@note Called in interrupt context - keep processing minimal` and `@warning Do not call blocking functions or allocate memory`. ESP-NOW send/recv callbacks run on the WiFi task in IDF v5, and the same file states that correctly at :397-399 for the recv side.
- **What:** BTC_TASK is a plain FreeRTOS task -- `CONFIG_BT_BTC_TASK_STACK_SIZE=8192` (sdkconfig:781), pinned core 0. The file nonetheless says "ISR-safe" at :373, :390, :401, :442 and :490, and emits literal log text `"ISR connect ..."` (:432) and `"ISR disconnect ..."` (:474). Line :739 of the same file states the truth: "BTC_TASK is a regular FreeRTOS task (not an ISR), so PSRAM-backed allocations are safe." The concrete consequence is at :385-388: when `ServerCallbacks::onConnect` finds no free connection slot it does `// No logging here - callback context` and a bare `return` -- while the same function happily calls `BLE_DEBUGF` on the success path ~45 lines later at :430-437.
- **Why it matters:** Two costs. First, operational: "all BLE connection slots are full, connection dropped" is exactly the event an operator needs and it is thrown away for a reason that does not exist -- logging is demonstrably legal in that function. Second, contagion: this is the single densest source of "an ISR does X" language in the codebase, and it is wrong. During this audit run the recon map itself flagged it as the most likely origin of bogus ISR findings, and the parallel false claim in the ESP-NOW layer shows the pattern already spread.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH=1 (FeatherS3 primary)
- **Direction:** Replace "ISR"/"ISR-safe" with "BTC_TASK" everywhere in Bluetooth.cpp and delete the `@note`/`@warning` pair at System_ESPNow.cpp:6098 (the real constraint -- keep it short, it is the WiFi task -- is already stated at :397-399). Then let the slot-exhaustion branch log through the same deferred pattern the rest of the callback uses.

### [LOW] g2_page_swap_w's sizing comment claims a high-water mark and headroom that cannot exist against its 3584-byte stack
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:11230`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:5291-5293 and :5207-5210 (httpd/https stack_size "words" comments); verified against /Users/morgan/esp/esp-idf/components/esp_http_server/src/httpd_main.c:530 and the port osal.h httpd_os_thread_create
- **What:** pageSwapInit() creates the worker with a 3584-byte stack (:11233) under a comment that reads "Measured HWM ~6.2 KB with glasses connected -> ~7.8 KB headroom" — a 14 KB total that the task does not have. Read correctly (peak used ~1.55 KB of 3584 B), the real headroom is roughly 2 KB, and the measured frames on this worker's own path make that meaningful: pageSwapWorkerLoop 336 B, invokePageFromMain 1008 B, g2BuildCreateMixedListText/g2BuildRebuildList 592-1024 B, debugQueuePrintf 352 B -> Print::vprintf 128 B, plus the worker dispatches arbitrary spec->render() and CustomSpec::run() callbacks whose depth is not bounded at this site.
- **Why it matters:** A future maintainer trimming DRAM will read "7.8 KB headroom" on a task that has roughly 2 KB and cut it, or will see an overflow report and conclude the number is already 14 KB so the stack cannot be the cause. The same fictional arithmetic sits on the HTTP/HTTPS server stacks (WebServer_Server.cpp:5291-5293 "7680 words = 30 KB, ~12 KB headroom (40%)" and :5207-5210 "11059 words = 44 KB"): esp_http_server passes config.stack_size straight through httpd_os_thread_create to xTaskCreatePinnedToCoreWithCaps, so those are 7680 and 11059 BYTES. Those two values happen to be fine (both exceed the IDF defaults of 4096 and 10240), but "fixing" the comment's arithmetic by multiplying would ask for 30-44 KB of internal DRAM and fail task creation on this device.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: any build with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES for the G2 site; all builds for the httpd sites; prior art: Recon anomaly B lists three 4x-myth comment sites (G2_Glasses.cpp:11424, :8917, System_Camera_DVP.cpp:988) plus the httpd pair; G2_Glasses.cpp:11230 is a fourth, distinct site not in that list.
- **Direction:** Correct these comments to bytes and re-derive the headroom claims, or delete the fabricated measurements outright rather than leaving numbers that no measurement could have produced. Anything a static_assert can check should be asserted instead of narrated.

### [LOW] G2 heartbeat worker sizes and gates a *contiguous* task stack using *total* free internal DRAM — the same class of error its own comment warns about
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:8135`
- **Also:** Correct in-file precedent: G2_Glasses.cpp:17897-17907. G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp:1100-1133 logs both free and largest but only for diagnostics, not as a gate.
- **What:** The gate reads `internalFree = heap_caps_get_free_size(MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL | MALLOC_CAP_8BIT)` (:8135-8136), picks `stackBytes` 3072/2560/2048 from thresholds on it (:8138-8144), then refuses to start if `internalFree < stackBytes + 1200` (:8146). Every one of those tests is against total free, but `xTaskCreatePinnedToCore` needs one *unbroken* block. The comment two lines above (:8131-8133) gets the neighbouring point exactly right — "xTaskCreate stack lives in **internal** DRAM… ESP.getFreeHeap() is misleading here" — and then the code makes the same mistake one level down. The correct idiom exists in this same file: `g2ShowMapPage` at :17901 gates on `heap_caps_get_largest_free_block(MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL | MALLOC_CAP_8BIT)` with a comment explicitly reasoning about contiguity.
- **Why it matters:** Under fragmentation — 8 KB free internal, largest block 2 KB — the gate computes `stackBytes = 2560`, `needApprox = 3760`, sees `8000 > 3760`, and proceeds. `xTaskCreatePinnedToCore` then fails on contiguity. The outcome is not a crash: `rc != pdPASS` is handled at :8157-8162. But the whole point of the gate is to produce the actionable message at :8147-8150 ("Dual BLE clients exhausted internal heap; heartbeats will not run") instead of the bare create-failure line, and in the fragmented case — the case the gate was written for — it produces the wrong one. The adaptive stack sizing is likewise picking 3072 when only 2048 would fit.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: any build with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES — FeatherS3 today
- **Direction:** Gate on largest-free-block (and pick the adaptive stack size from it too), matching `g2ShowMapPage` at :17901 rather than inventing a second convention. Total free is still worth logging alongside, since the two numbers together are what identify fragmentation.

### [LOW] gR1Cache ring telemetry is written field-by-field from BTC_TASK on core 0 and read unsynchronized by core-1 UI, yielding samples paired with the wrong timestamp
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Ring.cpp:284`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/G2_Ring.cpp:292-300 (ringNoteWear), :311-324 (ringSampleAgeSec consuming the pair), :1355 (g2RingGetTelemetry reader)
- **What:** `R1TelemetryCache gR1Cache` (:276-284) groups each metric as a (value, ringTs, rxMs, valid) quadruple — hr/hrv/spo2/temp/battery/wear — with no mutex, no sequence counter and no volatile. It is written by the notify decode path (`ringNoteWear` at :292-300 and the sibling metric setters), which runs on BTC_TASK (core 0) via `ringNotifyThunk` → `ringDumpFrame` (:749/:658). It is read by `g2RingGetTelemetry` (:1355 region) from the OLED R1 submenu and the G2 Bluetooth pages, both APP_CORE/core 1, and by CLI on core 0. Each setter writes the value first and the `valid` flag last, but nothing forces that order to be observed, and nothing keeps a reader from straddling two updates.
- **Why it matters:** With the ring streaming telemetry, open the OLED R1 submenu. Core 1 reads `hr` from sample N and then `hrTs`/`hrRxMs` from sample N+1 because core 0 wrote between the two loads. `ringSampleAgeSec()` (:311) computes the displayed age from that mismatched timestamp, so a fresh heart-rate reading can be labelled minutes old or vice versa. Individual fields are aligned scalars so there is no sub-word tearing; the defect is a mismatched tuple, i.e. a wrong age/staleness label on a health value.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: any build with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES — FeatherS3 today
- **Direction:** Sensor caches elsewhere in this tree all carry their own mutex (gImuCache, gGpsCache, gFmRadioCache…); gR1Cache is the outlier and could follow the same SensorCacheGuard convention, or publish each metric as one atomically-swapped struct.

### [NOTE] g2-fsm's stated priority relationship to the workers it coordinates is inverted
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_HijackFsm.cpp:322`
- **Also:** Related comment drift: tapDispatcherInit (G2_Glasses.cpp:11408-11410) claims "Same priority + stack as the page-swap worker" — the priorities match (2) but the stacks do not (6400 B vs 3584 B).
- **What:** The comment at :322-:324 reads "Priority 5 sits above idle and below the BLE / page-swap worker tasks so transitions drain promptly without preempting time-sensitive paths." g2-fsm is created at priority 5 (:334). g2_page_swap_w is created at tskIDLE_PRIORITY+2 = 2 (G2_Glasses.cpp:11233) and g2_tap_disp at 2 (G2_Glasses.cpp:11432). All three are on APP_CORE. The FSM therefore sits three priorities above the page-swap worker, not below it.
- **Why it matters:** applyEvent() performs lens-mirror side effects (g2LensApplyHijackActive at :270). Because the FSM preempts the page-swap and tap workers rather than yielding to them, an event posted while a page swap is in flight applies its mirror update before the swap's BLE frames reach the air — the opposite of the ordering the comment asserts was designed. No confirmed user-visible failure, but any future reasoning about hijack-state ordering that trusts this comment will be wrong.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: FeatherS3 (ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES)
- **Direction:** Decide the intent and make the code match: if the FSM genuinely should not preempt the UI workers, drop it to tskIDLE_PRIORITY+1; otherwise rewrite the comment to state that it deliberately preempts them, and why.


### Sensors / I2C / HAL — 10 findings (5 critical/high)

### [CRITICAL] FM-radio poll nests two manager transactions on the same bus — the inner one always self-deadlocks on the non-recursive bus mutex, so RDS/RSSI/seek-completion never run and the bus is held ~80% of the time
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/i2csensor_rda5807.cpp:427`
- **Also:** Same nesting bug class at i2csensor_sths34pf80.cpp:334 and :595 — filed separately
- **What:** `updateFMRadio()` opens `i2cTaskWithTimeout((uint8_t)gSettings.fmRadioBus, I2C_ADDR_FM_RADIO, FM_RADIO_I2C_CLOCK, 1000, ...)` and then, INSIDE that lambda, calls `i2cTransactionNACKTolerant((uint8_t)gSettings.fmRadioBus, I2C_ADDR_FM_RADIO, ..., 100, ...)` (line 429). Both resolve to the same `I2CDevice` (same address + same bus) and both go through `I2CDeviceManager::executeTransaction`, which does `xSemaphoreTake(busMutexes[bus], pdMS_TO_TICKS(device->adaptiveTimeoutMs))` (System_I2C_Manager.h:345). `busMutexes[]` is created with `xSemaphoreCreateMutex()` (System_I2C_Manager.cpp:69) — NON-recursive. The outer take succeeds; the inner take is the same task asking for a mutex it already owns, so it blocks until timeout and returns `ReturnType()`. The inner lambda body never executes. The outer lambda then does `return true;` unconditionally (line 481), so the manager calls `device->recordSuccess()` every cycle.
- **Why it matters:** With `fmradiostart` on any build that compiles the RDA5807 in: (1) `radio.checkRDS()` / `radio.getRadioInfo()` never run — `gFmRadioCache.rssi/snr/stereo/headphonesConnected` stay at their init values forever and `gFmRadioCache.lastUpdate`/`dataValid` are never stamped, so the shared sensor envelope reports `valid:false` with a frozen `ts` on web/OLED/G2/ESP-NOW/MQTT. (2) `gFmRadioCache.seekInProgress` is set true at line 655 and cleared ONLY at line 454 — inside the dead lambda — including the 6 s failsafe. One `fmradio seek` therefore latches "Seeking…" permanently, and G2_Glasses.cpp:5397 (`if (gFmRadioCache.seekInProgress) return; // one seek at a time`) then blocks every future seek for the rest of the boot. (3) Worst: the FM device is registered with `timeoutMs=1000` (line 158 / line 427), so `adaptiveTimeoutMs`=1000 — each `updateFMRadio()` holds the bus mutex while self-blocked for a full second, and `fmRadioTask` calls it every ~250 ms. The bus is unavailable to every other device ~80% of the time. With `fmRadioBus` defaulting to 0 (System_Settings.h:247) that starves the gamepad poll (80 ms mutex timeout, i2csensor_seesaw.cpp:512) and the sensor scans on the primary bus. (4) `i2chealth` shows the FM radio as 100% healthy the whole time because the outer lambda returns true.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: any build with CUSTOM_ENABLE_FM_RADIO=1 (currently 0 on the FeatherS3[D] config in tree); bus-0 starvation applies whenever fmRadioBus==inputBus
- **Direction:** The inner NACK-tolerant call is the only one that should exist — the outer `i2cTaskWithTimeout` wrapper adds nothing but the deadlock. Delete the outer wrapper and keep the single `i2cTransactionNACKTolerant` call. Separately, `executeTransaction`'s mutex-take failure is silent apart from a DEBUG_I2CF line; consider making a mutex timeout on a device whose bus mutex is already held by the calling task an explicit, loud error, since the clock-stack machinery (CLOCK_STACK_MAX=8, "nested-transaction safe") advertises a nesting capability the mutex cannot actually deliver.

### [HIGH] apdsColorPoll()'s unbounded data-ready spin hangs cmd_exec_task permanently, taking every command transport with it
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/i2csensor_apds9960.cpp:359`
- **What:** `apdsColorPoll()` waits with `while (!gAPDS9960->colorDataReady()) { delay(5); }` — no timeout, no error exit, no iteration cap. `Adafruit_APDS9960::colorDataReady()` (components/hardwareone_libs/Adafruit_APDS9960_Library/Adafruit_APDS9960.cpp:499-501) returns `_status.AVALID` from `read8(APDS9960_STATUS)`, and `read8` (:638-643) declares `uint8_t ret;` uninitialized, calls `this->read(reg, &ret, 1)` and discards its return value — so an I2C NAK yields stack garbage with no error signal. The call also bypasses the bus mutex entirely: the apds_task path at :526 correctly wraps the identical read in `i2cTaskWithTimeout(I2C_ADDR_APDS, 100000, 100, ...)`, this one does not.
- **Why it matters:** `cmd_apdscolor` (:103-108) is a registered CLI command, so `apdsColorPoll()` runs on cmd_exec_task — the single serializer for every command source in the firmware (serial, web, BLE, G2, ESP-NOW RCE, MQTT, automation sub-commands, voice). Unplug the APDS9960, glitch the STEMMA rail, or let the sensor reset and lose its AEN bit while `gApdsColorRunning` is still true, then run `apdscolor`: AVALID never sets and cmd_exec_task spins forever. `delay(5)` yields, so IDLE0 keeps feeding the TWDT and no watchdog fires (see the watchdog-config finding). Every later command queues behind it; the 6-slot `gCmdExecQ` fills; `submitAndExecuteSync` callers each burn 60 s (System_Utils.cpp:4848) before reporting "[ERROR] Command timed out".
