The device is then unreachable from every transport and only a power cycle recovers it. This is the one unbounded hardware poll left in the tree — the ToF driver's equivalent (i2csensor_vl53l4cx.cpp:86-91, :404-413) correctly bails on both a 250 ms deadline and a driver error.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: full-I2C builds only — gated on ENABLE_APDS_SENSOR, which is 0 on the current FeatherS3 config
- **Direction:** Give the wait the same shape the ToF driver already uses — a millis() deadline plus a bail on read failure — and route the read through i2cTaskWithTimeout so the CLI path takes the bus mutex like apds_task at :526 does. The uninitialized `ret` in Adafruit_APDS9960::read8 is worth noting as the reason no error can ever propagate.

### [HIGH] DS3231 TZ save/restore aliases getenv("TZ") into setenv("TZ") and permanently clobbers the process timezone to UTC
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/i2csensor_ds3231.cpp:233`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/i2csensor_ds3231.cpp:198-207 (rtcEarlyBootSync), :290-299 (rtcToUnixTime)
- **What:** Three functions do the same 'force TZ=UTC0, mktime, restore TZ' dance: rtcEarlyBootSync (:198-207), rtcSyncToSystem (:233-242), rtcToUnixTime (:290-299). Each does `char* oldTZ = getenv("TZ"); setenv("TZ","UTC0",1); tzset(); ... if (oldTZ) setenv("TZ", oldTZ, 1);`. getenv() returns a pointer INTO the environ string; newlib's _setenv_r overwrites that same buffer in place whenever the existing value is at least as long as the new one, so `oldTZ` no longer points at the saved value — it points at the literal "UTC0" that was just written over it. The 'restore' then re-sets TZ to "UTC0".
- **Why it matters:** Verified end-to-end. (a) sdkconfig has CONFIG_LIBC_NEWLIB=y; I extracted libc_a-setenv_r.o from the esp32s3 libc.a and disassembled it — at _setenv_r+0x4d it calls strlen(C) and `bltu a10,a8, +0xd0` branches to the malloc path ONLY when strlen(existing) < strlen(new); otherwise it falls into the in-place byte-copy loop at +0x58 writing into the very pointer _findenv_r (i.e. getenv) returns. (b) components/arduino/cores/esp32/esp32-hal-time.c setTimeZone() builds TZ as "UTC<h>DST<h>" (>=8 chars) and configTime() always calls it, so after setupNTP() the existing TZ value is always longer than "UTC0" (4) — the in-place path always wins. (c) Reachable path: System_Utils.cpp:2381 setupNTP() -> configTime() sets TZ="UTC8DST8"; NTP then times out (offline / blocked NTP); System_Utils.cpp:2483 calls rtcSyncToSystem() as the fallback -> TZ is left "UTC0" for the rest of the boot. Also reachable from cmd_rtcsync (i2csensor_ds3231.cpp:772), the RTC task's sync at :463, and merely printing the time with `rtc time` (:666 -> rtcToUnixTime). Consequence: every localtime_r()/mktime() consumer silently switches to UTC — log line timestamps (System_Utils.cpp:774), capture/log filenames (Clock::formatFilenameLocal), the HH:MM clock faces, and critically System_Automation.cpp nextFire()'s TIME/MONTHLY/YEARLY cases (:2321 localtime_r, :2331 mktime), so every clock-scheduled automation starts firing at the wrong wall-clock hour by the tz offset. The `rtc` display itself keeps looking correct because rtcLocalTime() (:340) does manual offset math, which masks the bug.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: Any build with ENABLE_RTC_SENSOR=1 — I2C_LEVEL_FULL (System_BuildConfig.h:440) or I2C_LEVEL_CUSTOM with CUSTOM_ENABLE_RTC. Not present on DISABLED/OLED_ONLY/STANDALONE levels.
- **Direction:** Copy the old TZ value into a local char buffer before calling setenv, or drop the env dance entirely — the RTC fields are already UTC, so a hand-rolled days-since-epoch conversion (the same shape rtcLocalTime() and WebPage_Sensors.cpp:457 already use to avoid setenv/tzset) needs no TZ at all.

### [HIGH] thermal and FM-radio sensors can never be restarted after any stop — the start path's stale-handle guard permanently blocks the only code that clears the stale handle
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/i2csensor_mlx90640.cpp:200`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/i2csensor_rda5807.cpp:374 (identical pattern for FM radio)
- **What:** thermalStartInternal() does `if (gThermalTaskHandle == nullptr) { createThermalTask(); } else { log "Thermal task already exists"; }`. createThermalTask() (System_TaskUtils.cpp:225-237) is the *only* code in the tree that ever assigns nullptr to gThermalTaskHandle — it does so in its own stale-handle recovery block at :228. The task itself exits via SENSOR_TASK_EXIT(THERMAL) at i2csensor_mlx90640.cpp:1483, and System_TaskUtils.h:215-216 documents explicitly that the handle must NOT be cleared there. handleDeviceStopped() (System_I2C.cpp:2346-2350) clears only gThermalRunning/gThermalLastStopTime. So after the first stop the handle is permanently non-null and the recovery path is unreachable. i2csensor_rda5807.cpp:374 has the identical shape for gFmRadioTaskHandle (its exit at :295 carries the comment "Do NOT clear gFmRadioTaskHandle here — let create function use eTaskGetState()", which is exactly the function this guard prevents from running).
- **Why it matters:** `thermalstop` then `thermalstart`: the second command takes the else branch, sets gThermalRunning=true, fires sensorStatusBumpWith("openthermal@queue"), and returns true. The CLI, the web UI and the sensor-status surfaces all report the thermal sensor as enabled, but no task exists to poll it — thermalTask self-deleted on the stop. Every downstream reader (cache, ESP-NOW sensor broadcast, OLED thermal mode) sees stale-or-empty data with no error anywhere. Same for `fmradiostop`/`fmradiostart`. It is also reachable without any user stop: checkTaskStackSafety() (System_I2C.h:213-228) clears the enabled flag on a near-overflow, which drives the task through the same self-delete — so one stack-safety bailout permanently disables the sensor until reboot. The other seven sensors call createXTask() unconditionally (i2csensor_bno055.cpp:175, vl53l4cx.cpp:231, apds9960.cpp:311, sths34pf80.cpp:319, pa1010d.cpp:190, seesaw.cpp:164, ds3231.cpp:610) and are not affected.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: builds with ENABLE_THERMAL_SENSOR / ENABLE_FMRADIO_SENSOR = 1 (I2C_LEVEL_FULL, or a CUSTOM level enabling them). Both are 0 on the current FeatherS3 config (System_BuildConfig.h:135, :139), so this is latent there and live on full-I2C builds.
- **Direction:** Delete the `if (handle == nullptr)` wrapper at both sites and call createThermalTask()/createFMRadioTask() unconditionally, matching the other seven sensors — the create helper already contains the idempotency check. (See the separate finding about that helper's eTaskGetState-on-freed-TCB before relying on it.)

### [HIGH] STHS34PF80 presence driver wraps its bus-aware register helpers in a bus-0 transaction — every register read self-deadlocks, so the sensor can never initialise
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/i2csensor_sths34pf80.cpp:334`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/i2csensor_sths34pf80.cpp:595 (poll path)
- **What:** `presenceInit()` returns `i2cTaskWithTimeout(I2C_ADDR_PRESENCE, 100000, 500, [&]{ ... readRegister(WHO_AM_I) ... })`. That 4-argument overload (System_I2C.h:135) routes to the legacy bus-0 path. Inside the lambda, `readRegister()` (line 133) calls `i2cDeviceTransaction(bus, I2C_ADDR_PRESENCE, 100000, 200, ...)` with `bus = gSettings.presenceBus`, which defaults to 0 (System_Settings.h:248). Same non-recursive `busMutexes[0]`, same task → the inner take blocks for `adaptiveTimeoutMs` and fails. Identical shape in the poll loop at line 595: `i2cTaskWithTimeout(I2C_ADDR_PRESENCE, 100000, 100, [&]{ return presencePoll(); })`, and `presencePoll()` (line 380) is built entirely out of `readRegister`/`readRegisters`. The in-file comment at lines 586-590 documents the bus-0/bus-aware split as a cosmetic 'hybrid driver' issue but misses that it is a self-deadlock.
- **Why it matters:** `openpresence` → `presenceStartInternal` → `presenceInit()`: the WHO_AM_I read blocks 500 ms on a mutex the same task holds, `readRegister` returns false, and the driver logs "Failed to read WHO_AM_I" and returns false — every time, on working hardware. The presence sensor can never come up with the default `presenceBus=0`. If the user moves it to `presenceBus=1`, init succeeds (different mutex) but the outer wrapper then holds bus 0's mutex and registers a phantom 0x5A device on bus 0, so health/auto-disable tracking is attributed to the wrong bus and bus-0 devices are blocked for the duration of every presence read.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: any build with CUSTOM_ENABLE_PRESENCE=1 (currently 0 in the in-tree FeatherS3[D] config)
- **Direction:** Drop the outer `i2cTaskWithTimeout` wrappers at lines 334 and 595 entirely — the register helpers already take the correct per-bus mutex and already do health tracking. If a coarser timeout wrapper is wanted around init, it must not be a bus transaction.

### [MEDIUM] gImuActions (IMUActionState) is read-modify-written entirely outside any lock by three tasks including one on the other core
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/i2csensor_bno055.cpp:627`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/i2csensor_bno055.cpp:256 (cmd_imuactions call), :577 (poll-task call); components/hardwareone/i2csensor_bno055_oled.h:28 (OLED render call); struct declared i2csensor_bno055.h:22-62
- **What:** `imuUpdateActions()` snapshots the cache fields under `SensorCacheGuard` and then, with the guard deliberately released, performs a long read-modify-write over `gImuActions` with no synchronization at all: `gImuActions.accelHistory[gImuActions.accelHistoryIndex] = accelMag; gImuActions.accelHistoryIndex = (idx + 1) % 10;` (:652-653), `gImuActions.shakeCount++` (:670), plus tapCount, stepCount, stepsInWindow, isShaking/isTilted/isRotating/isWalking flags, tiltAngle, orientation and lastOrientation — roughly 30 fields. It is called from three places: `imuPoll()` on `imu_task` (I2C_SENSOR_CORE = core 1, i2csensor_bno055.cpp:577 and :256), `displayIMUActions()` on the OLED render path (i2csensor_bno055_oled.h:28, loopTask core 1), and `cmd_imuactions` (i2csensor_bno055.cpp:256) on cmd_exec_task (core 0). The author was aware of the multi-caller problem — the header comment at i2csensor_bno055.h:120-124 says event posts and debounce counters 'would count renders instead of samples and double-post across cores' — and solved only the event-posting half with the `postEvents` flag. The state mutation itself was left unguarded.
- **Why it matters:** Run `imuactions` from any command surface (serial, web /api/cli, ESP-NOW remote) while the IMU poll task is running. cmd_exec on core 0 and imu_task on core 1 both execute `gImuActions.accelHistory[idx] = ...; idx = (idx+1) % 10;` — a lost index update makes two samples overwrite the same slot, corrupting the 10-sample variance window that shake detection thresholds on, and `shakeCount++`/`stepCount++` lose increments outright. `cmd_imuactions` then reads the same fields it is concurrently mutating (:262-277) for its own output. The failure is silent: wrong shake/step/tilt readings and a step counter that undercounts, with no error path.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: full-I2C builds only (ENABLE_IMU_SENSOR). NOT FeatherS3 — gated out on the current board config.
- **Direction:** gImuActions is the only sensor state struct in the tree with no mutex while gImuCache right beside it has one; giving it the same SensorCacheGuard treatment (or folding it under gImuCache.mutex) would match the house pattern. Deciding whether non-poll callers should mutate it at all — versus reading a snapshot — is the cheaper fix.

### [MEDIUM] DS3231 sync helpers clobber the process timezone to UTC via a getenv/setenv aliasing bug
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/i2csensor_ds3231.cpp:198`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/i2csensor_ds3231.cpp:233-242 (rtcSyncToSystem), :290-299 (rtcToUnixTime, reached from :666). Callers: System_Utils.cpp:2467, i2csensor_ds3231.cpp:463 and :772.
- **What:** Three functions do `char* oldTZ = getenv("TZ"); setenv("TZ","UTC0",1); tzset(); ... if (oldTZ) setenv("TZ", oldTZ, 1); ... tzset();` — `rtcEarlyBootSync` (:198), `rtcSyncToSystem` (:233) and `rtcToUnixTime` (:290). `getenv` returns a pointer INTO the environment string; POSIX explicitly says a later `setenv` may invalidate it. Newlib does worse: I disassembled `_setenv_r` in this build's ELF (422bdfe9-422be005) and it compares `strlen(existing_value)` against `strlen(new_value)` (`bltu a10, a8`) and, when the existing value is at least as long, copies the new value OVER IT IN PLACE (the byte loop at 422bdff6) and returns. So after `setenv("TZ","UTC0",1)`, `oldTZ` reads "UTC0", and `setenv("TZ", oldTZ, 1)` restores "UTC0" onto itself.
- **Why it matters:** Arduino's `configTime()` (components/arduino/cores/esp32/esp32-hal-time.c:24-44) always builds a TZ string of at least 8 chars — e.g. gmtOffset -28800 with daylightOffset 0 yields "UTC8DST8"; UTC yields "UTC0DST0". 8 >= 4, so the in-place branch is always taken. Concrete path: WiFi connects -> `setupNTP()` -> `configTime()` sets TZ="UTC8DST8". NTP then times out -> `syncNTPAndResolve` falls back to `rtcSyncToSystem()` (System_Utils.cpp:2467) -> TZ is permanently left as "UTC0". Every subsequent `localtime_r` returns UTC: `Clock::formatISO8601Local`/`formatFilenameLocal`/`formatHHMMLocal`, capture filenames, System_TimeAnchors' `%Y-%m-%d` day folders (:262-266), and — sharpest — `nextFire()`'s `localtime_r`/`mktime` in System_Automation.cpp:2321/2331, so a "07:00 daily" automation starts firing at 07:00 UTC. The `rtcsync` CLI command (:772) reproduces it on demand. Only the boot-time call is safe, because TZ is unset then and the code takes the `unsetenv` branch.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: ENABLE_RTC_SENSOR builds only (I2C_FEATURE_LEVEL==3, or CUSTOM_ENABLE_RTC=1; currently 0 in System_BuildConfig.h:139, so compiled out of the shipping FeatherS3 config)
- **Direction:** Do not touch the global TZ to convert a UTC broken-down time. Copy `oldTZ` into a local `char[64]` before overwriting if the dance must stay, or better, compute the UTC epoch arithmetically (days-from-civil / Howard Hinnant style) so no environment mutation and no `tzset()` is involved — that also removes the cross-task hazard where another task calls `localtime_r` during the UTC0 window.

### [MEDIUM] cmd_thermalread's human path reads the 768-entry thermal frame with no cache lock, 40 lines below a sibling that takes it correctly
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/i2csensor_mlx90640.cpp:350`
- **Also:** locked sibling at components/hardwareone/i2csensor_mlx90640.cpp:301-324; freeing writer at :168-178
- **What:** cmd_thermalread checks `if (!gThermalCache.thermalDataValid || !gThermalCache.thermalFrame)` once at :350, then loops `for (int i = 0; i < 768; i++) float t = gThermalCache.thermalFrame[i] / 100.0f;` at :356-361 and reads gThermalCache.thermalSeq at :365 — all with no lockThermalCache(). The JSON path in the same function delegates to thermalBuildSummaryJSON, which does take the lock (:301) around the identical 768-iteration walk (:313-323). ThermalCache carries a mutex (i2csensor_mlx90640.h:11) and the poll task's write path honours it (:904-905, memcpy into thermalFrame under lockThermalCache).
- **Why it matters:** thermalFrame is a heap (PSRAM) pointer that openthermal frees and nulls at :168-178 — under the lock, but the lock buys nothing against a reader that never takes it. Run `thermalread` from any surface while `openthermal` re-initialises from another (OLED, ESP-NOW remote, automation, a second CLI transport): the freeing task nulls the pointer between the :350 null check and the loop, and the reader either walks 1536 bytes of freed PSRAM or dereferences address 0. Concurrently with the poll task's 1536-byte memcpy the reader also produces a min/max/avg spliced from two different frames. This driver is the only one of the twelve i2csensor_*.cpp with a cache mutex and zero SensorCacheGuard uses.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: builds with ENABLE_THERMAL_SENSOR only — I2C_FEATURE_LEVEL 3 (System_BuildConfig.h:433). The current FeatherS3 config is level 4 with CUSTOM_ENABLE_THERMAL 0 (:136), so this is NOT active on the shipping board. Note the file is uncommitted-modified in the working tree.
- **Direction:** The locked sibling 40 lines above is the model — wrap the human path's walk in the same lockThermalCache/unlockThermalCache pair, or simply have it call thermalBuildSummaryJSON and format from the returned scalars so there is one locked reader instead of two divergent ones.

### [LOW] HAL_Input reads gInputCache with no mutex while every other reader in the tree uses SensorCacheGuard
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/HAL_Input.cpp:204`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/HAL_Input.cpp:209, :215
- **What:** `inputGetJoyX/JoyY/Buttons` each do `if (!gInputConnected || !gInputCache.dataValid) return 0; return gInputCache.<field>;` with no lock. Every other consumer of this cache in the tree — OLED_SetupWizard.cpp:470/667/771/928/1070/1172, System_SetupWizard.cpp:1665, System_SensorLogging.cpp:663, WebPage_Sensors.cpp:230, OLED_Utils.cpp:5971 — takes `SensorCacheGuard(gInputCache.mutex, …)` first. The writer is `inputTask` (`gamepad_task`, I2C_SENSOR_CORE = core 1, i2csensor_seesaw.cpp:618-648).
- **Why it matters:** A caller that reads joyX then joyY then buttons through these three accessors can straddle two poll updates and act on a joystick position from one sample with buttons from the next. Each field is an aligned 32-bit scalar so there is no sub-word tearing, and the FeatherS3 callers are on the same core as the writer, so this is an inconsistency rather than a fault. It is worth noting mainly because it is the single documented-convention violation for this cache.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with an input device (FeatherS3: seesaw gamepad)
- **Direction:** Either bring these three in line with the SensorCacheGuard convention used by every other reader, or document at the HAL boundary that they are deliberately best-effort — the current silent divergence is the problem.

### [LOW] ToF data-ready wait is a tight I2C spin holding the bus mutex for up to 250 ms with no yield
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/i2csensor_vl53l4cx.cpp:404`
- **Also:** i2csensor_vl53l4cx.cpp:86-91
- **What:** Both ToF read paths poll data-ready with `do { status = gVL53L4CX->VL53L4CX_GetMeasurementDataReady(&NewDataReady); ... } while (!NewDataReady);` and no `vTaskDelay` in the body — only a `millis() - startTime > 250` bail and an error bail. Sites: :404-413 (tofPoll, called under `i2cTaskWithTimeout(..., 500, ...)` from tofTask) and :86-91 (the single-shot read under `i2cDeviceTransaction(..., 200, ...)`).
- **Why it matters:** For up to 250 ms the task issues back-to-back I2C register reads at 100-200 kHz with the bus mutex held, saturating both the bus and its CPU slice. Every other device on bus 0 — thermal, gps, imu, apds, presence, rtc, and the OLED framebuffer push (HAL_Display.cpp:173, 15 ms take timeout) — is blocked for the duration; the OLED simply drops that frame. It is bounded so it cannot trip a watchdog, and the timeout/error handling here is the correct pattern (it is the model the APDS path should follow), but the missing inter-poll delay is a pure busy-wait inside it.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: full-I2C builds only — ENABLE_TOF_SENSOR, 0 on the current FeatherS3 config
- **Direction:** A short vTaskDelay between data-ready reads would cut the bus and CPU load with no effect on the 250 ms ceiling; the sensor's timing budget is 200 ms so sub-millisecond polling buys nothing.


### Web / HTTP — 15 findings (6 critical/high)

### [HIGH] Four web status endpoints execute command handlers inline on the unpinned httpd task, racing cmd_exec_task on the single shared gDebugBuffer result buffer
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebPage_ESPNow.cpp:563`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebPage_Bluetooth.cpp:41 (callers :51, :73, :79); components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:2997 (callers :3131, :3132); components/hardwareone/WebPage_Bond.cpp:1344; shared buffers at components/hardwareone/System_Debug.h:257 and the gEspNow->listBuffer in cmd_espnow_list
- **What:** espnowRunInternal(), bleRunInternal() (WebPage_Bluetooth.cpp:41), runInternalStatusCmd() (WebServer_Server.cpp:2997) and WebPage_Bond.cpp:1344 all call executeCommand() DIRECTLY from inside an HTTP handler. executeCommand() takes no lock of any kind — the whole command layer is serialized only by the convention that cmd_exec_task is its sole caller (submitAndExecuteSync queues, HardwareOne.cpp:1553). Command handlers return pointers into gDebugBuffer, one 4 KB PSRAM scratch buffer shared by every cmd_* handler in the tree (System_Debug.h:257-272, System_Debug.cpp:57). cmd_espnow_status (System_ESPNow.cpp:10163) formats into it incrementally with `p += n; remaining -= n;` across many snprintf calls; cmd_espnow_list serializes into a second shared buffer, gEspNow->listBuffer.
- **Why it matters:** cmd_exec_task is TASK_PRIORITY_LOW = 1 on core 0 (HardwareOne.cpp:1553, System_TaskUtils.h:127). The httpd task is priority tskIDLE+5 with core_id = tskNO_AFFINITY (esp_http_server.h:56, never overridden — verified WebServer_Server.cpp:5211/5305). So the race does not even need dual-core: a browser poll preempts an in-flight command on core 0, and on core 1 it runs genuinely in parallel. Concretely: a dashboard polling GET /api/espnow/status runs EIGHT commands back-to-back (WebPage_ESPNow.cpp:574-590) while a serial/OLED/automation/ESP-NOW-remote command is mid-format in the same buffer. Result is a spliced response — the web client gets a fragment of the other caller's output and the other caller gets a fragment of the web's. Bounded (every writer self-caps at its own snprintf limit and NUL-terminates), so this is silent output corruption, not memory unsafety. GET /api/ble/status, /api/logging/status and the bond endpoints have the same shape.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER (FeatherS3 included); the ESP-NOW and bond variants additionally need ENABLE_ESPNOW; prior art: PARTIAL — docs/CMD_JSON_CONVERSION_PLAN.md:71 names the shared static return buffer as the "#1 concurrency hazard" but frames it as a prerequisite to be solved BEFORE a future JSON-conversion Wave 1. 
- **Direction:** The cheapest correct fix is to route these helpers through submitAndExecuteSync like /api/cli already does, so cmd_exec_task remains the only executor. If inline execution must stay for latency, the shared return buffer has to stop being shared — a per-caller buffer keyed off ctx.transport, or a mutex spanning handler-call plus copy-out.

### [HIGH] handleMapFeaturesAPI walks the map name table with no MapCacheGuard while unloadMap() frees it
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebPage_Maps.cpp:90`
- **Also:** None — this is the only unguarded map.names[] reader in the tree. Every other reader takes the lock: OLED_Mode_Map.cpp:385 drawFeatures, :1696 mapSearch, :1863 routesList.
- **What:** The handler captures `const LoadedMap& map = MapCore::getCurrentMap()` at :67 and then loops `for (int i = 0; i < map.nameCount; i++) { String name = String(map.names[i].name); ... }` at :85-93 holding no lock at all. `MapCore::unloadMap()` (System_Maps.cpp:795-854) frees `_currentMap.names` at :834-837 under `MapCacheGuard` and only zeroes `_currentMap.nameCount` at :850 — the pointer is retired before the count, so there is a guaranteed window where names==nullptr and nameCount>0.
- **Why it matters:** The httpd task is tskNO_AFFINITY (HTTPD_DEFAULT_CONFIG; neither httpd_start config in WebServer_Server.cpp:5206-5222 / :5287-5309 sets core_id). Load a map, open /maps in a browser so it polls /api/maps/features, then run `mapunload` or `mapload` from any surface: the command runs on cmd_exec_task (core 0) via System_Maps.cpp:2086/2103, and loadMapFile calls unloadMap first. The lens map page (G2_Glasses.cpp:17763, core 1), the OLED file browser (OLED_Mode_FileBrowser.cpp:796) and the OLED map picker (OLED_Mode_Map.cpp:1106/1404/1425) on loopTask, and deleting the loaded map file (System_Filesystem.cpp:1113) all reach the same free. The loop body calls the String constructor — an external call — so the compiler must reload `map.names` every iteration: it reads either nullptr+offset (LoadProhibited panic) or a freed block. sdkconfig:2055 sets CONFIG_HEAP_POISONING_DISABLED=y, so the freed block keeps its old bytes and is immediately reusable, meaning the non-crashing variant silently ships reused heap contents into the HTTP response.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with ENABLE_MAPS + ENABLE_WEB_MAPS — including FeatherS3 today (System_BuildConfig.h:92 CUSTOM_ENABLE_WEB_MAPS 1)
- **Direction:** The convention already exists one file away and even carries the explanation: OLED_Mode_Map.cpp:383-386 reads "Reads map.names[] throughout, which unloadMap() frees; hold the map lock so a concurrent unload/load can't pull the name table out mid-draw." The web handler needs the same MapCacheGuard, and separately unloadMap's store order (pointer before count) is backwards for any lock-free reader.

### [HIGH] SSE hold loop spins forever after a write failure once uptime passes 24.86 days (signed cast of a zeroed sentinel timestamp)
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Events.cpp:249`
- **Also:** Same `(long)(millis() - X)` idiom is used correctly elsewhere (G2_Health.cpp:384, G2_Glasses.cpp:8061/13423, G2_Page_Network.cpp:224/581/870/1033, i2csensor_mlx90640.cpp:648) — those all compare against a REAL timestamp, never a 0 sentinel, so they are fine. WebServer_Events.cpp:249 is the only site that overloads 0 as "exit now".
- **What:** The `/events` handler's notice-flush loop is `while ((long)(millis() - holdStart) < (long)holdMs)` with `holdMs = 600UL`. The ONLY way out of that loop on a socket error is the inner branches setting `holdStart = 0` (lines 257 and 279) so the condition evaluates false. That sentinel works only because `(long)(millis() - 0)` is normally a large positive number. `millis()` returns `unsigned long` (32-bit); cast to `long` it goes NEGATIVE once uptime exceeds 2^31 ms = 24.855 days. From then on `(long)millis() < 600` is TRUE, so zeroing `holdStart` no longer terminates the loop.
- **Why it matters:** Device up >24.86 days; a browser with an open SSE stream closes the tab (or the socket errors) while notices are queued -> `sseSendNotice`/`sseWrite` returns false -> `holdStart = 0` -> outer `while` condition is `(long)(negative) < 600` = true forever. `sseDequeueNotice`/`sseDequeueEvent` drain to empty within a couple of passes, after which the body is just `delay(60)` and loop. The httpd handler never returns, permanently consuming one httpd worker task (and the session slot). `delay()` yields, so the task WDT never fires — it is a silent wedge. Repeat occurrences exhaust the httpd worker pool and the web server stops accepting connections. Note the same sentinel also fails during the first 600 ms of uptime, but that window is unreachable in practice.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Replace the sentinel with an explicit loop-control bool (e.g. `bool sockDead = false;` set on write failure, tested in the `while` condition), and make the elapsed test the unsigned form `(uint32_t)(millis() - holdStart) < holdMs`. Do not keep using a timestamp value as a control flag.

### [HIGH] POST /api/cli allocates from attacker-controlled Content-Length with no cap and no null check → guaranteed NULL store → panic reboot
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:3194`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:4804 handleAdminDenyUser — same shape, also uncapped, also unchecked; its immediate sibling handleAdminApproveUser at :4764 DOES check `if (buf)`. Correct exemplars in the same tree: WebServer_Server.cpp:5033-5044 (cap 32768 + null check), WebServer_Server.cpp:2680-2692 (cap 4096 + null check), WebPage_Bond.cpp:1479-1489 (cap 32768), WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp:659-673 (cap 512 KB + PSRAM-then-DEFAULT fallback + null check).
- **What:** handleCLICommand does `std::unique_ptr<char,void(*)(void*)> buf((char*)ps_alloc(req->content_len + 1, AllocPref::PreferPSRAM, "http.cli.exec"), free);` with no bound on req->content_len and no check that the pointer is non-null. `req->content_len` is taken verbatim from the client's Content-Length header (ESP-IDF httpd_parse.c:371 assigns it with no cap; there is no Kconfig body limit). ps_alloc tries MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM and then falls back to plain malloc(); on failure it returns nullptr. Line 3204 then executes `buf.get()[received] = '\0'`.
- **Why it matters:** An authenticated non-guest client sends `POST /api/cli` with `Content-Length: 8000000` and a body. esp_http_server does not cap `content_len`, so `ps_alloc(8000001)` is attempted; with the LLM or ESP-NOW working set resident, PSRAM cannot satisfy it and internal DRAM certainly cannot, so `buf` is nullptr. The `if (req->content_len > 0)` guard at :3197 is true, so `httpd_req_recv(req, nullptr, ...)` runs and then `buf.get()[received] = '\0'` writes to address 0 → StoreProhibited panic and reboot. Even when the allocation succeeds it is an unbounded remote memory-exhaustion lever. The neighbouring `/api/cli/batch` handler gets this exactly right (`content_len == 0 || > 32768` reject at :5033 plus an `if (!buf)` OOM return at :5041), as do handleFileWrite (:1758-1766), handlePasswordChangePage (:2419-2428) and handleUserSettings (:2681-2689) — so this is a missed site, not a house style.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 3 finders; board: all boards built with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER=1; prior art: docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md:77-78 (cited at the same line). Re-verified: UNFIXED as of the current working tree.
- **Direction:** Apply the same two lines the sibling handlers already use: reject content_len outside a sane range before allocating, then bail with 500 if the allocation returns null. Copy the WebServer_Server.cpp:5033-5044 block verbatim rather than inventing a new shape.

### [HIGH] Turning off HTTP or WiFi from the web Settings page strands gDeferWrites=true, silently disabling settings persistence device-wide until a reboot
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:5101`
- **Also:** WebPage_Bond.cpp sends beginwrite/savesettings to a remote worker as fire-and-forget ESP-NOW commands; a dropped savesettings frame strands the worker the same way.
- **What:** The web Settings page always sends its saves as one batch: ['beginwrite', ...cmds, 'savesettings'] (WebPage_Settings.h:113). handleCliBatch executes them in order but breaks out of the loop the moment `server == NULL` (:5100-5101) and returns early (:5110). cmd_beginwrite sets gDeferWrites = true (System_Settings.cpp:3064) and only cmd_savesettings (:3070) clears it. There is no timeout, no scope guard and no other reset path.
- **Why it matters:** `httpEnabled` is a registered SETTING_BOOL with cmdKey "httpenabled" (System_WiFi.cpp:1867), rendered on the Settings page like any other toggle. Toggling it off sends `httpenabled 0` inside the batch. handleSettingCommand's SETTING_BOOL case (System_Settings.cpp:2665-2673) skips the write because gDeferWrites is set, then calls applyMasterEnableDisable("httpenabled") (:2670 -> :2550) which runs `closehttp`; cmd_httpstop (System_WiFi.cpp:1274-1276) does httpd_stop() and `server = NULL`. Control returns to handleCliBatch, which breaks before reaching `savesettings`. Result: (a) the toggle the user just made is never persisted and comes back on after reboot, and (b) gDeferWrites stays true for the rest of the boot, so EVERY later setSetting()/handleSettingCommand() on EVERY surface (serial, OLED, G2, BLE, ESP-NOW remote) is RAM-only and lost at reboot, while each command still reports success. `wifienabled 0` -> `closewifi` (System_WiFi.cpp:410-411) reaches the same state.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: KNOWN - docs/SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT.md §4.6 (rated MED, plan-only). Status unchanged; the cited line moved 4779 -> 5101. Reporting because the doc described the strand generically and did not identi
- **Direction:** Make the deferral scope-bound rather than a bare global - e.g. arm it inside handleCliBatch and clear it unconditionally on every exit path (including the `server == NULL` break), or give it a watchdog deadline after which it self-clears with a logged warning.

### [HIGH] `WebMirrorBuf::append`/`appendDirect` write the NUL terminator one byte past the allocation on an exact fit
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Utils.cpp:91`
- **Also:** WebServer_Utils.cpp:140 — `appendDirect` is a verbatim copy of the same logic with the same off-by-one.
- **What:** The trim loop is `while (len + need > cap) { drop a line }`, so it exits as soon as `len + addNL + slen <= cap`. The code then does `buf[len++]='\n'; memcpy(buf + len, s, slen); len += slen; buf[len] = '\0';` — with the final `len` allowed to equal `cap`. `init()` allocates exactly `cap` bytes (`buf = ps_alloc(cap, ...)`, WebServer_Utils.cpp:35), so `buf[cap]` is one byte past the block. The header comment on `cap` says "maximum bytes stored (excluding null)", which is the contract the allocation does not honour.
- **Why it matters:** `gWebMirror` is 8192 bytes in PSRAM and is appended to for every broadcast line, so it wraps continuously during any verbose session. Each wrap drops whole lines until the condition is satisfied, so `len + need == cap` lands exactly with probability roughly 1/average-line-length per wrap — a matter of minutes of logging, not a theoretical corner. The result is a one-byte 0x00 written into the ESP-IDF heap block header (or the poison word) immediately after the 8 KB PSRAM allocation, i.e. silent heap-metadata corruption that surfaces later as a free()/realloc failure far from here.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all (ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER)
- **Direction:** Either allocate `cap + 1` in `init()` (matching the documented "excluding null" contract) or make the trim loop condition `len + need >= cap`. `assignFrom` and `snapshotTo` already reserve the terminator correctly, so only the two append paths are wrong.

### [MEDIUM] Bonded peer-FS reply callback overwrites the entry array the web handler is mid-stream over
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebPage_Bond.cpp:1777`
- **Also:** webFsStatReplyCallback (:1791-1797) and webFsGetAckCallback (:1802-1808) have the same never-cleared-expectedReqId shape, though their payloads are single structs rather than an array streamed across blocking writes.
- **What:** `webFsListReplyCallback` (:1773-1787) runs on the ESP-NOW receive task, takes no lock (it cannot — sWebFsBridgeMutex is held by the httpd task for the whole request), and memcpy's straight into sWebFsBridge.hdr / .entries / .entryCount for any reply whose reqId matches. `sWebFsBridge.expectedReqId` is set at :1887 and never cleared. The handler then streams `sWebFsBridge.entries[i]` across up to 32 blocking httpd_resp_send_chunk calls at :1933-1961.
- **Why it matters:** Two concrete triggers, both from the code as written. (a) The protocol module times out at 5 s and synthesizes an IO_ERROR reply (comment at :1889-1891); the real reply arriving afterwards still matches the never-cleared expectedReqId and lands in the bridge — possibly while the handler is inside its chunk loop, splicing one directory's names into another's response. (b) Lines 1877-1880 clear received/entryCount/hdr *before* fsListSendRequest returns the new reqId at :1882 and expectedReqId is updated at :1887; a late reply for the previous request arriving in that send window passes the still-old expectedReqId, sets received=true and gives the semaphore, so the new request's 6 s wait returns immediately with the *previous* path's listing.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: ENABLE_BONDED_MODE + ENABLE_ESPNOW — System_BuildConfig.h:320 sets ENABLE_BONDED_MODE to 0, so NOT FeatherS3 today
- **Direction:** Clearing expectedReqId to 0 the moment the semaphore take returns closes both triggers, since the callback already rejects on mismatch. Copying the entries out into a request-local snapshot before the chunk loop would additionally remove the need for the callback and the streamer to agree on anything.

### [MEDIUM] gSettings String buffers handed raw to blocking chunked socket writes on the unpinned httpd task
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebPage_MQTT.cpp:107`
- **Also:** handleSettingsGet (WebServer_Server.cpp:2523) calls buildSettingsJsonDoc on the httpd task, which walks the same heap-backed String fields — the read-side twin of §4.5's save-side race.
- **What:** `httpd_resp_send_chunk(req, gSettings.mqttHost.c_str(), HTTPD_RESP_USE_STRLEN);` and the same shape at :129 (mqttCACertPath), :137 (mqttUser) and :153 (mqttBaseTopic). HTTPD_RESP_USE_STRLEN means httpd strlen()s the pointer and then writes from it; both happen on the raw Arduino String heap buffer, with no copy taken first. config.send_wait_timeout is 20 s (HTTPS) / 30 s (HTTP) (WebServer_Server.cpp:5211, :5305), so the pointer can be held live across seconds of blocked socket write.
- **Why it matters:** gSettings has no mutex (confirmed still true — see clean list), and Arduino String SSO is 14 bytes, so a real broker hostname, CA path, username or topic is always heap-allocated. Reassigning any of them frees the old buffer. The httpd task is tskNO_AFFINITY, so it is a genuine cross-core reader of state that cmd_exec_task (core 0, `mqtthost`/`mqttuser`/`mqtttopic`), the G2 hijack workers (which call setSetting directly per SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT §4.5), the OLED settings editor on loopTask, and automation-driven sub-commands all write. Because the httpd task blocks inside lwip send() while the chunk goes out, priority-1 cmd_exec_task gets scheduled during exactly that window even on the same core. The read then returns freed heap and that content is transmitted to the browser — a use-after-free whose payload is whatever the allocator has since put there, adjacent to the MQTT password field. This is the direct sibling of the confirmed WebPage_Maps handleGPSTracksAPI finding: capture a pointer, hold it across blocking chunked writes, let another task free it.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all builds with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER + ENABLE_MQTT; prior art: Adjacent to docs/SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT.md §4.5, which covers the save-time serializer rather than the web reader. Status check requested by the task: §4.5's core claim is UNCHANGED — grep for gSett
- **Direction:** Copy each String into a request-local char buffer (or a local String) before the chunk write, so the socket never holds a pointer into shared state. Every other handler in the tree already does this — WebPage_MQTT.cpp is the sole place the raw form survived.

### [MEDIUM] Per-session SSE notice and event rings are read-modify-written from loopTask, BTC_TASK and httpd with no synchronization
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Events.cpp:96`
- **Also:** handleEvents (WebServer_Events.cpp:176-291) additionally binds sessIdx once and then reads/writes gSessions[sessIdx] across a 600 ms hold loop with delay(60). Secondary exposure: setSession's copy-assignment `gSessions[idx] = s` (WebServer_Server.cpp:363) can realloc the sid/user/ip String buffers (verified via WString.cpp changeBuffer -> realloc) while a fan-out loop on another task is reading gSessions[i].user.c_str() at :155.
- **What:** `sseEnqueueEvent` (:80-114) and `sseEnqueueNotice` (WebServer_Server.cpp:4948-4970) memcpy/strncpy into `s.eventDataQ[eqTail]` / `s.noticeQueue[nqTail]` and read-modify-write eqHead/eqTail/eqCount and nqHead/nqTail/nqCount. `sseDequeueEvent` (:117-125) and `sseDequeueNotice` (WebServer_Server.cpp:4972-4979) RMW the same fields from the httpd task. No lock, no atomics, none of the fields are volatile.
- **Why it matters:** Producers run on at least two other tasks: loopTask/core 1 via systemEventsNotifyTick -> broadcastEventToSessionsIf (HardwareOne.cpp:2414, System_Notifications.cpp:849) and broadcastSensorStatusToAllSessions (HardwareOne.cpp:2374); and BTC_TASK/core 0 via ringPushStatusEvent -> blePushEvent -> broadcastEventToAllSessions (G2_Ring.cpp:866/1151/1283 -> BLE_Events.cpp:199). The consumer is the unpinned httpd task. A ring notification arriving while a browser polls /api/events means eqCount++ on one core races eqCount-- on the other: a lost decrement replays a stale slot as a duplicate toast, a lost increment drops a notification silently. In the drop-oldest branch the producer writes exactly the slot the consumer is constructing a String from, and the terminating NUL is stored only after the memcpy (:87-94), so a payload longer than the previous one leaves a window with no terminator inside the 128-byte slot and strlen walks into the adjacent queue entries. All indices are taken modulo capacity and the slots are fixed arrays inside the session struct, so this is lost/garbled notifications, not memory unsafety.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER
- **Direction:** A short portMUX around the index updates on both the enqueue and dequeue side is the minimum; the toast queue in OLED_UI.cpp already has that shape with sToastMux. Note the reset path is not the problem — `gSessions[i] = SessionEntry()` move-assigns from an empty temporary, which per WString.cpp:246-273 keeps the buffer and just sets len=0, so it does not free.

### [MEDIUM] `handleFileRead` ignores `httpd_resp_send_chunk` failures and keeps reading the whole file under the global FS mutex after the client has gone
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:1727`
- **Also:** WebServer_Server.cpp:1305 is the one site that captures the return (`esp_err_t r = httpd_resp_send_chunk(...)`); the other ~585 chunk sends across the web layer are unchecked, though most are short fixed strings where the consequence is only a wasted call
- **What:** `handleFileRead` (WebServer_Server.cpp:1635-1735) takes `FsLockGuard fsGuard("handleFileRead")` at :1700 and a `PollPauseGuard` earlier, then loops `while (true) { n = f.readBytes(buf, 512); if (n == 0) break; httpd_resp_send_chunk(req, buf, n); }` at :1719-1728. The chunk send's `esp_err_t` is discarded and there is no `break` on failure. Both guards live until the function returns.
- **Why it matters:** When the browser cancels a download or the socket dies, `httpd_resp_send_chunk` returns ESP_ERR_HTTPD_RESP_SEND and every later call returns immediately too — but the loop's exit condition is `readBytes() == 0`, so it reads the file to the end regardless. `path` can point at `/sd/...`, where files run to tens of megabytes; at 512 bytes per iteration that is a long stretch during which `gFsMutex` is held and sensor polling is paused. Everything that needs the FS blocks behind it: `command-audit.log` appends (i.e. every command on every surface), settings writes, the automation scheduler's `automations.json` rewrite on each fire, sensor logging. Any authenticated user can trigger it repeatedly by starting and aborting a download, and it is indistinguishable from a hang. Contrast `handleFileWrite` at :1863-1873, which does break on a short write — the pattern exists in the same file.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Break the loop when the chunk send returns anything but ESP_OK. The same one-line guard belongs in any other bounded-by-file-size streaming loop; the short static-HTML sends are noise and can stay as they are.

### [MEDIUM] httpd is the only unpinned task in the system, and the policy's "documented choice" requirement is unmet
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:5286`
- **Also:** Adjacent and more severe, but belonging to P10/P11 (track A1) — noted only because it is in the same two config blocks: the comments at :5290-:5293 and :5207-:5210 assert httpd_config_t.stack_size is in WORDS ("11059 words = 44 KB", "7680 words = 30 KB"). It is BYTES — httpd_main.c:531 passes it unscaled to xTaskCreatePinnedToCore. The HTTP task really gets 7,680 B against the same comment's stated ~18 KB measured peak.
- **What:** HTTPD_DEFAULT_CONFIG() sets .core_id = tskNO_AFFINITY (esp-idf/components/esp_http_server/include/esp_http_server.h:56) and .task_priority = tskIDLE_PRIORITY+5. Neither the HTTPS branch (:5202) nor the HTTP branch (:5286) overrides core_id — grep for "core_id" in WebServer_Server.cpp returns nothing. httpd_main.c:530-534 passes config.core_id straight to xTaskCreatePinnedToCore. Every one of the 41 firmware xTaskCreate* sites passes an explicit core; this IDF-created task is the sole exception. System_TaskUtils.h:165-166 permits tskNO_AFFINITY "ONLY for short-lived workers that touch no shared bus and aren't latency-sensitive, AND only as a documented choice" — httpd is none of those and there is no note at either site.
- **Why it matters:** System_TaskUtils.h:165-166 permits tskNO_AFFINITY "ONLY for short-lived workers that touch no shared bus and aren't latency-sensitive, AND only as a *documented* choice". The httpd task is long-lived, is priority 5, and takes gFsMutex with portMAX_DELAY on essentially every request (file listing, /api/files/view, map tiles). When the scheduler places it on core 1 it preempts every I2C sensor poller (prio 1, pinned to I2C_SENSOR_CORE=1) and the g2 page/tap/session workers (prio 2) for the whole duration of a request — which is exactly the mid-transaction starvation the core-split policy's "hard rule" was written to prevent after the FeatherS3 out-and-about crash loop. Neither call site carries the documentation the policy requires, so the placement was never actually decided.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: All boards with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER
- **Direction:** Set config.core_id explicitly at both sites — PRO_CORE per the policy's "I/O belongs with the radio it serves" rule — or, if floating really is wanted, add the documented-choice comment the policy demands. Worth deciding together with task_priority: 5 puts a web request above every I2C poller on whichever core it lands.

### [MEDIUM] Per-session SSE notice/event rings are an unsynchronized multi-producer queue drained by the unpinned httpd task
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:4948`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebServer_Events.cpp:80 and :117 (event ring); producers at G2_Ring.cpp:921, G2_Glasses.cpp:13628, System_Notifications.cpp:854, WebServer_Server.cpp:1091
- **What:** sseEnqueueNotice() (:4948-4970) and sseDequeueNotice() (:4972-4979) read-modify-write s.nqHead, s.nqTail and s.nqCount — plain non-volatile ints in SessionEntry (WebServer_Server.h:75-89) — with no synchronization. sseEnqueueEvent()/sseDequeueEvent() (WebServer_Events.cpp:80-125) do the same for eqHead/eqTail/eqCount. The enqueue side also memcpy's up to 96 bytes (NOTICE_MAX_LEN) or 128+16 bytes (EVENT_DATA_MAX/EVENT_NAME_MAX) into the slot the dequeue side may be copying out of.
- **Why it matters:** The consumer is handleEvents() on the httpd task, which is tskNO_AFFINITY (WebServer_Events.cpp:249-291, draining in a 600 ms hold loop). The producers are on other tasks and other cores: ringPushStatusEvent → blePushEvent → broadcastEventToAllSessions runs from G2_Ring.cpp:921 inside onDisconnect on BTC_TASK (core 0); G2_Glasses.cpp:13628 from the G2 workers on core 1; System_Notifications.cpp:854 from the notification tick on loopTask (core 1); enqueueTargetedRevokeForSessionIdx (WebServer_Server.cpp:1091) from user-admin commands on cmd_exec_task (core 0). The queue depths make this the common path rather than a corner: NOTICE_QUEUE_SIZE is 2 and EVENT_QUEUE_SIZE is 4, so any burst immediately enters the drop-oldest branch, which advances s.nqHead / s.eqHead — the consumer's own field — from the producer's core. Concretely: walk out of BLE range with the web dashboard open. Core 0's onDisconnect does eqCount++ while the httpd task does eqCount--; a lost decrement replays a stale slot as a duplicate SSE event, a lost increment strands the notice and leaves needsNotificationTick set, which pins the browser's SSE `retry:` hint at 1000 ms (WebServer_Events.cpp:190) — a permanent 1 Hz reconnect storm against the single httpd task. This is the same shape A8 filed as the OLED banner queue, with a much larger producer set.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER; the BLE-origin producers need ENABLE_BLUETOOTH — both true on FeatherS3
- **Direction:** Same remedy the toast queue in OLED_UI.cpp already uses — a short portMUX around both the enqueue and the dequeue index updates. The copy-out in sseDequeue* returns a String built from the slot, so the lock can cover the whole slot read without holding across any socket I/O.

### [LOW] A second family of HTTP-path ps_alloc results is dereferenced without a null check (bounded sizes, so lower blast radius than /api/cli)
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Utils.cpp:239`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:3492 (/login POST body, capped 2048 but unchecked), components/hardwareone/HardwareOne.cpp:590 ("http.query" buffer, unchecked).
- **What:** getHeaderValue does `std::unique_ptr<char,void(*)(void*)> buf((char*)ps_alloc(len + 1, ...), free);` then immediately `httpd_req_get_hdr_value_str(req, name, buf.get(), len + 1)`. IDF's implementation (esp-idf/components/esp_http_server/src/httpd_parse.c:1059-1112) validates only `r` and `field`, never `val`, and reaches `strlcpy(val, val_ptr, val_size)` with val==NULL.
- **Why it matters:** On allocation failure the httpd task stores to address 0 and panics. Sizes here are bounded (header length is capped by CONFIG_HTTPD_MAX_REQ_HDR_LEN, query length by the URI limit, the /login body by an explicit 2048 cap), so this requires genuine total exhaustion of both PSRAM and the internal heap rather than an attacker-chosen size — which is why it ranks LOW while the /api/cli site ranks HIGH. It is still an unhandled internal-allocation failure on the request path of a device whose internal heap routinely sits below its own 40,960 B warning threshold.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER)
- **Direction:** Add `if (!buf) return false;` (or the endpoint's 500 path) immediately after each construction — the same one-liner the neighbouring handlers at WebServer_Server.cpp:2688, :3789 and :4765 already have.

### [NOTE] handleCameraStream's takeover machinery cannot function and its infinite loop wedges the single httpd worker
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebPage_Sensors.cpp:760`
- **What:** The handler implements a single-stream lock with `s_streamOwner`, `s_streamLastBeat` and a generation counter `s_streamGen` (:723-745), then enters `while (true)` at :760 which exits only on client disconnect, camera stop, or `myGen != s_streamGen`. The generation check assumes a second invocation of this handler can run concurrently and bump the counter.
- **Why it matters:** ESP-IDF's httpd runs exactly one worker task — HTTPD_DEFAULT_CONFIG, and neither httpd_start config in WebServer_Server.cpp (:5206-5222 HTTPS, :5287-5309 HTTP) changes the worker model. The codebase states this itself at WebServer_Server.cpp:274-277: "all handlers run sequentially on httpd's worker task — max_open_sockets controls concurrent TCP connections but not concurrent handler execution." So s_streamGen can never be bumped by another request while this loop runs, the takeover and stale-owner logic is unreachable, and the loop holds the only httpd worker for the entire MJPEG connection — /api/cli, the SSE stream and every page are unserviceable until the client goes away.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: ENABLE_CAMERA_SENSOR builds only — 0 on FeatherS3 (System_BuildConfig.h:183-187)
- **Direction:** Not a P21/P22 data race, filed because it falls out of the same concurrency-model question this audit was scoped around: a handler that does not return is an availability bug on a single-worker server, and the lock it carries is dead code built on the opposite assumption. Bounding the loop (as the removed 300-frame cap did) or driving frames from a separate task would resolve both halves.

### [NOTE] handleCameraStream parks the process's only httpd task in an unbounded while(true), making the entire web server unreachable for the duration of an MJPEG stream
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebPage_Sensors.cpp:760`
- **What:** handleCameraStream enters `while (true)` and streams multipart JPEG frames until the client disconnects or a newer stream bumps s_streamGen. There is exactly one httpd task in the process (esp_http_server creates a single thread — verified at esp-idf/components/esp_http_server/src/httpd_main.c:530; the restore-only server in WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp reuses the same global handle and only runs in first-time-setup mode).
- **Why it matters:** This is not a data race, but it is the direct structural consequence of the single-httpd-task model that the rest of this audit relies on. While an MJPEG client is attached, no other HTTP request can be serviced at all — not /api/events (the SSE channel every page holds open), not /api/cli, not the login page. The stream self-heals only via the 5 s staleness window at :734 or client disconnect. Worth recording because the same single-task property is what makes the handler-vs-handler statics in this tree safe, so anyone tempted to fix this by enabling async handlers would invalidate that safety for gWebMessagesBuf, the EXT_RAM_BSS_ATTR static response buffers, and s_streamOwner simultaneously.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: builds with ENABLE_CAMERA_SENSOR — XIAO ESP32S3 Sense only (System_BuildConfig.h:184). Not FeatherS3.
- **Direction:** No correctness fix is required. If concurrency is ever wanted here, the async-handler route must be paired with making the per-handler statics request-local first — otherwise it converts a liveness limitation into a set of memory-safety bugs.


### Other — 14 findings (5 critical/high)

### [HIGH] The base (non-Sense) XIAO board block is unreachable — every ESP32-S3 build force-defines XIAO_ESP32S3_SENSE_ENABLED, so a plain XIAO gets the Sense pin map
- **Where:** `CMakeLists.txt:236`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/CMakeLists.txt:453 (second, redundant -D), components/hardwareone/System_BuildConfig.h:176 (the header #define the comment calls 'the only thing that tells the board chain'), :813 (Sense block), :854 (dead base block), :184 / :195 (ENABLE_CAMERA_SENSOR / ENABLE_MICROPHONE_SENSOR keyed off the same macro)
- **What:** Root CMakeLists.txt:235-237 does `if(CONFIG_IDF_TARGET_ESP32S3) add_compile_definitions(XIAO_ESP32S3_SENSE_ENABLED)` — unconditional for the whole S3 family, not per-board. components/hardwareone/CMakeLists.txt:452-455 adds the same -D again. Because boards/xiao_s3.defaults sets CONFIG_ARDUINO_VARIANT="XIAO_ESP32S3" (which becomes -DARDUINO_XIAO_ESP32S3_DEV via components/arduino/CMakeLists.txt:424), the Sense branch at System_BuildConfig.h:813 always matches and the base-XIAO branch at :854 can never be selected by any build this build system produces.
- **Why it matters:** Flashing a plain XIAO ESP32-S3 with `HW_BOARD=xiao_s3` silently applies the expansion-board pin map: SD_CS=21, SD_SCK=7, SD_MISO=8, SD_MOSI=9, MIC 42/41, CAMERA_AVAILABLE=1, ENABLE_CAMERA_SENSOR=1, ENABLE_MICROPHONE_SENSOR=1, USER_LED_PIN=-1. System_VFS.cpp tryMountSD() runs at boot and calls SPI.begin(7, 8, 9, 21) plus pinMode/digitalWrite on GPIO21 — GPIO21 is the base XIAO's on-board user LED and 7/8/9 are header pins D8/D9/D10, so boot stomps whatever the user has wired there. Worse, the header comment at System_BuildConfig.h:166-176 tells a maintainer this #define is the switch between base and Sense; deleting or zeroing line 176 changes nothing because the two CMake -D's still define it. (The same comment's claim that it is inert on FeatherS3 IS correct — every use site is nested under ARDUINO_XIAO_ESP32S3_DEV — so the primary board is not affected.)
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: XIAO ESP32-S3 (stretch goal). FeatherS3 unaffected.
- **Direction:** Remove the two CMake -D's (or gate them on HW_BOARD, not on CONFIG_IDF_TARGET_ESP32S3) and leave System_BuildConfig.h:176 as the single switch; better, add a separate boards/xiao_s3_sense.defaults so base vs Sense is chosen by HW_BOARD like every other board.

### [HIGH] Arduino `File::flush()` and `File::close()` are `void` — every fflush/fsync/fclose error in the tree is structurally undetectable, so no writer can see a full filesystem
- **Where:** `components/arduino/libraries/FS/src/vfs_api.cpp:352`
- **Also:** components/arduino/libraries/FS/src/vfs_api.cpp:294 (close); components/arduino/libraries/FS/src/FS.h:57,:70; components/hardwareone/System_VFS.cpp (all open paths)
- **What:** `VFSFileImpl::flush()` (vfs_api.cpp:352) calls `fflush(_f)` and `fsync(fileno(_f))` and returns `void`. `VFSFileImpl::close()` (:294) calls `fclose(_f)` and returns `void`. `File::flush()`/`File::close()` (FS.h:57/:70) mirror that. There is no raw `fopen`/`fwrite`/`fclose`/`fsync`/`fileno` anywhere in components/hardwareone — I grepped the whole component and every one of the ~59 write-mode opens goes through `VFS::open`/`openGuarded` → Arduino `File`. So the only error signal available to any writer in this codebase is the byte count returned by `write()`/`print()`.
- **Why it matters:** That byte count is a *buffered-stdio* count, not a flash-level one. `fopen`'d streams are buffered by newlib, and underneath, esp_littlefs is configured with `CONFIG_LITTLEFS_CACHE_SIZE=512` (sdkconfig:2870). littlefs only calls `lfs_alloc` when a cache/block boundary is crossed, so for any write whose tail sits inside the last 512 bytes — which is *every* file's tail, and the *entire* file for anything under 512 B such as `/system/boot_anchors.json` or `/system/.pending_credential_setup` — `LFS_ERR_NOSPC` surfaces at `lfs_file_sync`, i.e. inside `fsync()`/`fclose()`. Both are swallowed. Concrete failure: LittleFS reaches 100% (uncapped `/logging_captures/photos`, `/logging_captures/tracks`, and the `/battery.csv` root append are all unbounded); a caller opens a state file "w", writes 400 bytes, gets 400 back, calls `flush()` and `close()` — both void — and every success check in the codebase passes while zero bytes reached flash. On the `sr_8mb` layout (128 KB LittleFS against a 100 KB overflow reserve) this state is reachable within minutes of boot.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** This is the substrate under findings 2–5 and 8–14 — fix it once rather than at each call site. Add a checked-commit helper in `System_VFS` (e.g. `VFS::closeChecked(File&)`) that reaches the underlying `FILE*` to run `fflush`/`fsync`/`fclose` and propagate their return, and route the ~10 state-file writers (`writeText`, `writeSettingsJson`, `writeIdentityFile`, `saveMeshPeers`, `saveEspNowDevices`, `saveIpBans`, `WaypointManager::save`) through it. Anything short of that leaves the whole P39 class undetectable no matter how many `write()` counts are checked.

### [HIGH] A failed LittleFS commit is undetectable by any writer: File::flush() returns void and File::close() discards fclose(), so ENOSPC never reaches the caller
- **Where:** `components/arduino/libraries/FS/src/vfs_api.cpp:304`
- **Also:** System_Utils.cpp:812 writeText; System_Settings.cpp:1072/1160 writeSettingsJson/writeDebugJson; System_ESPNow_Identity.cpp:110/406; System_User.cpp:1327; WebServer_Server.cpp:963; System_ImageManager.cpp:226-276; System_SensorLogging.cpp:915
- **What:** `VFSFileImpl::close()` (:294) does `fclose(_f); _f = NULL;` and discards the return; `VFSFileImpl::flush()` (:352) does `fflush()` + `fsync(fileno(_f))` and returns void. `File::close()`/`File::flush()` in FS.h are both `void`. Underneath, `vfs_littlefs_close` (managed_components/joltwallet__littlefs/src/esp_littlefs.c:1751-1790) calls `lfs_file_close`, which performs the deferred `lfs_file_sync` and can return LFS_ERR_NOSPC → errno + -1 → fclose returns EOF. sdkconfig has CONFIG_LITTLEFS_CACHE_SIZE=512 and CONFIG_LITTLEFS_FLUSH_FILE_EVERY_WRITE unset (sdkconfig:2870, 2878), so any write smaller than 512 bytes never touches flash until close.
- **Why it matters:** On a full LittleFS, `writeText` (System_Utils.cpp:812-826) opens the tmp, `f.print(in)` returns the full length from the stdio/lfs cache, `f.flush()` swallows the ENOSPC from fsync, `f.close()` swallows it from fclose, and the function returns true. `writeTextAtomic` then renames a truncated or empty tmp over the live file — users.json, pending_users.json, automations.json, boot_anchors.json, notifications.json, pet.json. The same hole defeats the byte-count checks that look careful: `serializeJson(doc, file)` in writeSettingsJson (System_Settings.cpp:1072) counts what Print::write returned, which is the cache-accepted count, so `bytesWritten == measureJson` even when nothing reached flash. The prescription in SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT §4.7 ("check bytesWritten < measureJson") therefore would NOT fix this.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: Adjacent to SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT.md §4.7 (weak success check) and recon_persist.md #2 (CountingDecorator), but the fsync/fclose-swallowing mechanism — which makes the byte-count fix insufficient —
- **Direction:** Add a checked-commit helper that calls fflush+fsync+fclose through the raw descriptor and reports failure, and route every state-file writer through it before the rename. Until then, treat the 100 KB overflow reserve as the only real defence and make sure it is actually preserved (see the log-cap overcommit finding).

### [HIGH] Nothing in the system can recover a hung task: TWDT panic is off with zero firmware subscribers, and 14 boot-path FATAL handlers wedge forever
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/HardwareOne.cpp:1312`
- **Also:** System_Debug.cpp:608,619,630,651,673; System_I2C_Manager.cpp:63,72,80; HardwareOne.cpp:1327,1490,1505,1516,1613,1626,1639
- **What:** The boot path answers every unrecoverable init failure with `while (1) delay(1000);` — HardwareOne.cpp:1312 (filesystem), :1327 (WiFi array alloc), :1490 (automation init), :1505 (cmd queue), :1516 (cmd task), :1613 (sensor queue task), :1626 (sessions alloc), :1639 (logout-reasons alloc); System_Debug.cpp:608/619/630/651/673; System_I2C_Manager.cpp:63/72/80. No watchdog can end that state. sdkconfig:1793-1798 sets `CONFIG_ESP_TASK_WDT_EN/INIT=y`, `TIMEOUT_S=5`, idle CPU0+CPU1 subscribed, but `# CONFIG_ESP_TASK_WDT_PANIC is not set` — I traced IDF `task_wdt.c:495-509`, which reads `panic` from config and, when false, only prints backtraces and returns. `esp_task_wdt_add/_reset/_delete` appear 0 times across components/hardwareone and main, so IDLE0/IDLE1 are the only subscribers. The RTC watchdog the bootloader arms for 9000 ms (sdkconfig:477-479) is disabled by IDF before app_main ever runs (`esp_system/startup_funcs.c:177-186`, ESP_SYSTEM_INIT_FN(init_disable_rtc_wdt, SECONDARY, 999), which `startup.c:216` runs before `esp_startup_start_app()`). And `delay(1000)` is `vTaskDelay`, so IDLE keeps running and even the TWDT warning never prints; the INT WDT (1500 ms) cannot fire because interrupts stay enabled.
- **Why it matters:** Concrete path: `initAutomationSystem()` (HardwareOne.cpp:1488) returns false when `ensureAutomationsCache()` (System_Automation.cpp:519-530) fails its `heap_caps_calloc(64, sizeof(AutomationsCacheEntry), MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL | MALLOC_CAP_8BIT)` — roughly 1.5 KB of internal DRAM, on a project whose whole audit history is internal-DRAM pressure. A transient shortage there permanently bricks the device over an optional feature that the same if/else already knows how to skip. Four of the wedges (System_I2C_Manager.cpp:63/72/80 and HardwareOne.cpp:1312/1327) fire before `oledEarlyInit()` at :1603, so the panel never lights and there is no LED, no reboot, and no console line beyond a single `Serial.println`. From outside the device is indistinguishable from dead hardware. A power cycle re-runs the identical path.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (sdkconfig.esp32:1396-1401 and :296-298 are identical)
- **Direction:** Separate the truly-fatal cases (filesystem, command executor) from the optional ones (automation, sessions, logout-reasons, debug pool) and let the optional ones log-and-continue with the feature off. For the genuinely fatal ones, prefer a bounded delay then `esp_restart()` over an unbounded park — and consider whether a real TWDT subscription (or `CONFIG_ESP_TASK_WDT_PANIC`) is wanted now that no task subscribes at all.

### [HIGH] Sixteen boot-path FATAL paths park the device in `while (1) delay(1000)` forever, and no watchdog exists that can reset it
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/HardwareOne.cpp:1339`
- **Also:** HardwareOne.cpp:1354, :1529, :1544, :1555, :1654, :1667, :1680; System_Debug.cpp:608, :619, :630, :651, :673; System_I2C_Manager.cpp:63, :72, :80
- **What:** Every FATAL init failure in setup() prints one line and then executes `while (1) delay(1000);` (HardwareOne.cpp:1654 uses the equivalent `while (1) vTaskDelay(...)`). Arduino's `delay()` is `vTaskDelay(ms/portTICK_PERIOD_MS)` (esp32-hal-misc.c:212-214), so the `main` task yields on every iteration and IDLE0 keeps running. The Task WDT has only IDLE0/IDLE1 subscribed (CONFIG_ESP_TASK_WDT_CHECK_IDLE_TASK_CPU0/1=y) and CONFIG_ESP_TASK_WDT_PANIC is unset, so it is both un-tripped and un-lethal. The bootloader RTC WDT (CONFIG_BOOTLOADER_WDT_ENABLE=y, 9000 ms) has already been disabled by IDF's `init_disable_rtc_wdt` (esp-idf/components/esp_system/startup_funcs.c:177-186, SECONDARY/prio 999) inside `do_secondary_init()`, which runs before `esp_startup_start_app()` creates the main task. Firmware never re-arms it: grep for `esp_task_wdt_*`, `rtc_wdt`, `wdt_hal`, `enableLoopWDT`, `disableCore0WDT` across components/hardwareone and main returns zero matches.
- **Why it matters:** Flash wear or a data-partition fault that survives LittleFS's auto-format makes `initFilesystem()` return false (System_Filesystem.cpp:63-68), and HardwareOne.cpp:1337-1340 then wedges at boot phase 2. `oledEarlyInit()` (:1644) and `initNeoPixelLED()` (:1568) have not run yet, so the panel is blank and the LED dark; WiFi, ESP-NOW, BLE and the serial CLI never start. The device draws power, displays nothing, never reboots, and — because there is no panic — leaves no crash record either: `crashRecordSetPhase()` writes the phase byte to RTC, but a hang never reboots, so `crashRecordBootConsume()` is never reached to report it. To an operator the unit is indistinguishable from dead hardware, and it will fail identically on every power cycle. The same shape applies to the eight allocation-failure wedges (debug buffer/pool/queues, WiFi network array, sessions array, logout-reasons array, cmd-exec queue) and the three I2C-manager mutex wedges.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: Partially — docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §5.1 documents that the TWDT is inert for hangs in general, and §5.2/§5.3 discuss crash-loop breakers. Neither enumerates these sixteen deliberate wedge sit
- **Direction:** These are the strongest candidates for `esp_restart()` instead of an infinite park: a reboot at least leaves an ESP_RST_SW trail and gives the next boot a chance. If a hang is genuinely preferred for the alloc-failure cases, the ones that fire before `oledEarlyInit()` should at minimum leave a distinguishable RTC marker or drive the LED, so a field unit is not silent. Worth adding a line to System_CrashRecord.h's "WHAT IT IS NOT" section stating that a wedge is the one boot failure class the module structurally cannot see.

### [MEDIUM] cmd_exec_task is the lowest-priority task on core 0 while its own producer, espnow_task, sits four priorities above it on the same core
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/HardwareOne.cpp:1514`
- **Also:** The inverse case is already solved and documented at HardwareOne.cpp:1508-1513 — cmd_exec was moved off core 1 specifically because llm_gen at priority 2 was preempting it. The core-0 producer/consumer inversion was not considered in that move.
- **What:** cmd_exec_task is created at TASK_PRIORITY_LOW (=1) pinned to core 0; debug_out is also priority 1 on core 0 (System_Debug.cpp:660). On that same core sit espnow_task at TASK_PRIORITY_HIGH (=5, System_ESPNow.cpp:9404), espnow_tx at 5 (System_ESPNow_Tx.cpp:156), sr_snip_wr at 3 (System_ESPSR.cpp:1348), plus the IDF wifi task (23), BT controller (23), esp_timer (22), sys_evt (20) and BTC/BTU. espnow_task explicitly defers its heavy work — Ed25519/X25519 session crypto and ESP-NOW file-slot flash finalisation — down to cmd_exec_task via submitDeferredToCmdExec (System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:1011, :1030, :1343; System_ESPNow.cpp:4295), precisely because cmd_exec has the deeper stack. espnow_task's loop is `processMeshHeartbeats(); vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(10));` (System_ESPNow.cpp:9385-9391), and processMeshHeartbeats spans ~960 lines including the entire RX-ring drain.
- **Why it matters:** A producer at priority 5 hands work to a consumer at priority 1 on the same core. cmd_exec_task can only run inside espnow_task's 10 ms delay window, minus whatever the priority-23 WiFi task and the BLE stack take. Under sustained ESP-NOW RX (the documented ~9.2 KB/s file streaming) each processMeshHeartbeats pass grows, so the deferred crypto and the deferred flash writes that the transfer itself depends on get the least CPU exactly when they are most needed — and every serial, web, MQTT, OLED and BLE command result queues behind them. gCmdExecQ is only 6 deep (HardwareOne.cpp:1502) and submitCommandAsync uses a 0-tick send, so an automation dispatching a multi-command list in that window drops sub-commands (handled and logged at System_Automation.cpp:116-145, but still dropped).
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: All boards with ENABLE_ESPNOW
- **Direction:** Either raise cmd_exec_task to TASK_PRIORITY_NORMAL so it is not beneath its own producer, or lower espnow_task from HIGH to NORMAL — its 10 ms poll is not the real-time path, the ESP-NOW RX callback is. Measure with `perftop` (IDLE0) under a live file transfer before and after, as the policy header prescribes.

### [MEDIUM] No crash-loop backoff, and the RTC crash counter is blind to exactly the reset reasons a bootloop produces
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/HardwareOne.cpp:1291`
- **Also:** HardwareOne.cpp:1420-1422 (the divergent reset-reason set)
- **What:** `HardwareOne.cpp:1284-1296` increments `rtcCrashCount` only for `ESP_RST_TASK_WDT | ESP_RST_INT_WDT | ESP_RST_PANIC | ESP_RST_BROWNOUT`. Twenty lines later the SYSEVT_CRASH block (:1420-1422) treats a wider set as crashes: it adds `ESP_RST_WDT`, `ESP_RST_CPU_LOCKUP` and `ESP_RST_PWR_GLITCH`. Those three are real members of `esp_reset_reason_t` (esp-idf/components/esp_system/include/esp_system.h:32,39,40) and `ESP_RST_WDT` is specifically the RTC/other-watchdog reset. Separately, `rtcCrashCount` is never consumed by any control flow: its only readers are display sites (System_Utils.cpp:1575, :1822, :2538, :2549) and the settings mirror at :1380. No boot autostart is gated on it and there is no degraded/safe-boot mode.
- **Why it matters:** A deterministic panic inside any boot autostart step repeats forever with nothing counting or breaking the cycle. `hardwareone_setup()` starts WiFi (:1703), BLE/G2 client + boot reconnect (:1835-1841), sensors (:1873), camera (:1879), mic/SR (:1892-1902), HTTP (:1913), LLM load (:1931), MQTT (:1942) and ESP-NOW (:2088) — every one of them from persisted `*AutoStart` flags. If one of those faults on a given device/environment, each reboot re-runs it identically. The counter that would let the boot path notice is (a) never read and (b) does not even increment on `ESP_RST_WDT` or `ESP_RST_CPU_LOCKUP`, so a device stuck in a watchdog bootloop reports crashCount=0. The RAM-flush overlay right next door (`ramFlushConsumeOverlay()`, :1436) carries an explicit comment about invalidating before applying "so a panic while restoring can't replay the same state into a boot loop" — the persistent autostart flags never got the equivalent treatment.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** At minimum align the counter's reset-reason set with the SYSEVT_CRASH block so a watchdog bootloop is visible. Then consider having the boot path consult rtcCrashCount to skip the optional autostarts (camera/LLM/SR/ESP-NOW/MQTT) above some threshold, which turns a brick into a reachable device.

### [MEDIUM] setCpuFrequencyDrained()'s 2 s bus-mutex allowance can be exceeded by legitimate holders, after which it switches the CPU clock unguarded
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/HardwareOne.cpp:2177`
- **Also:** HardwareOne.cpp:2207 (wake path), :2286 (idle-enter path)
- **What:** `setCpuFrequencyDrained()` takes each I2C bus mutex with `xSemaphoreTake(m, pdMS_TO_TICKS(2000))` and records success in `held[b]`; if the take times out it leaves `held[b]` null and calls `setCpuFrequencyMhz(mhz)` anyway (:2180). The comment at :2160-2169 justifies this: "The 2 s take outlasts the worst legitimate holder; on timeout the bus is already wedged (bus-recovery territory), so switch anyway."
- **Why it matters:** That premise does not hold on a full-I2C build. On bus 0 the thermal poll is granted a 1500 ms transaction timeout (i2csensor_mlx90640.cpp:1526, "Thermal frame read takes 400-800ms at 100kHz; needs generous timeout") and the ToF poll 500 ms (i2csensor_vl53l4cx.cpp:763), plus gps/imu/apds/presence/rtc/fm each holding their own. All the sensor pollers run at TASK_PRIORITY_LOW, the same priority, so a waiter is not jumped to the front: one in-flight 1500 ms thermal read with a ToF read already queued exceeds 2000 ms with the bus perfectly healthy. The function then does the exact thing it exists to prevent — switch the CPU clock while a task is mid-I2C-transaction — which the comment at :2281-2285 records as the cause of "reset=panic(4) every ~10 min ... the whole 2026-07-17 crash-loop day". It fires from powerSaveTick's idle-enter and wake paths, i.e. unattended.
- **Confidence:** PLAUSIBLE — board: full-I2C builds (thermal + ToF compiled); harmless on the current FeatherS3 config where both are 0 and only the gamepad/OLED are on the bus
- **Direction:** Either raise the allowance above the sum of the longest per-device timeouts on that bus, or treat a failed take as "do not switch this cycle, retry next tick" rather than proceeding — the anti-flap cooldown at :2264 already makes deferring cheap. Reasoning from the actual per-device timeout table would settle which.

### [MEDIUM] The main task's serial/OLED command path parks the whole loop for up to 62 seconds, freezing every loop-driven service
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/HardwareOne.cpp:2646`
- **Also:** System_Utils.cpp:4906 (2 s enqueue) and :4922 (60 s wait); OLED_Utils.cpp:3010, :3022 (same call from the OLED menu)
- **What:** `hardwareone_loop()` runs on the IDF `main` task — priority 1, core 0, 8192 B (CONFIG_ESP_MAIN_TASK_AFFINITY_CPU0=y, ESP_TASK_MAIN_PRIO = ESP_TASK_PRIO_MIN+1), because CONFIG_AUTOSTART_ARDUINO is unset and the project supplies its own `app_main` (main/hardwareone-idf.cpp:26-56). Its serial drain calls `submitAndExecuteSync`, which blocks on `xQueueSend(..., pdMS_TO_TICKS(2000))` and then `xSemaphoreTake(r->done, pdMS_TO_TICKS(60000))` while `cmd_exec_task` runs the command.
- **Why it matters:** For that window nothing else in the loop body executes: `oledUpdate()`, `processOLEDBootSequence()`, `powerSaveTick()`, `localDisplaySessionTick()`, `ledEffectTick()`, the automation tick, `systemEventsNotifyTick()`, `systemEventLogTick()`, `wifiEventLogDrain()`, `mqttTick()`, `bleUpdateStreams()`, `sensorLogTick()`, `timeAnchorsTick()`, `batteryLogTick()`. A command that legitimately runs long — the ~12 s mesh-passphrase PBKDF2 called out at System_ESPNow.cpp:12950, or an ESP-NOW file send at the HW-measured ~9.2 KB/s, where a 500 KB file is ~54 s — freezes the display and the power-save/idle-logout timers for its whole duration. If `cmd_exec_task` itself deadlocks, every typed line costs a further full 60 s of frozen UI, and with TWDT PANIC off nothing breaks that. The identical path is reached from the OLED menu (OLED_Utils.cpp:3010), so a menu action can freeze the screen the user is looking at.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: The 60 s / 2 s values are documented in docs/CMD_ROUTING_MQTT_VOICE_2026-07-24.md:51, but framed purely as an MQTT/voice response-time concern; the main-loop-starvation consequence is not covered ther
- **Direction:** The loop is the wrong place for a synchronous 60 s wait. `submitCommandAsync` already exists and the Phase-1 result-delivery path already knows how to hand a result back to serial; routing the loop's serial drain through it would leave the loop free. `loopHealthTick()` (HardwareOne.cpp:1060-1091) already warns after the fact at a 200 ms threshold, which is a useful confirmation signal if you want to measure this before changing it.

### [LOW] CONFIG_PICO_PSRAM_CS_IO=10 is inert on both ESP32 board files, and the comment sends future PSRAM debugging to a dead end
- **Where:** `boards/feather_esp32_v2.defaults:24`
- **Also:** boards/qtpy_esp32.defaults (same CONFIG_PICO_PSRAM_CS_IO=10 line and comment)
- **What:** Both classic-ESP32 board files set CONFIG_PICO_PSRAM_CS_IO=10 and document it as the PSRAM chip-select, with the Feather V2 comment adding 'UNVERIFIED on Feather V2 hardware specifically. If psram reports 0 KB PSRAM on first boot, this is the line to check against the module.' IDF only consults that Kconfig on the PICO-D4 / U4WDH efuse branch (esp-idf/components/esp_psram/esp32/esp_psram_impl_quad.c:882). Both of these boards carry PICO-V3-02 silicon (QT Py ESP32 Pico = ESP32-PICO-V3-02; Feather ESP32 V2 = ESP32-PICO-MINI-02), which takes the branch at :890-892 and hardcodes PICO_V3_02_PSRAM_CLK_IO=10 / PICO_V3_02_PSRAM_CS_IO=9 (defined at :127-128).
- **Why it matters:** A maintainer bringing up the Feather V2 who sees 0 KB PSRAM will follow the comment, spend time trying different CS values, and observe zero change — because the driver never reads that setting on this silicon. The stated 'validated with GPIO10' provenance for the QT Py is also false: the value never reached the PSRAM driver. Separately, the real PSRAM pins on these boards are GPIO9 (CS) and GPIO10 (CLK), which is worth recording in the board files since neither board block currently documents them as reserved.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: Adafruit Feather ESP32 V2 and Adafruit QT Py ESP32 (classic ESP32 boards)
- **Direction:** Delete the CONFIG_PICO_PSRAM_CS_IO lines from both board files and replace the comment with a note that PICO-V3-02 PSRAM is fixed at CLK=GPIO10 / CS=GPIO9 by the IDF driver, so those two GPIOs are unavailable on both boards.

### [LOW] No allocation-failure hook is registered anywhere, and Arduino String turns an OOM into a silently-empty string — so every heap failure on this device is invisible
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/HardwareOne.cpp:190`
- **Also:** The one purpose-built diagnostic that does exist, gPsAllocFallbacks (System_MemUtil.h:18-26), is still observable only via ESP_LOGW and a global nobody prints; and the tagged ps_realloc overload at System_MemUtil.h:222-236 is still missing the __psAllocReportFallback() call its three siblings have (verified unchanged since the prior audit).
