That file's entire body is inside `#if ESP_IDF_VERSION < ESP_IDF_VERSION_VAL(5, 4, 0)` (components/arduino/cores/esp32/esp32-hal-i2c.c:19). This project builds on IDF v5.5.1, so it compiles to nothing; the live implementation is esp32-hal-i2c-ng.c, guarded `>= 5.4.0`, which uses driver/i2c_master.h and has no i2c_hw_fsm_reset and no I2C_ENTER_CRITICAL nesting.
- **Why it matters:** The interrupt watchdog is the only backstop for a core that is spinning with interrupts disabled — a mismatched spinlock, a runaway taskENTER_CRITICAL region, or a stalled flash cache-disable window. At 1500 ms instead of IDF's 300 ms that window is 5x wider before the hardware fires, and the INT_WDT panic dump is the primary diagnostic for exactly that class of hang. The cost is being paid for a code path that no longer exists in the binary. Concretely: the A8 audit already found a 1024-byte PSRAM memcpy inside taskENTER_CRITICAL (OLED_Mode_Map.cpp:1199) whose comment understates its cost 20x — that is the shape this relaxed budget hides. Note System_I2C_Manager.cpp:288 sets a 100 ms Wire timeout and reasons that it is "well under CONFIG_ESP_INT_WDT_TIMEOUT_MS (1500ms)"; it is also well under 300 ms, so that constraint does not pin the value.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Re-derive the number against the driver actually built (i2c_master) rather than inheriting it, or keep 1500 ms but replace the justification with the real current reason — the present comment will mislead the next person who tries to tighten it.

### [MEDIUM] PSRAM task stacks are enabled project-wide and one live worker uses one, against the standing "never put task stacks in PSRAM" rule
- **Where:** `sdkconfig.defaults:231`
- **Also:** sdkconfig:2028 (live CONFIG_FREERTOS_TASK_CREATE_ALLOW_EXT_MEM=y) and sdkconfig:3229 (deprecated alias, mapped by esp-idf components/freertos/sdkconfig.rename:8); components/hardwareone/G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp:1090-1119 (the PSRAM-stack spawn); components/hardwareone/System_LLM.cpp:174-175 (a comment restating the rule this config relaxes)
- **What:** sdkconfig.defaults:231 sets CONFIG_SPIRAM_ALLOW_STACK_EXTERNAL_MEMORY=y in the SHARED, all-targets defaults file. IDF v5.5 renamed it to CONFIG_FREERTOS_TASK_CREATE_ALLOW_EXT_MEM, whose Kconfig is `default n if IDF_TARGET_ESP32 / default y` — i.e. IDF turns it OFF on the classic ESP32 on purpose, and its help text says a task with a PSRAM stack "should only be used for tasks where the stack is never accessed while the cache is disabled" and, for ESP32 specifically, must make "no direct calls, but also no Bluetooth/WiFi". Setting it in the shared file overrides that per-target decision for both ESP32-classic boards. There is one live consumer: G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp:1099-1119 spawns g2_img_probe with an 8 KB MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM stack via xTaskCreateStaticPinnedToCore when the internal xTaskCreate fails — and that worker runs BLE TX (sendPbFragmented via G2FastLinkGuard) and guarded VFS reads.
- **Why it matters:** Two distinct problems. (1) The rule and the config disagree with no acknowledgement anywhere: System_LLM.cpp:174 states "task stacks in PSRAM have never been safe on this HW", while sdkconfig.defaults enables the mechanism and G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp:1093 cites the sdkconfig line as authorisation. Whichever is right, a future reader gets contradictory guidance from two files in the same tree. (2) On the ESP32-classic boards (feather_esp32_v2 has CONFIG_BT_ENABLED=y, and sdkconfig.defaults.esp32:47 additionally turns SPIRAM_CACHE_WORKAROUND off), this setting re-enables exactly the combination IDF disables by default, and the one task that uses it does BLE work — the specific thing the Kconfig help forbids. The fallback only triggers under internal-DRAM exhaustion, which is precisely the fragmented state the codebase documents as its steady state with a model loaded, so it is not a theoretical path.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: S3 boards: the setting is a no-op (IDF already defaults it y) but the g2_img_probe path is live on FeatherS3. ESP32-classic boards: the setting flips IDF's deliberate `default n`.
- **Direction:** Decide the rule once. If PSRAM stacks are genuinely forbidden, the honest fix is to delete the fallback and let the spawn fail loudly rather than silently downgrade to a stack the project says is unsafe. If the FeatherS3 exception is deliberate, move the setting out of the shared defaults into sdkconfig.defaults.esp32s3 so the classic-ESP32 build keeps IDF's default, and say so next to the rule in System_LLM.cpp.

### [LOW] CONFIG_ARDUINO_EVENT_RUNNING_CORE=1 places the highest-priority non-IDF task in the system on the core the placement policy reserves for I2C and compute
- **Where:** `sdkconfig:614`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_TaskUtils.h:148-172 (the policy); components/arduino/libraries/Network/src/NetworkEvents.cpp:80-82 (the spawn); CMakeLists.txt:239-247 (the stack-overflow history on this task)
- **What:** System_TaskUtils.h's placement policy assigns "BLE-reply / command I/O (cmd_exec) and serial/log I/O, and the ESP-NOW real-time path" to Core 0, and reserves Core 1 for the Arduino loop, I2C and compute — calling the I2C rule "the hard rule — no exceptions". arduino_events, which dispatches every WiFi/network event, is created at ESP_TASKD_EVENT_PRIO-1 = 19 (ESP_TASK_PRIO_MAX 25 minus 5, minus 1) on ARDUINO_EVENT_RUNNING_CORE. sdkconfig:614 has that as 1. This is arduino-esp32's dual-core default (Kconfig.projbuild:71-74, `default ARDUINO_EVENT_RUN_CORE1 if !FREERTOS_UNICORE`) — it was never overridden, so the policy was written without this task being considered.
- **Why it matters:** Every I2C-touching task in the tree pins to Core 1 at priority 1-5. arduino_events sits above all of them at 19 on the same core, so a WiFi connect/disconnect/GOT_IP burst preempts an in-flight I2C transaction — the failure mode the header describes as bus-storm-to-panic(4), just arriving from a different direction than the unpinned-onto-Core-0 case it was written for. This is not hypothetical for this task: CMakeLists.txt:239-247 records that the disconnect-while-connected handler chain on this exact task overflowed its stack and panicked on 2026-07-20, i.e. it does run substantial work during WiFi teardown. The placement is also the opposite of what the policy prescribes for network I/O.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all dual-core (both targets)
- **Direction:** Worth deciding rather than inheriting: CONFIG_ARDUINO_EVENT_RUN_CORE0 would put network events with the radio stack the policy already assigns to Core 0. If Core 1 is preferred for a reason, the policy comment should list arduino_events as a documented exception so the next reader does not conclude Core 1 is quiet.

### [LOW] sdkconfig.defaults comment blocks state the opposite of the values directly beneath them (Bluetooth, optimization level, default board)
- **Where:** `sdkconfig.defaults:60`
- **Also:** sdkconfig.defaults.esp32s3:9, :27-47, :53-55; components/hardwareone/System_BuildConfig.h:223; CMakeLists.txt:72-77
- **What:** Three in-file contradictions in the build config itself. (1) sdkconfig.defaults:59-75 is a banner reading "Bluetooth — currently DISABLED at the SDK level" and "The application-level mirror is ENABLE_BLUETOOTH=0 in System_BuildConfig.h"; line 79 is `CONFIG_BT_ENABLED=y` and System_BuildConfig.h:223 is `#define ENABLE_BLUETOOTH 1`. The same stale banner is repeated at sdkconfig.defaults.esp32s3:27-43 ("Bluetooth disabled on ESP32-S3 — frees ~14 KB IRAM...") immediately above `CONFIG_BT_ENABLED=y` at :44. (2) sdkconfig.defaults:182-183 declares `# Compiler options / CONFIG_COMPILER_OPTIMIZATION_SIZE=y`, but sdkconfig.defaults.esp32s3:55 sets `CONFIG_COMPILER_OPTIMIZATION_PERF=y` under an unrelated "# XIAO Sense expansion board features" heading (:53), and being layered later it wins — sdkconfig:719-720 shows SIZE unset, PERF set, and build/compile_commands.json shows -O2 on all 145 hardwareone translation units. sdkconfig.esp32 keeps SIZE (-Os). (3) sdkconfig.defaults.esp32s3:9 says "(default HW_BOARD is xiao_s3.)"; CMakeLists.txt:72-77 defaults esp32s3 to `feathers3`.
- **Why it matters:** Each of these is load-bearing for a decision someone will make from the file. The BT banner tells a reader BT is off, so BTC/BTU stack sizes, GATT cache and BT_ALLOCATION_FROM_SPIRAM_FIRST look inert when they are all live. The optimization contradiction means "the project builds -Os" is false for every S3 image while true for every ESP32-classic image — so flash-budget and code-size reasoning done on one board does not transfer, and a latent missing-`volatile` behaves differently per board. The HW_BOARD comment names the wrong default board, which differs in PSRAM mode (OCT vs QUAD), flash size (8 vs 16 MB) and partition table.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: (1) all; (2) board-gated — -O2 on esp32s3, -Os on esp32-classic; (3) esp32s3 only
- **Direction:** Delete or invert the two stale Bluetooth banners; move the PERF line out from under the XIAO Sense heading and reconcile it with the SIZE declaration in the shared file (state explicitly that S3 builds -O2 and classic builds -Os, or unify); fix the xiao_s3/feathers3 default-board sentence.

### [LOW] The two sdkconfig defaults files disagree on optimization level with no comment acknowledging it; the S3 build is -O2 while the shared file says -Os
- **Where:** `sdkconfig.defaults.esp32s3:55`
- **Also:** sdkconfig.defaults:182-185 (CONFIG_COMPILER_OPTIMIZATION_SIZE=y under a "# Compiler options" header); sdkconfig:718-720 (PERF is what actually landed)
- **What:** sdkconfig.defaults:183 sets CONFIG_COMPILER_OPTIMIZATION_SIZE=y. sdkconfig.defaults.esp32s3:55 sets CONFIG_COMPILER_OPTIMIZATION_PERF=y, and it sits under the comment "# XIAO Sense expansion board features (camera, mic, SD) / # Add to compiler defines", which describes something else entirely. IDF layers sdkconfig.defaults then sdkconfig.defaults.<target> then the board file, later wins, so PERF is effective — confirmed by sdkconfig:720. The ESP32-classic path has no such line and gets SIZE.
- **Why it matters:** The two targets are compiled at different optimisation levels and nothing in either file says so. That matters here specifically because sdkconfig.defaults.esp32:34-38 records that the ESP32-classic BT build "overruns the ESP32-classic's IRAM by a few hundred bytes (iram0_0_seg overflowed by 468 B)" and works around it with FREERTOS_PLACE_FUNCTIONS_INTO_FLASH — a margin that would move if someone "unified" the optimisation flag without realising the classic build is only fitting because it is -Os. On the S3 side, -O2 is spending flash in a factory partition that this audit found is 124 KB too small the moment ESP-SR is enabled. Also, whichever is intended, the SIZE line in the shared file is dead on the primary board and reads as authoritative.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: ESP32-S3 gets PERF (-O2); ESP32-classic gets SIZE (-Os) from the shared file; prior art: docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md:196 mentions "-O2" in passing when discussing live asserts, but does not note the conflict.
- **Direction:** Make the choice explicit in one place. If -O2 on S3 is deliberate (the LLM/PSRAM-bandwidth work suggests it might be), move the line under its own comment saying why and note that the classic build stays -Os on purpose.

### [NOTE] P8 answer: zero IRAM_ATTR in first-party code — all 110,941 B of IRAM, costing 95,232 B of DRAM address space, comes from IDF/BT/PHY
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_BuildConfig.h:220`
- **Also:** Managed/vendored components do use IRAM_ATTR (Adafruit_NeoPixel `espShow`, TFT_eSPI SPI callbacks, W5500/W6100 ISR handlers) — those are outside components/hardwareone/ and are what the library figures above include.
- **What:** `grep -rn IRAM_ATTR components/hardwareone/ main/` returns zero hits (only two prose mentions, at `System_BuildConfig.h:220` and `i2csensor_ds3231.cpp:2`). Confirmed against the checked-in link map (`build/hardwareone-idf.map`, 2026-07-27): `libhardwareone.a` contributes **0 bytes** to `.iram0.text`. Measured segment facts from that map — `iram0_0_seg` 0x57700 (358,144 B) with `.iram0.text` 0x1b22b (111,147 B) + `.iram0.vectors` 0x404 (1,028 B); `dram0_0_seg` 0x53700 (341,760 B) with `.dram0.dummy` 0x17700 (95,232 B), `.dram0.data` 0x6488 (25,736 B), `.dram0.bss` 0xd758 (54,304 B). `.dram0.dummy` is precisely the DRAM aliased away by IRAM code: 0x40374000 + 0x1b62f − 0x40378000, rounded up. Top IRAM contributors: libfreertos 18,531 · libbtdm_app 16,947 · libesp_hw_support 16,238 · libspi_flash 11,296 · libhal 8,811 · libheap 6,687 · libphy 5,029 · libesp_ringbuf 4,265 · libesp_system 3,998 · libesp_driver_rmt 3,257.
- **Why it matters:** Not a defect — this is the assigned P8 measurement, and the answer changes the priority order. 93 KB of the 334 KB DRAM segment is gone to IRAM before a single byte of application data is placed, and the firmware has no lever on any of it: every byte is IDF, Bluedroid, PHY or the RMT/SPI/I2C `*_IN_IRAM` sdkconfig options (`GPTIMER_ISR_HANDLER_IN_IRAM`, `I2C_MASTER_ISR_HANDLER_IN_IRAM`, `RMT_TX/RX_ISR_HANDLER_IN_IRAM`, `RMT_ENCODER_FUNC_IN_IRAM`, `SPI_MASTER_ISR_IN_IRAM`, `ESP_SYSTEM_IN_IRAM`). The corollary matters for A2/A3: with zero `IRAM_ATTR` in the tree, no first-party ISR or callback is IRAM-resident, so any P1 fix would be *adding* IRAM and paying DRAM for it — the trade in P8's second sentence, priced here at 1:1.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: measured on the FeatherS3 image in build/; the esp32 secondary target has no checked-in map and is unmeasured
- **Direction:** No action needed on first-party code. If IRAM ever needs reclaiming, `CONFIG_HEAP_PLACE_FUNCTION_INTO_FLASH` is unset and libheap holds 6,687 B — but that is an sdkconfig decision with cache-disable implications, not a code change, and should be weighed against P1 before touching.

### [NOTE] USER_LED_PIN is defined per board but read by no code — the SD_CS collision its comment 'resolves' has no effect
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_BuildConfig.h:828`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_BuildConfig.h:868 (base XIAO defines USER_LED_PIN 21)
- **What:** The Sense block defines USER_LED_PIN -1 with the comment 'User LED - DISABLED on Sense board (GPIO21 is used for SD_CS). The expansion board SD card takes priority over the base board LED.' The base block defines it as 21. A tree-wide grep finds no reader of USER_LED_PIN anywhere in components/hardwareone or main.
- **Why it matters:** The comment reads as a resolved hardware conflict, which will make a future reviewer skip past the fact that GPIO21 is double-purposed. In reality nothing ever drives the user LED, so the only code that touches GPIO21 is the SD path (System_VFS.cpp:118-119 pinMode/digitalWrite CS) — and, per the base-XIAO finding above, that path runs even on a board with no SD slot.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: XIAO ESP32-S3 base and Sense blocks only
- **Direction:** Either wire USER_LED_PIN up to something (a boot heartbeat) or delete both definitions and the comment, so the pin map does not document a conflict resolution that isn't real.

### [NOTE] Feather ESP32 V2 drives strapping pin GPIO0 as the NeoPixel data line, and nothing releases it before ESP.restart()
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_BuildConfig.h:799`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_NeoPixel.cpp:24 (Adafruit_NeoPixel pixels(NUMPIXELS, NEOPIXEL_PIN_DEFAULT, ...)), components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:2105 (ESP.restart with no GPIO teardown)
- **What:** NEOPIXEL_PIN_DEFAULT is 0 for this board, matching Adafruit's own variant (components/arduino/variants/adafruit_feather_esp32_v2/pins_arduino.h defines PIN_NEOPIXEL 0), so the firmware is following the real hardware rather than inventing the assignment. GPIO0 is the classic-ESP32 boot-mode strapping pin: LOW at reset selects serial download boot. The WS2812 data line idles LOW between frames, and no reboot path (cmd_reboot -> ESP.restart, or a watchdog/panic restart) restores GPIO0 to input first.
- **Why it matters:** If the strapping value is re-latched on a software or watchdog reset while the RMT output still holds GPIO0 LOW, the ROM would select download boot instead of running the app. I could not confirm this: on ESP32 the strapping register is documented as latching on the CHIP_PU rising edge (which esp_restart does not toggle), the GPIO matrix is reset early in the restart sequence, and the Feather V2 has an external pull-up plus BOOT button on GPIO0 — all of which argue the pin recovers to HIGH inside the sampling window. Filed PLAUSIBLE because I could not observe it and this board is not the primary target.
- **Confidence:** PLAUSIBLE — board: Adafruit Feather ESP32 V2 only (classic ESP32)
- **Direction:** If this board is ever brought back into rotation, drive GPIO0 to a HIGH/input state in the reboot path before ESP.restart() (a pinMode(0, INPUT) alongside the existing pre-restart delay) — cheap insurance, no downside.

### [NOTE] Prior audit doc is wrong: g2_tap_disp is described as "unpinned" but is pinned to APP_CORE
- **Where:** `docs/SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT.md:106`
- **What:** docs/SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT.md:106 lists "the **unpinned** g2_tap_disp worker" among the concurrent gSettings writers, and docs/audit-recon/recon_prior.md:140 and :308-309 propagate the claim. The actual create call is xTaskCreatePinnedToCore(tapDispatcherWorkerLoop, "g2_tap_disp", 6400, nullptr, tskIDLE_PRIORITY+2, &gTapTaskHandle, APP_CORE) at components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:11432.
- **Why it matters:** The settings-race argument in that doc rests partly on g2_tap_disp floating across cores. It does not float — but the race is still real, because g2_tap_disp on APP_CORE (1) genuinely runs concurrently with cmd_exec_task on PRO_CORE (0), which is the actual cross-core writer pair. Anyone who checks the pinning, finds the doc wrong, and dismisses the finding would be discarding a live concurrency bug on a false refutation.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: FeatherS3; prior art: Correcting docs/SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT.md §4.5 and docs/audit-recon/recon_prior.md rows 140 / 308-309.
- **Direction:** Amend the doc to read "pinned to APP_CORE, i.e. a different core from cmd_exec_task" — the conclusion survives, only the mechanism changes.

### [NOTE] CONFIG_SPI_FLASH_AUTO_SUSPEND is off, so every flash read, program and erase is a full dual-core cache-off stop — posture note, not a defect
- **Where:** `sdkconfig:2560`
- **Also:** sdkconfig:1670-1671 (SPIRAM_FETCH_INSTRUCTIONS/RODATA off), sdkconfig:402 (APP_BUILD_TYPE_RAM off), sdkconfig:2579-2582 (yield-during-erase settings), sdkconfig:2564 (SPI_FLASH_PLACE_FUNCTIONS_IN_IRAM=y).
- **What:** `# CONFIG_SPI_FLASH_AUTO_SUSPEND is not set`. Combined with SPIRAM_FETCH_INSTRUCTIONS and SPIRAM_RODATA also off and APP_BUILD_TYPE_RAM off, IDF's SPI_FLASH_CACHE_NO_DISABLE macro (spi_flash_os_func_app.c:26) evaluates false, so spi1_start()/spi1_end() take the cache_disable/cache_enable branch on every single esp_flash_* call — reads included, not just writes and erases.
- **Why it matters:** This is the root multiplier behind the three timing findings above: there is no cheap flash access on this build. Mitigations that ARE in place and are working: CONFIG_SPI_FLASH_YIELD_DURING_ERASE=y with ERASE_YIELD_DURATION_MS=20 / ERASE_YIELD_TICKS=1 inserts a 1-tick yield between sectors (a single sector erase always exceeds 20 us*1000, so the yield fires on every iteration of the multi-sector loop), and WRITE_CHUNK_SIZE=8192 bounds a single program window. What they cannot do is shorten one sector erase, because the cache stays off for the whole busy-poll inside spi_flash_chip_generic_erase_sector. Enabling auto-suspend would remove the cache-off window for erases, but it is flash-part dependent (the chip must support suspend/resume) and interacts with CONFIG_SPI_FLASH_HPM_ON=y, so it is a hardware-validated change, not a config flip. Recording it here so nobody re-derives it.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: Only thin prior coverage: docs/ESP32_PITFALL_AUDIT.md P40 ("Covered by A3, A6") and an incidental mention in HEAP_OFFLOAD_SWEEP_2026-07-24.md §2b.
- **Direction:** Leave as-is unless the exact flash part on the FeatherS3 is known to support suspend/resume; if it is, an sdkconfig experiment with CONFIG_SPI_FLASH_AUTO_SUSPEND is the single highest-leverage change for every finding above and must be validated on hardware under BLE+ESP-NOW load.

### [NOTE] Stack-overflow detection is canary-only, heap poisoning is off, and stack-protector is NORM — an overflow that clears the canary into an adjacent heap block has no detector at any layer
- **Where:** `sdkconfig:1977`
- **Also:** sdkconfig:2008 (CONFIG_FREERTOS_WATCHPOINT_END_OF_STACK unset); sdkconfig:2055 (CONFIG_HEAP_POISONING_DISABLED=y); sdkconfig:733 (CONFIG_COMPILER_STACK_CHECK_MODE_NORM=y); sdkconfig:1759 (CONFIG_ESP_SYSTEM_PANIC_REBOOT_DELAY_SECONDS=0)
- **What:** CONFIG_FREERTOS_CHECK_STACKOVERFLOW_CANARY=y with CONFIG_FREERTOS_WATCHPOINT_END_OF_STACK unset. Per IDF's Kconfig help, the canary is "magic bytes at the end of the stack area" checked "on each context switch" — detection is deferred, and only fires if those specific bytes were written. The watchpoint alternative, which traps immediately on the first write past the boundary, is off. Heap poisoning is fully disabled, and -fstack-protector is NORM (functions with char arrays only, checked on return).
- **Why it matters:** This is the exact combination P10 describes: a large stack local (the tree has several — the g2_img_probe worker keeps a ~1 KB pixel buffer plus a ~1.1 KB protobuf body on its stack) can move the stack pointer past the canary in one frame setup and land in the neighbouring heap allocation without touching the magic bytes. The canary then still reads intact at the next context switch, heap poisoning would have caught the corrupted block header but is off, and NORM stack-protector only covers functions that happen to contain a char array and only at return. The failure surfaces later as heap corruption blamed on whatever allocation was scribbled. Worth stating as a combination because each of the three configs is individually documented as an accepted tradeoff, and the interaction is where the detection gap actually is. This project has a demonstrated history of stack overflows (arduino_events at 2560, g2_img_probe at 4096, BTC_TASK at 3072), so the class is live rather than theoretical.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: KNOWN in parts — docs/CRASH_HISTORY_DESIGN.md:16 (canary detection and the 0-second reboot destroying the message), docs/HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT_MECHANICS.md:64 (poisoning disabled), docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENIN
- **Direction:** CONFIG_FREERTOS_WATCHPOINT_END_OF_STACK is the cheap half — it uses a hardware watchpoint rather than runtime cost, and it converts the deferred canary check into an immediate trap for the common cases. Heap poisoning LIGHT would close the rest but costs per-allocation. Diagnostic build first, given the memory monitor already tracks high-water marks.

### [NOTE] ESP-SR wake-word/command models are still built (~2.98 MB srmodels.bin) on every build even though ENABLE_ESP_SR=0 and the selected partition table has no model partition
- **Where:** `sdkconfig.defaults:195`
- **Also:** sdkconfig:665-715; components/hardwareone/idf_component.yml (esp-sr, optional: true); build/srmodels/srmodels.bin
- **What:** System_BuildConfig.h:293 is `#define ENABLE_ESP_SR 0`, so CMakeLists.txt:129-148 selects partitions_no_sr_16mb.csv (copied to partitions.csv), which has no `model` partition. But sdkconfig.defaults still enables the full ESP-SR model bundle — CONFIG_MODEL_IN_FLASH, CONFIG_USE_AFE, CONFIG_USE_WAKENET, CONFIG_SR_WN_WN9_HILEXIN, CONFIG_USE_MULTINET, CONFIG_SR_MN_EN_MULTINET7_QUANT (mirrored live at sdkconfig:665-715) — and espressif/esp-sr is still an (optional) dependency in components/hardwareone/idf_component.yml. The result is build/srmodels/srmodels.bin at 3,052,238 bytes, regenerated 2026-07-26.
- **Why it matters:** Not a runtime defect: I verified build/flasher_args.json and build/flash_args list only bootloader.bin, partition-table.bin and hardwareone-idf.bin, so srmodels.bin is never written and cannot collide with the model-less layout. The cost is build time, 3 MB of build-tree disk, and a config that reads as if speech recognition is enabled when the app-level flag says it is not — the same class of mismatch that makes the ESP_SR partition-picker logic hard to reason about.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Gate the ESP-SR model kconfig block (or the esp-sr dependency rule) on the same ENABLE_ESP_SR flag the partition picker already reads, so the two stop disagreeing.

### [NOTE] CONFIG_ARDUINO_LOOP_STACK_SIZE is listed under "Stack sizes - tuned for this app" but has no effect
- **Where:** `sdkconfig.defaults:54`
- **Also:** components/arduino/cores/esp32/main.cpp:16-25; sdkconfig:603, :1772
- **What:** sdkconfig.defaults sets `CONFIG_ARDUINO_LOOP_STACK_SIZE=8192` in the block headed "# Stack sizes - tuned for this app". That symbol is consumed only by components/arduino/cores/esp32/main.cpp, entirely inside `#if CONFIG_AUTOSTART_ARDUINO` (main.cpp:25), and CONFIG_AUTOSTART_ARDUINO is not set (sdkconfig:603). No loopTask is ever created; app_main runs loop() inline on the main task.
- **Why it matters:** Anyone budgeting stack for the main loop will read 8192 from this line and be right by coincidence only — the real number is CONFIG_ESP_MAIN_TASK_STACK_SIZE=8192 (sdkconfig:1772). If someone raises one and not the other, the loop's real stack does not move. By contrast CONFIG_ARDUINO_SERIAL_EVENT_TASK_STACK_SIZE=2048 in the same block *is* live (HWCDC.cpp:67 uses it for the USB-CDC event task), so the block cannot be dismissed wholesale.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: partially — docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md:160 notes CONFIG_AUTOSTART_ARDUINO is off and enableLoopWDT() is therefore unreachable; the stack-size consequence is not mentioned
- **Direction:** Either remove the inert line or annotate it as inert-while-AUTOSTART_ARDUINO-is-off and point at CONFIG_ESP_MAIN_TASK_STACK_SIZE as the real knob.

### [NOTE] sdkconfig.defaults' BT_ALLOCATION_FROM_SPIRAM_FIRST note describes the PSRAM mode as octal; the primary board is quad
- **Where:** `sdkconfig.defaults:96`
- **Also:** sdkconfig:1661 (CONFIG_SPIRAM_MODE_QUAD=y); boards/feathers3.defaults:24 (quad); boards/xiao_s3.defaults:19 (oct)
- **What:** The comment says the setting "was silently dropped when commit 9a23158 ('BSS-to-PSRAM diversion') regenerated sdkconfig against the wrong PSRAM target; it never got restored when the SPIRAM mode was fixed back to octal." The active board is FeatherS3, whose board file sets CONFIG_SPIRAM_MODE_QUAD=y, and the live sdkconfig:1661 confirms quad. The setting itself is genuinely restored and present at sdkconfig:987 — that part of the note is accurate and the regression is fixed.
- **Why it matters:** Only a reader hazard, but a specific one: this comment is the audit trail for a regression that already cost a debugging cycle, and it now describes a PSRAM mode the primary board does not use. Someone re-checking "did the octal fix land" against a quad build will not be able to reconcile the two, and the natural resolution — assuming the comment is current and the config is wrong — is backwards. The XIAO/octal framing was correct when written; primary-board ownership moved.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: FeatherS3 (quad) — the note reads as if XIAO (octal) were still primary
- **Direction:** A one-word correction, or a dated note that the octal reference is XIAO-era history. Flagged only because this comment exists specifically to survive future sdkconfig regenerations.


### Docs / meta — 4 findings (0 critical/high)

### [MEDIUM] Brownout is at the LEAST sensitive threshold IDF offers (2.44 V), not "max sensitivity" as the hardening audit records
- **Where:** `docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md:157`
- **Also:** sdkconfig:1571,1578 (CONFIG_ESP_BROWNOUT_DET_LVL_SEL_7=y, CONFIG_ESP_BROWNOUT_DET_LVL=7); esp-idf components/esp_hw_support/power_supply/port/esp32s3/Kconfig.power (the voltage table and `default ESP_BROWNOUT_DET_LVL_SEL_7`)
- **What:** PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §5 lists as already-solid: "brownout at max sensitivity with flash-write protection". The config is CONFIG_ESP_BROWNOUT_DET_LVL_SEL_7=y. On ESP32-S3 the Kconfig choice runs SEL_7=2.44V, SEL_6=2.56V, SEL_5=2.67V, SEL_4=2.84V, SEL_3=2.98V, SEL_2=3.19V, SEL_1=3.30V — level 7 is the LOWEST threshold in the list and it is IDF's own default. Nothing in sdkconfig.defaults, the target files, or the board files touches brownout at all, so this value was never chosen; it is simply the default that was never examined. (The intuition that a higher number means a higher threshold holds on the original ESP32 and is inverted on the S3, which is the likely source of the error.)
- **Why it matters:** The claim is load-bearing in two places. First, it is the reason brownout appears in the audit's "already solid" list and therefore was never re-examined. Second, §5.2 argues against a crash-count breaker partly because "on a LiPo wearable, a sagging battery or a WiFi-TX current spike can trip it repeatedly" — that argument silently assumes the detector fires early, when at 2.44 V it fires as late as the part allows. The practical gap is the band between the SPI flash's minimum operating voltage (commonly 2.7 V for QSPI parts) and 2.44 V: in that band the CPU keeps executing and issuing flash writes while the flash is below spec, and CONFIG_SPI_FLASH_BROWNOUT_RESET only helps once the detector has actually fired. Given this device writes settings.json, users.json and LittleFS logs continuously, that is the window in which a sag produces filesystem corruption rather than a clean reset.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: ESP32-S3 boards (voltage table is S3-specific; the ESP32-classic level table differs); prior art: docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md:157 asserts the opposite; docs/ESP32_PITFALL_AUDIT.md:327 lists the check as open (A18/A21).
- **Direction:** Two separate things: correct the doc claim (it is the input to a decision that was already made), and separately decide whether 2.44 V is the right threshold for a battery-powered board that writes flash constantly — raising it trades a few extra resets for keeping flash writes inside spec. No code change either way.

### [LOW] docs/BOARD_SWITCHING.md tells the reader to change PSRAM mode in a file that no longer holds it and that is overridden by the board layer
- **Where:** `docs/BOARD_SWITCHING.md:194`
- **Also:** docs/BOARD_SWITCHING.md:189, :227, :276, :296
- **What:** Line 194 and the troubleshooting entry at line 296 both instruct: "Edit sdkconfig.defaults.esp32s3, change CONFIG_SPIRAM_MODE_OCT=y to CONFIG_SPIRAM_MODE_QUAD=y". sdkconfig.defaults.esp32s3 contains no SPIRAM_MODE line at all — its own header (:5-6, :14) says mode is board-specific and lives in boards/<name>.defaults — and CMakeLists.txt:161-163 layers the board file *after* sdkconfig.defaults.esp32s3, so even adding the line there would be overridden. Line 227 states as a chip-family fact "ESP32-S3: Octal SPI PSRAM at 80 MHz (CONFIG_SPIRAM_MODE_OCT)", wrong for the primary board (feathers3 is QUAD, sdkconfig:1661). Line 189's FeatherS3 table row says Flash Size 8 MB; boards/feathers3.defaults sets 16 MB. Line 276 says boards/xiao_s3.defaults is the "default if HW_BOARD unset"; CMakeLists.txt:74-76 defaults to feathers3.
- **Why it matters:** The doc's own troubleshooting section for the exact symptom it warns about ("FeatherS3 reports 0 KB PSRAM") prescribes an edit that has no effect, so a reader hitting silent no-PSRAM boot follows the fix, rebuilds, and gets the same result with no signal about why. The flash-size row would also steer a reader to the 8 MB partition set on a 16 MB board.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: esp32s3 (feathers3 / xiao_s3); prior art: partially — docs/DOCS_ACCURACY_AUDIT_2026-07-26.md §1.1 and §1.2 caught the same class of error, but its stated scope is README.md / QUICKSTART.md / USERGUIDE.md only; BOARD_SWITCHING.md was not audit
- **Direction:** Rewrite the PSRAM-mode instructions to point at boards/<board>.defaults and the HW_BOARD env var, correct the FeatherS3 flash-size row to 16 MB, fix the default-board line, and qualify line 227 as per-board rather than per-chip-family.

### [LOW] docs/BOARD_SWITCHING.md contradicts the code on the FeatherS3 I2C2 pin mapping, the battery backend, and the default HW_BOARD
- **Where:** `docs/BOARD_SWITCHING.md:291`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_BuildConfig.h:938-945 (SDA=16/SCL=15, marked empirically verified), :920 (BATTERY_BACKEND_FUEL_GAUGE 1); sdkconfig.defaults.esp32s3 header comment vs CMakeLists.txt:74
- **What:** The doc's FeatherS3[D] section states "STEMMA QT I2C (secondary): SDA=GPIO15, SCL=GPIO16 ... Not currently used by the codebase; would require a second Wire1 bus instance" and "Battery monitoring: ... Currently disabled in the codebase (ADC-based battery_monitor can't talk to the fuel gauge)". The code says the opposite on all three counts: I2C2_SDA_PIN_DEFAULT=16 / I2C2_SCL_PIN_DEFAULT=15 (with a comment recording that the obvious 15/16 assignment was empirically wrong), the dual-bus manager does use bus 1, and BATTERY_BACKEND_FUEL_GAUGE=1 with i2csensor_max17048.cpp present. Separately, sdkconfig.defaults.esp32s3's header says "(default HW_BOARD is xiao_s3.)" while CMakeLists.txt:74 defaults esp32s3 to feathers3.
- **Why it matters:** Someone bringing up a second STEMMA QT device follows the doc, wires or configures SDA=15/SCL=16, gets no ACK, and re-derives the swap the code already recorded. The stale "battery disabled" line similarly invites re-implementing a driver that exists. Documentation drift on a pin map is the same failure mode as a wrong pin map, just slower.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: Unexpected Maker FeatherS3[D]
- **Direction:** Update the FeatherS3[D] section to match System_BuildConfig.h:938-945 (SDA=16, SCL=15, in use), drop the 'battery disabled' paragraph, and fix the stale default-board line in sdkconfig.defaults.esp32s3's header.

### [NOTE] Pitfall checklist P30 omits GPIO3, the fourth ESP32-S3 strapping pin
- **Where:** `docs/ESP32_PITFALL_AUDIT.md:223`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_VFS.cpp:1155 is the only site that touches GPIO3 (pinMode(3, INPUT) inside cmd_sddiag, XIAO-only, read-only — harmless)
- **What:** P30 states 'boot mode depends on GPIO0/45/46 state at reset' and directs the pin-map check against that three-pin list. The ESP32-S3 has four strapping pins: GPIO0 (chip boot mode), GPIO3 (JTAG signal source select), GPIO45 (VDD_SPI voltage select), GPIO46 (ROM message printing / boot mode).
- **Why it matters:** An auditor or maintainer following the checklist would clear a pin map that assigned an output to GPIO3 without flagging it. Nothing in the current pin map does — GPIO3 is unclaimed on FeatherS3 and on both XIAO blocks — so no live defect, but the checklist would not have caught one.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: documentation — applies to both ESP32-S3 boards; prior art: docs/ESP32_PITFALL_AUDIT.md P30 (this finding corrects it)
- **Direction:** Add GPIO3 to the P30 pin list so future pin-map reviews cover all four S3 strapping pins.


### ESP-NOW / radio mesh — 2 findings (0 critical/high)

### [MEDIUM] WIFI_PS_NONE is set through the raw IDF call, leaving Arduino's cached sleep mode at WIFI_PS_MIN_MODEM, which the Arduino STA_START handler re-applies
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:9738`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:1094 (same raw call, same stale cache); components/arduino/libraries/WiFi/src/STA.cpp:114 (the re-apply); components/arduino/libraries/WiFi/src/WiFiGeneric.cpp:381 (the cached default)
- **What:** Both power-save calls in the firmware are raw esp_wifi_set_ps(WIFI_PS_NONE) — initEspNow at 9738 and connectWiFiIndex's success path at System_WiFi.cpp:1094. WiFi.setSleep() is never called anywhere in components/hardwareone (verified by grep), so WiFiGenericClass::_sleepEnabled keeps its compile-time default, which on a non-S2 target is WIFI_PS_MIN_MODEM (WiFiGeneric.cpp:378-382). STA.cpp:112-116 re-applies esp_wifi_set_ps(WiFi.getSleep()) on every ARDUINO_EVENT_WIFI_STA_START, delivered asynchronously on the `arduino_events` task (NetworkEvents.cpp:75-82, priority ESP_TASKD_EVENT_PRIO-1, pinned to core 1 per CONFIG_ARDUINO_EVENT_RUNNING_CORE=1). initEspNow's own WiFi.mode(WIFI_AP_STA) at 9728 takes the NULL→AP_STA branch and reaches espWiFiStart()/esp_wifi_start() (WiFiGeneric.cpp:599), which raises exactly that event.
- **Why it matters:** On the ESP-NOW-first boot path — WiFi autostart off or wifienabled 0, so HardwareOne.cpp:1743 is skipped and cmd_espnow_init at :2132 brings the driver up from NULL — the ordering of "esp_wifi_set_ps(NONE) at line 9738" versus "arduino_events runs _onStaArduinoEvent and applies MIN_MODEM" is decided purely by FreeRTOS scheduling across two queue hops, not by the code. If the event lands after line 9738, power save silently reverts to MIN_MODEM for exactly the scenario the comment at 9732-9737 says it is protecting ("a device that never associates — out and about, home SSID absent — the exact case where the mesh must stay reachable"), and nothing re-asserts it until a successful WiFi join. CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_STA_DISCONNECTED_PM_ENABLE=y (sdkconfig:1871) makes the disconnected state a modem-sleep state ("Chip will do modem-sleep when rf module is not in use any more"), which is precisely the state an ESP-NOW-only device lives in. The stale-cache half is not a race: any future STA start deterministically re-applies MIN_MODEM.
- **Confidence:** PLAUSIBLE — board: all (the WIFI_PS_MIN_MODEM default applies to every non-ESP32-S2 target, i.e. both the S3 primary and the ESP32 secondary)
- **Direction:** Use WiFi.setSleep(WIFI_PS_NONE) instead of the raw esp_wifi_set_ps at both sites so Arduino's cached _sleepEnabled matches the intent and the STA_START re-apply becomes a no-op. Worth a bench confirmation of the current behaviour: boot with wifienabled 0 + espnowAutoStart on, then read back esp_wifi_get_ps().

### [NOTE] Dead and algebraically meaningless timestamp expression in the fragment-reassembly path
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5418`
- **What:** `unsigned long totalTime = millis() - (e->lastUpdateMs - (millis() - e->lastUpdateMs));` — this simplifies to `2*millis() - 2*e->lastUpdateMs`, i.e. twice the elapsed reassembly time, and calls `millis()` twice so the two reads can disagree. The variable is never referenced afterward; none of the four DEBUGF lines that follow (:5419-5422) consume it.
- **Why it matters:** No runtime effect — it is dead. It is worth naming because it looks like an intended "reassembly took N ms" metric that was silently lost: if anyone resurrects it as-is they get a number that is 2x the true value. It also suggests -Wunused-variable is not enabled for this component, which is itself worth knowing.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders
- **Direction:** Delete it, or restore the intent as `const uint32_t totalTime = millis() - e->firstFragmentMs;` (requires tracking the first-fragment stamp) and print it in the completion log.


### LLM — 2 findings (0 critical/high)

### [MEDIUM] LLM load/unload is called from three different tasks with no mutual exclusion; gLLM.mutex guards only worker creation
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_LLM.cpp:249`
- **What:** `LLMRuntime::mutex` (declared System_LLM_Internal.h:292) is taken in exactly one function in the whole tree — `llmEnsureWorker()` at :249/:290. Nothing else takes it: it does not cover the model blobs, the run state, the load path or the unload path. Meanwhile `llmLoadModel()` (:921) and `llmUnload()` (:1008) are invoked directly from three tasks: httpd/tskNO_AFFINITY (WebPage_LLM.cpp:239 and :258 call them inline in the handler), loopTask/core 1 (OLED_Mode_LLM.cpp:233), and cmd_exec_task/core 0 (System_LLM.cpp:2691/2706).
- **Why it matters:** POST /api/llm/load while the OLED model picker is loading means both tasks run llmUnload() then both allocate into the same gLLM.weightsData / stateData / tokenizer fields — the loser's multi-megabyte PSRAM blob is leaked and the two loaders interleave writes into one runtime struct. POST /api/llm/unload while an OLED-initiated load is inside loadWeights frees the blocks that load is filling. The quiesce loop at :1019-1031 only covers the generation worker, never another loader or unloader, and even that is a bounded ~1 s poll that falls through and frees anyway if a long forward() pass has not observed stopRequested. Separately, `llmLoadModel` opens with `if (!gLLM.mutex) llmInit();` and `llmInit()` (:914-919) does `memset(&gLLM, 0, sizeof(gLLM))` — two tasks racing first use each create a mutex, leak one, and memset the struct under each other.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: ENABLE_ONDEVICE_LLM builds only — System_BuildConfig.h:306 sets it to 0, so NOT FeatherS3 today
- **Direction:** The mutex already exists and is already reached from every relevant task — extending it to wrap the whole load and the whole unload (and making llmInit run once at boot rather than lazily) closes this without new machinery. The guided-menu module in the same subsystem (System_LLM_Menu.cpp:9-18) documents exactly this task set and already solves it properly with sMenuLock; that is the model to copy.

### [MEDIUM] llmUnload() runs unlocked from the httpd task and from cmd_exec_task, and its quiesce loop yields — two unloads interleave into a double free
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_LLM.cpp:1008`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebPage_LLM.cpp:239 (load path, same collision); components/hardwareone/System_LLM.cpp:933, :2706; components/hardwareone/OLED_Mode_LLM.cpp:233
- **What:** llmUnload() takes no lock. Its first action is a quiesce loop (:1019-1031) that calls vTaskDelay for up to ~1.1 s, i.e. it explicitly yields the CPU before freeing anything. It then runs an unguarded sequence of llmPsramFree() calls (:1033-1059); llmPsramFree (:306-311) is `if (ptr && *ptr) { heap_caps_free(*ptr); *ptr = nullptr; }` — check-then-free, not atomic. gLLM.mutex is declared at System_LLM_Internal.h:292 but has exactly two uses in the entire tree, both inside llmEnsureWorker() (:249 take, :290 give). It protects worker-task creation and nothing else — no gLLM runtime state, no weights, no RunState, no tokenizer, no KV cache.
- **Why it matters:** POST /api/llm/unload calls llmUnload() directly on the httpd task (WebPage_LLM.cpp:258), which is tskNO_AFFINITY. `llmunload` on cmd_exec_task (core 0, System_LLM.cpp:3041 → :2706) and `llmload` (which calls llmUnload at :933) reach the same code, as does the OLED loader on loopTask/core 1 (OLED_Mode_LLM.cpp:233). Because the httpd path parks in vTaskDelay for up to a second before touching any pointer, a CLI or OLED unload issued during that window runs the free sequence concurrently: both tasks observe gLLM.weightsData non-null and both call heap_caps_free on it. That is a PSRAM double free — heap corruption, typically surfacing as an abort in a later unrelated allocation. Separately, the quiesce is bounded rather than guaranteed: after 100 x 10 ms it frees the weights regardless, so a generation whose per-token latency exceeds that budget (plausible on quad PSRAM, which this project documents as bus-bound) keeps reading freed weight blocks on llm_gen, core 1.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: builds with ENABLE_ONDEVICE_LLM only. Currently 0 (System_BuildConfig.h:306), so NOT the shipping FeatherS3 config — this is latent for anyone flipping the flag on.
- **Direction:** llmUnload/llmLoadModel need to be mutually exclusive across all four entry surfaces, and gLLM.mutex is the obvious existing handle for it — its current coverage is one function. The bounded-wait-then-free-anyway tail should either become an unbounded wait on a worker acknowledgment or refuse the unload and report busy, rather than freeing under a task that may still be reading.


---

## Verified clean (platform)

- **Heap instrumentation posture verified against sdkconfig, matching the mechanics doc's claims:** `CONFIG_HEAP_POISONING_DISABLED=y`, `CONFIG_HEAP_TRACING_OFF=y`, `CONFIG_HEAP_TASK_TRACKING` unset, `CONFIG_HEAP_USE_HOOKS` unset, `CONFIG_HEAP_ABORT_WHEN_ALLOCATION_FAILS` unset. Also confirmed: no `heap_caps_register_failed_alloc_callback` anywhere in the firmware — noted as a gap in finding 4 rather than as a defect on its own.
- **Internal-DRAM monitoring exists and is genuinely surfaced (P6's main question: yes).** `System_MemoryMonitor.cpp:434-450` runs an always-on Tier-1 DRAM-pressure watch that is deliberately *outside* the `DEBUG_MEMORY` gate — a cheap `heap_caps_get_free_size(MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL|MALLOC_CAP_8BIT)` at most once/second, warning below 40 KB, rate-limited to one line per 10 s. `heap_caps_get_minimum_free_size` (min-ever) and `heap_caps_get_largest_free_block` are both sampled. A shipping device with all debug flags off still warns before it OOMs.
- **No first-party DMA buffer allocation exists at all.** `MALLOC_CAP_DMA` appears in `components/hardwareone/` only in diagnostics (`System_Utils.cpp:3501-3502`, `:4006-4007`). The one DMA-bearing path the firmware drives is I2S PDM, and `HAL_Audio.cpp:55-113` checks the `esp_err_t` from `i2s_new_channel`, `i2s_channel_init_pdm_rx_mode` and `i2s_channel_enable`, tearing the channel down and nulling the handle on each failure. Board-gated to `ENABLE_MICROPHONE_SENSOR` (0 on FeatherS3).
- **No first-party `IRAM_ATTR` anywhere.** `components/hardwareone/` and `main/` contain zero uses; the link map confirms `libhardwareone.a` contributes 0 bytes to `.iram0.text`. Firmware code cannot be shrinking the DRAM pool via IRAM growth, so P8's regression risk is entirely a future-change risk, not a present one.
- **The LLM loader does real fragmentation recovery rather than just failing.** `System_LLM_Model.cpp:709-750`: internal hot-state alloc falls back to PSRAM on failure; the large cold-state alloc, on failure, queries `heap_caps_get_largest_free_block(MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM)`, recomputes how many context slots fit the largest *contiguous* block, and shrinks `seq_ctx` to fit. It reasons about contiguity vs. total free explicitly (:717-719) — the correct mental model, applied.
- **The debug/broadcast mainline is fixed-buffer, confirming the mechanics doc's "do not fix the logging lane".** `debugOutputTask` (`System_Debug.cpp:193-283`) pulls pre-allocated `DebugMessage*` from a pool and formats into `char formattedMsg[DEBUG_MSG_SIZE+32]` for the serial and web-mirror sinks; the pool's growth allocation goes to `MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM` (`:438-439`) with a proper failure state. Only the `[ERROR]` and `[EVENT]` file lanes build a `String`, and both are edge-triggered and deduped.
- **The two per-main-loop-lap event drains allocate nothing.** `systemEventLogTick` (`System_Events.cpp:460-533`) and `systemEventsNotifyTick` (`System_Notifications.cpp:766+`) are entirely stack-buffer based — `SystemEvent evs[8]`, `char line[DEBUG_MSG_SIZE]`, `char msg[64]`, `char json[128]`, `snprintf` throughout, zero `String`, zero heap. Both also carry lazy-resolution guards so idle laps cost nothing. These were prime P7 suspects (per-event churn on the hottest loop) and they are clean.
- **`CONFIG_SPIRAM_MALLOC_RESERVE_INTERNAL=32768`** in both `sdkconfig` and `sdkconfig.esp32` — 32 KB of internal DRAM is held back from PSRAM-preferring `malloc()` so internal-only and DMA requests can still be served under pressure. The config-level mitigation for P6 is in place on both targets.
- **`cmd_memreport --json` (`System_Utils.cpp:3979-4010`) is correct throughout** — `ESP.getHeapSize()` used directly as the DRAM total, and `heapCaps.internalFree` / `internalLargest` / `dmaFree` / `dmaLargest` all emitted with the right caps. This is the surface an app or the web UI would consume.
- **`heapLogSummary()` (`HardwareOne.cpp:1192-1215`) gets the cap right and documents why.** It uses `MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL|MALLOC_CAP_8BIT` for largest-free-block with a comment recording the earlier bug: "MALLOC_CAP_8BIT alone matches PSRAM too… (was returning ~1.9 MB PSRAM)." Evidence the project already learned this lesson once.
- **`printMemoryReport()` computes internal fragmentation correctly and for the right pool.** `System_Utils.cpp:3499-3512` uses `MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL|MALLOC_CAP_8BIT` for both free and largest, prints `frag=%2u%%` as `100 - (largest*100)/free`, and reports the DMA pool separately. Its comment (:3493-3498) states the P6 mechanism precisely: "when it diverges from total free, the heap is fragmented and big allocs will fail even with apparent headroom." `[MEMSAMPLE]`'s own fragmentation line (`System_MemoryMonitor.cpp:213-220`) uses the same correct formula.
- /api/cli and /api/cli/batch — by far the highest-volume web command surface — do NOT execute inline. handleCLICommand (WebServer_Server.cpp:3254) goes through submitAndExecuteSync, which allocates an ExecReq, xQueueSends it to gCmdExecQ and blocks on a binary semaphore (System_Utils.cpp:4906-4935), so the command runs on cmd_exec_task. The serialization invariant holds for this path. Its timeout path is also correct: on a 60 s timeout it sets r->abandoned rather than freeing, explicitly handing ownership to cmd_exec_task (:4932) — no use-after-free, no double free.
- All 7 `micros()` sites (System_Maps.cpp:1303-1305, System_I2C_Manager.h:339-390) take unsigned differences over microsecond-to-millisecond spans, far below the 71.6-minute micros() wrap. All 9 `esp_timer_get_time()` sites use int64 (HardwareOne.cpp:1027/1033, System_LLM.cpp:405-406, System_Notifications.cpp:800/891, System_Utils.cpp:752/764) — no 32-bit truncation.
- All four G2 pending-deadline checks guard the sentinel before the signed cast: `gWifiPendingDeadlineMs > 0 && (int32_t)(millis() - gWifiPendingDeadlineMs) < 0` at G2_Page_Network.cpp:223-224, 580-581, 869-870, 1032-1033. I specifically checked whether a 0 deadline plus >24.8-day uptime could make `(int32_t)millis() < 0` read as "pending forever" — the `> 0` guard prevents it in all four.
- All four partition CSVs are arithmetically exact. Parsed and summed every row: no_sr_16mb and sr_16mb both end at exactly 0x1000000; no_sr_8mb and sr_8mb both end at exactly 0x800000. No overlaps, no gaps, no overruns, app partitions 64 KB-aligned, data partitions 4 KB-aligned, and CONFIG_PARTITION_TABLE_OFFSET=0x8000 with MD5 leaves 0x9000 clear for nvs. (The problems I found are in the DOCS that describe these tables, and in the SR factory sizing — not in the tables' internal consistency.)
- All map readers other than the one web handler are correct. OLED_Mode_Map.cpp:385 (drawFeatures), :1696 (mapSearch) and :1863 (routesList) take MapCacheGuard with explicit comments naming the free they are protecting against. The remaining getCurrentMap() references in OLED_Mode_Map.cpp (:281, :707, :1110, :1360, :1587, :1812, :2227) and in G2_Glasses.cpp (:17693, :17841) touch only map.valid, map.header.* and map.nameCount — embedded scalars, never the heap-allocated names[] or tileDir[].
- All micros() usage is wrap-safe. Only 7 call sites exist (System_Maps.cpp:1303-1305, System_I2C_Manager.h:339/346/371/375/390) and every one is `micros() - start` unsigned subtraction stored into uint32_t. There is not a single absolute `micros() > deadline` comparison. This matters disproportionately because micros() wraps every 71.6 minutes, not 49.7 days.
- ArduinoJson deserialization is not a stack hazard on any task. parseVariant is genuinely recursive but its frame is only 64-96 B depending on the Reader instantiation, and ARDUINOJSON_DEFAULT_NESTING_LIMIT is 10 (hardwareone_libs/ArduinoJson/src/ArduinoJson/Configuration.hpp:93, unoverridden), bounding the recursion at under 1 KB. Deeply-nested attacker-supplied JSON cannot walk a task off its stack.
- Automation condition variables are resolved on the condition side only — there is no variable substitution INTO the action string. `evaluateCondition` (System_Automation.cpp:2566) parses `sensor op value` into three stack buffers and compares against live sensor reads; the THEN/ELSE command text is copied verbatim out of the original string (:3419, :3434) and queued unchanged. I found no `%var%`, `${}`, or templating pass anywhere on the action path.
- BATTERY_VBUS_SENSE_PIN 34 on the FeatherS3 is used as a plain digital input (System_Battery.cpp:70 pinMode INPUT, :80 digitalRead) — not as an ADC channel. That is correct: GPIO34 is not an ADC pin at all on ESP32-S3, so no ADC2/WiFi interaction is created by the VBUS sense path.
- Boot autostart commands do not run on the main task's 8 KB stack. runUnifiedSystemCommand (System_Utils.cpp:4957-4977) routes through submitAndExecuteSync to cmd_exec_task, so opencamera/openmic/openhttp/llmload/openmqtt execute on the stack that was deliberately sized for them. Measured main-task frames from the shipped ELF: hardwareone_setup 544 B, hardwareone_loop 560 B.
- Boot recovery ordering is correct: cleanupLogOrphan runs for the six registered logs (System_Filesystem.cpp:94-99) BEFORE the generic orphan .tmp sweep (:136-170), so the sweep cannot delete a .tmp that was the only surviving copy of a log.
- Boot recovery ordering is correct: cleanupLogOrphan x6 (System_Filesystem.cpp:94-99) runs BEFORE the generic .tmp sweep (:136-170), so a recoverable rotation orphan is promoted before the sweep could delete it. Getting this backwards would silently destroy log data on every boot.
- Both scan paths arbitrate correctly. `scanBusForDevices` and `scanBusForDevicesSmart` (System_I2C.cpp:1262, 1314) take the per-bus mutex per probe rather than for the whole 126-address sweep, and use `PollPauseGuard` (RAII), so the pause depth is balanced on every return path including the early `return` when the phase-1 lock fails.
- CONFIG_BT_ALLOCATION_FROM_SPIRAM_FIRST is genuinely set and genuinely effective. Verified live at sdkconfig:987 AND pinned in sdkconfig.defaults (with the 9a23158-regression note), so a regeneration cannot drop it again. Its only Kconfig dependency is BT_BLUEDROID_ENABLED (esp-idf/components/bt/host/bluedroid/Kconfig.in:1231-1233), which is =y via sdkconfig.defaults.esp32s3:46. CONFIG_BT_BLE_DYNAMIC_ENV_MEMORY=y (sdkconfig:988) is likewise set and satisfied. The regression the docs describe is fixed and stays fixed. (Caveat, not a finding: the pinning comment says the mode was "fixed back to octal" — the primary board is now QUAD, so that sentence is stale even though the setting is right.)
- CONFIG_BT_ALLOCATION_FROM_SPIRAM_FIRST=y IS genuinely present in the live sdkconfig (line 987), not just in the defaults file — the commit-9a23158 regression is repaired and has stayed repaired. Its companion CONFIG_BT_BLE_DYNAMIC_ENV_MEMORY=y is also live (sdkconfig:988). This was the specific item the brief asked me to verify rather than trust.
- CONFIG_ESPTOOLPY_FLASHMODE="dio" sitting next to CONFIG_ESPTOOLPY_FLASHMODE_QIO=y is NOT a bug and should not be "fixed". IDF derives the image-header string as "dio" for both QIO and QOUT on purpose (esp-idf components/esptool_py/Kconfig.projbuild:76-84: "we use esptool.py to flash bootloader in dio mode for QIO/QOUT, bootloader then upgrades itself to quad mode during initialisation"). QIO is genuinely in effect at runtime.
- CONFIG_PM_ENABLE is not set (sdkconfig:1647), so there is no automatic light sleep or dynamic frequency scaling that could power down the radio behind the application's back. The automatic idle power-save path (HardwareOne.cpp:2238-2346) only blanks the OLED and lowers the CPU clock — it never calls esp_light_sleep_start, and the clock switch is properly drained against in-flight I2C (setCpuFrequencyDrained, :2219-2236).
- CONFIG_PM_ENABLE is off (sdkconfig:1647), so there is no DFS/light-sleep layer silently changing CPU frequency behind the known setCpuFrequencyMhz-vs-gps_task issue.
- CONFIG_SPI_FLASH_AUTO_SUSPEND posture verified as NOT SET (sdkconfig:2560) — this is the correct/default posture. Auto-suspend on S3 is chip-dependent and experimental, and none of the prerequisites (CONFIG_SPI_FLASH_SOFTWARE_RESUME, a vendor-locked chip driver) are configured. It should not be enabled as a fix for the timing findings above.
- CONFIG_SPI_FLASH_WRITE_CHUNK_SIZE=8192 (sdkconfig:2582) bounds the program-side acquire, and littlefs programs in 512 B units anyway (CACHE_SIZE=512), so page-program cache-off windows are ~1 ms — writes are not the problem, erases are. CONFIG_SPI_FLASH_DANGEROUS_WRITE_ABORTS=y and CONFIG_SPI_FLASH_BROWNOUT_RESET=y are both sane.
- CONFIG_STDATOMIC_S32C1I_SPIRAM_WORKAROUND=y is set (sdkconfig:2483), which matters for P21. The S32C1I compare-swap instruction does not work on external RAM, so without this an __atomic_* on a PSRAM-resident variable would be silently non-atomic. With it, IDF routes atomics through library stubs that take the S32C1I fast path for internal addresses and a spinlock-guarded path for PSRAM addresses (esp-idf components/newlib/priv_include/esp_stdatomic.h:52-58). The codebase's use of __atomic_add_fetch is therefore correct wherever the target lives.
- CRASH PATH - crashPanicHook (System_CrashRecord.cpp:125-173) touches no PSRAM, no heap, and no flash. It writes RTC_NOINIT_ATTR slow-RTC statics only, reads g_panic_abort_details and esp_app_get_elf_sha256_str(), and invalidates the magic first / writes it last with explicit compiler barriers. Its own forbidden-list comment (:119-124) is accurate.
- Camera XCLK generation was verified to be a real LEDC output rather than an assumption: managed_components/espressif__esp32-camera/target/xclk.c:19-63 does ledc_timer_config + ledc_channel_config on config.pin_xclk, which System_Camera_DVP.cpp:241 sets to XCLK_GPIO_NUM (10). This is what makes finding 2 a genuine conflict rather than a cosmetic one.
- Camera framebuffer PSRAM is a task-context touch, not an ISR one. # CONFIG_CAMERA_PSRAM_DMA is not set (sdkconfig:2800) with config.fb_location = CAMERA_FB_IN_PSRAM (System_Camera_DVP.cpp:345, :406) means GDMA lands in internal line buffers and the driver's cam_task copies into the PSRAM framebuffer. P2 and P4 are clean on this path. XIAO-only in any case.
- Core dump to flash is disabled (CONFIG_ESP_COREDUMP_ENABLE_TO_NONE=y, sdkconfig:1903-1905). No panic-context flash write exists from that path.
- Coredump config is internally consistent even though it is disabled: CONFIG_ESP_COREDUMP_ENABLE_TO_NONE=y and no coredump partition exists in any of the four CSVs. Enabling coredump-to-flash without adding the partition would be a hard init failure, so the two halves agree. Correctly documented as a known gap in docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md:189.
- Coredump is compiled out and no coredump partition exists in any of the five CSVs (CONFIG_ESP_COREDUMP_ENABLE_TO_NONE=y, sdkconfig:1905). Confirmed, but already documented in PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md:189, R1_RING_DISCONNECT_INVESTIGATION_2026-07-28.md:131-132 and CRASH_HISTORY_DESIGN.md:35 — not re-filed.
- Dual-core is correctly configured for the documented core-split policy: CONFIG_FREERTOS_UNICORE is not set, and every radio/system task is pinned to core 0 by config as the policy requires — ESP_WIFI_TASK_PINNED_TO_CORE_0, BT_CTRL_PINNED_TO_CORE=0, BT_BLUEDROID_PINNED_TO_CORE=0, ESP_TIMER_TASK_AFFINITY_CPU0, ESP_TIMER_ISR_AFFINITY_CPU0, ESP_MAIN_TASK_AFFINITY_CPU0. (arduino_events is the exception, reported separately.)
- Duplicate create sites are all properly guarded: sensor_queue_task (HardwareOne.cpp:1652 + System_I2C.cpp:3052) both gate on !queueProcessorTask; g2_wifi_pending (G2_Page_Network.cpp:1134 + :1360) both gate on !gWifiPendingTaskActive under the single tap-dispatcher task; g2_img_probe's two sites are mutually exclusive by construction.
- Every web settings mutation is serialized through cmd_exec_task. handleCLICommand and handleCliBatch call submitAndExecuteSync (WebServer_Server.cpp:3254, :5093), which queues to cmd_exec_task (System_Utils.cpp:4764-4823) rather than executing inline; grep confirms zero setSetting / writeSettingsJson call sites in any Web*.cpp or Web*.h. The httpd task is a gSettings reader only.
- Exactly one IRAM_ATTR and zero DRAM_ATTR exist in first-party code (System_CrashRecord.cpp:93). recon_isr.md section 0 reports zero IRAM_ATTR -- that count is now stale, because System_CrashRecord.cpp is a new untracked file. Corollary for P3: there is no first-party IRAM function reading a flash-resident const table, because the one IRAM function's only const input is a caller-supplied char*, and it runs with the cache enabled.
- ExecReq (System_CommandTypes.h:104), at 2048 + 4096 bytes plus context, is never stack-allocated — both submit paths (System_Utils.cpp:4791 and :4891) allocate it from PSRAM with an explicit comment saying why.
- Filesystem access before initMutexes() is unlocked but provably safe. readSettingsJson (HardwareOne.cpp:1345), writeSettingsJson (:1355), readDebugJson (:1363) and the FsLockGuard at :1340 all run before gFsMutex exists at :1469, and every guard in System_Mutex.cpp degrades to a no-op on a null handle. At that point in boot only the IDF `main` task exists — cmd_exec is created at :1514, sensor_queue at :1611, debug_out inside initDebugSystem at :1476 — so there is no second FS writer to race.
- Flash erase cannot trip the interrupt watchdog: CONFIG_SPI_FLASH_YIELD_DURING_ERASE=y with ERASE_YIELD_DURATION_MS=20 and ERASE_YIELD_TICKS=1, so a LittleFS format or log rotation on the main task releases the cache-disable periodically instead of holding it past the 1500 ms CONFIG_ESP_INT_WDT_TIMEOUT_MS budget.
- FreeRTOS core config matches the SMP assumptions the concurrency docs rely on. CONFIG_FREERTOS_HZ=1000 (Arduino requirement, as sdkconfig.defaults states), CONFIG_FREERTOS_UNICORE not set, CONFIG_FREERTOS_NUMBER_OF_CORES=2, CONFIG_FREERTOS_USE_TRACE_FACILITY + GENERATE_RUN_TIME_STATS on (so uxTaskGetSystemState/vTaskList/perftop actually work), CONFIG_FREERTOS_PLACE_FUNCTIONS_INTO_FLASH off on S3 (it is deliberately on only for the IRAM-tight ESP32-classic build).
- FsLockGuard and MapCacheGuard implement same-task reentrancy correctly: both check xSemaphoreGetMutexHolder against the current task, skip the take, leave `held` false, and therefore skip the give in the destructor (System_Mutex.cpp:84-97, :119-134). Nested guards on one task cannot release the outer lock early.
- G2 BLE transmit pacing is driven by the MEASURED connection interval rather than a fixed throughput assumption — g2LinkIsSlow / effectiveConnIntTicks / pickImgEnvelopeGapMs (G2_Glasses.cpp:15690-15734), with an explicit comment documenting why the earlier ring-keyed heuristic was wrong. Controller over-submit (esp_ble_gattc_write_char rc=-1) is handled by aborting the burst and draining, never by an in-lock retry, with the reasoning and the field evidence recorded inline (7506-7528). Lock order bleCentralTx → writeMutex is stated and consistent (7550-7576, 7603-7609), and heartbeats treat gate contention as a clean skip rather than a TX wedge (7778-7813).
- HAL_Display's SPI-TFT pin blocks are unreachable, which matters because they hardcode classic-ESP32 pins (DISPLAY_SPI_MOSI 23, SCLK 18, MISO 19, CS 5, DC 16, RST 17) that would be wrong or nonexistent on S3. DISPLAY_TYPE is 1 (SSD1306/I2C) at System_BuildConfig.h:152, so only the I2C branch compiles, and the two pinMode(DISPLAY_BL_PIN, ...) calls at HAL_Display.cpp:107 and :127 are guarded by #if DISPLAY_BL_PIN >= 0 — no pinMode(-1) is reachable.
- Health tracking after an unplug behaves sanely and does not spin. A removed device NACKs (status 2) → 3 consecutive errors → `degraded` → `executeTransaction` skips it at the `isDegraded()` gate → `isDegraded()` self-clears 30 s after the last error to allow a retry → on re-plug the first success calls `recordSuccess()`, which clears `degraded`, resets `consecutiveErrors`, halves the ratcheted `adaptiveTimeoutMs` back toward base, and emits a recovery log. The adaptive-timeout decay in particular is correct and closes the 'one storm leaves a permanent 5 s mutex wait' hole its own comment describes.
- IRAM_ATTR discipline is effectively perfect: exactly ONE IRAM_ATTR in all of components/hardwareone/ + main/ (System_CrashRecord.cpp:93, untracked), and parsing the .iram0.text block of build/hardwareone-idf.map shows zero contributions from libhardwareone.a or libarduino.a. There is no first-party IRAM creep to police.
- LITTLEFS INTERNAL BUFFERS - not in PSRAM. CONFIG_LITTLEFS_MALLOC_STRATEGY_DEFAULT=y and CONFIG_LITTLEFS_MALLOC_STRATEGY_SPIRAM is not set (sdkconfig:2882/2884), and the buffers are read_size/prog_size=128, cache_size=512, lookahead=128 - two orders of magnitude below CONFIG_SPIRAM_MALLOC_ALWAYSINTERNAL=16384, so they land internal. A PSRAM prog buffer here would have been a genuine cache-off fault.
- LittleFS.format() — a whole-partition erase of 10604 KB — is reachable only from the boot mount-failure path (System_Filesystem.cpp:60-69). There is no runtime `format` command for the internal FS (System_VFS.cpp:994 and System_SensorLogging.cpp:1230 are SD-format and sensorlog-file paths respectively). No multi-second whole-device stall can be triggered from the CLI/web.
- Login-lockout comparison FORMS are all wrap-safe (`(now - windowStart) > WINDOW` unsigned, `(long)(now - lockedUntil) >= 0` signed against a <=30 min lockout). The defect I found there is ordering/policy, not rollover.
- MapCore's own lifecycle functions are correct. loadMapFile (System_Maps.cpp:341-346) and unloadMap (:795-800) both take MapCacheGuard BEFORE FsLockGuard in the documented global order (map then FS), the guard is reentrant so the nested unload inside loadMapFile no-ops, and loadMapFile holds it across the entire unload-plus-reallocate so no renderer can observe a half-built cache.
- NEOPIXEL_AVAILABLE is defined as (NEOPIXEL_PIN_DEFAULT >= 0) (System_NeoPixel.cpp:19), not (> 0), so the Feather ESP32 V2's legitimate NeoPixel on GPIO0 is not mistaken for the 'no NeoPixel' sentinel. A >0 test there would have silently disabled the LED and, transitively, the STEMMA QT power rail on that board.
- NO SETTING VALUE BECOMES A COMMAND VERB AT AUTOSTART. All seven boot autostart calls are string literals (`opencamera`, `srstart`, `openmic` x2, `openhttp`, `openmqtt` — HardwareOne.cpp:1923-1986). The single non-literal is `"llmload " + gSettings.llmDefaultModel` (:1974), where the setting can only alter llmload's argument; the verb is fixed and the `remote:`/`@` prefix test at System_Utils.cpp:4569 examines the start of the line, which is always `llmload`.
- NVS is a non-issue for both wear and stall: exactly one application namespace ("bootstate"/"bootcount", System_BootState.cpp), written once per boot. `Preferences prefs` at HardwareOne.cpp:381 has zero uses. P36/P37 do not bite here.
- NVS wear (P36) is a non-issue on this firmware, and I confirmed it rather than assuming it. There is exactly ONE application NVS user in the whole tree: System_BootState.cpp, namespace "bootstate", key "bootcount" (u32), written once per boot from loadAndIncrementBootSeq plus an on-demand `bootcount reset`. The Preferences object declared at HardwareOne.cpp:381 has zero uses — a dead global, not a persistence path. Nothing writes NVS per-event or per-loop.
- Nine of the twelve i2csensor_*.cpp drivers use SensorCacheGuard (ano_encoder 7 sites, bno055 5, sths34pf80 4, apds9960/ds3231/pa1010d/rda5807/seesaw/vl53l4cx 3 each). Of the three with zero, max17048 and pca9685 have no cache struct and no shared reader state at all — nothing to guard. Only mlx90640 has a declared cache mutex with an unguarded reader, filed above.
- No FreeRTOS API misuse from ISR context. No task is created, no mutex taken, and no blocking call made from any interrupt handler; the debug path correctly branches on xPortInIsrContext() to use xQueueReceiveFromISR / xQueueSendFromISR (System_Debug.cpp:744-768) and refuses heap allocation in ISR context (:415-:420).
- No I2C access from ISR context anywhere in components/hardwareone — zero IRAM_ATTR functions in System_I2C.cpp, System_I2C_Manager.cpp or any i2csensor_*.cpp, and no Wire/i2c call reachable from a registered handler. P14/P15 are clean for this subsystem.
- No I2C access from any ISR. The only `IRAM_ATTR` function in the whole application tree is `crashCopyStr` (System_CrashRecord.cpp:93), which touches no peripheral. Every I2C transaction in this codebase runs from a normal FreeRTOS task context.
- No NEW gSettings writer was introduced on the web surface. Every gSettings access in Web*.cpp and Web*.h is a read — a grep for `gSettings.<field> =` and `setSetting(` across all web translation units returns only unrelated JavaScript string literals in WebPage_Games.h. The httpd task reads gSettings (handleSettingsGet at WebServer_Server.cpp:2523 via buildSettingsJsonDoc, and the MQTT/Bond/LLM pages) but never writes it.
- No OTA and no core dump exist in this image (no ota partitions in any of the four partition CSVs, zero esp_ota_* calls, CONFIG_ESP_COREDUMP_ENABLE_TO_NONE=y at sdkconfig:1905). Application NVS use is exactly one namespace/key written exactly once per boot (System_BootState.cpp:44-53). That eliminates three of the four flash-writer classes named in the assignment — the entire flash-write surface of this firmware is LittleFS plus one 4-byte NVS commit per boot.
- No SettingEntry writes through a pointer of the wrong WIDTH. The mechanical type cross-check found zero rows where a uint8_t/uint16_t/float/bool/String field is tagged with a wider type - the 2026-05-18 heap-corruption class of bug is absent. The only mismatches are 13 signed/unsigned int32 aliases, filed separately as a NOTE.
- No `esp_err_t` (or `int`) result assigned to a variable and then never read. I scripted a scan over all 339 .cpp/.h files for `esp_err_t x = esp_*(...)` / `nvs_*` / `i2c_*` / `httpd_*` followed by 45 lines with no use of `x` — zero hits. Where returns are captured in this codebase, they are used. The problem is entirely bare-statement calls, not dead variables.
- No `gets`, no `alloca`, no `vsprintf` anywhere in components/hardwareone (grepped and hand-checked). No `strcat` outside OLED_UI.cpp:215, which appends a 2-char literal to a stack buffer whose maxChars is bounded by DISPLAY_WIDTH.
- No application code subscribes to the Task WDT anywhere — grep for esp_task_wdt_add / esp_task_wdt_reset / esp_task_wdt_delete / esp_task_wdt_deinit / enableLoopWDT across components/hardwareone/ and main/ returns zero matches, and CONFIG_AUTOSTART_ARDUINO is off. This exactly matches docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §5.1's claim; re-verified, still true, nothing has changed.
- No filesystem or flash operation occurs inside any critical section. All 11 portMUX sites in components/hardwareone were checked: every critical section is scalar updates or a bounded memcpy (longest is ringTxQEnqueue's ~1.9 KB shift at G2_Ring.cpp:145-184). A flash op inside taskENTER_CRITICAL would deadlock, since spi_flash_disable_interrupts_caches_and_other_cpu() needs esp_ipc_call_nonblocking to succeed (cache_utils.c:162) — the codebase never gets near that.
- No first-party DMA-fed plain-malloc buffer exists — this closes the audit prerequisite docs/HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT_MECHANICS.md §5 named as "the main thing standing between this analysis and a safe change". MALLOC_CAP_DMA appears in components/hardwareone/ only inside diagnostic readouts (System_Utils.cpp:3568/3569/4073/4074), never in an allocation. The PDM mic path passes ordinary buffers to i2s_channel_read (HAL_Audio.cpp:279, System_Microphone.cpp), which memcpys out of the driver's own DMA descriptors. There is no SPI display, no SD-over-SPI DMA buffer, and the NeoPixel pixel buffer is a few tens of bytes (well under any plausible ALWAYSINTERNAL value). Camera is compiled out on this board.
- No flash operation can trip either watchdog on this build. INT WDT (1500 ms, CHECK_CPU1=y) is fed from the FreeRTOS systick ISR, which IDF allocates with ESP_INTR_FLAG_IRAM (freertos/port_systick.c:72) so esp_intr_noniram_disable() cannot mask it; the feed itself (int_wdt.c:104 tick_hook) is ESP_SYSTEM_IRAM_ATTR and FreeRTOS is fully IRAM-resident (CONFIG_FREERTOS_IN_IRAM=y, PLACE_FUNCTIONS_INTO_FLASH not set). TWDT (5 s) has only IDLE0/IDLE1 subscribed — zero esp_task_wdt_add calls in the firmware, and Arduino leaves loopTaskWDTEnabled false — and CONFIG_ESP_TASK_WDT_PANIC is not set, so a trip prints and does not reset. This is the answer to the P40 'can that trip a watchdog' question: no.
- No long I2C timeout exists that could starve a core. The wire-level timeout is fixed at 100 ms (`wire->setTimeOut(100)`, System_I2C_Manager.cpp, with a comment explicitly tying it to the 1500 ms INT_WDT). The `timeoutMs` argument on `i2cDeviceTransaction*` is a MUTEX-acquire timeout, consumed as `xSemaphoreTake(mutex, pdMS_TO_TICKS(device->adaptiveTimeoutMs))` (System_I2C_Manager.h:345), i.e. a blocking wait rather than a spin — so even the 5000 ms value at i2csensor_bno055.cpp:356 costs nothing in scheduler terms.
- No narrow-type truncation of timestamps. Grep for uint16_t/uint8_t/int16_t fields with Ms/Time/At/Millis names returned only System_Notifications.h:55-56 (`durMs`, `cooldownMs`) — both are DURATIONS bounded well under 65535, not timestamps.
- No nested-transaction deadlock in the drivers that are actually compiled into the current FeatherS3[D] config. I hand-checked every candidate my scanner flagged: seesaw (i2csensor_seesaw.cpp:312/317/323/335), PCA9685 (:256/267), GPS (:153/174) and the OLED boot path (OLED_Utils.cpp:4654/4687, :6812/6824) are all SEQUENTIAL transactions, not nested ones. Only the FM radio and presence drivers — both compiled out today — actually nest.
- No portMUX spinlock in this codebase is shared with an interrupt handler, so taskENTER_CRITICAL (rather than the ...FROM_ISR variant) is the correct primitive at all 35 first-party critical-section sites. The longest bodies are bounded struct copies (System_Events.cpp:314-310 copies at most 48 x ~164 B; G2_Ring.cpp:125-184 shifts at most 7 x 273 B) — microseconds against a 1500 ms Int WDT (CONFIG_ESP_INT_WDT_TIMEOUT_MS=1500).
- No recursion in any firmware task's reachable call graph. Built a call graph from the ELF disassembly (35,864 resolved edges, including l32r+callx8 far calls) and ran cycle detection from every task entry — no cycles reached from firmware code. All recursion in the image is confined to library parsers with their own depth limits.
- No self-deadlock on the command queue. Automations deliberately use the async submit path (queueAutomationSubCommand, System_Automation.cpp:116) with the reason spelled out at :88 — "avoids deadlock when already on cmd_exec task". I checked all runUnifiedSystemCommand / submitAndExecuteSync callers: the autostart block (HardwareOne.cpp:1879-1942) runs in setup() on loopTask, MQTT on the MQTT task, SR voice commands on sr_task, web on httpd, OLED on loopTask. Nothing calls the blocking submit from cmd_exec_task itself.
- No self-deadlock via the synchronous command path. `submitAndExecuteSync` (System_Utils.cpp:4764) has no "am I already cmd_exec_task" check and would block 60 s if called from a handler, but I traced all 11 call sites and none runs on cmd_exec_task: HardwareOne.cpp:1879-1942 and :2590 and OLED_Utils.cpp:3010/3022 are the main task, WebServer_Server.cpp:3254/5093 the httpd task, System_MQTT.cpp:544 the main loop's mqttTick, System_ESPSR.cpp:206 srTask (documented as deliberate at :182-187). The `extern runUnifiedSystemCommand` declared at System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:68 is unused, and automations dispatch through `submitCommandAsync` instead (System_Automation.cpp:116).
- No signed 32-bit timestamp storage exists anywhere in the tree. I grepped every assignment of millis()/micros() to a typed variable and every declaration of a *Ms/*Time/*At field: all are uint32_t or unsigned long (32-bit UNSIGNED on Xtensa), never int/long/int32_t. The ~24.8-day signed-timestamp class the brief warned about does not exist as a storage bug — the single instance (WebServer_Events.cpp:249) is a signed CAST at the comparison, not a signed variable.
- No signed 32-bit timestamps exist anywhere in the tree. A PCRE lookbehind sweep for `(int|long|int32_t|int16_t|int8_t|float|double|time_t) <name> = millis()/micros()` across all 339 .cpp/.h files returns zero hits, as does a search for `(int)millis()`, `(long)millis()`, `(int32_t)millis()` and float/double casts. The ~24.8-day signed-rollover failure mode described in P42 does not exist here. Every `unsigned long now = millis()` local (about 200 of them) is 32-bit unsigned on Xtensa, i.e. identical to uint32_t — no 64-bit/32-bit mismatch either.
- No task stack is placed in PSRAM anywhere in the firmware except the single documented g2_img_probe fallback (G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp:1108). CONFIG_SPIRAM_ALLOW_STACK_EXTERNAL_MEMORY=y is set in sdkconfig:3229 (it is the prerequisite for that fallback), but grep of every xTaskCreateStatic* site confirms the only other static-stack task, llm_gen (System_LLM.cpp:275), allocates its stack and TCB with heap_caps_malloc(MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL) as required.
- No task stack other than the g2_img_probe fallback is in PSRAM. System_LLM.cpp:265-270 allocates its 12 KB worker stack and TCB with MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL and documents the rule at :173-175; every other create site passes a plain depth to xTaskCreate*, which allocates internal. The only two xTaskCreateStatic call sites in the tree are System_LLM.cpp:275 (internal, correct) and G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp:1114 (the finding).
- No unsigned underflow in the G2 image-probe pacing slices. `leftMs = sliceEndMs - millis()` (G2_Glasses.cpp:22213/22394/22606) is computed after the loop condition already proved `millis() < sliceEndMs`, and even if millis() ticks past between the two reads the huge underflowed value is immediately clamped by `leftMs < kSliceMs ? leftMs : kSliceMs` to 50 ms. Safe.
- Non-IRAM ISRs are MASKED during flash ops, never faulted -- so P1's classic 'Cache Error panic' cannot happen here. esp_intr_noniram_disable() (esp-idf/components/esp_hw_support/intr_alloc.c:959) masks every interrupt not allocated with ESP_INTR_FLAG_IRAM for the duration. Every peripheral interrupt this firmware causes is in that set: I2C (i2c_driver_install(port, mode, 0,0,0), esp32-hal-i2c.c:141), I2S PDM (# CONFIG_I2S_ISR_IRAM_SAFE is not set, sdkconfig:1298), camera LCD_CAM + GDMA (sdkconfig:2804, :1553), RMT/NeoPixel (sdkconfig:1337), UART0 (sdkconfig:1382). The real exposure is lost or delayed interrupts, not a crash.
- OLED DISPLAY PATH - the SSD1306 framebuffer is internal (Adafruit_SSD1306 is constructed with global operator new at HAL_Display.cpp:64 and its 1 KB buffer is malloc'd inside begin(), both far under the 16 KB ALWAYSINTERNAL threshold), matching the docs/HEAP_OFFLOAD_SWEEP section-4 exclusion for gDisplay. The PSRAM map double-buffers are memcpy'd into it by the CPU; the I2C leg uses the legacy driver with a non-IRAM ISR (intr_alloc_flags=0), which is masked during flash ops rather than faulting. The board-gated ST7789/ILI9341 SPI branches (HAL_Display.cpp:87-120) are Adafruit_GFX direct-write with no framebuffer, so no PSRAM buffer reaches SPI DMA on those boards either.
