- OLED and G2 settings editors resolve the save command identically — no cmdKey/jsonKey fallback drift. Both include System_SettingsEditorCore.h (OLED_SettingsEditor.cpp:16, G2_Page_Settings.cpp:50). `settingsEditorCommandName()` (System_SettingsEditorCore.cpp:161-164) returns `entry->cmdKey` and nothing else; OLED's `setSettingValue`/`setSettingValueStr` (OLED_SettingsEditor.cpp:160/:179) also read cmdKey only, with an explicit comment recording that the jsonKey fallback was removed. Both surfaces therefore fire the same command for a given setting, and the registry audit confirms all 276 cmdKey bindings resolve.
- Ordinary xTaskCreate cannot put a stack in PSRAM even with CONFIG_FREERTOS_TASK_CREATE_ALLOW_EXT_MEM=y. Traced esp-idf/components/freertos/heap_idf.c: pvPortMalloc/pvPortMallocStack allocate with portFREERTOS_HEAP_CAPS = MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL|MALLOC_CAP_8BIT (heap_idf.c:43,56). The flag only relaxes xPortcheckValidStackMem (heap_idf.c:97-106), i.e. the validity check on a caller-supplied xTaskCreateStatic buffer. So the "never put task stacks in PSRAM" rule is not silently violated by normal task creation anywhere in the tree — only by the one explicit MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM static-stack site reported above.
- P1 has no first-party attack surface at all: `grep -rn 'IRAM_ATTR|DRAM_ATTR' components/hardwareone/ main/` (excluding EXT_RAM_BSS_ATTR) returns exactly 0 hits, and the firmware registers zero interrupt handlers of its own (no esp_intr_alloc, gpio_isr_handler_add, attachInterrupt, timer_isr_callback_add). Every ISR in the image belongs to IDF, the Arduino core or a managed component. Confirms the recon_isr map.
- P12 (task function returns instead of vTaskDelete) — CLEAN, verified independently of the recon map. Parsed all 40 task-entry function bodies across 30 files: every one either loops forever (for(;;) / while(true)) or reaches vTaskDelete on every exit path. The three bare `return;` statements that a naive scan flags (System_Microphone.cpp:259, System_Microphone.cpp:1132, and one in System_ESPSR.cpp srTask) each sit on the line immediately after vTaskDelete(NULL) on the same path — unreachable dead code, not a fallthrough. No task entry can fall off its end.
- P18 classic failure mode is structurally absent: the bootloader's RTC watchdog is disabled by IDF before app_main can run. `ESP_SYSTEM_INIT_FN(init_disable_rtc_wdt, SECONDARY, BIT(0), 999)` in esp_system/startup_funcs.c:177-186 is compiled in because `CONFIG_BOOTLOADER_WDT_DISABLE_IN_USER_CODE` is not set, and startup.c:216 runs do_secondary_init() before esp_startup_start_app(). No amount of slow work inside hardwareone_setup() can trip it.
- P19 is structurally absent. `portDISABLE_INTERRUPTS`, `portENABLE_INTERRUPTS`, `noInterrupts()`, `interrupts()` and `taskDISABLE_INTERRUPTS` have ZERO occurrences across all of components/hardwareone/ and main/. There is no site anywhere in first-party code that mistakes interrupt masking for a cross-core lock.
- P2 checked and clear for the radio stacks despite aggressive PSRAM settings. CONFIG_SPIRAM_TRY_ALLOCATE_WIFI_LWIP=y (sdkconfig:1683) pushes WiFi/LWIP dynamic buffers toward PSRAM, but CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_STATIC_TX_BUFFER=y with CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_TX_BUFFER_TYPE=0 keeps the DMA-visible TX buffers static/internal, and the dynamic RX buffers are consumed by the WiFi task rather than the MAC ISR. CONFIG_BT_ALLOCATION_FROM_SPIRAM_FIRST=y applies to the Bluedroid host (task context), not the controller's ISR-side pool.
- P20 is structurally absent. `vTaskSuspendAll`, `xTaskResumeAll`, `vTaskSuspend(` and `vTaskResume(` have ZERO occurrences across components/hardwareone/ and main/. Scheduler suspension is never used for data protection, and no task is ever suspended as a synchronization device.
- P21 spot-check: the LLM cross-task publish flags are correctly volatile. gLLMResultLen and gLLMResultDone (System_LLM.cpp:156-157) are `volatile`, written by llm_gen on core 1 and read by the HTTP/chat drain path, with the give/take notification pair providing the barrier. The -O2 hoisting hazard I was looking for is not present at this site.
- P24 mechanically verified: I parsed the argument list of every xTaskCreate / xTaskCreatePinnedToCore / xTaskCreateStatic* / xTaskCreateLogged call in components/hardwareone/. All 41 firmware sites pass an explicit core argument. There is zero use of tskNO_AFFINITY anywhere in firmware code. No pthread_create, std::thread or esp_pthread anywhere in the component.
- P25 — Only ONE I2C driver is linked. IDF is 5.5.1, so esp32-hal-i2c.c (legacy driver/i2c.h) is fully excluded by `#if ESP_IDF_VERSION < 5.4.0` and esp32-hal-i2c-ng.c (driver/i2c_master.h) is the active implementation; esp32-camera picks driver/sccb-ng.c for the same reason (CONFIG_SCCB_HARDWARE_I2C_DRIVER_NEW=y). Verified against the linked ELF: nm shows zero occurrences of i2c_driver_install, i2c_master_cmd_begin or i2c_cmd_link_create, and i2c_new_master_bus / i2c_master_transmit / i2c_master_probe / i2c_master_bus_rm_device are all present. Both hal .c files are listed in components/arduino/CMakeLists.txt:36-37, but the version guards make exactly one active — the 'both linked' failure mode P25 warns about is genuinely absent. The manager also correctly removed the old i2c_filter_enable() calls that would have conflicted (System_I2C_Manager.cpp:23-26).
- P26 — The bus-recovery routine itself is textbook-correct for a slave holding SDA low: wire->end() to release the peripheral, pinMode(SCL, OUTPUT) + pinMode(SDA, INPUT_PULLUP), up to 9 SCL pulses with an early exit the moment digitalRead(SDA) goes high, then a hand-generated STOP (SDA low -> SCL high -> SDA high), then re-begin. It also resets health only for devices on the recovered bus and re-pins bus 0's ISR to CPU1 across the recovery.
- P26 — a genuine bus-recovery routine exists and is textbook-correct. `performBusRecovery` (System_I2C_Manager.cpp:340) does `wire->end()` → `pinMode(SCL, OUTPUT)` / `pinMode(SDA, INPUT_PULLUP)` → up to 9 SCL pulses with early exit the moment SDA releases → an explicit STOP condition → re-begin at the bus's default clock → per-bus health reset. It is per-bus (correct mutex, correct pins, correct Wire instance), and `i2c_master_bus_reset` is deliberately not used — the manual clock-out is strictly stronger, since a controller-side reset cannot free a slave stuck mid-byte.
- P27 — Every actual I2C hardware transfer has a bounded timeout. initBus() calls wire->setTimeOut(100) for each bus (System_I2C_Manager.cpp:290) with a correct rationale (100 ms is well under CONFIG_ESP_INT_WDT_TIMEOUT_MS=1500). _timeOutMillis is a TwoWire member that survives end()/begin(), so the 100 ms value persists across bus recovery even though performBusRecovery only re-applies setClock. TwoWire passes it straight into i2cWrite/i2cRead/i2cWriteReadNonStop, which pass it into i2c_master_transmit / i2c_master_receive / i2c_master_transmit_receive / i2c_master_probe. No portMAX_DELAY reaches the IDF transfer API anywhere in this codebase.
- P27 — The manager's bus-mutex acquisition is always bounded: xSemaphoreTake(mutex, pdMS_TO_TICKS(device->adaptiveTimeoutMs)) in executeTransaction, 2000 ms in performBusRecovery and in both scan phases, 100 ms in registerDevice, timeoutMs in i2cProbeAddress/i2cConfirmRead. adaptiveTimeoutMs is both capped at 5000 (recordError TIMEOUT branch) and halved back toward baseTimeoutMs on every success (recordSuccess, System_I2C_Manager.cpp:788-790), so a transient storm cannot leave a device permanently holding a 5-second blocking window.
- P27 — no `portMAX_DELAY` ever reaches the I2C peripheral. `initBus` sets `wire->setTimeOut(100)` on BOTH buses (System_I2C_Manager.cpp:290), and `esp32-hal-i2c-ng.c` passes `_timeOutMillis` straight into `i2c_master_probe` (line 259), `i2c_master_transmit` (271), `i2c_master_receive` (317) and `i2c_master_transmit_receive` (362). The 100 ms bound is deliberately chosen under `CONFIG_ESP_INT_WDT_TIMEOUT_MS` (1500), and the comment says so. An absent or unplugged device returns `ESP_ERR_NOT_FOUND`/`ESP_ERR_TIMEOUT` within 100 ms, never hangs.
- P27 — the mutex layer is bounded too. `executeTransaction` takes the bus mutex with `pdMS_TO_TICKS(device->adaptiveTimeoutMs)` (System_I2C_Manager.h:345), `performBusRecovery` uses a 2000 ms take, the scan paths use 200/2000 ms, and `setCpuFrequencyDrained` uses 2000 ms. The `portMAX_DELAY` takes that do exist (`queueMutex` in enqueue/dequeue/isInQueue/getQueuePosition, `beginBusOnCpu1`'s completion semaphore) guard short, non-blocking critical sections, not wire traffic.
- P28 — No self-deadlock in the error path. recordError()'s BUS_ERROR branch calls performBusRecovery(), which retakes the same bus mutex — and executeTransaction is careful to run probeDeviceStatus/classifyI2CError while still holding the mutex but to call recordError only AFTER xSemaphoreGive(mutex) (System_I2C_Manager.h:406-436). I traced both the void and non-void branches; the ordering is correct in both. The comment at lines 393-397 accurately describes what the code does.
- P28 — Per-bus mutexes are genuinely per-bus (busMutexes[NUM_BUSES], indexed off device->bus with a bounds clamp), and checkBusRecoveryNeeded() evaluates each bus independently, so a degradation cascade on the secondary bus can no longer tear down the healthy primary — the regression its comment describes has really been fixed.
- P28 — the self-deadlock that `recordError`→`performBusRecovery` would cause is correctly avoided. `executeTransaction` probes and classifies the failure while it still holds the bus mutex, stashes the result, releases the mutex, and only then calls `recordError` (System_I2C_Manager.h:392-436). The code matches its own comment exactly. Verified by tracing `recordError`'s BUS_ERROR branch back into `performBusRecovery`'s `xSemaphoreTake` on the same handle.
- P29 (ADC2 unusable while WiFi is active) does NOT apply: the only ESP32 ADC usage in the whole tree is System_Battery.cpp's ADC backend, and it is ADC1 throughout — adc1_config_width, adc1_config_channel_atten, adc1_get_raw, esp_adc_cal_characterize(ADC_UNIT_1) at :103-108 and :127, plus the recharacterize at :606-607. Grep for adc2_/ADC2_CHANNEL/ADC_UNIT_2/adc_oneshot across components/hardwareone and main returns nothing. The analogRead() calls in i2csensor_seesaw.cpp:337-374/520-521 are the Seesaw co-processor's own ADC read over I2C, not an ESP32 ADC channel.
- P29 (ADC2 vs WiFi) is fully clean: a sweep for adc2_*, ADC_UNIT_2, adc_oneshot, touchRead and hallRead across components/hardwareone and main returns nothing. The only chip-ADC use is the legacy ADC1 path in System_Battery.cpp:103-133 (adc1_config_width, adc1_config_channel_atten, adc1_get_raw, esp_adc_cal_characterize(ADC_UNIT_1, ...)), compiled only when BATTERY_BACKEND_ADC=1, which is true on exactly one board (Adafruit Feather ESP32 V2). The analogRead() calls in i2csensor_seesaw.cpp:337-521 are the Seesaw's own ADC over I2C, not the ESP32's. The WiFi/ADC2 arbiter conflict therefore cannot occur on any supported board.
- P29 (ADC2 vs WiFi) — CLEAN on every board. The only ADC use in the firmware is BATTERY_BACKEND_ADC, and it is ADC1 only: adc1_config_width / adc1_config_channel_atten / adc1_get_raw / esp_adc_cal_characterize(ADC_UNIT_1) at components/hardwareone/System_Battery.cpp:101-134, on BATTERY_ADC_CHANNEL = ADC1_CHANNEL_7 (System_Battery.h:12). Per System_BuildConfig.h that backend is enabled on exactly one board — Adafruit Feather ESP32 V2 (classic ESP32, GPIO35 = ADC1_CH7, line 804-807). Every other board is BATTERY_BACKEND_FUEL_GAUGE (FeatherS3, MAX17048 over I2C) or the USB-only stub. The FeatherS3's VBUS sense pin (GPIO34) is read with digitalRead, not the ADC. No analogRead, no adc2_*, no ADC_UNIT_2, no adc_continuous anywhere in components/hardwareone (the i2csensor_seesaw.cpp analogRead calls are on the remote Seesaw MCU, not the ESP32).
- P29 (ADC2 vs WiFi) — no ADC2 usage anywhere in first-party code. Zero hits for adc2_get_raw, ADC_UNIT_2, adc_oneshot with unit 2, or a bare ESP32 analogRead() across components/hardwareone, main and third_party. The only ADC path in the firmware is System_Battery.cpp:104/127, explicitly on ADC1 (adc1_config_width / adc1_config_channel_atten / adc1_get_raw, ADC1_CHANNEL_7). The analogRead() hits in i2csensor_seesaw.cpp:337-521 are the Adafruit Seesaw co-processor's own ADC read over I2C, not the ESP32's. The hardware conflict this pitfall describes cannot occur.
- P30 (S3 strapping pins) is clean on both ESP32-S3 boards: no board block and no pinMode/digitalWrite/gpio_set_direction/gpio_set_level site assigns GPIO0, GPIO3, GPIO45 or GPIO46 as an output. The FeatherS3 map (I2C1 8/9, I2C2 16/15, NeoPixel 40, LDO2 39, VBUS 34) and both XIAO maps (I2C 5/6, LED/SD-CS 21, SD SPI 7/8/9, PDM 42/41, camera 10/40/39/48/11/12/14/16/18/17/15/38/47/13) contain none of them. The single GPIO3 touch is a read-only pinMode(3, INPUT) in cmd_sddiag (System_VFS.cpp:1155).
- P31 (octal PSRAM steals GPIO33-37) does NOT bite on the stretch-goal board: I walked every pin in the XIAO ESP32-S3 Sense block plus the hardcoded camera map in System_Camera_DVP.cpp:149-165 and none falls in 33-37. The XIAO map is octal-PSRAM safe as written.
- P34 (BLE send-path structure under contention) — the G2 fragmented-burst writer is well built for a contended radio: it takes the central TX gate and the per-temple write mutex for ONE envelope, releases both across the 15 ms inter-fragment gap, and drains queued ring TX in that gap (G2_Glasses.cpp:7681-7731). Heartbeats skipped because another peer holds the gate, or because the 50 ms take timed out, are explicitly NOT counted toward the plugin-silent threshold (:7780-7810). The 15 ms gap and the 1500 ms mutex/gate timeouts are empirically derived and documented. Nothing here assumes an uncontended radio — the problem filed above is the scan duty cycle, not the send path.
- P36 (NVS wear) is a non-issue for application data. System_BootState.cpp is the only application NVS user in the tree — one namespace ("bootstate"), one u32 key, written exactly once per boot from loadAndIncrementBootSeq. nvs_open and nvs_set return values are checked. The Preferences object declared at HardwareOne.cpp:381 has zero uses.
- P38 generic form stays refuted on this stack: truncate-in-place is NOT power-loss-unsafe on LittleFS. LFS_O_TRUNC only marks the in-RAM handle dirty; the directory-entry commit happens at sync/close, so a power cut mid-"w"-rewrite leaves the OLD file fully intact, and the allocator keeps the old blocks reserved via the committed metadata. Matches SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT.md §2 and recon_persist.md §0. Every finding above is a short-write/ENOSPC or multi-file residue, not the refuted power-loss chain.
- P4 / DMA generally: components/hardwareone and main contain ZERO MALLOC_CAP_DMA allocations and zero heap_caps_aligned_alloc calls. The only MALLOC_CAP_DMA references are read-only diagnostics (System_Utils.cpp:3501-3502, 4006-4007). No first-party buffer is handed to a DMA engine directly — every DMA-capable peripheral is reached through a driver that owns its own descriptors.
- P4 / I2S: every PSRAM audio buffer (System_ESPSR.cpp:1968/1974/1981/1987, System_ESPSR.cpp:1212/1251, System_Microphone.cpp:1127, G2_Glasses.cpp:1686) reaches the driver only through i2s_channel_read (HAL_Audio.cpp:108/279), which memcpys out of driver-owned DMA descriptors on the calling task. No PSRAM pointer is ever programmed into an I2S DMA descriptor.
- P4 / SD-SPI: PSRAM buffers written to SD (e.g. System_Camera_Video.cpp:261 writing the PSRAM index, the G2 mic WAV path) are safe. IDF detects them — sdmmc_write_sectors/sdmmc_read_sectors test esp_ptr_external_ram(src) and route to a bounced internal DMA buffer (esp-idf/components/sdmmc/sdmmc_cmd.c:446-464 and :584-602). This closes out the H5/SPI2 handoff the ISR recon map flagged as the single highest-value cross-check.
- P4 / camera: config.fb_location = CAMERA_FB_IN_PSRAM (System_Camera_DVP.cpp:345/406) is SAFE. CONFIG_CAMERA_PSRAM_DMA is not set (sdkconfig:2800), so cam_hal.c:64 sets g_psram_dma_mode=0, GDMA lands in cam_obj->dma_buffer allocated with MALLOC_CAP_DMA (cam_hal.c:522) and the descriptors with MALLOC_CAP_DMA (cam_hal.c:439); the cam task memcpys into the PSRAM framebuffer from task context. First-party code never calls cam_set_psram_mode(), and System_Camera_DVP.cpp:389-410 documents the tradeoff correctly.
- P4 CAMERA - clean and deliberate. CONFIG_CAMERA_PSRAM_DMA is not set (sdkconfig:2800), so the CAM/GDMA peripheral writes internal DRAM line buffers and the driver's cam task memcpys each completed frame into the PSRAM framebuffer. System_Camera_DVP.cpp:373-410 reads that exact symbol, sets fb_location = CAMERA_FB_IN_PSRAM with fb_count=1 only in the non-PSRAM-DMA branch, and documents the tradeoff. The no-PSRAM branch (:411-422) correctly drops to CAMERA_FB_IN_DRAM and caps at QVGA. No first-party buffer is ever handed to the camera as a DMA target.
